General Science
From 1979-2025

UPSC PYQs - 1980
Q1. Skylab crashed on July 11, 1979 in
(a) Pacific Ocean
(b) Atlantic Ocean
(c) Mediterranean Sea
(d) Indian Ocean near Western Australia
The correct option is (d).
• Skylab was the first space station launched
by the United States, operational from 1973
to 1979.
• As its orbit decayed, NASA could not re-boost
it, resulting in an uncontrolled reentry on July
11, 1979.
• Though expected to fall into a remote ocean
region, debris scattered over Western
Australia and parts of the southeastern
Indian Ocean.
• The crash generated global media attention
and public curiosity about space debris safety.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. India has launched Symphonie Satellite
Telecommunication Experiment Project (STEP). It is a
joint project of the
(a) S and T Department and Indian Space Research
Organisation
(b) Ministry of Energy and Space Commission
(c) Ministry of Defence and Indian Space Research
Organisation
(d) Indian Space Research Organisation and Ministry
of Industry
The correct option is (a).
• The Satellite Telecommunications
Experiments Project (STEP) (1977–1979) was
a joint project between the Indian Space
Research Organisation (ISRO) and the Science
and Technology (S&T) Department—
specifically, the Posts and Telegraphs
Department under the Ministry of
Communications.
• It utilized the Symphonie satellite, a FrancoGerman communication satellite, to conduct
telecommunication experiments.

•

•

The project helped India develop expertise in
satellite-based communication, TV
broadcasting, and remote area connectivity,
forming the foundation for the INSAT
program.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1981

Q1. Put the following mass communication media in
the sequence in which they came to India:
1. Radio
2. Television
3. Films
4. Printing
Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
(a) 1, 3, 2, 4
(b) 1, 4, 3, 2
(c) 2, 4, 3, 1
(d) 4, 3, 1, 2
The correct option is (d).
• Printing: Introduced first by the Portuguese in
1556 at Goa, marking the beginning of mass
communication in India.
• Films: The Lumière brothers’ films were
screened in Bombay in 1896, and the first
Indian film, Raja Harishchandra, was made by
Dadasaheb Phalke in 1913.
• Radio: Broadcasting began in 1923 with the
Radio Club of Bombay; All India Radio (AIR)
was established in 1936.
• Television: Introduced later in 1959 with
experimental telecasts from Delhi; regular
daily broadcasts began in 1965.
• Thus, the correct chronological order is
Printing → Films → Radio → Television (4, 3,
1, 2).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Which of the following statements is true?
(a) Bhaskara and Aryabhata were put into orbit from a
Chinese Cosmodrome
(b) Bhaskara and Aryabhata were put into orbit from a
French cosmodrome
(c) Rohini II is going to be launched from Moscow

(d) Rohini, the third Indian satellite, was launched
from Sriharikota
The correct option is (d).
• Rohini RS-1, the third Indian satellite, was
launched on July 18, 1980, from Sriharikota,
India, using the SLV-3 launch vehicle.
• This successful mission marked India’s first
indigenous launch, making it the seventh
nation with orbital launch capability.
• Aryabhata (1975) and Bhaskara-I (1979) were
launched from Kapustin Yar, USSR, under the
Indo-Soviet space cooperation program.
• They were not launched from China or
France.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Total solar eclipse was observed in February
1980 in India at
(a) Delhi
(b) Puri
(c) Bombay
(d) Calcutta
The correct option is (b).
• The total solar eclipse occurred on February
16, 1980, with the path of totality passing
over southern and eastern India.
• The cities under or near the path of totality
included Puri (Odisha), Hyderabad, and parts
of Andhra Pradesh and Karnataka.
• Delhi, Bombay, and Calcutta experienced
only partial eclipses.
• Hence, based on astronomical records, the
correct answer is Puri.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Hybridisation is useful in developing improved
crops by
(a) Creating new and better genes
(b) Eliminating undesirable genes
(c) Producing favourable combination of genes
(d) By encouraging favourable genes and removing
unfavourable genes
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•
•

•

Hybridisation is the crossing of genetically
distinct plants to combine desirable traits
from both parents.
The process aims to produce a favourable
combination of genes, such as combining
high yield from one parent with disease
resistance from another.
It does not create new genes but rather
recombines existing ones.
The selection process following hybridisation
identifies and stabilizes the best
combinations.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1982

Q1.America's space shuttle is —
(a) Voyager
(b) Apollo
(c) Soyuz
(d) Columbia
Correct Option: (d)
• Columbia was the first operational space
shuttle of the United States (NASA).
o It was launched in 1981 under NASA’s
Space Shuttle Program.
o Tragically, Space Shuttle Columbia
was destroyed during re-entry in
2003, leading to the death of all seven
astronauts.
• Other options:
o (a) Voyager: An interstellar space
probe launched in 1977, not a
manned shuttle.
o (b) Apollo: A series of manned lunar
missions, e.g., Apollo 11 was the first
to land humans on the Moon (1969).
o (c) Soyuz: A Russian (Soviet)
spacecraft series, used to transport
astronauts to and from the
International Space Station (ISS).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1983
Q1.A radio station is to be set up in a remote village
without any electrical power. The following options
are available:
1. Laying transmission lines from a power station
to the village
2. Installing diesel generators
3. Using rechargeable batteries
4. Using solar panels/stills
Of these, the last method is an example of —
(a) Appropriate technology
(b) Ultimate technology
(c) Redundant technology
(d) Obsolete technology
Correct Option: (b)
• Ultimate technology refers to the most
advanced and sustainable form of technology
that directly transforms natural or renewable
sources into usable energy or power.
• Solar panels convert sunlight (solar energy)
directly into electricity using photovoltaic
cells, without relying on any conventional
power source.
• This reflects a transition from idea → natural
power → practical application, a hallmark of
ultimate technology.
• Other options:
o (a) Appropriate technology → smallscale, low-cost, suited for local needs.
o (c) Redundant technology → outdated
by newer advances.
o (d) Obsolete technology → no longer
in use or replaced.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2.In April 1982, INSAT-1A was launched from —
(a) Cape Canaveral
(b) A Soviet cosmodrome
(c) Sriharikota
(d) Thumba
Correct Option: (a)
• INSAT-1A (Indian National Satellite System)
was India’s first multipurpose satellite, used

for communication, meteorology, and
broadcasting.
• It was launched in April 1982 from Launch
Complex 17A at Cape Canaveral Air Force
Station, USA, using a Delta rocket.
• The satellite, however, failed after a few
months due to technical issues, but paved the
way for later satellites in the INSAT series.
• Other options:
o (b) Soviet cosmodrome → used for
Indian satellites launched on Soviet
rockets (e.g., Aryabhata, Bhaskara).
o (c) Sriharikota → India’s own launch
site for later satellites (ISRO’s Satish
Dhawan Space Centre).
o (d) Thumba → Sounding rocket launch
site, not for INSAT-type missions.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1984
Q1. Write in chronological order
1. 'Man's landing on moon
2. Discovery of radioactivity
3. First nuclear explosion
4. Launching of first satellite by India
The correct order is
(a) 3, 2, 4, 1
(b) 3, 2, 1, 4
(c) 3, 1, 4, 2
(d) 2, 3, 1, 4
The correct option is (d).
• Discovery of radioactivity (1896): Discovered
by Henri Becquerel, marking a major
breakthrough in atomic science.
• First nuclear explosion (1945): The Trinity
test was conducted by the United States on
July 16, 1945, during World War II.
• Man’s landing on the Moon (1969): On July
20, 1969, Neil Armstrong and Buzz Aldrin
became the first humans to walk on the Moon
during NASA’s Apollo 11 mission.
• India’s first satellite launch (1975):
Aryabhata, India’s first satellite, was launched
on April 19, 1975, from the Soviet Union.

•
•

Therefore, the chronological order is 2 → 3 →
1 → 4.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q2. The first geostationary satellite launched by
India is
(a) Aryabhata
(b) Bhaskara
(c) APPLE
(d) INSAT 1-B
The correct option is (c).
• A geostationary satellite orbits the Earth at
about 35,786 km above the equator,
appearing stationary relative to the surface.
• APPLE (Ariane Passenger Payload
Experiment) was India’s first experimental
geostationary communication satellite,
launched on June 19, 1981, by the European
Space Agency (ESA) using an Ariane rocket.
• Aryabhata (1975) and Bhaskara (1979, 1981)
were Low Earth Orbit (LEO) satellites.
• INSAT-1B (1983) was India’s first operational
geostationary satellite, but APPLE was the
first experimental one.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Which of the following discoveries is/are
associated with Einstein?
1. Theory of relativity
2. Quantum theory
3. Radioactivity
4. Rocket propulsion
The correct options are:
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 1, 2 and 3
(c) 1, 2 and 4
(d) 1 only
The correct option is (a).
• Theory of relativity: Proposed by Albert
Einstein in 1905 (Special) and 1915 (General),
revolutionizing our understanding of space,
time, and gravity.
• Quantum theory: Einstein contributed to it by
explaining the photoelectric effect (1905),
introducing the concept of light quanta

•

•

•
•

(photons) — for which he received the Nobel
Prize in 1921.
Radioactivity: Discovered by Henri Becquerel
(1896) and later studied by Marie and Pierre
Curie, not by Einstein.
Rocket propulsion: Based on Newton’s laws
of motion, with major contributions by
Robert Goddard, Tsiolkovsky, and Hermann
Oberth.
Hence, only (1) and (2) are associated with
Einstein.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q4. The Indian Space Research Organisation is
situated at
(a) Trivandrum
(b) Bangalore
(c) Ahmedabad
(d) Thumba
The correct option is (b).
• The Indian Space Research Organisation
(ISRO) has its headquarters in Bangalore
(Bengaluru), Karnataka, at Antariksh Bhavan.
• Bangalore is the administrative and
operational center of ISRO.
• Other key centers include:
o Vikram Sarabhai Space Centre
(VSSC): Trivandrum – responsible for
launch vehicle development.
o Space Applications Centre (SAC):
Ahmedabad – focuses on satellite
payloads and applications.
o Thumba Equatorial Rocket Launching
Station (TERLS): Thumba – used for
launching sounding rockets.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5.Chips used in integrated circuits in computers are
made of —
Options:
(a) Mica
(b) Manganese
(c) Silicon
(d) Zinc
Correct Option: (c)

•

Integrated Circuits (ICs), also called
microchips, are made on a small wafer of
semiconductor material, typically silicon.
• Silicon is used because:
o It is a semiconductor, meaning it can
conduct electricity under certain
conditions.
o It allows miniaturization of
components and reliable
performance.
o It forms stable silicon dioxide (SiO₂),
ideal for making insulating layers.
• ICs are used in computers, calculators,
mobile phones, and electronic devices.
• Types:
o Analog ICs, Digital ICs, and Mixedsignal ICs.
• Other options:
o (a) Mica → used as an insulator.
o (b) Manganese → used in alloys.
o (d) Zinc → used in galvanization, not
semiconductor technology.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6.India has decided to acquire _______ to match
Pakistan's F-16.
(a) Jaguars
(b) MiG-29
(c) Mirage-2000
(d) Harpoon
Correct Option: (b)
• India acquired MiG-29 fighter aircraft from
Russia to balance Pakistan’s F-16s obtained
from the USA.
• MiG-29 (Fulcrum) —
o A twin-engine, air-superiority fighter
jet developed by the Soviet Union.
o Known for its agility, speed, and
combat performance.
o Forms part of India’s IAF fleet since
the mid-1980s.
• Comparison:
o Pakistan’s F-16 Fighting Falcon →
American-made multirole fighter.

o

India’s response → MiG-29 provided
technological parity at that time.
• Other options:
o (a) Jaguars — ground-attack aircraft
(Anglo-French).
o (c) Mirage-2000 — inducted later for
precision bombing (e.g., Kargil
operations).
o (d) Harpoon — an anti-ship missile,
not a fighter.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7.India carried out its first underground nuclear
explosion at —
(a) Thumba
(b) Pokhran
(c) Trombay
(d) Khemkaran
Correct Option: (b)
• India’s first underground nuclear test was
conducted at Pokhran Test Range, Rajasthan.
• Date: 18 May 1974
• Codename: Smiling Buddha
• Prime Minister: Indira Gandhi
• Purpose: Described as a "Peaceful Nuclear
Explosion (PNE)", though it demonstrated
nuclear capability.
• Technical details:
o Yield: Claimed 12 kilotons (estimated
4–6 kt by U.S. intelligence).
o Depth: 107 meters underground
vertical shaft.
• Significance:
o Marked India’s entry into the nuclearcapable nations club.
o Paved the way for Pokhran-II tests in
1998 under PM Atal Bihari Vajpayee.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1985
Q1.State the chronological order in which the
following events took place:
1. Launching of APPLE
2. Launching of Rohini

3. Antarctica expedition
4. Indian cosmonaut
Codes:
(a) 3-1-2-4
(b) 2-3-1-4
(c) 2-1-3-4
(d) 3-2-1-4
Answer : (a)
• Rohini Satellite (RS-1): July 18, 1980 – India’s
first satellite placed into orbit by the
indigenous SLV-3 rocket.
• APPLE (Ariane Passenger Payload
Experiment): June 19, 1981 – India’s first
experimental communication satellite,
launched by Ariane rocket from Kourou,
French Guiana.
• Antarctica Expedition (Dakshin Gangotri):
December 1981 – India’s first scientific
expedition to Antarctica.
• Indian Cosmonaut (Rakesh Sharma): April 3,
1984 – First Indian in space aboard Soyuz T-11
with the Soviet crew.
Chronologically:
2 (Rohini 1980) → 1 (APPLE 1981) → 3 (Antarctica
1981) → 4 (Cosmonaut 1984)
• The official key (a) 3-1-2-4 is historically
incorrect, likely a misprint in the original
source.
• The accurate sequence based on verified
dates is (c) 2-1-3-4.
Hence, option (c) is factually correct, though (a)
appears in the old answer key.
Q2.What is tissue culture?
(a) Preparation of fragments of the cells of organism
for biochemical examination
(b) Japanese culture
(c) Name given to a special type of surgery
(d) None of the above
Correct Option: (a)
• Tissue culture is the growth of living cells or
tissues in an artificial nutrient medium under
controlled conditions.
• It involves the preparation of small fragments
of cells taken from a plant or animal and

allowing them to grow or multiply in a
laboratory environment.
• Used in:
o Plant propagation, biomedical
research, and genetic engineering.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3.Genetic code is made up by the four-letter
alphabet forming the pairs —
Options:
(a) A–C, C–A, T–G, G–T
(b) A–G, G–A, C–A, T–C
(c) T–G, G–T, A–C, C–A
(d) A–T, T–A, C–G, G–C
Correct Option: (d)
• DNA (Deoxyribonucleic acid) is a doublestranded molecule with complementary base
pairing:
o Adenine (A) always pairs with
Thymine (T)
o Cytosine (C) always pairs with
Guanine (G)
• The pairing rule is due to hydrogen bonding:
o A = T (2 hydrogen bonds)
o C ≡ G (3 hydrogen bonds)
• These pairs form the genetic code that stores
hereditary information.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4.Which of the following is correct for tea?
(a) Alkaline and nicotine
(b) Alkaline and caffeine
(c) Acidic and nicotine
(d) Acidic and caffeine
Correct Option: (d)
• Tea contains caffeine, a natural stimulant
alkaloid.
• Freshly brewed tea has a slightly acidic pH
(~4.9), depending on the brewing strength —
longer brewing lowers the pH further.
• Nicotine is found in tobacco, not in tea.
• Therefore, tea is acidic and contains caffeine.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1986
Q1.Interleukin, recently discovered, is a cure for —
(a) Arthritis
(b) Diabetes
(c) Cancer
(d) Influenza
Correct Option: (c)
• Interleukins (ILs) are a group of cytokines —
small signaling proteins released by white
blood cells (leukocytes) that regulate immune
responses.
• They play a key role in cell communication,
inflammation, and activation of immune
cells.
• Certain interleukins (like IL-2, IL-12, IL-15)
have been found to stimulate the immune
system to attack cancer cells.
• IL-2 (Interleukin-2), in particular, is used as an
immunotherapy treatment for cancers such
as renal cell carcinoma and melanoma.
• Thus, interleukin is being studied and used as
a potential cure or treatment aid for cancer.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2.Which of the following statements is incorrect?
(a) Entropy is a quantity introduced to give clear
expression to the results of thermodynamics.
(b) Change in entropy is defined as the ratio of the
amount of heat taken up to the absolute
temperature.
(c) Change in entropy is directly proportional to the
amount of heat taken up.
(d) The entropy of a system is a measure of its degree
of disorder.
Correct Option: (d)
• (a) Correct — Entropy was introduced as a
thermodynamic quantity to mathematically
express the Second Law of Thermodynamics,
describing the direction of natural processes.
• (b) Correct — For a reversible process,
𝑑𝑄𝑟𝑒𝑣
𝑑𝑆 =
𝑇
where dS = change in entropy, dQ = reversible heat
absorbed, T = absolute temperature.

•

(c) Correct — At constant temperature, ΔS ∝
Q, i.e., entropy change is directly
proportional to heat absorbed.
• (d) Incorrect (in classical thermodynamics
context) —
o While modern statistical mechanics
interprets entropy as a measure of
disorder, in classical thermodynamics
it is defined quantitatively in terms of
heat and temperature, not
“disorder.”
o Hence, though the statement is
conceptually true in the microscopic
sense, it’s not the thermodynamic
definition of entropy used in this
context.
Hence, option (d) is correct (as the incorrect
statement in this question).
UPSC PYQs - 1987
Q1.The hybrids of which of the following plants are
available for commercial production?
1. Rice
2. Wheat
3. Jute
4. Pulses
Codes:
(a) 1, 2 and 4
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 3 and 4
(d) 2, 3 and 4
Correct Option: (a)
• Hybrid variety means crossing two genetically
different plants to produce offspring with
improved traits such as higher yield, disease
resistance, and adaptability.
• (1) Rice — Yes
o India is one of the largest producers
of hybrid rice in the world.
o Hybrid rice varieties have shown 15–
20% higher yield than traditional
varieties.
o Example: KRH-1, PHB-71, and Arize
6444.

•

(2) Wheat — Yes
o Hybrid wheat varieties have been
developed and used commercially in
limited areas.
o Example: Pusa Hybrid Wheat (PHW65).
• (3) Jute — No
o Hybrid jute varieties were not
available for large-scale commercial
production until very recently.
o Traditional breeding dominated the
jute sector.
• (4) Pulses — Yes
o Hybrid varieties have been developed
in some pulses like pigeon pea
(arhar/tur) and mung bean, though
adoption remains limited.
Hence, option (a) — 1, 2 and 4 — is correct.
Q2.Which of the following was the first to escape out
of the Solar System?
(a) Pioneer 10
(b) Voyager I
(c) Voyager II
(d) Soyuz
Correct Option: (b)
• Voyager 1 was launched by NASA on
September 5, 1977 to study the outer planets
and beyond.
• It became the first spacecraft to enter
interstellar space in August 2012, officially
crossing the heliopause — the boundary
where the Sun’s solar wind no longer
dominates.
• It remains the farthest human-made object
from Earth, traveling over 24 billion
kilometers as of 2025.
• Other missions:
o Pioneer 10: Launched in 1972; first to
cross the asteroid belt and reach
Jupiter, but lost contact in 2003
before reaching interstellar space.
o Voyager II: Launched in 1977, also
exited the heliosphere, but after
Voyager 1 (in 2018).

o

Soyuz: Russian manned spacecraft,
operates in Earth orbit, not
interplanetary space.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1988
Q1.Match the Columns
List I (Forms) List II (Quantities)
A. Cusec

1. Pressure

B. Byte

2. Intensity of earthquakes

C. Richter

3. Rate of flow

D. Bar

4. Computer memory

Codes:
ABCD
(a) 4 1 2 3
(b) 2 4 1 3
(c) 3 4 2 1
(d) 3 2 1 4
Correct Option: (c)
• Cusec: Unit of flow = 1 cubic foot per second
→ measures rate of flow of fluids.
• Byte: Basic unit of digital memory (8 bits) →
measures computer memory.
• Richter: Scale for measuring intensity
(magnitude) of earthquakes.
• Bar: Unit of pressure, equivalent to
105 N/m2.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2.Match the Columns
List I (Instruments) List II (What they measure)
A. Anemometer

1. Speed of rotation

B. Ammeter

2. High temperatures

C. Tachometer

3. Wind speed
4. Electric current

D. Pyrometer
5. Pressure difference
Select the correct answer from the codes given
below:
Codes:

ABCD
(a) 4 3 5 1
(b) 3 4 1 2
(c) 3 5 4 1
(d) 1 2 5 4
Correct Option: (b)
• Anemometer → Measures wind speed or air
current velocity.
• Ammeter → Measures electric current (in
amperes).
• Tachometer → Measures rotational speed
(e.g., RPM in engines).
• Pyrometer → Measures very high
temperatures (e.g., furnaces, kilns).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3.The Indian Air Force has MIG planes. The most
advanced version of MIG at present is called —
(a) MIG-25
(b) MIG-27
(c) MIG-29
(d) MIG-31
Correct Option: (c)
• MiG-29 (Fulcrum): Twin-engine airsuperiority fighter developed by the Soviet
Union.
• Inducted into the Indian Air Force in 1985,
known for high maneuverability and speed
(Mach 2.25).
• Equipped for multirole missions including air
defense and precision strikes.
• Other options:
o MiG-25: High-altitude reconnaissance
aircraft.
o MiG-27: Ground-attack aircraft.
o MiG-31: Interceptor version, not in
Indian service.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4.A recently developed technique for monitoring
foetal growth uses —
(a) X-rays
(b) Microwaves
(c) Ultrasonics
(d) Ultraviolet rays

Correct Option: (c)
• Ultrasonics (Ultrasound): Uses highfrequency sound waves to create images of
the foetus.
• It helps monitor:
o Foetal heart rate and heartbeat.
o Position and presentation of foetus.
o Size, growth, and blood flow through
umbilical cord.
• Safe and non-invasive, unlike X-rays or UV
rays.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5.A balloon filled with air is weighed (W) so that it
just floats in water. When it is pushed slightly deeper
into water, it will —

(a) Sink to the bottom
(b) Stay at that depth
(c) Come back to its original position
(d) Sink a little further but not reach the bottom
Correct Option: (c)
• A floating balloon experiences buoyant force
= weight of displaced water.
• When pushed downward:
o Volume and density of water
displaced increase slightly.
o Buoyant force > Weight, causing the
balloon to rise back.
• This stable equilibrium ensures it returns to
the original floating position.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6.The major ingredient of leather is —
(a) Collagen
(b) Polymer

(c) Carbohydrate
(d) Nucleic Acid
Correct Option: (a)
• Collagen is a fibrous structural protein found
in animal tissues — skin, tendons, bones,
cartilage.
• Leather is made by tanning animal hides,
which stabilizes collagen fibers to prevent
decomposition.
• Collagen provides strength, flexibility, and
durability to leather.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1990
Q1. For developing inbred lines in farm animals, the
following factors are taken into consideration:
1. The crossbreds should be of outstanding type.
2. They should not carry any detrimental genes.
3. Progeny tested animals.
4. Herd size should be large.
Of the statements given above, which one of the
following combinations of factors is most essential?
(a) 1 and 3
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 4
(d) 2 and 4
The correct option is (d).
• Inbreeding is the mating of closely related
individuals to fix desirable traits from a
superior ancestor.
• Factor 2: Animals must not carry detrimental
genes, as inbreeding can magnify harmful
recessive traits, leading to inbreeding
depression.
• Factor 4: A large herd size allows adequate
selection options, helps maintain genetic
diversity, and reduces the risk of genetic
bottleneck.
• While “outstanding type” (1) and “progeny
tested animals” (3) are desirable, they are not
as essential as ensuring genetic soundness
and population stability.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q2. An asteroid recently discovered by the scientists
of the Indian Institute of Astrophysics has been
named after:
(a) Venu Bappu
(b) Indira Gandhi
(c) C. V. Raman
(d) S. Ramanujam
The correct option is (a).
• Asteroid 2599 Bappu, discovered in 1977, was
named in honor of Manali Kallat Vainu Bappu
(M. K. Vainu Bappu).
• Vainu Bappu is known as the Father of
Modern Indian Astronomy and was the first
Director of the Indian Institute of
Astrophysics (IIA).
• The Vainu Bappu Observatory (VBO) at
Kavalur, Tamil Nadu, is also named after him.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following effects of genetic
engineering:
1. Disease resistance
2. Growth promotion
3. Cloning
4. Longevity
Of these effects, those that have been tried out with a
certain amount of success include:
(a) 1, 3 and 4
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1, 2 and 4
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Disease resistance (1): Successfully
demonstrated in genetically modified crops
such as Bt cotton and Bt corn, which produce
insecticidal proteins to resist pests.
• Growth promotion (2): Achieved in animals
like AquAdvantage salmon, genetically
engineered to grow faster using an introduced
growth hormone gene.
• Cloning (3): The first successful mammalian
clone, Dolly the sheep (1996), proved the
viability of cloning in higher organisms.
• Longevity (4): While genes linked to lifespan
have been identified, no practical or

•

•

consistent longevity extension in complex
animals has yet been achieved.
Therefore, disease resistance, growth
promotion, and cloning have been tried out
successfully.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1991

Q1. Which one of the following techniques has
helped to solve many mysteries involving murders,
robberies, and rapes?
(a) Gene splicing
(b) Computer tomography
(c) DNA fingerprinting
(d) Gene cloning
The correct option is (c).
• DNA fingerprinting, also known as DNA
profiling, is a forensic technique used to
identify individuals by characteristics of their
DNA.
• A person's DNA is unique (except for identical
twins).
• This technique analyzes specific regions of
DNA called short tandem repeats (STRs) or
variable number tandem repeats (VNTRs),
which vary greatly from person to person.
• In criminal investigations, DNA samples (such
as blood, semen, saliva, or hair) collected
from a crime scene can be compared to the
DNA profile of a suspect.
• A match can provide very strong evidence
linking the suspect to the crime.
• It is also used to resolve paternity disputes.
• Gene splicing and cloning are genetic
engineering techniques, while computer
tomography is a medical imaging method.
Hence correct option is (c).
Q2. On a half-moon night, the angle between the
moon and the sun when measured from the earth is
about:
(a) 45°
(b) 60°

(c) 90°
(d) 180°
The correct option is (c).
• The phases of the Moon are determined by
the relative positions of the Sun, Earth, and
Moon.
• We see the Moon because it reflects sunlight.
• A half-moon (also known as a first quarter or
third quarter moon) occurs when exactly half
of the Moon's visible surface is illuminated by
the Sun, as seen from Earth.
• For this to happen, the Sun, Earth, and Moon
must form a right-angled triangle, with the
Earth at the vertex of the right angle.
• Therefore, the angle between the Sun and
the Moon, when measured from our
perspective on Earth, is approximately 90
degrees.
• A 180° angle corresponds to a Full Moon
(Earth is between the Sun and Moon), and a
0° angle corresponds to a New Moon (Moon
is between the Sun and Earth).
Hence correct option is (c).
Q3. Which one of the following is not a satellite of
Jupiter?
(a) Io
(b) Callisto
(c) Europa
(d) Titan
The correct option is (d).
• Jupiter has a large system of moons, with the
four largest being the Galilean moons,
discovered by Galileo Galilei.
• Io, Europa, and Callisto are three of these
four Galilean moons and are indeed satellites
of Jupiter (the fourth is Ganymede).
• Titan, however, is the largest moon of the
planet Saturn.
• It is the second-largest moon in the solar
system and is unique for having a dense,
planet-like atmosphere.
Hence correct option is (d).

Q4. Tomahawk, a cruise missile, was used in:
(a) World War II
(b) Iran-Iraq War
(c) Gulf War
(d) World War I
The correct option is (c).
• The Tomahawk Land Attack Missile (TLAM) is
a long-range, all-weather, subsonic cruise
missile.
• It was developed in the 1970s and entered
service with the U.S. Navy in the 1980s.
• Its first major combat use, where it gained
widespread public recognition, was during the
1991 Gulf War (Operation Desert Storm).
• Hundreds of Tomahawk missiles were
launched from U.S. Navy ships and
submarines to strike high-value targets in Iraq
and Kuwait with great precision, playing a key
role in the initial stages of the air campaign.
• It was not in service during the World Wars or
the Iran-Iraq War.
Hence correct option is (c).
UPSC PYQs - 1993
Q1. Which one of the diagrams given below
represents most closely the path of a geostationary
satellite in space?

The correct option is (b).
• A geostationary satellite appears fixed over
one point on Earth when observed from the
ground.
• To achieve this, it must:
o Orbit directly above the equator.
o Have an orbital period of exactly 24
hours (same as Earth’s rotation).

o

•
•

Follow a circular orbit, not an
elliptical one.
Diagram (a) shows an inclined orbit, (c) a polar
orbit, and (d) an elliptical orbit.
Diagram (b) correctly shows a circular orbit in
the equatorial plane, which is characteristic
of a geostationary satellite.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. If a mouse of over eight times its normal size has
been produced by introducing a human growth
hormone gene, the technique involved is called:
(a) Hybridisation
(b) Genetic engineering
(c) Mutation breeding
(d) Hormonal feeding
The correct option is (b).
• The described process involves inserting a
human gene (growth hormone) into a mouse
genome to give it a new characteristic —
increased growth rate.
• This transfer of genes across species is the
core of genetic engineering (also called
recombinant DNA technology).
• Hybridisation (a) is cross-breeding between
species or varieties.
• Mutation breeding (c) uses chemicals or
radiation to induce random mutations.
• Hormonal feeding (d) involves giving
hormones externally, not inserting genes.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following approaches comes
under the category of biotechnology?

1. Use of living organisms or substances
obtained from them in industrial processes.
2. Modernizing commercial industries to
produce products for use in biological
research.
3. Use of modern technology to investigate
biological disorders.
4. Use of industrial technology to increase the
biosphere.
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 1, 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1 and 2
(d) 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1: Correct – Biotechnology uses
living organisms or their products in
industrial processes (e.g., fermentation,
antibiotic production, enzyme synthesis).
• Statement 2: Correct – It includes industrial
applications for producing bioresearch tools
and biological reagents.
• Statement 3: Correct – Modern
biotechnological tools (e.g., PCR, ELISA, DNA
sequencing) are used to diagnose biological
disorders.
• Statement 4: Incorrect – “Increasing the
biosphere” is not a recognized goal of
biotechnology.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following seeds can benefit a
patient of diabetes mellitus by normalizing his blood
sugar level?
(a) Coriander seeds
(b) Mustard seeds
(c) Cumin seeds
(d) Fenugreek seeds
The correct option is (d).
• Fenugreek seeds (Trigonella foenumgraecum) contain soluble fiber that slows
carbohydrate absorption, helping control
blood sugar levels.
• They also improve insulin sensitivity and may
stimulate insulin secretion.

•
•

These properties make them useful for
managing diabetes mellitus.
Other seeds listed (a–c) have no significant
glucose-regulating properties.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q5. Scratching eases itching because:
(a) it removes the outer dust in the skin
(b) it kills the germs
(c) it stimulates certain nerves which direct the brain
to increase the production of antihistaminic chemicals
(d) it suppresses the production of enzymes which
cause itching
The correct option is (c).
• The itch sensation and pain sensation travel
through related nerve pathways.
• When we scratch, it causes mild pain signals
that reach the brain faster and override the
itch sensation, offering temporary relief.
• This process is related to neural interference,
not actual antihistamine production.
• Options (a), (b), and (d) are incorrect —
scratching doesn’t clean, kill germs, or affect
enzymes directly.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. Haemophilia is a hereditary disease carried by:
(a) females and expressed by females
(b) females and expressed by males
(c) males and expressed by females
(d) males and expressed by males
The correct option is (b).
• Haemophilia is an X-linked recessive genetic
disorder affecting blood clotting.
• Females (XX) can carry the defective gene on
one X chromosome without showing
symptoms.
• Males (XY) who inherit the defective gene
express the disease because they lack a
second X chromosome to compensate.
• Thus, haemophilia is carried by females and
expressed by males.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q7. The blood pressure values of four persons are
given below:
(1) Mrs. X : 90/60
(2) Mr. X : 160/120
(3) Mr. Y : 120/80
(4) Mrs. Y : 140/100
Who among the following has normal blood pressure?
(a) Mrs. X
(b) Mr. X
(c) Mr. Y
(d) Mrs. Y
The correct option is (c).
• Normal blood pressure is 120/80 mmHg.
• Mrs. X (90/60): Low BP (hypotension).
• Mr. X (160/120): Very high BP (severe
hypertension).
• Mr. Y (120/80): Normal BP — ideal standard.
• Mrs. Y (140/100): Hypertension (Stage 1 or 2).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. Biofilms are:
(a) colour films used by biologists to photograph living
organisms
(b) photomicrographs of microorganisms from various
habitats
(c) accumulations of microorganisms at surfaces such
as those of a rock, a tooth or an oil droplet
(d) photographic films made through fermentation
processes of certain industrial microorganisms
The correct option is (c).
• A biofilm is a community of microorganisms
(bacteria, fungi, algae) that adhere to surfaces
and are embedded in a self-produced slimy
matrix (EPS).
• Found on rocks, teeth (plaque), medical
devices, and industrial pipelines.
• They protect microbes from environmental
stress and antibiotics.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9. In many developing countries, three major
causes of death and disease are:
(a) lack of safe drinking water, increasing use of
pesticides and thinning of the ozone layer
(b) contaminated food, global warming and industrial

chloro-fluoro-carbons
(c) polluted air, greenhouse effect and soil erosion
(d) dirty water, contaminated food and polluted air
The correct option is (d).
• Dirty water: Causes cholera, typhoid,
dysentery, and diarrheal diseases.
• Contaminated food: Leads to food poisoning
and malnutrition.
• Polluted air: Causes respiratory infections,
asthma, cardiovascular diseases, and lung
cancer.
• These are direct and immediate threats to
human health in developing countries.
• Issues like ozone thinning or global warming
are long-term and less directly fatal.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1995
Q1. In the field of space technology, India has
demonstrated during 1994, her capability to:
(a) design advanced intercontinental ballistic missile
warning systems
(b) build satellites
(c) build stealth missiles
(d) launch geo-stationary satellites
The correct option is (d).
• The key milestone for India in 1994 was the
successful launch of the Polar Satellite
Launch Vehicle (PSLV-D2) on October 15,
1994.
• This marked India’s first major success in
launching a home-built satellite (IRS-P2) using
a domestically developed launch vehicle — a
crucial step toward the capability to launch
geostationary satellites.
• While the PSLV launches satellites into polar
and sun-synchronous orbits, this success
demonstrated the technological readiness
that later enabled the development of the
GSLV (Geosynchronous Satellite Launch
Vehicle) — capable of placing satellites in
geostationary orbit.

•

•

•

India had already been building satellites
(Aryabhata in 1975, Bhaskara in 1979), so (b)
was not a new 1994 achievement.
(a) and (c) relate to defense technologies
(missile warning and stealth systems), not
civilian space achievements.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q2. Comet Shoemaker-Levy 9 hit the planet:
(a) Pluto
(b) Mars
(c) Jupiter
(d) Saturn
The correct option is (c).
• Comet Shoemaker-Levy 9 (D/1993 F2) was
discovered in March 1993 by Eugene and
Carolyn Shoemaker and David Levy.
• The comet was captured by Jupiter’s gravity
and eventually broke into more than 20
fragments due to tidal forces.
• Between July 16 and July 22, 1994, these
fragments collided with Jupiter’s
atmosphere, creating massive explosion scars
visible even from Earth-based telescopes.
• This event was the first direct observation of
an extraterrestrial collision in the solar
system — a landmark in astronomy.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Cryogenic engines find applications in:
(a) sub-marine propulsion
(b) frost-free refrigerators
(c) rocket technology
(d) researches in superconductivity
The correct option is (c).
• A cryogenic engine (or cryogenic rocket
engine) uses super-cooled liquid propellants
— typically liquid hydrogen (LH₂) as fuel and
liquid oxygen (LOX) as oxidizer.
• These are stored at extremely low
temperatures (cryogenic conditions):
o Liquid oxygen: below –183°C
o Liquid hydrogen: below –253°C
• The use of cryogenic propellants provides
high efficiency (specific impulse), making

•

•

•

them ideal for upper stages of rockets that
need to launch heavy payloads into
geostationary or deep-space orbits.
India’s GSLV (Geosynchronous Satellite
Launch Vehicle) uses such a cryogenic upper
stage.
The other options are irrelevant:
o (a) Submarines use nuclear or dieselelectric propulsion.
o (b) Refrigerators use compressorbased cooling, not cryogenic engines.
o (d) Superconductivity research
involves low temperatures but not
propulsion systems.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1996

Q1. Which one of the following is a modern tank?
(a) Bhim
(b) Akash
(c) Arjun
(d) Prithvi
The correct option is (c).
• The Arjun is India's indigenous thirdgeneration Main Battle Tank (MBT)
developed by the Defence Research and
Development Organisation (DRDO) for the
Indian Army.
• The project began in the 1970s, and the tank
entered service in the 2000s, making it a
modern tank in the context of this question.
• It is designed to provide the army with a
state-of-the-art tank with superior firepower,
high mobility, and excellent protection.
• Bhim is a self-propelled howitzer, not a main
battle tank.
• Akash is a medium-range, surface-to-air
missile system developed by DRDO.
• Prithvi is a tactical surface-to-surface shortrange ballistic missile, not a tank.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. Which one of the following satellites is to be
launched from India in 1996?

(a) IRS-P2
(b) IRS-P3
(c) IRS-1C
(d) IRS-1D
The correct option is (b).
• IRS-P3 was an experimental remote sensing
satellite built by the Indian Space Research
Organisation (ISRO).
• It was successfully launched on March 21,
1996, aboard a Polar Satellite Launch Vehicle
(PSLV-D3) rocket from the Satish Dhawan
Space Centre, Sriharikota, India.
• Its mission was to test various imaging
sensors for future satellite applications.
• IRS-P2 was launched earlier, in October 1994.
• IRS-1C was launched in December 1995 from
Baikonur Cosmodrome on a Russian rocket.
• IRS-1D was launched later, in September
1997.
• Therefore, IRS-P3 was the satellite launched
from India in 1996.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1997
Q1. The world's fastest computer has been able to
perform (as of Dec. 1996):
(a) 10⁶ operations per second
(b) 10⁹ operations per second
(c) 10¹² operations per second
(d) 10¹⁵ operations per second
The correct option is (c).
• Computer performance is measured in FLOPS
(Floating-point Operations Per Second).
• In December 1996, the world’s fastest
supercomputer was the ASCI Red, developed
by Intel for the U.S. Department of Energy.
• It was the first computer to break the
teraflop barrier, meaning it could perform
over one trillion (10¹²) calculations per
second.
• For comparison:
o 10⁶ FLOPS = 1 Megaflop
o 10⁹ FLOPS = 1 Gigaflop
o 10¹² FLOPS = 1 Teraflop

o

•

10¹⁵ FLOPS = 1 Petaflop (achieved
about a decade later)
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. Which one of the following conditions is most
relevant for the presence of life on Mars?
(a) Atmospheric composition
(b) Thermal conditions
(c) Occurrence of ice caps and frozen water
(d) Occurrence of ozone
The correct option is (c).
• The presence of water is the most crucial
requirement for life as we know it.
• While Mars’s atmosphere is too thin and its
temperatures too low for stable liquid water,
frozen water and ice caps have been
confirmed.
• These findings suggest that Mars may have
been warmer and wetter in the past, possibly
supporting microbial life.
• It also raises the possibility of subsurface
liquid water existing today.
• The atmospheric composition, thermal
conditions, and lack of an ozone layer make
Mars hostile to life, but the existence of
frozen water remains the strongest evidence
for potential life.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. The sequencing of the entire genome (the
totality of all genes) of an organism was completed
in 1996. The organism was:
(a) Albino mouse
(b) Yeast
(c) Human being
(d) Plasmodium vivax
The correct option is (b).
• In 1996, scientists completed the sequencing
of the entire genome of the yeast
Saccharomyces cerevisiae, also known as
baker’s yeast.
• This was the first complete genome sequence
of a eukaryotic organism (organisms with a
nucleus in their cells).

•

•

•

Yeast was chosen because it is a simple,
single-celled eukaryote and an important
model organism in genetics and molecular
biology.
The project was a major step toward
sequencing more complex genomes,
including the human genome (completed in
2003).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2000

Q1. The memory of a computer is commonly
expressed in terms of Kilobytes or Megabytes. A byte
is made up of:
(a) eight binary digits
(b) eight decimal digits
(c) two binary digits
(d) two decimal digits
The correct option is (a).
• The fundamental unit of information in
computing is a bit, which represents a single
binary digit — either 0 or 1.
• A byte is a standard unit of digital information
that consists of eight binary digits (bits).
• This group of 8 bits is the smallest
addressable unit of memory in many
computer architectures.
• Larger memory units such as kilobytes (KB),
megabytes (MB), and gigabytes (GB) are
multiples of bytes.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. For reproducing sound, a CD (Compact Disc)
audio player uses a:
(a) quartz crystal
(b) titanium needle
(c) laser beam
(d) barium titanate ceramic
The correct option is (c).
• A Compact Disc (CD) stores digital audio as
microscopic pits and lands on its reflective
surface.
• A CD player reads this data using a highly
focused laser beam.

•

•

•
•

When the laser beam hits a land, it reflects
strongly to the detector; when it hits a pit, the
reflection weakens.
The player’s electronics interpret these
changes as binary data (1s and 0s), which is
then converted to analog sound.
Unlike vinyl record players, CDs use optical
reading, not physical contact.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q3. Which one of the following organisms can serve
as a biofertiliser for a rice crop?
(a) Blue-green algae
(b) Rhizobium sp.
(c) Mycorrhizal fungi
(d) Azotobacter sp.
The correct option is (a).
• Biofertilizers are living microorganisms that
enhance nutrient availability to plants.
• Rice fields are flooded environments,
favoring the growth of blue-green algae
(cyanobacteria).
• Many species of cyanobacteria can fix
atmospheric nitrogen, converting it into
forms usable by plants.
• Rhizobium forms nodules with legumes, not
rice.
• Azotobacter is a free-living nitrogen-fixer, but
less effective in flooded soils.
• Mycorrhizal fungi help in phosphorus uptake,
not nitrogen fixation.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. Insect-resistant cotton plants have been
genetically engineered by inserting a gene from
a/an:
(a) virus
(b) bacterium
(c) insect
(d) plant
The correct option is (b).
• Bt cotton is an insect-resistant variety
developed through genetic engineering.

•

•

•
•

The “Bt” refers to the bacterium Bacillus
thuringiensis, which produces Cry proteins
toxic to specific insect larvae.
The Cry gene from this bacterium is inserted
into the cotton plant’s DNA, enabling the
plant to produce its own natural insecticide.
These plants are highly resistant to bollworms
and other pests.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. Hybridoma technology is a new biotechnological
approach for the commercial production of:
(a) monoclonal antibodies
(b) interferon
(c) antibodies
(d) alcohol
The correct option is (a).
• Hybridoma technology allows for the
production of large quantities of identical
(monoclonal) antibodies.
• It involves fusing a normal antibodyproducing B-lymphocyte with a cancerous
myeloma cell.
• The resulting hybridoma can both produce a
specific antibody and multiply indefinitely.
• Because all hybridoma cells are clones, the
antibodies they produce are identical, called
monoclonal antibodies (mAbs).
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2001
Q1. The range of the Agni-II missile is around:
(a) 500 km
(b) 2000 km
(c) 3500 km
(d) 5000 km
The correct option is (b).
• The Agni missile series is a family of medium
to intercontinental-range ballistic missiles
developed by India's Defence Research and
Development Organisation (DRDO). Each
successive version in the series generally has a
longer range and more advanced capabilities.

•

The Agni-II is a medium-range ballistic missile
and is an improvement over the Agni-I.
• Its operational range is generally stated to be
between 2,000 km and 3,500 km, depending
on the payload weight. The most commonly
cited figure for its standard range is around
2,000 km.
• Agni-I has a shorter range (around 700-1200
km), while Agni-III, Agni-IV, and Agni-V have
progressively longer ranges, with Agni-V being
an Intercontinental Ballistic Missile (ICBM)
with a range exceeding 5,000 km.
• Given the options, 2,000 km is the most
accurate and standard figure for the Agni-II's
range.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2002
Q1. With reference to the latest developments in
stem cell research, consider the following
statements:
1. The only source of human stem cells are the
embryos at the blastocyst stage.
2. The stem cells can be derived without causing
destruction to blastocysts.
3. The stem cells can regenerate themselves in
vitro virtually forever.
4. Indian research centres also created a few cell
lines which can be developed into many types
of tissues.
Which of these statements are correct?
(a) 1, 2, 3 and 4
(b) 1, 2 and 3
(c) 1, 2 and 4
(d) 3 and 4
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect — embryonic stem
cells are not the only source; adult stem cells
also exist in tissues like bone marrow, blood,
and fat.
• Statement 2 is incorrect — at that time,
deriving embryonic stem cells required
destroying the blastocyst. Non-destructive
methods came later.

•

•

•

Statement 3 is correct — pluripotent stem
cells can self-renew indefinitely in vitro
under suitable conditions.
Statement 4 is correct — Indian research
centers had successfully created human stem
cell lines that could develop into various
tissue types.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q2. In the year 2001, NASA launched a spacecraft to
Mars named:
(a) Mars Climate Orbiter
(b) Mars Global Surveyor
(c) Mars Odyssey
(d) Mars Polar Lander
The correct option is (c).
• Mars Odyssey was launched on April 7, 2001,
and entered Mars orbit on October 24, 2001.
• Its mission was to study water and ice
presence, analyze surface composition, and
measure radiation on Mars.
• Mars Climate Orbiter and Mars Polar Lander
(1990s) both failed.
• Mars Global Surveyor (1997–2006) was
another successful but earlier mission.
