Q4. With reference to Indian satellites and their
launchers, consider the following statements:
1. All the INSAT-series of satellites were
launched abroad.
2. PSLVs were used to launch IRS-series of
satellites.
3. India used the indigenously built cryogenic
engines for the first time for powering the
third stage of GSLV.
4. GSAT, launched in the year 2001, has payloads
to demonstrate digital broadcasts and
internet services.
Which of these statements are correct?
(a) 1, 2, 3 and 4
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1, 2 and 4
(d) 1 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct — early INSAT
satellites were launched abroad using Ariane
(France) and U.S. launchers.
• Statement 2 is correct — PSLVs launched the
IRS series into polar sun-synchronous orbits.
• Statement 3 is incorrect — initial GSLV flights
used Russian cryogenic engines; India’s own
was first tested in 2014 (GSLV-D5).
• Statement 4 is correct — GSAT-1 (2001)
tested digital audio, data, and video
broadcasting technologies.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. Assertion (A): Artificial satellites are always
launched from the earth in the eastward direction.
Reason (R): The earth rotates from west to east and
so the satellite easily attains the escape velocity.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is false — satellites are not
always launched eastward; polar orbits use
north–south directions.
• Reason (R) is true — Earth’s rotation from
west to east provides a velocity boost,
reducing the fuel requirement.
• However, the launch aims for orbital velocity,
not escape velocity (which is needed to leave
Earth’s gravity entirely).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2003
Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. India launched its first full-fledged
meteorological satellite (METSAT) in
September, 2002.
2. For the first time, the space vehicle PSLV-C4
carried a payload of more than 1000 kg into a
geosynchronous orbit.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) Only 1
(b) Only 2
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: On September 12,
2002, ISRO launched METSAT, India’s first
dedicated meteorological satellite, marking a
significant milestone in India’s weather
monitoring capabilities. It was later renamed
Kalpana-1 in honor of astronaut Dr. Kalpana
Chawla.

•

•

•
•

Statement 2 is correct: The launch used PSLVC4, which for the first time placed a 1060 kg
payload (METSAT) into a Geosynchronous
Transfer Orbit (GTO).
This was a key achievement since PSLV had
primarily been used for polar and low Earth
orbits until then.
Hence, both statements are factually correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. The Genetic Engineering Approval Committee,
whose permission is required for cultivation of any
genetically modified crop such as Bt-Cotton in India,
is under the Union Ministry of:
(a) Agriculture
(b) Environment and Forests
(c) Commerce and Industry
(d) Rural Development
The correct option is (b).
• The Genetic Engineering Appraisal
Committee (GEAC) is the apex regulatory
authority for genetically modified organisms
(GMOs) in India.
• It was established under the Rules for the
Manufacture, Use, Import, Export and
Storage of Hazardous
Microorganisms/Genetically Engineered
Organisms or Cells (1989), framed under the
Environment (Protection) Act, 1986.
• Because it operates under this Act, it
functions under the Ministry of Environment,
Forest and Climate Change (MoEFCC),
formerly known as the Ministry of
Environment and Forests.
• The GEAC is responsible for approving largescale use, field trials, and commercial release
of genetically modified crops like Bt-Cotton.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2004
Q1. Consider the following statements:
(1) Adam Osborne produced the first portable
computer
(2) Ian Wilmut created the first cloned sheep.

(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement (1) is correct: Adam Osborne
founded the Osborne Computer Corporation
and introduced the Osborne 1 in 1981, which
became the first commercially successful
portable computer.
o It weighed about 24 pounds, had a 5inch display, and could be carried like
a suitcase, making it revolutionary for
its time.
• Statement (2) is correct: Sir Ian Wilmut, a
British embryologist at the Roslin Institute
(Scotland), led the team that created Dolly
the sheep in 1996, the first mammal cloned
from an adult somatic cell.
o This achievement was a major
breakthrough in genetic engineering
and cloning research.
• Since both statements are factually correct,
the correct answer is (c).
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2005
Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. A geostationary satellite is at an approximate
height of 10,000 km
2. FM transmission of music is of very good
quality because the atmospheric or manmade
noises which have generally frequency
variations can do little harm.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect: A geostationary
satellite orbits the Earth at a height of
approximately 35,786 km (around 36,000 km)
above the equator, not 10,000 km. This

•

•

precise altitude ensures the satellite’s orbital
period matches the Earth’s rotation, keeping
it fixed relative to a point on Earth.
Statement 2 is correct: FM (Frequency
Modulation) transmission maintains a
constant amplitude, encoding signals through
frequency variations.
o Most natural and man-made noise
causes amplitude variations, not
frequency ones.
o As FM receivers detect only
frequency changes, such noise has
minimal impact, resulting in clear,
high-quality sound, especially
compared to AM transmission, which
is more prone to distortion.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. Which one of the following is the landmark
performance of Dr. V. Mohan Reddy, an Indian-born
doctor working in USA, during February, 2005?
(a) Research on human stem cell which is likely to
revolutionise treatment of deadly disease like cancer
(b) Successful open heart procedure called arterial
switch performed on an infant
(c) Research on genetic engineering which can help in
treatment of deadly disease like AIDS
(d) Successful brain surgery to revive the function of
nearly dead brain of a premature born infant
The correct option is (b).
• In February 2005, Dr. V. Mohan Reddy, a
renowned pediatric cardiac surgeon,
achieved global recognition for a
groundbreaking open-heart surgery.
• He successfully performed an arterial switch
operation on a premature infant weighing
only 1.4 kg, suffering from transposition of
the great arteries, a severe congenital heart
defect.
• The procedure was highly complex due to the
small size and fragility of the newborn.
• The surgery was conducted in Mumbai and
was hailed as a landmark medical feat in
pediatric cardiac surgery.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2006
Q1. Which one of the following pairs is not correctly
matched?
Recent Scientific Achievement : Country
(a) Creating the World’s first mouse : United Kingdom
(b) Cloning a human embryo for the first time :
Germany
(c) Guiding a spacecraft for Collision with a comet :
United States of America
(d) Landing a spacecraft on an asteroid : Japan
The correct option is (a).
• Pair (a) is not correctly matched: The first
computer mouse was invented by Douglas
Engelbart in the United States in the 1960s.
o The United Kingdom had no role in
this invention, so this pair is incorrect.
• Pair (b): Although the first claimed cloning of
a human embryo came from South Korea
(later discredited), and Britain was among the
pioneers in cloning research, Germany’s strict
anti-cloning laws make this pairing highly
unlikely.
• Pair (c): Correctly matched. In July 2005,
NASA’s Deep Impact spacecraft successfully
guided an impactor to collide with Comet
Tempel 1, marking a major scientific
achievement for the United States.
• Pair (d): Correctly matched. In November
2005, Japan’s Hayabusa spacecraft landed on
the asteroid Itokawa to collect samples,
showcasing Japan’s space technology
capabilities.
• Since the question asks for the pair that is not
correctly matched, and the invention of the
mouse was American, not British, the answer
is (a).
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. Consider the following statements in respect of
India’s advanced satellite INSAT 4A:
1. INSAT 4A was launched in December, 2005
from New Mexico.

2. The European commercial launch service
provider Arianespace was associated with the
launch of INSAT 4A.
3. Tata-Sky—a digital cable service provider—is
associated with DTH television broadcasting
services from INSAT-4A.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 1 and 2, only
(c) 2 and 3, only
(d) 1 and 3, only
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is incorrect: INSAT-4A was
launched on December 22, 2005, but not
from New Mexico. It was launched from
Kourou, French Guiana, the site used by
Arianespace for commercial satellite
launches.
• Statement 2 is correct: The launch was
conducted by Arianespace, the European
commercial launch service provider, using
their Ariane 5 rocket.
• Statement 3 is correct: INSAT-4A was a
communication satellite with C-band and Kuband transponders, designed to enhance
Direct-To-Home (DTH) television services.
o Tata Sky (now Tata Play) used the Kuband transponders on INSAT-4A for
its DTH broadcasting services.
• Therefore, only statements 2 and 3 are
correct.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2007
Q1. Which of the following types is used by
computed tomography employed for visualization of
the internal structure of human body?
(a) X-rays
(b) Sound Waves
(c) Magnetic resonance
(d) Radioisotopes
The correct option is (a).

•

•
•

•
•

•

Computed Tomography (CT), or CT scan, is an
advanced imaging method that provides
detailed internal body images.
It uses X-rays taken from multiple angles
around the body.
A computer processes these 2-D X-ray images
to produce cross-sectional “slices” of bones,
tissues, and blood vessels.
These slices can be combined to generate 3-D
images of internal structures.
Sound waves (b) are used in Ultrasound,
Magnetic resonance (c) is used in MRI, and
Radioisotopes (d) are used in PET scans.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2. Which one of the following pairs is not correctly
matched?
(a) Cosmic Background Explore (COBE): Satellite
programme
(b) Falcon : Under-sea cable system
(c) Discovery : Space Shuttle
(d) Atlantis : Space Station
The correct option is (d).
• (a) Correctly matched — The Cosmic
Background Explorer (COBE) was a NASA
satellite launched in 1989 to measure
infrared and microwave radiation from the
early universe.
• (b) Correctly matched — Falcon is an undersea cable system connecting India, the
Middle East, and Europe, used for
telecommunication.
• (c) Correctly matched — Discovery was one of
NASA’s Space Shuttles, which flew missions
from 1984 to 2011.
• (d) Not correctly matched — Atlantis was also
a Space Shuttle orbiter, not a Space Station.
o The well-known space stations are
Mir, Skylab, and the International
Space Station (ISS).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Recently, the European Union and other six
countries including India signed the International
Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor (ITER) Project.

Which one of the following was not a signatory to it?
(a) Canada
(b) China
(c) Japan
(d) USA
The correct option is (a).
• ITER (International Thermonuclear
Experimental Reactor) is an international
scientific megaproject aimed at
demonstrating the feasibility of nuclear
fusion as a sustainable, carbon-free energy
source.
• The signatories (2006) were:
o European Union (EU)
o India
o Japan
o China
o Russia
o South Korea
o United States of America
• Canada was part of the early planning but
withdrew before the agreement was signed.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2008
Q1. ISRO successfully conducted a rocket test using
cryogenic engines in the year 2007. Where is the
test-stand used for the purpose, located?
(a) Balasore
(b) Thiruvananthapuram
(c) Mahendragiri
(d) Karwar
The correct option is (c).
• A cryogenic engine is a rocket engine that
uses propellants such as liquid hydrogen and
liquid oxygen stored at extremely low
temperatures.
• Developing this technology is crucial for
launching heavy satellites into
Geosynchronous Transfer Orbit (GTO).
• ISRO’s main facility for testing liquid and
cryogenic rocket engines is the ISRO
Propulsion Complex (IPRC).

•
•

•

This facility is located at Mahendragiri, in the
Tirunelveli district of Tamil Nadu.
The 2007 ground test of India’s indigenously
developed cryogenic upper stage for the
GSLV at this site was a major milestone in
India’s space program.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. What is the purpose of the US Space Agency’s
Themis Mission, which was recently in the news?
(a) To study the possibility of life on Mars
(b) To study the satellites of Saturn
(c) To study the colourful display of high latitude skies
(d) To build a Space laboratory to study the stellar
explosions
The correct option is (c).
• The THEMIS Mission (Time History of Events
and Macroscale Interactions during
Substorms) was launched by NASA in 2007.
• Its primary objective was to study the cause
of geomagnetic substorms, which lead to the
brilliant auroras—the colourful displays of
high-latitude skies like the Aurora Borealis
and Aurora Australis.
• The mission consisted of five identical probes
placed in different orbits around Earth to
study changes in the magnetosphere.
• This helped scientists locate where and when
auroral substorms begin.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. In which one of the following locations is the
International Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor
(ITER) Project to be built?
(a) Northern Spain
(b) Southern France
(c) Eastern Germany
(d) Southern Italy
The correct option is (b).
• The ITER Project is an international fusion
research initiative aimed at demonstrating
the feasibility of nuclear fusion as a
sustainable, carbon-free energy source.
• After global competition, Southern France
was selected as the project site.

•

•

•

The specific location is Cadarache, near SaintPaul-lès-Durance, in the Provence-Alpes-Côte
d’Azur region.
The site was chosen for its existing nuclear
research infrastructure and geological
stability.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q4. Which one of the following is a spacecraft?
(a) Apophis
(b) Cassini
(c) Spitzer
(d) TechSar
The correct option is (b).
• Cassini was a robotic spacecraft launched
jointly by NASA, the European Space Agency
(ESA), and the Italian Space Agency (ASI) to
study Saturn, its rings, and moons.
• The mission included the Cassini orbiter and
the Huygens lander (which landed on Titan).
• Spitzer is also a spacecraft — an infrared
space telescope.
• Apophis is an asteroid, not a spacecraft.
• TechSar is an Israeli reconnaissance satellite,
but in the context of planetary exploration,
Cassini is the most notable spacecraft.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Selene-1, the lunar orbiter mission belongs to
which one of the following?
(a) China
(b) European Union
(c) Japan
(d) USA
The correct option is (c).
• SELENE-1 (Selenological and Engineering
Explorer), also called Kaguya, was a Japanese
lunar orbiter mission.
• It was developed and launched by the Japan
Aerospace Exploration Agency (JAXA) in
September 2007.
• The mission included a main orbiter and two
subsatellites.
• Its objectives were to study the Moon’s
origin, structure, and evolution, and to

•
•

develop technologies for future lunar
exploration.
It was the largest lunar mission since Apollo.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2009

Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. The Shourya missile flies with a speed of more
than 8 Mach.
2. The range of Shourya missile is more than
1600 km.
Which of the statements given above is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: The Shourya missile is
a hypersonic surface-to-surface missile
capable of speeds above Mach 5. Reports
confirm it achieves speeds exceeding Mach 7
or 8 during flight.
• Statement 2 is correct: The range of the
Shourya missile is between 750 km and 1,900
km. Since its upper limit exceeds 1,600 km,
the statement is factually correct.
• Therefore, both statements are correct.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. WIMAX is related to which one of the following?
(a) Biotechnology
(b) Space technology
(c) Missile technology
(d) Communication technology
The correct option is (d).
• WiMAX (Worldwide Interoperability for
Microwave Access) is a wireless
communication technology.
• It provides high-speed broadband
connectivity over long distances and is used
for Wireless Metropolitan Area Networks
(WMANs).

•

•

WiMAX is similar to Wi-Fi, but it covers larger
areas and was considered a key technology
for 4G mobile communication systems.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q3. In the year 2008, which one of the following
conducted a complex scientific experiment in which
sub-atomic particles were accelerated to nearly the
speed of light?
(a) European Space Agency
(b) European Organization for Nuclear Research
(c) International Atomic Energy Agency
(d) National Aeronautics and Space Administration
The correct option is (b).
• The described experiment refers to the Large
Hadron Collider (LHC) operated by CERN
(European Organization for Nuclear
Research).
• In September 2008, CERN circulated the first
beams of protons through the 27 km LHC
tunnel, accelerating them to near-light speed.
• The goal was to study fundamental particles
and forces, leading to the discovery of the
Higgs boson in 2012.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. In the context of Indian news in recent times,
what is MCX - SX?
(a) A kind of supercomputer
(b) Title of Moon Impact Probe
(c) Stock Exchange
(d) Nuclear-Powered submarine
The correct option is (c).
• MCX-SX (MCX Stock Exchange Ltd.) was
India’s third national stock exchange, after
BSE and NSE.
• It was originally set up for currency
derivatives trading but later got SEBI approval
to operate as a full-fledged stock exchange.
• It was subsequently renamed the
Metropolitan Stock Exchange of India Ltd.
(MSEI).
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. In the context of alternative sources of energy,
ethanol as a viable bio-fuel can be obtained from
(a) Potato
(b) Rice
(c) Sugarcane
(d) Wheat
The correct option is (c).
• Ethanol is a biofuel produced through
fermentation of sugars by yeast.
• Sugarcane contains sucrose, a simple sugar
that is easily fermentable, making ethanol
production highly efficient.
• Brazil is a global leader in sugarcane-based
ethanol fuel.
• Although ethanol can be made from potato,
rice, or wheat, they require an additional
conversion step from starch to sugar, making
the process more complex.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. Consider the following statements:
1. INS Sindhughosh is an aircraft carrier.
2. INS Viraat is a submarine.
Which of the statements given above is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect: INS Sindhughosh is
a diesel-electric submarine, the lead vessel of
the Sindhughosh-class of the Indian Navy. It is
not an aircraft carrier.
• Statement 2 is incorrect: INS Viraat was a
Centaur-class aircraft carrier, serving as the
flagship of the Indian Navy until 2017. It was
not a submarine.
• Since both statements are incorrect, the
correct answer is neither 1 nor 2.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2010
Q1. Consider the following statements:

1. The satellite Oceansat-2 launched by India
helps in estimating the water vapour content
in the atmosphere.
2. Satellite helps predicting the onset of
monsoons.
3. Satellite helps monitoring the pollution of
coastal waters.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Oceansat-2, launched by ISRO in 2009, was
dedicated to oceanographic and atmospheric
studies.
• Statement 1 is correct: It carried the ROSA
(Radio Occultation Sounder for Atmospheric
Studies) instrument, which provided data on
pressure, temperature, and humidity, helping
estimate water vapour content in the
atmosphere.
• Statement 2 is correct: Its Ku-band
Scatterometer measured ocean surface wind
speed and direction, crucial for predicting
monsoons and cyclones.
• Statement 3 is correct: The Ocean Colour
Monitor (OCM) detected phytoplankton
concentrations and suspended sediments,
helping monitor coastal pollution.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Genetically modified "Golden rice" has been
engineered to meet human nutritional requirements.
Which one of the following statements best qualifies
golden rice?
(a) The grains have been fortified with genes to
provide three times higher grain yield per acre than
other high yielding varieties
(b) Its grains contain pro-vitamin A which upon
ingestion is converted to vitamin A in the human body
(c) Its modified genes cause the synthesis of all the
nine essential amino acids
(d) Its modified genes cause the fortification of its
grains with vitamin D

The correct option is (b).
• Golden Rice is genetically modified to combat
Vitamin A deficiency.
• It contains genes that enable production of
beta-carotene in the rice endosperm.
• Beta-carotene is a pro-vitamin A, which the
human body converts into Vitamin A after
ingestion.
• The golden-yellow color of the rice is due to
beta-carotene.
• It does not increase yield (a), produce amino
acids (c), or synthesize Vitamin D (d).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following:
1. Bluetooth device
2. Cordless phone
3. Microwave oven
4. Wi-Fi device
Which of the above can operate between 2.4 and 2.5
GHz range of radio frequency band?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 and 4 only
(c) 1, 2 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (d).
• The 2.4 GHz ISM band is widely used for
wireless communication technologies.
• Bluetooth devices operate between 2.402–
2.480 GHz.
• Cordless phones (many models) use the 2.4
GHz range.
• Microwave ovens use 2.45 GHz microwaves
for heating.
• Wi-Fi devices (IEEE 802.11b/g/n) use 2.412–
2.484 GHz.
• All four operate in this band.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. India-based Neutrino Observatory is included by
the Planning Commission as a mega science project
under the 11th Five-Year Plan. In this context,
consider the following statements:
1. Neutrinos are chargeless elementary particles
that travel close to the speed of light.

2. Neutrinos are created in nuclear reactions of
beta decay.
3. Neutrinos have a negligible, but non zero
mass.
4. Trillions of Neutrinos pass through human
body every second.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 3 only
(b) 1, 2 and 3 only
(c) 2, 3 and 4
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is correct: Neutrinos are
chargeless and travel nearly at light speed.
• Statement 2 is correct: They are produced
during nuclear beta decay and in fusion
reactions like those in the Sun.
• Statement 3 is correct: Neutrino oscillation
experiments proved they have tiny, non-zero
mass.
• Statement 4 is correct: Trillions of solar
neutrinos pass through every person each
second.
• All four statements are true.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q5. Hydrogen fuel cell vehicles produce one of the
following as "exhaust".
(a) NH₃
(b) CH₄
(c) H₂O
(d) H₂O₂
The correct option is (c).
• A hydrogen fuel cell combines hydrogen and
oxygen to produce electricity.
• The chemical reaction is 2H₂ + O₂ → 2H₂O +
Energy.
• The only by-product is water (H₂O), emitted
as vapor.
• These vehicles are zero-emission at point of
use.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. Recently, LASIK (Laser Assisted In Situ
Keratomileusis) procedure is being made popular for

vision correction. Which one of the following
statements in this context is not correct?
(a) LASIK procedure is used to correct refractive errors
of the eye
(b) It is a procedure that permanently changes the
shapes of the cornea
(c) It reduces a person's dependence on glasses or
contact lenses
(d) It is a procedure that can be done on the person of
any age
The correct option is (d).
• Statement (a): Correct — LASIK corrects
refractive errors like myopia, hyperopia, and
astigmatism.
• Statement (b): Correct — It uses a laser to
reshape the cornea permanently.
• Statement (c): Correct — It reduces
dependence on glasses or contact lenses.
• Statement (d): Not correct — LASIK is not for
all ages; usually for adults 18+ with stable
vision.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q7. Which bacterial strain, developed from natural
isolates by genetic manipulations, can be used for
treating oil spills?
(a) Agrobacterium
(b) Clostridium
(c) Nitrosomonas
(d) Pseudomonas
The correct option is (d).
• Bioremediation uses microbes to degrade
pollutants.
• Pseudomonas species can break down
hydrocarbons in crude oil.
• Dr. Ananda Mohan Chakrabarty developed a
genetically modified Pseudomonas putida
that efficiently degraded oil.
• Agrobacterium is used in plant genetic
engineering, while Clostridium and
Nitrosomonas have other biochemical roles.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Which feature of some species of blue-green
algae helps promote them as bio-fertilizers?

(a) They convert atmospheric methane into ammonia
which the crop plants can absorb readily
(b) They induce the crop plants to produce the
enzymes which help convert atmospheric nitrogen to
nitrates
(c) They have the mechanism to convert atmospheric
nitrogen into a form that the crop plants can absorb
readily
(d) They induce the roots of the crop plants to absorb
the soil nitrates in larger quantities
The correct option is (c).
• Blue-green algae (Cyanobacteria) are
photosynthetic bacteria that fix atmospheric
nitrogen.
• Species like Anabaena and Nostoc contain
nitrogenase enzyme, converting N₂ into NH₃.
• The fixed nitrogen enriches soil fertility and
benefits rice paddies.
• They do not act through methane conversion
or enzyme induction in plants.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9. Which among the following do/does not belong
to the GSM family of wireless technologies?
(a) EDGE
(b) LTE
(c) DSL
(d) Both EDGE and LTE
The correct option is (c).
• EDGE (Enhanced Data rates for GSM
Evolution) is part of GSM (2.75G).
• LTE (Long-Term Evolution) evolved from
GSM/UMTS (4G) networks.
• DSL (Digital Subscriber Line) is a wired
broadband technology over copper lines, not
part of GSM.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q10. With reference to the treatment of cancerous
tumours, a tool called cyberknife has been making
the news. In this context, which one of the following
statements is not correct?
(a) It is a robotic image guided system
(b) It delivers an extremely precise dose of radiation
(c) It has the capability of achieving sub-millimetre

accuracy
(d) It can map the spread of tumour in the body
The correct option is (d).
• CyberKnife is a robotic stereotactic
radiosurgery system.
• It uses a robotic arm and image guidance to
target tumors with highly precise radiation
doses.
• It achieves sub-millimeter accuracy even with
patient movement.
• It is a treatment device, not a diagnostic tool
— it cannot map tumor spread.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2011
Q1. A new optical disc format known as the Bluray
disc (BD) is becoming popular. In what way is it
different from the traditional DVD?
1. DVD supports standard definition video while
BD supports high definition video.
2. Compared to a DVD, the BD format has
several times more storage capacity.
3. Thickness of BD is 2.4 mm while that of DVD is
1.2 mm.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is correct: Blu-ray Disc (BD)
supports high-definition video (1080p and
above), while DVD supports only standard
definition (480p/576p).
• Statement 2 is correct: Blu-ray uses a blueviolet laser (405 nm) which allows greater
data density, giving 25 GB (single-layer) and
50 GB (dual-layer) capacity—much higher
than DVD’s 4.7 GB or 8.5 GB.
• Statement 3 is incorrect: Both discs have a
thickness of 1.2 mm; BD is not 2.4 mm thick.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. Microbial fuel cells are considered a source of
sustainable energy. Why?
1. They use living organisms as catalysts to
generate electricity from certain substrates.
2. They use a variety of inorganic materials as
substrates.
3. They can be installed in waste water
treatment plants to cleanse water and
produce electricity.
Which of the following statements given above is/ are
correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is correct: Microbial fuel cells
(MFCs) use living bacteria as biocatalysts to
oxidize substrates and generate electricity.
• Statement 2 is correct: MFCs can use both
organic and inorganic compounds (e.g.,
sulfides) as substrates.
• Statement 3 is correct: MFCs can be
integrated with wastewater treatment
plants, simultaneously purifying water and
producing energy.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. What is the difference between blue-tooth and
Wi-Fi devices?
(a) Bluetooth uses 2.4 GHz radio frequency band,
whereas Wi-Fi can use 2.4 GHz or 5 GHz frequency
band.
(b) Bluetooth is used for wireless local area networks
(WLAN) only, whereas Wi-Fi is used for wireless wide
area networks (WWAN) only.
(c) When information is transmitted between two
devices using blue-tooth technology, the devices have
to be in the line of sight of each other, but when Wi-Fi
technology is used the devices need not be in the line
of sight of each other.
(d) The statements (a) and (b) given above are correct
in this context.
The correct option is (a).

•
•

•

•
•

Bluetooth operates exclusively in the 2.4 GHz
ISM band.
Wi-Fi operates in both 2.4 GHz and 5 GHz
bands depending on the IEEE 802.11
standard.
Statement (b) is wrong: Wi-Fi forms WLANs,
while Bluetooth forms WPANs for short-range
communication.
Statement (c) is incorrect since neither
requires line of sight (unlike infrared).
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q4. A genetically engineered form of brinjal, known
as the Bt-brinjal, has been developed. The objective
of this is:
(a) To make it pest-resistant.
(b) To improve its taste and nutritive qualities.
(c) To make it drought-resistant.
(d) To make its shelf-life longer.
The correct option is (a).
• Bt-brinjal contains a gene from Bacillus
thuringiensis (Cry1Ac).
• This gene enables the plant to produce a toxin
that kills Fruit and Shoot Borer larvae, making
it pest-resistant.
• The objective was not to improve taste, shelflife, or drought resistance.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. What is "Virtual private network”?
(a) It is a private computer network of an organization
where the remote users can transmit encrypted
information through the server of the organization.
(b) It is a computer network across a public internet
that provides users access to their organization's
network while maintaining the security of the
information transmitted.
(c) It is a computer network in which users can access
a shared pool of computing resources through a
service provider.
(d) None of the statements (a), (b), and (c) given
above is correct description of virtual private network.
The correct option is (b).

•

•

•
•
•

A Virtual Private Network (VPN) is a secure
encrypted connection established over the
public internet.
It allows remote users to access their
organization’s private network securely as if
they were physically present in the office.
It maintains data confidentiality and integrity
while using public infrastructure.
Option (a) is incomplete and option (c)
describes cloud computing, not VPN.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q6. The surface of a lake in severe winter freezes,
but the water at its bottom is still liquid. What is the
reason?
(a) Ice is a bad conductor of heat.
(b) Since the surface of the lake is at the same
temperature as the air, no heat is lost.
(c) The density of water is maximum at 4° Celsius.
(d) None of the statements a, b, and c given above is
correct.
The correct option is (a).
• Water has maximum density at 4°C, so cold
water sinks until the entire lake reaches 4°C.
• As the surface cools further, it freezes into ice,
which floats due to lower density.
• Ice is a poor conductor of heat, so it insulates
the water below, preventing it from freezing.
• Thus, while (c) explains the initial layering, (a)
directly explains why the bottom water
remains liquid.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2012
Q1. To meet its rapidly growing energy demand,
some opine that India should pursue research and
development on thorium as the future fuel of
nuclear energy. In this context what advantage does
thorium hold over uranium?
1. Thorium is far more abundant in nature than
uranium.
2. On the basis per unit mass of mined mineral,
thorium can generate more energy compared
to natural uranium.

3. Thorium produces less harmful waste
compared to uranium.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Thorium is estimated to be three to four
times more abundant in the Earth’s crust
than uranium.
• India has large reserves of thorium, especially
in monazite sands along its coast.
• In a suitable reactor, thorium (Th-232)
converts to Uranium-233 (U-233), producing
more energy per unit mass than natural
uranium.
• The thorium fuel cycle is more efficient;
almost all thorium can be utilized.
• Thorium waste is less harmful and contains
fewer long-lived transuranic elements like
plutonium and americium.
• The radiotoxicity of thorium waste decays to
safe levels within hundreds of years, unlike
uranium waste, which lasts for tens of
thousands of years.
• Hence option correct (d).
Q2. What are the reasons for the people's resistance
to the introduction of BT brinjal in India?
1. Bt. brinjal has been created by inserting a
gene from a soil fungus into its genome.
2. The seeds of BT brinjal are terminator seeds
and therefore, the farmers have to buy the
seeds before every season from the seed
companies.
3. There is an apprehension that the
consumption of BT brinjal may have adverse
impact on health.
4. There is some concern that the introduction
of BT brinjal may have adverse effect on the
biodiversity.
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:

(a) 1, 2 and 3 only
(b) 1 and 3 only
(c) 3 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is incorrect — Bt brinjal was
created using a gene from Bacillus
thuringiensis (Bt), a soil bacterium, not a
fungus.
• Statement 2 is incorrect — Bt brinjal
developed for India did not use terminator
technology; farmers could save seeds though
licensing discouraged it.
• Statement 3 is correct — There was public
concern about potential health risks and lack
of long-term studies on the effects of
consuming GM foods.
• Statement 4 is correct — Concerns were
raised about loss of biodiversity, possible
gene transfer to wild relatives, and harm to
non-target organisms.
• Hence option correct (c).
Q3. Other than resistance to pests, what are the
prospects for which genetically engineered plants
have been created?
1. To enable them to withstand drought
2. To increase the nutritive value of the produce
3. To enable them to grow and do
photosynthesis in spaceships and space
stations
4. To increase their shelf life
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 and 4 only
(c) 1, 2 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (c).
• Genetic engineering allows introduction of
desirable traits beyond pest resistance.
• Drought resistance: Genes that help plants
conserve water or tolerate dry conditions
have been added.

•

•

•

•

Increased nutritive value: Example — Golden
Rice enriched with beta-carotene to combat
Vitamin A deficiency.
Photosynthesis in space: Incorrect — Plants
already perform photosynthesis; challenges in
space are physical, not genetic.
Shelf life: Example — Flavr Savr tomato
engineered to slow ripening, increasing shelf
stability.
Hence option correct (c).

Q4. Biomass gasification is considered to be one of
the sustainable solutions to the power crisis in India.
In this context which of the following statements is
correct?
1. Coconut shells, groundnut shells and rice husk
can be used in biomass gasification.
2. The combustible gases generated from
biomass gasification consist of hydrogen and
carbon dioxide only.
3. The combustible gases generated from
biomass gasification can be used for direct
heat generation but not in internal
combustion engines.
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• Biomass gasification converts agricultural
residues (like coconut shells, groundnut
shells, rice husk) into combustible gases.
• The produced gas, called producer gas, mainly
contains hydrogen (H₂) and carbon monoxide
(CO) — both combustible gases.
• It also contains non-combustible gases like
CO₂ and N₂.
• Producer gas can be used for direct heating
and also in internal combustion engines after
purification.
• Hence only statement 1 is correct.
• Hence option correct (a).

Q5. With reference to 'stem cells', frequently in the
news which of the following statements is/are
correct?
1. Stem cells can be derived from mammals
only.
2. Stem cells can be used for screening new
drugs.
3. Stem cells can be used for medical therapies.
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect — Stem cells are
found in all multicellular organisms, not just
mammals (e.g., plant meristems).
• Statement 2 is correct — Induced pluripotent
stem cells (iPSCs) are used in drug testing and
toxicity screening on human cell lines.
• Statement 3 is correct — Stem cells are the
foundation of regenerative medicine, used
for tissue and organ repair.
• Hence option correct (b).
Q6. Which of the following is/are cited by the
scientists as evidence/evidences for the continued
expansion of universe?
1. Detection of microwaves in space
2. Observation of redshift phenomenon in space
3. Movement of asteroids in space
4. Occurrence of supernova explosions in space
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1, 3 and 4
(d) None of the above can be cited as evidence
The correct option is (a).
• Microwave detection refers to the Cosmic
Microwave Background (CMB) radiation —
remnant heat from the Big Bang, evidence for
an expanding universe.

•

•

•

•

Redshift observation: Light from distant
galaxies shifts toward the red end of the
spectrum, showing they are moving away —
direct evidence of universal expansion.
Asteroid movement is a local solar system
phenomenon and unrelated to cosmic
expansion.
Supernova explosions alone are not
evidence, though Type Ia supernovae help
measure expansion rate.
Hence option correct (a).
UPSC PYQs - 2013

Q1. Recombinant DNA technology (Genetic
Engineering) allows genes to be transferred
1. across different species of plants
2. from animals to plants
3. from microorganisms to higher organisms
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Recombinant DNA technology (Genetic
Engineering) enables transfer of genes across
unrelated species, breaking natural
reproductive barriers.
• Across different species of plants: Commonly
done — e.g., herbicide-resistant crops
developed by transferring genes between
plant species.
• From animals to plants: Possible — e.g.,
insertion of fish genes for frost resistance
into plants in experiments.
• From microorganisms to higher organisms:
Widely used — e.g., Bt cotton created by
transferring a gene from the bacterium
Bacillus thuringiensis into the cotton plant.
• Since all three gene transfer possibilities are
achievable through this technology, all
statements are correct.
• Hence option correct (d).

Q2. The efforts to detect the existence of Higgs
boson particle have become frequent news in the
recent past. What is/are the
importance/importances of discovering this particle?
1. It will enable us to understand as to why
elementary particles have mass.
2. It will enable us in the near future to develop
the technology to transferring matter from
one point to another without traversing the
physical space between them.
3. It will enable us to create better fuels for
nuclear fission.
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• The Higgs boson is a fundamental particle
predicted by the Standard Model of particle
physics.
• Statement 1 is correct: The Higgs boson is
linked to the Higgs field, an all-pervading field
responsible for giving mass to elementary
particles through interaction.
• Statement 2 is incorrect: Concepts like
teleportation or instant matter transfer are
unrelated to the physics of the Higgs boson.
• Statement 3 is incorrect: The Higgs boson has
no role in nuclear fission or the creation of
better nuclear fuels.
• Thus, only the first statement correctly
represents its importance.
• Hence option orrect (a).
Q3. Mycorrhizal biotechnology has been used in
rehabilitating degraded sites because mycorrhiza
enables the plants to
1. resist drought and increase absorptive area
2. tolerate extremes of pH
3. resist disease infestation
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:

(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Mycorrhiza is a symbiotic relationship
between fungi and plant roots, improving
plant survival in degraded soils.
• Resist drought and increase absorptive area:
The fungal hyphae extend root reach,
enhancing nutrient and water absorption,
making plants more drought-tolerant.
• Tolerate extremes of pH: Mycorrhizal fungi
buffer root environments and stabilize soil
pH, helping plants adapt to adverse
conditions.
• Resist disease infestation: The fungal
network forms a physical barrier against
pathogens, and improved nutrition boosts the
plant’s disease resistance.
• Because mycorrhizal symbiosis provides all
these benefits, it is vital for restoring
degraded ecosystems.
• Hence option correct (d).
UPSC PYQs - 2014
Q1. There is some concern regarding the
nanoparticles of some chemical elements that are
used by the industry in the manufacture of various
products. Why?
1. They can accumulate in the environment, and
contaminate water and soil.
2. They can enter the food chains.
3. They can trigger the production of free
radicals.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Nanoparticles are extremely small particles
with unique properties, leading to
environmental and health concerns.

•

•

•

•

They can accumulate in soil and water,
persisting in the environment and causing
contamination.
They can enter food chains, being absorbed
by microorganisms, plants, and animals, and
bioaccumulating through trophic levels.
They can trigger free radical formation,
generating reactive oxygen species (ROS) that
cause oxidative stress and cellular damage in
living organisms.
Hence option correct (d).

Q2. With reference to Agni-IV Missile, which of the
following statements is/are correct?
1. It is a surface-to-surface missile.
2. It is fuelled by liquid propellant only.
3. It can deliver one-tonne nuclear warheads
about 7500 km away.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• Agni-IV is a surface-to-surface ballistic missile
developed by DRDO.
• It is two-staged and powered by solid
propellants, not liquid fuel.
• Its operational range is 3,500–4,000 km, not
7,500 km.
• Hence, only statement 1 is correct.
• Hence option correct (a).
Q3. With reference to two non-conventional energy
sources called 'coalbed methane' and 'shale gas',
consider the following statements:
1. Coalbed methane is the pure methane gas
extracted from coal seams, while shale gas is a
mixture of propane and butane only that can
be extracted from fine-grained sedimentary
rocks.
2. In India abundant coalbed methane sources
exist, but so far no shale gas sources have
been found.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 incorrect: Coalbed Methane
(CBM) is mostly methane, but not purely; it
contains CO₂ and nitrogen. Shale gas is also
mostly methane, not just propane or butane.
• Statement 2 incorrect: India has both CBM
and shale gas reserves — shale gas has been
found in Cambay, Krishna-Godavari, and
Cauvery basins.
• Hence both statements are incorrect.
• Hence option correct (d).
Q4. In addition to fingerprint scanning, which of the
following can be used in the biometric identification
of a person?
1. Iris scanning
2. Retinal scanning
3. Voice recognition
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Iris scanning analyses the unique patterns of
the iris, providing high accuracy.
• Retinal scanning examines the capillary
pattern in the retina, unique for every
individual.
• Voice recognition identifies individuals based
on vocal features such as pitch, tone, and
cadence.
• All three are widely used biometric
techniques.
• Hence option correct (d).
Q5. Which of the following pair is/are correctly
matched?
Spacecraft : Purpose

1. Cassini-Huygens : Orbiting the Venus and
transmitting data to the Earth
2. Messenger : Mapping and investigating the

3. Voyager 1 and 2 : Exploring the outer solar
system
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• Cassini-Huygens explored Saturn and its
moons, not Venus.
• MESSENGER orbited Mercury to map and
study its composition and surface.
• Voyager 1 and 2 explored Jupiter, Saturn,
Uranus, and Neptune — now travelling into
interstellar space.
• Hence, pairs 2 and 3 are correct.
• Hence option correct (b).
Q6. Consider the following techniques/phenomena:
1. Budding and grafting in fruit plants
2. Cytoplasmic male sterility
3. Gene silencing
Which of the above is/are used to create transgenic
crops?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) None
The correct option is (b).
• Budding and grafting are horticultural
propagation techniques, not genetic
engineering methods.
• Cytoplasmic male sterility helps in hybrid
seed production in transgenic breeding
programs.
• Gene silencing is a biotechnological tool used
to suppress unwanted genes (e.g., in virusresistant or slow-ripening crops).
• Hence, 2 and 3 are involved in transgenic crop
creation.
• Hence option correct (b).

Q7. Consider the following statements:
1. Maize can be used for the production of
starch.
2. Oil extracted from maize can be a feedstock
for biodiesel.
3. Alcoholic beverages can be produced by using
maize.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Maize is used globally for starch production
from its endosperm.
• Maize oil, extracted from the germ, can be
converted into biodiesel feedstock.
• Fermented maize produces ethanol, used in
alcoholic beverages like bourbon and
bioethanol fuels.
• All three uses are correct.
• Hence option correct (d).
Q8. With reference to technologies for solar power
production, consider the following statements:
1. 'Photovoltaic' is a technology that generates
electricity by direct conversion of light into
electricity, while “Solar Thermal” is a
technology that utilizes the Sun's rays to
generate heat which is further used in
electricity generation process.
2. Photovoltaic generates Alternative Current
(AC), while Solar Thermal generates Direct
Current (DC).
3. India has manufacturing base for Solar
Thermal technology, but not for
Photovoltaics.
Which of the statements given above is / are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) None
The correct option is (a).

•

•

•

•
•
•

Photovoltaic (PV) converts sunlight directly
into electricity using semiconductors
(photoelectric effect).
Solar Thermal focuses sunlight to produce
heat, then steam to drive turbines for power
generation.
PV produces DC, not AC; Solar Thermal
produces AC through conventional
generators.
India manufactures both PV and Solar
Thermal components.
Hence only statement 1 is correct.
Hence option correct (a).
UPSC PYQs - 2015

Q1. The Genetic Engineering Appraisal Committee is
constituted under the
(a) Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006
(b) Geographical Indications of Goods (Registration
and Protection) Act, 1999
(c) Environment (Protection) Act, 1986
(d) Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972
The correct option is (c).
• The Genetic Engineering Appraisal
Committee (GEAC) is the apex regulatory
body in India responsible for appraising
activities involving the large-scale use of
hazardous microorganisms and genetically
engineered organisms (GMOs).
• The GEAC was established under the 'Rules
for the Manufacture, Use, Import, Export and
Storage of Hazardous
Microorganisms/Genetically Engineered
Organisms or Cells, 1989'.
• These rules were notified by the Ministry of
Environment and Forests (now Ministry of
Environment, Forest and Climate Change)
under the authority of the Environment
(Protection) Act, 1986.
• Therefore, the parent legislation that provides
the constitutional and legal basis for the GEAC
is the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986.
• Hence option correct (c).

Q2. In which of the following activities are Indian
Remote Sensing (IRS) satellites used?
1. Assessment of crop productivity
2. Locating ground water resources
3. Mineral exploration
4. Telecommunications
5. Traffic studies
Select the correct answer using the code given below
(a) 1, 2 and 3 only
(b) 4 and 5 only
(c) 1 and 2 only
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5
The correct option is (a).
• Indian Remote Sensing (IRS) satellites are
Earth observation satellites.
• They are equipped with sensors that observe
the Earth's surface in various parts of the
electromagnetic spectrum.
• Their primary purpose is resource monitoring
and management.
• 1, 2, and 3 are correct. The data from IRS
satellites is widely used for assessment of
crop productivity, locating ground water
resources, and mineral exploration.
• 4 is incorrect. Telecommunications services
are primarily provided by geostationary
satellites such as the INSAT/GSAT series, not
by the IRS satellites which are typically in
polar orbits.
• 5 is incorrect. While satellite imagery can aid
urban planning, large-scale traffic studies are
generally not a primary application of the IRS
system.
• Therefore, the core applications listed are 1,
2, and 3.
• Hence option correct (a).
Q3. The term ‘IndARC’ sometimes seen in the news,
is the name of:
(a) an indigenously developed radar system inducted
into Indian Defence
(b) India’s satellite to provide services to the countries
of Indian Ocean Rim
(c) a scientific establishment set up by India in
Antarctic region

(d) India’s underwater observatory to scientifically
study the Arctic region
The correct option is (d).
• IndARC is the name of India's first-ever
moored underwater observatory.
• It was deployed in 2014 in the Kongsfjorden
fjord, Svalbard, located in the Arctic region.
• The purpose of the IndARC observatory is to
collect real-time oceanographic data
(temperature, salinity, currents) to
scientifically study the Arctic's climate and its
influence on the Indian monsoon.
• It is not a radar system, a satellite, or an
Antarctic establishment.
• Hence option correct (d).
Q4. With reference to bio-toilets used by the Indian
Railways, consider the following statements:
1. The decomposition of human waste in the
bio-toilets is initiated by a fungal inoculum.
2. Ammonia and water vapour are the only end
products in this decomposition which are
released into the atmosphere.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• The bio-toilets developed by DRDO and used
by Indian Railways are based on anaerobic
digestion.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The decomposition
is initiated by an inoculum consisting of a
consortium of anaerobic bacteria, not fungi.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. Anaerobic
decomposition produces methane (CH₄),
carbon dioxide (CO₂), and water (H₂O);
ammonia is not the primary gaseous end
product released into the atmosphere.
• Since both statements are factually incorrect,
the correct option is (d).
• Hence option correct (d).

Q5. With reference to ‘fuel cells’ in which hydrogenrich fuel and oxygen are used to generate electricity,
consider the following statements :
1. If pure hydrogen is used as a fuel, the fuel cell
emits heat and water as by-products.
2. Fuel cells can be used for powering buildings
and not for small devices like laptop
computers.
3. Fuel cells produce electricity in the form of
Alternating Current (AC).
Which of the statements given above is / are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. The electrochemical
reaction in a hydrogen fuel cell combines
hydrogen and oxygen, producing water (H₂O)
and heat as by-products.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. Fuel cells are
scalable; they can power buildings and small
devices (compact fuel cells exist for laptops
and portable electronics).
• Statement 3 is incorrect. Fuel cells inherently
produce Direct Current (DC); to obtain AC, an
inverter is needed.
• Since only statement 1 is correct, the correct
option is (a).
• Hence option correct (a).
Q6. With reference to ‘fly ash’ produced by the
power plants using the coal as fuel, which of the
following statements is/are correct?
1. Fly ash can be used in the production of bricks
for building construction
2. Fly ash can be used as a replacement for some
of the Portland cement contents of concrete
3. Fly ash is made up of silicon dioxide and
calcium oxide only, and does not contain any
toxic elements.
Select the correct answer using the code given below
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 only

(c) 1 and 3
(d) 3 only
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. Fly ash bricks are
commonly used and can be stronger and
lighter than traditional clay bricks.
• Statement 2 is correct. Fly ash is a pozzolanic
material and is widely used as a
supplementary cementitious material,
replacing part of Portland cement in concrete.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. Fly ash contains
silicon dioxide and calcium oxide, but also
aluminum oxide, iron oxide, and trace toxic
elements (arsenic, lead, mercury, cadmium)
present in coal.
• Since statements 1 and 2 are correct, the
correct option is (a).
• Hence option correct (a).
Q7. With reference to the use of nanotechnology in
health sector, which of the following statements
is/are correct?
1. Targeted drug delivery is made possible by
nanotechnology.
2. Nanotechnology can largely contribute to
gene therapy.
Select the correct answer using the code given below
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. Nanoparticles can be
engineered as drug carriers to target specific
cells (e.g., cancer cells), delivering high drug
concentrations while minimizing harm to
healthy tissues.
• Statement 2 is correct. Nanoparticles are
being developed as non-viral vectors for gene
therapy, encapsulating and protecting genetic
material for delivery to target cells.
• Since both statements describe valid
applications, the correct option is (c).
• Hence option correct (c).

Q8. In the context of modern scientific research,
consider the following statements about ‘IceCube’, a
particle detector located at South Pole, which was
recently in the news :
1. It is the world’s largest neutrino detector,
encompassing a cubic kilometre of ice.
2. It is a powerful telescope to search for dark
matter
3. It is buried deep in the ice.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is correct. The IceCube Neutrino
Observatory covers a volume of one cubic
kilometre of Antarctic ice, making it the
world’s largest neutrino detector.
• Statement 2 is correct. While primarily
detecting high-energy neutrinos, IceCube is
also used in dark matter searches (e.g.,
looking for neutrino excesses from the Sun or
galactic center due to dark matter
annihilation).
• Statement 3 is correct. The detector’s optical
sensors are deployed deep in the ice (1,450–
2,450 meters) to shield from cosmic-ray
background.
• Since all three statements are correct, the
correct option is (d).
• Hence option correct (d).
Q9. With reference to ‘Near Field Communication
(NFC) Technology’, which of the following
statements is/are correct?
1. It is a contactless communication technology
that uses electromagnetic radio fields
2. NFC is designed for use by devices which can
be at a distance of even a metre from each
other
3. NFC can use encryption when sending
sensitive information.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.

(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. NFC is a short-range
contactless communication technology using
electromagnetic radio fields.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. NFC is designed for
very short ranges (typically ~4 cm); it is not
for distances like one metre (that would be
Bluetooth).
• Statement 3 is correct. NFC supports security
mechanisms, and applications can use
encryption for sensitive data (e.g., contactless
payments).
• Since statements 1 and 3 are correct, the
correct option is (c).
• Hence option correct (c).
Q10. The term ‘Goldilocks Zone’ is often seen in the
news in the context of
(a) the limits of habitable zone above the surface of
the Earth
(b) regions inside the Earth-like planets in outer space
(c) search for the Earth-like planets in outer space
(d) search for meteorites containing precious metals
The correct option is (c).
• In astronomy, the "Goldilocks Zone" refers to
the Circumstellar Habitable Zone (CHZ) — the
region around a star where conditions are
"just right" (not too hot, not too cold) for
liquid water to exist on a planet’s surface.
• It is widely used in the search for exoplanets,
particularly when assessing whether a newly
discovered planet could be Earth-like and
potentially habitable.
• Hence option correct (c).
UPSC PYQs - 2016
Q1. Why does the Government of India promote the
use of 'Neem-coated Urea' in agriculture?
(a) Release of Neem oil in the soil increases nitrogen
fixation by the soil micro-organisms

(b) Neem coating slows down the rate of dissolution
of urea in the soil
(c) Nitrous oxide, which is a greenhouse gas, is not at
all released into atmosphere by crop fields
(d) It is a combination of a weedicide and a fertilizer
for particular crops
The correct option is (b).
• Urea is a nitrogen-based fertilizer that is
highly soluble in water.
• When applied to the soil, it can dissolve very
quickly, leading to loss through leaching or
volatilization.
• The neem oil coating acts as a nitrification
inhibitor.
• This coating slows down the dissolution of
urea and ensures a gradual, sustained release
of nitrogen.
• This increases Nitrogen Use Efficiency (NUE),
reduces groundwater contamination, and
prevents illegal diversion of urea for
industrial use.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. With reference to 'LiFi', recently in the news,
which of the following statements is/are correct?
(1) It uses light as the medium for high-speed data
transmission.
(2) It is a wireless technology and is several time faster
than 'Wi-Fi'.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• LiFi (Light Fidelity) is a wireless
communication technology using the visible
light spectrum, infrared, or ultraviolet light.
• It uses LED light bulbs that flicker at very high
speeds to transmit data.
• The visible light spectrum is 10,000 times
larger than the radio frequency spectrum
used by Wi-Fi.
• Hence, LiFi is several times faster than Wi-Fi.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q3. In the context of the developments in
bioinformatics, the term 'transcriptome', sometimes
seen in the news, refers to
(a) a range of enzymes used in genome editing
(b) the full range of mRNA molecules expressed by an
organism
(c) the description of the mechanism of gene
expression
(d) a mechanism of genetic mutation taking place in
cells
The correct option is (b).
• In genetics, the genome is the complete set
of DNA in an organism.
• The process of creating an RNA copy from a
DNA template is called transcription.
• The transcriptome is the complete set of RNA
transcripts (including mRNA and non-coding
RNA) produced by an organism or cell.
• It represents the full range of mRNA
molecules expressed, indicating which genes
are active.
• The study of the transcriptome is called
transcriptomics.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. The FAO accords the status of 'Globally
Important Agricultural Heritage Systems (GIAHS)' to
traditional agricultural systems. What is the overall
goal of this initiative?
1. To provide modern technology, training in
modern farming methods and financial
support to local communities of identified
GIAHS so as to greatly enhance their
agricultural productivity
2. To identify and safeguard eco-friendly
traditional farm practices and their associated
landscapes, agricultural biodiversity and
knowledge systems of the local communities
3. To provide Geographical Indication status to
all the varieties of agricultural produce in such
identified GIAHS
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 and 3 only
(b) 2 only

(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• The GIAHS initiative, led by the FAO, aims to
recognize and protect unique, traditional
agricultural systems.
• Statement 1 is incorrect – its goal is
conservation, not modernization.
• Statement 2 is correct – it seeks to safeguard
eco-friendly practices, landscapes,
agricultural biodiversity, and traditional
knowledge.
• Statement 3 is incorrect – the FAO does not
grant Geographical Indication (GI) status.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. 'Net metering' is sometimes seen in the news in
the context of promoting the
(a) production and use of solar energy by the
households/consumers
(b) use of piped natural gas in the kitchens of
households
(c) installation of CNG kits in motor-cars
(d) installation of water meters in urban households
The correct option is (a).
• Net metering is a billing mechanism for
consumers generating their own electricity,
typically via rooftop solar panels.
• Surplus power is exported to the grid, and the
meter runs backward to credit the household.
• Consumers are billed only for the net
electricity drawn from the grid.
• It encourages adoption of solar power and
improves renewable energy participation.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

by NSTL.

Q7. What is 'Greased Lightning-10 (GL-10)', recently

propulsion and VTOL capability.

Q8. Which of the following statements is/are
correct? Viruses can infect
(1) Bacteria
(2) Fungi
(3) Plants
Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(b) 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only

(phages).

Viruses infect bacteria, fungi, plants, and

formations.

o Cambay Basin (Gujarat)
