Q10. India is an important member of the
'International Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor'.
If this experiment succeeds, what is the immediate
advantage for India?
(a) It can use thorium in place of uranium for power
generation
(b) It can attain a global role in satellite navigation
(c) It can drastically improve the efficiency of its
fission reactors in power generation
(d) It can build fusion reactors for power generation
The correct option is (d).
• The International Thermonuclear
Experimental Reactor (ITER) is a global
nuclear fusion research project.
• It aims to demonstrate the feasibility of
nuclear fusion as a viable energy source.
• Success would enable India to develop its
own fusion reactors for power generation.

•
•

Fusion offers clean, safe, and virtually
limitless energy.
Thorium and fission are unrelated to this
fusion project.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q11. In the cities of our country, which among the
following atmospheric gases are not normally
considered in calculating the value of Air Quality
Index?
1. Carbon dioxide
2. Carbon monoxide
3. Nitrogen dioxide
4. Sulphur Dioxide
5. Methane
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1, 2 and 3 only
(b) 2, 3 and 4 only
(c) 1, 4 and 5 only
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5
The correct option is (c).
• The Air Quality Index (AQI) communicates the
level of air pollution based on pollutants with
direct and short-term health impacts.
• In India, the National AQI includes eight
pollutants:
o Particulate Matter (PM10)
o Particulate Matter (PM2.5)
o Nitrogen Dioxide (NO₂)
o Sulphur Dioxide (SO₂)
o Carbon Monoxide (CO)
o Ozone (O₃)
o Ammonia (NH₃)
o Lead (Pb)
• Carbon dioxide (CO₂) and Methane (CH₄) are
greenhouse gases important for climate
change, but not for AQI calculation since they
don’t cause acute health effects at normal
levels.
• Thus, Carbon dioxide (1) and Methane (5) are
not included in AQI.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q16. Consider the following statements: The
Mangalyaan launched by ISRO

1. Is also called the Mars Orbiter Mission
2. Made India the second country to have a
spacecraft orbit the Mars after USA
3. Made India the only country to be successful
in making its spacecraft orbit the Mars in its
very first attempt
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: Mangalyaan is India’s
first interplanetary mission, officially known
as the Mars Orbiter Mission (MOM).
• Statement 2 is incorrect: India became the
fourth space agency to orbit Mars, after the
Soviet space program, NASA (USA), and the
European Space Agency (ESA) — not the
second.
• Statement 3 is correct: India was the first and
only country to succeed in its first attempt to
send a spacecraft to Mars.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q17. With reference to 'Astrosat', the astronomical
observatory launched by India, which of the
following statements is/are correct?
1. Other than USA and Russia, India is the only
country to have launched a similar
observatory into space.
2. Astrosat is a 2000 kg satellite placed in an
orbit at 1650 km above the surface of the
Earth.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect: Astrosat is India’s
first multi-wavelength space observatory,
but other agencies like the European Space
Agency (ESA) and Japan (JAXA) have launched

•

•

similar observatories. Thus, India is not the
only other country.
Statement 2 is incorrect: The lift-off mass of
Astrosat was about 1513 kg, not 2000 kg. It
was placed in a Low Earth Orbit (LEO) at an
altitude of approximately 650 km, not 1650
km.
Since both statements are incorrect, neither 1
nor 2 is correct.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2017

Q1. Organic Light Emitting Diodes (OLEDs) are used
to create digital display in many devices. What are
the advantages of OLED displays over Liquid Crystal
displays?
1. OLED displays can be fabricated on flexible
plastic substrates.
2. Roll-up displays embedded in clothing can be
made using OLEDs.
3. Transparent displays are possible using
OLEDs.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 and 3 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) None of the above statements is correct
The correct option is (c).
• OLED (Organic Light Emitting Diode) displays
have several key advantages over traditional
LCD (Liquid Crystal Display) technology.
• Statement 1 is correct. Unlike LCDs, which
require a rigid glass substrate and a backlight,
OLEDs are made from thin films of organic
materials that emit their own light. These
organic layers can be deposited onto flexible
plastic substrates, allowing for the creation of
curved and bendable screens.
• Statement 2 is correct. The ability to fabricate
OLEDs on flexible substrates directly enables
the possibility of creating roll-up displays.
This technology has been demonstrated in
prototypes and is moving towards
commercialization. In theory, such flexible

•

•

displays could be embedded in clothing or
other textiles.
Statement 3 is correct. Because OLEDs do not
require the multiple layers of a traditional LCD
(like the backlight and liquid crystal layer), it
is possible to make them transparent. When
the display is off, the screen can be clear like a
piece of glass, and when turned on, it displays
an image. This technology is already being
used in some high-end commercial
applications.
Since all three statements describe real and
significant advantages of OLED technology,
the correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. The terms 'Event Horizon', 'Singularity', 'String
Theory' and 'Standard Model' are sometimes seen in
the news in the context of
(a) Observation and understanding of the Universe
(b) Study of the solar and the lunar eclipses
(c) Placing satellites in the orbit of the Earth
(d) Origin and evolution of living organisms on the
Earth
The correct option is (a).
• All the terms listed are fundamental
concepts in modern physics and cosmology,
used to describe the universe at its most
fundamental and extreme levels.
• Event Horizon: In the context of general
relativity, this is the boundary around a black
hole beyond which nothing, not even light,
can escape. It is a key concept in the study of
black holes and the extreme physics of the
universe.
• Singularity: This is a point in spacetime where
physical quantities like density and
gravitational field become infinite.
Singularities are predicted to exist at the
center of black holes and are also
hypothesized to have existed at the very
beginning of the universe (the Big Bang).
• String Theory: This is a theoretical framework
in physics that attempts to unify all
fundamental forces and particles by

•

•

modeling them as tiny, vibrating "strings." It
is a leading candidate for a "theory of
everything" that describes the entire
universe.
Standard Model: This is the current, welltested theory of particle physics that
describes the fundamental particles and
three of the four fundamental forces. It
provides the foundation for our
understanding of the universe at the
subatomic level.
Since all these terms are central to physics
and cosmology, they are all used in the
context of the observation and
understanding of the Universe.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. With reference to agriculture in India, how can
the technique of 'genome sequencing', often seen in
the news, be used in the immediate future?
1. Genome sequencing can be used to identify
genetic markers for disease resistance and
drought tolerance in various crop plants.
2. This technique helps in reducing the time
required to develop new varieties of crop
plants.
3. It can be used to decipher the host-pathogen
relationships in crops.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Genome sequencing is the process of
determining the complete DNA sequence of
an organism. This information has
revolutionary applications in agriculture and
crop improvement.
• Statement 1 is correct. By sequencing the
genomes of many different varieties of a
crop, scientists can compare them and
identify specific genes or genetic markers that
are associated with desirable traits like
disease resistance or drought tolerance. This

•

•

•

allows for more precise and efficient
breeding.
Statement 2 is correct. Traditional plant
breeding involves many generations of
crossing and selection, which can take many
years. By using genetic markers identified
through genome sequencing, breeders can
screen seedlings at a very early stage to see if
they carry the desired genes, without having
to wait for the plant to mature. This process,
known as Marker-Assisted Selection (MAS),
significantly reduces the time required to
develop new varieties.
Statement 3 is correct. By sequencing the
genomes of both the crop plant (the host)
and the organism that infects it (the
pathogen), scientists can study their
interaction at the molecular level. This helps
to decipher the host-pathogen relationship,
understanding which plant genes confer
resistance and which pathogen genes are
responsible for virulence, leading to better
disease control strategies.
Since all three statements are valid
applications of genome sequencing in
agriculture, the correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q4. It is possible to produce algae based biofuels, but
what is/are the likely limitation(s) of developing
countries in promoting this industry?
1. Production of algae based biofuels is possible
in seas only and not on continents.
2. Setting up and engineering the algae based
biofuel production requires high level of
expertise/ technology until the construction is
completed.
3. Economically viable production necessitates
the setting up of large scale facilities which
may raise ecological and social concerns.
Select the correct answer using the code given
below:
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only

(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Algae for biofuels
can be cultivated in various environments, not
just in seas. They can be grown in open ponds
on land (using freshwater, brackish water, or
even wastewater) and in closed systems
called photobioreactors, which can be
located on continents. In fact, most current
research focuses on land-based cultivation
systems.
• Statement 2 is correct. The technology for
producing biofuels from algae on a
commercial scale is still developing and
complex. Setting up and engineering the
cultivation systems (ponds or bioreactors),
harvesting the algae, and extracting the oil
requires high levels of technical and
engineering expertise, which can be a
significant barrier for developing countries.
• Statement 3 is correct. To be economically
viable, algae biofuel production would need
to be done at a very large scale. This would
require large areas of land (for ponds) and
large amounts of water and nutrients. This
raises potential ecological concerns (impact
on land use, water resources) and social
concerns (competition for land and water
with agriculture).
• Since statements 2 and 3 represent likely
limitations, the correct option is (b).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. What is the application of Somatic Cell Nuclear
Transfer Technology?
(a) Production of biolarvicides
(b) Manufacture of biodegradable plastics
(c) Reproductive cloning of animals
(d) Production of organisms free of diseases
The correct option is (c).
• Somatic Cell Nuclear Transfer (SCNT) is the
laboratory technique at the heart of cloning.
• The process involves taking a somatic cell
(any body cell other than a sperm or egg cell)

•

•

•

from one organism and an egg cell from
another. The nucleus is removed from the egg
cell, and the nucleus from the somatic cell is
transferred into the enucleated egg.
The reconstructed egg, now containing the
genetic material of the somatic cell donor, is
stimulated to divide. If this developing
embryo is implanted into the uterus of a
surrogate mother, it can develop into a new
organism that is a genetic clone of the
somatic cell donor.
This entire process is the basis for
reproductive cloning of animals. The most
famous example of an animal created using
SCNT is Dolly the sheep.
SCNT is not used for producing biolarvicides,
biodegradable plastics, or for producing
disease-free organisms (other techniques like
meristem culture are used for that in plants).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. The term ‘M-STrIPES’ is sometimes seen in the
news in the context of
(a) Captive breeding of Wild Fauna
(b) Maintenance of Tiger Reserves
(c) Indigenous Satellite Navigation System
(d) Security of National Highways
The correct option is (b).
• M-STrIPES stands for Monitoring System for
Tigers - Intensive Protection and Ecological
Status.
• It is a software-based monitoring system
launched by the National Tiger Conservation
Authority (NTCA) in India.
• The system is designed to assist in the patrol
and maintenance of tiger reserves. Forest
guards use a GPS-enabled mobile application
to record their patrol routes, sightings of
tigers and other wildlife, and evidence of
illegal activities (like poaching or habitat
destruction).
• This data is then uploaded to a central
system, allowing wildlife managers to analyze
patrol intensity, monitor tiger populations,
and make more effective decisions for the

protection and management of tiger
habitats.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. In India, it is legally mandatory for which of the
following to report on cyber security incidents?
1. Service providers
2. Data centres
3. Body corporate
Select the correct answer using the code given
below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• In India, the Information Technology Act,
2000, and the rules made under it, govern
cyber security.
• The Indian Computer Emergency Response
Team (CERT-In) is the national nodal agency
for responding to computer security
incidents.
• Under the CERT-In Rules (The Information
Technology (The Indian Computer Emergency
Response Team and Manner of Performing
Functions and Duties) Rules, 2013), it is
legally mandatory for a range of organizations
to report cyber security incidents to CERT-In
in a timely manner.
• The rules explicitly mandate reporting for:
o Service providers (including telecom
providers, network service providers,
and internet service providers)
o Intermediaries (which includes a very
broad range of online platforms)
o Data centres
o Body corporate (a general term
covering companies and
corporations)
• Since all three entities listed are legally
required to report such incidents, the correct
option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q8. What is the purpose of ‘evolved Laser
Interferometer Space Antenna (eLISA)’ project?
(a) To detect neutrinos
(b) To detect gravitational waves
(c) To detect the effectiveness of missile defence
system
(d) To study the effect of solar flares on our
communication systems
The correct option is (b).
• eLISA (evolved Laser Interferometer Space
Antenna), now known simply as LISA, is a
space mission concept being developed by
the European Space Agency (ESA).
• Its sole purpose is to detect and measure
gravitational waves.
• Unlike ground-based detectors like LIGO and
Virgo, which are sensitive to high-frequency
gravitational waves (from stellar-mass black
hole mergers), LISA is designed to be a spacebased observatory that can detect lowfrequency gravitational waves.
• These low-frequency waves are expected to
be produced by much larger cosmic events,
such as the mergers of supermassive black
holes at the centers of galaxies. The mission
will consist of three spacecraft flying in a
triangular formation, using lasers to measure
the minute changes in distance between them
caused by a passing gravitational wave.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2018
Q1. Consider the following:
1. Birds
2. Dust blowing
3. Rain
4. Wind blowing
Which of the above spread plant diseases?
(a) 1 and 3 only
(b) 3 and 4 only
(c) 1, 2 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (d).

•

•

•

•

•

Plant pathogens (like fungi, bacteria, and
viruses) can be spread from an infected plant
to a healthy one through various
environmental and biological vectors.
Birds: Birds can physically carry pathogens on
their feet, feathers, or beaks. They can also
ingest infected fruit or seeds and disperse the
pathogens in their droppings to new
locations.
Dust blowing: Soil particles can harbor
dormant spores of fungal pathogens. When
this soil becomes dust and is blown by the
wind, it can transport these pathogens over
long distances, depositing them on healthy
plants.
Rain: Rain, particularly through rain-splash, is
a major mechanism for spreading diseases. A
single raindrop hitting an infected leaf can
create an aerosol of thousands of smaller
droplets, each potentially carrying bacteria or
fungal spores, which then land on nearby
healthy plants.
Wind blowing: Wind is one of the most
effective and common ways plant diseases
are spread. It can carry lightweight fungal
spores, bacteria, and even small insects that
act as vectors over vast distances.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q2. In which of the following areas can GPS
technology be used?
1. Mobile phone operations
2. Banking operations
3. Controlling the power grids
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• The Global Positioning System (GPS) provides
three key pieces of information: Position,
Navigation, and Timing (PNT). While it is most
famous for its positioning capabilities, its

•

•

•

extremely precise timing signal is critical for
many modern technologies.
Mobile phone operations: GPS is used
extensively in mobile phones for locationbased services like mapping and navigation.
Furthermore, the precise timing signal from
GPS is used to synchronize the clocks of cell
towers, which is essential for managing the
handover of calls and data sessions.
Banking operations: Modern financial
systems rely on high-speed transactions that
must be recorded in the correct sequence.
The extremely accurate time provided by GPS
is used to timestamp these transactions
across different computer networks. This is
crucial for everything from ATM withdrawals
to high-frequency stock market trading.
Controlling the power grids: The electrical
power grid is a vast, interconnected network.
Phasor Measurement Units (PMUs), placed
throughout the grid, use the precise timing
signal from GPS to take perfectly
synchronized measurements of voltage and
current, allowing for effective monitoring and
control.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q3. When the alarm of your smartphone rings in the
morning, you wake up and tap it to stop the alarm
which causes your geyser to be switched on
automatically. The smart mirror in your bathroom
shows the day’s weather and also indicates the level
of water in your overhead tank. After you take some
groceries from your refrigerator for making
breakfast, it recognises the shortage of stock in it
and places an order for the supply of fresh grocery
items. When you step out of your house and lock the
door, all lights, fans, geysers and AC machines get
switched off automatically. On your way to the
office, your car warns you about traffic congestion
ahead and suggests an alternative route, and if you
are late for a meeting, it sends a message to your
office accordingly.
In the context of emerging communication
technologies, which one of the following terms best

applies to the above scenario?
(a) Border Gateway Protocol
(b) Internet of Things
(c) Internet Protocol
(d) Virtual Private Network
The correct option is (b).
• The scenario describes a network of everyday
physical objects—a smartphone, geyser,
mirror, refrigerator, lights, car—that are
embedded with sensors, software, and other
technologies for the purpose of connecting
and exchanging data with other devices and
systems over the internet.
• This concept of a network of interconnected
"smart" devices that can communicate and
act autonomously based on collected data is
the definition of the Internet of Things (IoT).
• The other options are specific networking
technologies: Border Gateway Protocol (BGP)
is a routing protocol; Internet Protocol (IP) is
the fundamental protocol for addressing and
routing data packets; Virtual Private Network
(VPN) is a technology for creating a secure,
encrypted connection.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Consider the following statements:
1. Light is affected by gravity.
2. The Universe is constantly expanding.
3. Matter warps its surrounding space-time.
Which of the above is/are the prediction/ predictions
of Albert Einstein’s General Theory of Relativity, often
discussed in the media?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Albert Einstein's General Theory of Relativity
(1915) is our modern theory of gravitation.
• Statement 3 is correct. The central idea of
General Relativity is that gravity is a
manifestation of the curvature of spacetime.
Matter and energy tell spacetime how to

•

•

curve, or "warp." This is the fundamental
principle of the theory.
Statement 1 is correct. A direct consequence
of statement 3 is that the path of light will be
affected by gravity. As light travels through
the warped spacetime around a massive
object, its path appears to be bent
(gravitational lensing), which was observed
during the solar eclipse of 1919.
Statement 2 is correct. When Einstein first
applied his equations to the universe as a
whole, he found they predicted a dynamic
universe—either expanding or contracting.
Observations by Edwin Hubble later
confirmed that the Universe is indeed
constantly expanding, a solution consistent
with General Relativity.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q5. With reference to India’s satellite launch
vehicles, consider the following statements:
1. PSLVs launch the satellite useful for Earth
resources monitoring whereas GSLVs are
designed mainly to launch communication
satellites.
2. Satellites launched by PSLV appear to remain
permanently fixed in the same position in the
sky, as viewed from a particular location on
Earth.
3. GSLV Mk III is a four staged launch vehicle
with the first and third stages using solid
rocket motors; and the second and fourth
stages using liquid rocket engines.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 2
(d) 3 only
The correct option is (a).
• The satellite-launch vehicles (rockets)
created by ISRO are the PSLV (Polar Satellite
Launch Vehicle) and GSLV (Geosynchronous
Satellite Launch Vehicle). While GSLVs are
primarily made to launch communication
satellites, PSLVs are meant to launch

•

•

•

satellites helpful for monitoring Earth
resources.
Statement 1 is correct. The PSLV is typically
used for Earth observation and resource
monitoring satellites (polar / sun-synchronous
orbits), while the GSLV is designed for heavier
communication satellites to GTO /
geostationary slots.
Statement 2 is incorrect. Satellites that
appear permanently fixed in the same
position in the sky are in Geostationary Orbit,
which are placed by GSLV (not PSLV). PSLV
typically places satellites in polar or sunsynchronous orbits, which do not appear
fixed.
Statement 3 is incorrect. GSLV Mk III (LVM3)
is a three-stage launch vehicle: first stage —
two S200 solid rocket boosters; second stage
— core L110 liquid-fueled stage; third stage
— C25 cryogenic upper stage. It is not a fourstaged vehicle with the configuration
described.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q6. “3D printing” has applications in which of the
following?
1. Preparation of confectionery items
2. Manufacture of bionic ears
3. Automotive industry
4. Reconstructive surgeries
5. Data processing technologies
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1, 3 and 4 only
(b) 2, 3 and 5 only
(c) 1 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5
The correct option is (d).
• 3D printing, or additive manufacturing, is a
versatile technology with applications across a
vast range of fields.
• Preparation of confectionery items: 3D
printing is used in the food industry to create
intricate and customized designs with
materials like chocolate, sugar, and pasta.

•

•

•

•

Manufacture of bionic ears: In medicine, 3D
printing is used to create custom implants
and prosthetics. Researchers have
successfully 3D-printed scaffolds in the shape
of a human ear, which can then be seeded
with cells to grow a "bionic ear."
Automotive industry: The automotive
industry uses 3D printing extensively for rapid
prototyping, creating custom jigs and tools,
and manufacturing lightweight, complex final
parts.
Reconstructive surgeries: Surgeons use 3Dprinted models of a patient's anatomy to plan
complex surgeries. Custom-fit implants
(cranial plates, joint replacements) are
manufactured using 3D printing.
Data processing technologies: While 3D
printing doesn't process data itself, it is used
to manufacture components essential for
data processing technologies — custom
enclosures, prototypes for hardware, and
experimental 3D-printed circuit boards.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2019

Q1. With reference to the recent developments in
science, which one of the following statements is not
correct?
(a) Functional chromosomes can be created by joining
segments of DNA taken from cells of different species.
(b) Pieces of artificial functional DNA can be created in
laboratories.
(c) A piece of DNA taken out from an animal cell can
be made to replicate outside a living cell in a
laboratory.
(d) Cells taken out from plants and animals can be
made to undergo cell division in laboratory petri
dishes.
The correct option is (a).
• Statement (a) is not correct. A chromosome
is a highly complex and large structure of
organized DNA.
• While scientists can cut and join segments of
DNA from different species (creating

•

•

•

•

recombinant DNA), they cannot assemble
these pieces to create a complete, new,
functional chromosome that would work
within a cell.
The complexity, regulation, and structural
integrity of a whole chromosome are far
beyond current capabilities.
Statement (b) is correct. Scientists can
synthesize pieces of artificial functional DNA
in a laboratory. This is a routine process used
in genetic engineering and synthetic biology
to create genes or other DNA sequences for
research and biotechnology applications.
Statement (c) is correct. A piece of DNA can
be made to replicate millions of times outside
a living cell using a laboratory technique
called the Polymerase Chain Reaction (PCR).
Statement (d) is correct. Cell culture is a
standard laboratory technique where cells
taken from plants and animals are grown in a
controlled environment (like a petri dish) with
a nutrient-rich medium. These cells can be
made to undergo cell division (mitosis) under
these conditions.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2. ‘RNA interference (RNAi)’ technology has gained
popularity in the last few years. Why?
1. It is used in developing gene silencing
therapies.
2. It can be used in developing therapies for the
treatment of cancer.
3. It can be used to develop hormone
replacement therapies.
4. It can be used to produce crop plants that are
resistant to viral pathogens.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1, 2 and 4
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1 and 4 only
The correct option is (a).
• RNA interference (RNAi) is a natural biological
process in which RNA molecules inhibit gene

•

•

•

•

•

expression or translation, by neutralizing
targeted mRNA molecules.
This mechanism has been harnessed as a
powerful research tool and has significant
therapeutic and agricultural potential.
Statement 1 is correct. The primary
application of RNAi technology is as a method
for gene silencing. By introducing a specific
double-stranded RNA molecule that matches
the sequence of a target gene's mRNA, the
technology can effectively "silence" or turn
off that gene. This is its fundamental
mechanism.
Statement 2 is correct. Cancer is often caused
by the over-activity of certain genes called
oncogenes. RNAi can be used to specifically
target and silence these oncogenes, thereby
inhibiting tumor growth. This makes it a
promising approach for developing new
therapies for the treatment of cancer.
Statement 3 is incorrect. Hormone
replacement therapies involve supplementing
the body with hormones that it is not
producing sufficiently (e.g., insulin for
diabetes or estrogen after menopause). RNAi
is a gene silencing technology; it cannot be
used to produce or replace hormones.
Statement 4 is correct. RNAi can be used to
make plants resistant to viruses. By
engineering a plant to produce doublestranded RNA that matches a viral gene, the
plant's own RNAi machinery can target and
destroy the viral RNA when the virus infects
the cell, thus making the plant resistant to
that viral pathogen.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. Which of the following are the reasons for the
occurrence of multi-drug resistance in microbial
pathogens in India?
1. Genetic predisposition of some people
2. Taking incorrect doses of antibiotics to cure
diseases
3. Using antibiotics in livestock farming
4. Multiple chronic diseases in some people

Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1, 3 and 4
(d) 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (b).
• Multi-drug resistance (MDR) occurs when a
microorganism (like a bacterium) becomes
resistant to multiple antimicrobial drugs that
were originally effective for treating infections
caused by it.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Antibiotic resistance
is a trait that develops in microbes, not
humans. A person's genetic predisposition
does not determine whether the bacteria
infecting them are resistant to antibiotics.
• Statement 2 is correct. Taking incorrect doses
of antibiotics (e.g., not completing the full
course or taking doses that are too low) is a
major driver of resistance. This practice can
kill the susceptible bacteria but allow the
partially resistant ones to survive, multiply,
and evolve into fully resistant strains.
• Statement 3 is correct. The widespread use of
antibiotics in livestock farming, often for
growth promotion or disease prevention
rather than treatment, exposes a vast
population of bacteria to these drugs. This
creates a large reservoir of resistant bacteria
and resistance genes, which can then be
transferred to human pathogens.
• Statement 4 is incorrect. While people with
multiple chronic diseases may be more
susceptible to infections and may use
antibiotics more frequently (increasing their
personal risk of harboring resistant bacteria),
their health status itself is not a reason for
the occurrence of resistance in the microbial
population at large. The resistance develops
in the microbes due to exposure to
antibiotics.
• Therefore, the primary drivers listed are the
incorrect use in humans and the use in
livestock.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q4. What is Cas9 protein that is often mentioned in
the news?
(a) A molecular scissors used in targeted gene editing
(b) A biosensor used in the accurate detection of
pathogens in patients
(c) A gene that makes plants pest-resistant
(d) A herbicidal substance synthesized in genetically
modified crops
The correct option is (a).
• The term Cas9 protein is a key component of
the revolutionary CRISPR-Cas9 gene-editing
system.
• In this system, the Cas9 protein acts as a pair
of "molecular scissors." It is an enzyme
(specifically, a nuclease) that can cut DNA.
• It is guided to a specific location in an
organism's genome by a guide RNA (gRNA)
molecule. Once at the target site, the Cas9
protein makes a precise double-strand break
in the DNA.
• This cut then allows scientists to either
disable the existing gene or insert a new
piece of DNA at that location, enabling highly
targeted gene editing.
• It is not a biosensor, a pest-resistance gene
itself (though it can be used to insert one), or
a herbicide.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. Which one of the following statements is not
correct?
(a) Hepatitis B virus is transmitted much like HIV.
(b) Hepatitis B, unlike Hepatitis C, does not have a
vaccine.
(c) Globally, the number of people infected with
Hepatitis B and C viruses are several times more than
those infected with HIV.
(d) Some of those infected with Hepatitis B and C
viruses do not show the symptoms for many years.
The correct option is (b).
• Statement (a) is correct. Both the Hepatitis B
virus (HBV) and HIV are transmitted through
contact with infected blood and certain body
fluids. Common modes of transmission
include unprotected sexual contact, sharing

•

•

•

of contaminated needles, and mother-tochild transmission during childbirth.
Statement (b) is not correct. This statement is
factually reversed. There has been a safe and
effective vaccine for Hepatitis B since the
1980s. Conversely, there is currently no
vaccine for Hepatitis C.
Statement (c) is correct. Globally, the number
of people living with chronic Hepatitis B or
Hepatitis C is estimated to be in the hundreds
of millions, which is several times more than
the number of people living with HIV.
Statement (d) is correct. Both Hepatitis B and
C can lead to chronic infections. In many
cases, an infected person can remain
asymptomatic for many years or even
decades while the virus slowly damages their
liver, potentially leading to cirrhosis or liver
cancer later in life.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q6. With reference to communications technologies,
what is/are the difference/differences between LTE
(Long-Term Evolution) and VoLTE (Voice over LongTerm Evolution)?
1. LTE is commonly marketed as 3G and VoLTE is
commonly marketed as advanced 3G.
2. LTE is data-only technology and VoLTE is
voice-only technology.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. LTE (Long-Term
Evolution) is the technology standard for 4G
wireless communication. It is not marketed as
3G. VoLTE is an advanced feature of 4G, not
an "advanced 3G."
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The original LTE
standard was indeed a data-only technology.
When a voice call was made on an early 4G
phone, the network would have to switch
down to an older 2G or 3G network to handle

•

the call. VoLTE (Voice over Long-Term
Evolution) is the technology that allows voice
calls to be carried over the 4G LTE data
network, instead of the older voice network.
It is not a "voice-only" technology; it is a
feature that enables voice on top of the LTE
data network, allowing for simultaneous
voice and data use and higher quality calls.
Since both statements are incorrect, the
correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q7. In the context of digital technologies for
entertainment, consider the following statements:
1. In Augmented Reality (AR), a simulated
environment is created and the physical world
is completely shut out.
2. In Virtual Reality (VR), images generated from
a computer are projected onto real -life
objects or surroundings.
3. AR allows individuals to be present in the
world and improves the experience using the
camera of smart – phones or PCs.
4. VR closes the world, and transposes an
individual, providing a complete immersion
experience.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 and 4
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) 4 only
The correct option is (b).
• This question tests the fundamental
difference between Augmented Reality (AR)
and Virtual Reality (VR).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. This describes
Virtual Reality (VR), not Augmented Reality.
In VR, the user's view of the physical world is
completely blocked and replaced by a
simulated environment.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. This describes
Augmented Reality (AR), not Virtual Reality.
In AR, digital information (images, text) is
overlaid onto the user's view of the real
world.

•

•

Statement 3 is correct. Augmented Reality
(AR) works by taking the user's view of the
real world, typically through the camera of a
smartphone or smart glasses, and overlaying
digital information on top of it. This
"augments" or improves the user's
experience of their real-world surroundings.
The popular game Pokémon Go is a classic
example.
Statement 4 is correct. Virtual Reality (VR)
aims to create a sense of full immersion. It
uses a headset that closes off the outside
world and replaces it with a computergenerated, 360-degree virtual environment,
making the user feel as if they have been
transposed into a different place.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q8. Consider the following statements: A digital
signature is __________
1. an electronic record that identifies the
certifying authority issuing it
2. used to serve as a proof of identity of an
individual to access information or server on
Internet
3. an electronic method of signing an electronic
document and ensuring that the original
content is unchanged
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• This question distinguishes between a
"digital signature" and a "digital signature
certificate."
• Statement 3 is correct. A digital signature is a
cryptographic mechanism used to verify the
authenticity and integrity of a digital
message or document. It is an electronic
method of signing that ensures the original
content is unchanged (integrity) and confirms
the identity of the signer (authenticity and
non-repudiation).

•

•

Statement 1 is incorrect. This statement
describes a Digital Signature Certificate
(DSC), not the signature itself. A DSC is the
electronic record or file, issued by a
Certifying Authority, that binds a public key
to an individual's identity.
Statement 2 is incorrect. This also describes
the function of a Digital Signature Certificate
(DSC). The certificate is used as proof of
identity to access secure information or
services online. The digital signature is the
result of using the certificate to sign
something.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q9. In the context of wearable technology, which of
the following tasks is/are accomplished by wearable
devices?
1. Location identification of a person
2. Sleep monitoring of a person
3. Assisting the hearing impaired person
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Wearable technology refers to electronic
devices that can be worn as accessories,
embedded in clothing, implanted in the
user's body, or even tattooed on the skin.
• 1. Location identification of a person: This is
a common feature. Many wearable devices,
such as smartwatches and GPS trackers,
include GPS sensors for location
identification, used for fitness tracking,
navigation, and personal safety.
• 2. Sleep monitoring of a person: This is
another very common application. Fitness
trackers and smartwatches use sensors like
accelerometers and heart rate monitors to
track movement and vital signs during the
night, allowing them to provide data on sleep
patterns, duration, and quality.

•

3. Assisting the hearing impaired person:
Modern digital hearing aids are a
sophisticated form of wearable technology.
They are worn in or on the ear and use
advanced electronics to amplify and process
sound, directly assisting a hearing-impaired
person.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q10. For the measurement/ estimation of which of
the following are satellite images/remote sensing
data used?
1. Chlorophyll content in the vegetation of a
specific location.
2. Greenhouse gas emissions from rice paddies
of a specific location.
3. Land surface temperatures of a specific
location.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Remote sensing is the science of obtaining
information about Earth's surface without
being in direct contact with it, primarily
through the use of satellite or aircraft-based
sensors.
• 1. Chlorophyll content: This is a standard
application. Healthy vegetation reflects
strongly in the near-infrared part of the
spectrum and absorbs red light due to
chlorophyll. By measuring the difference in
reflectance at these wavelengths, satellites
can calculate vegetation indices (like NDVI),
which are highly correlated with the amount
of chlorophyll content and overall plant
health.
• 2. Greenhouse gas emissions: This is an
advanced but operational application.
Satellites equipped with specialized
spectrometers (like TROPOMI on Sentinel5P) can detect the unique absorption
signatures of gases in the atmosphere. This

•

technology is used to monitor and estimate
greenhouse gas emissions, including
methane (CH₄) from large areas like rice
paddies or oil and gas fields.
3. Land surface temperatures: Satellites
carrying thermal infrared sensors can
measure the thermal radiation emitted from
the Earth's surface. This data is then used to
calculate and map land surface temperatures
over large areas, which is crucial for weather
forecasting, climate monitoring, and
agricultural applications.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q11. Recently, scientists observed the merger of
giant ‘blackholes’ billions of light years away from
the Earth. What is the significance of this
observation?
(a) ‘Higgs boson particles’ were detected.
(b) ‘Gravitational waves’ were detected.
(c) Possibility of intergalactic space travel through
‘wormhole’ was confirmed.
(d) It enabled the scientists to understand
‘singularity’.
The correct option is (b).
• The first direct observation of the merger of
two giant black holes was announced in
February 2016 by the LIGO and Virgo
collaborations. This event, and subsequent
similar observations, have been of
monumental significance in physics and
astronomy.
• The primary significance of this observation
was the first-ever direct detection of
gravitational waves.
• Gravitational waves are "ripples" in the fabric
of spacetime that are created by massive
accelerating objects, such as merging black
holes. Their existence was predicted by Albert
Einstein's General Theory of Relativity a
century earlier, but they had never been
directly observed.
• This detection confirmed a major prediction
of Einstein's theory and opened up a

•

completely new way to observe the universe
(gravitational-wave astronomy).
The event did not involve the detection of
Higgs bosons (a), confirmation of wormholes
(c), or a direct observation of the singularity
inside the black hole (d).
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q12. Consider the following statements:
1. According to the Indian Patents Act, a
biological process to create a seed can be
patented in India.
2. In India, there is no Intellectual Property
Appellate Board.
3. Plant varieties are not eligible to be patented
in India.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 3 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Section 3(j) of the
Indian Patents Act, 1970, explicitly excludes
"plants and animals in whole or any part
thereof other than micro-organisms but
including seeds, varieties and species and
essentially biological processes for
production or propagation of plants and
animals" from being patented. A biological
process to create a seed falls under this
exclusion.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The Intellectual
Property Appellate Board (IPAB) was a quasijudicial body established in India in 2003 to
hear appeals against the decisions of the
Registrar of Trade Marks and Geographical
Indications and the Controller of Patents.
(Note: The IPAB was abolished in 2021, and
its functions were transferred to the High
Courts. However, at the time this question
was asked in 2019, the IPAB was fully
functional.) Therefore, the statement that
there is "no" such board was false at the time.

•

•

Statement 3 is correct. As stated in the
analysis for statement 1, Section 3(j) of the
Indian Patents Act makes plant varieties (and
seeds) not eligible to be patented. Instead,
new plant varieties in India are protected
under a different law, The Protection of Plant
Varieties and Farmers' Rights (PPV&FR) Act,
2001.
Since only statement 3 is correct, the correct
option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2020
•

Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. Genetic changes can be introduced in the cells
that produce eggs or sperms of a prospective
parent.
2. A person’s genome can be edited before birth
at the early embryonic stage.
3. Human induced pluripotent stem cells can be
injected into the embryo of a pig.
Which of the statements given above is/are
correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 2 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is correct. This describes
germline gene therapy. In this process, DNA
is introduced into germ cells (sperm or eggs).
The genetic changes are therefore heritable
and would be passed on to subsequent
generations. While technically possible, it is
highly controversial and currently banned for
human application in most countries due to
ethical concerns.
• Statement 2 is correct. Using gene-editing
technologies like CRISPR-Cas9, it is technically
possible to edit the genome of an embryo at
a very early stage. This is known as embryonic
gene editing. The goal would be to correct a
gene mutation that causes a serious disease
before the embryo develops. Like germline

therapy, this is also ethically contentious and
subject to strict regulation.
Statement 3 is correct. This refers to the
creation of a human-animal chimera.
Scientists have successfully injected human
induced pluripotent stem cells (iPSCs) into
the early-stage embryos of pigs and other
animals. The goal of this research is to see if
it's possible to grow human organs (like a
kidney or pancreas) inside an animal host,
which could then potentially be used for
transplantation, addressing the shortage of
donor organs.
Since all three statements describe
procedures that are technically possible with
the current state of biotechnology (though
subject to ethical and legal constraints), the
correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q2. What is the importance of using Pneumococcal
Conjugate Vaccines in India?
1. These vaccines are effective against
pneumonia as well as meningitis and sepsis.
2. Dependence on antibiotics that are not
effective against drug resistant bacteria can
be reduced.
3. These vaccines have no side effects and cause
no allergic reactions.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is correct. Pneumococcal
Conjugate Vaccines (PCV) protect against
diseases caused by the bacterium
Streptococcus pneumoniae (pneumococcus).
This bacterium is a leading cause of severe
illness in children, including pneumonia (lung
infection), bacterial meningitis (infection of
the lining of the brain and spinal cord), and
sepsis (bloodstream infection). The vaccine is
effective against these conditions.

•

•

•

Statement 2 is correct. Widespread
vaccination against bacterial diseases like
pneumococcus reduces the overall number of
infections. This, in turn, reduces the overall
need to use antibiotics. By decreasing
antibiotic use, we can slow down the
development and spread of drug-resistant
bacteria, thus reducing our dependence on
antibiotics and helping to combat
antimicrobial resistance (AMR).
Statement 3 is incorrect. This is an absolute
statement that is false for virtually all vaccines
and medicines. The PCV, like all vaccines, can
have side effects, which are usually mild (like
fever, soreness at the injection site, or
irritability). While rare, serious allergic
reactions can also occur. The claim of "no side
effects and cause no allergic reactions" is
incorrect.
Since statements 1 and 2 are correct, the
correct option is (b).
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. With the present state of development, Artificial
Intelligence can effectively do which of the
following?
1. Bring down electricity consumption in
industrial units
2. Create meaningful short stories and songs
3. Disease diagnosis
4. Text -to -Speech Conversion
5. Wireless transmission of electrical energy
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1, 2, 3 and 5 only
(b) 1, 3 and 4 only
(c) 2, 4 and 5 only
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5
The correct option is (b).
• 1. Bring down electricity consumption: This is
a proven application. AI algorithms are used
to optimize energy use in large facilities like
data centers and industrial units. For example,
Google has used AI from DeepMind to predict
and manage cooling systems in its data

•

•

•

•

•

centers, significantly reducing electricity
consumption.
2. Create meaningful short stories and songs:
While AI models (like large language models)
can generate text that resembles stories and
music, the ability to create truly "meaningful"
art is subjective and highly debatable. At the
time of this question, and even now, AI's
creative output often lacks the depth,
coherence, and emotional resonance of
human-created art. This is a weak or incorrect
claim in the context of "effectively do."
3. Disease diagnosis: This is a major and
effective application of AI. Machine learning
models, particularly deep learning, are
trained on vast datasets of medical images
(like X-rays, CT scans, MRIs) to detect and
diagnose diseases such as cancer or diabetic
retinopathy, often with accuracy rivaling or
exceeding human experts.
4. Text-to-Speech Conversion: This is a wellestablished and effective AI technology.
Modern Text-to-Speech (TTS) systems use
neural networks to generate highly natural
and human-like speech from text, and are
used in virtual assistants, accessibility tools,
and more.
5. Wireless transmission of electrical energy:
This is a field of physics and electrical
engineering, primarily based on principles like
magnetic resonance or microwaves. It is not
an application of Artificial Intelligence. AI
might be used to optimize such a system, but
it cannot "do" the transmission itself.
Based on this analysis, the tasks AI can
effectively do are 1, 3, and 4.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q4. With reference to Visible Light Communication
(VLC) technology, which of the following statements
are correct?
1. VLC uses electromagnetic spectrum
wavelengths 375 to 780 nm
2. VLC is known as long – range optical wireless
communication

3. VLC can transmit large amounts of data faster
than Bluetooth
4. VLC has no electromagnetic interference
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1, 2 and 3 only
(b) 1, 2 and 4 only
(c) 1, 3 and 4 only
(d) 2, 3 and 4 only
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. Visible Light
Communication (VLC) utilizes the visible light
portion of the electromagnetic spectrum. This
corresponds to wavelengths roughly between
375 nm (violet) and 780 nm (red).
• Statement 2 is incorrect. A key characteristic
and limitation of VLC is that light cannot
penetrate opaque objects like walls. This
makes it inherently a short-range technology,
suitable for indoor environments. It is not
known as "long-range" optical wireless
communication.
• Statement 3 is correct. The visible light
spectrum is thousands of times wider than
the radio frequency spectrum used by
technologies like Bluetooth. This vast
bandwidth allows VLC (often marketed as LiFi) to achieve much higher data transmission
rates, potentially in the Gbps range, which is
significantly faster than Bluetooth.
• Statement 4 is correct. Radio frequency (RF)
technologies like Wi-Fi and cellular networks
can interfere with each other and with
sensitive electronic equipment. Because VLC
uses light instead of radio waves, it does not
create or suffer from this type of
electromagnetic interference (EMI), making it
a safe and useful technology in EMI-sensitive
environments like hospitals and airplanes.
• Since statements 1, 3, and 4 are correct, the
correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. With reference to “Blockchain Technology”
consider the following statements:

1. It is a public ledger that everyone can inspect,
but which no single user controls.
2. The structure and design of blockchain is such
that all the data in it is about crypto currency
only.
3. Applications that depend on basic features of
blockchain can be developed without
anybody’s permission.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 2 only
(d) 1 and 3 only
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is correct. This describes the
core nature of a public blockchain. It is a
distributed public ledger, meaning a copy is
held by many participants, and anyone can
view the transactions on it ("everyone can
inspect"). It is decentralized, meaning that no
single user or entity controls the network;
control is distributed among the participants.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. This is a common
misconception. While blockchain technology
was first developed for cryptocurrency
(Bitcoin), its application is not limited to it.
The technology's ability to create a secure,
immutable, and transparent ledger makes it
useful for many other applications, including
supply chain management, voting systems,
healthcare records, and smart contracts. The
statement that the data is "about crypto
currency only" is false.
• Statement 3 is correct. This refers to the
"permissionless" nature of public blockchains
like Ethereum or Bitcoin. Because the
network is open and decentralized, anyone
can develop and deploy applications (like
smart contracts or dApps) on the platform
without needing permission from a central
authority.
• Since statements 1 and 3 are correct, the
correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. With reference to carbon nanotubes, consider
the following statements:
1. They can be used as carriers of drugs and
antigens in the human body.
2. They can be made into artificial blood
capillaries for an injured part of the human
body.
3. They can be used in biochemical sensors.
4. Carbon nanotubes are biodegradable.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2, 3 and 4 only
(c) 1, 3 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. Due to their unique
structure and high surface area, carbon
nanotubes (CNTs) can be functionalized (have
molecules attached to their surface) and used
as nanoscale carriers for delivering drugs,
genes, and antigens to specific cells in the
body.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. While CNTs have
many biomedical applications, they are not
used to make artificial blood capillaries. Their
properties are not suitable for creating the
complex, flexible, and biocompatible
structure of a capillary vessel. Materials like
biocompatible polymers are used for this
purpose.
• Statement 3 is correct. The electrical
properties of CNTs are highly sensitive to
their environment. When specific molecules
bind to their surface, it changes their
electrical conductivity. This property makes
them excellent candidates for use as highly
sensitive biochemical sensors to detect
specific proteins, DNA, or other biomarkers.
• Statement 4 is correct. Research has shown
that certain enzymes, particularly peroxidases
found in some white blood cells and
microorganisms, can break down or degrade
carbon nanotubes. Therefore, they are
considered to be biodegradable, although the
rate and conditions can vary.

•

Based on the provided explanations,
statements 1, 3, and 4 are correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q7. “The experiment will employ a trio of spacecraft
flying in formation in the shape of an equilateral
triangle that has sides one million kilometers long,
with lasers shining between the craft.” The
experiment in question refers to
(a) Voyager – 2
(b) New Horizons
(c) LISA Pathfinder
(d) Evolved LISA
The correct option is (d).
• The description perfectly matches the
mission profile of LISA (Laser Interferometer
Space Antenna), which was formerly known
as eLISA (Evolved LISA).
• The LISA mission is designed to be a spacebased gravitational-wave observatory.
• It will consist of three spacecraft flying in a
triangular formation, separated by millions of
kilometers.
• Lasers will be shone between the spacecraft
to measure the distance between them with
extreme precision.
• A passing gravitational wave will minutely
alter these distances, which the
interferometer will detect.
• LISA Pathfinder (c) was a smaller, precursor
mission designed to test the core
technologies needed for the full LISA mission,
but it did not consist of the full threespacecraft constellation.
• Voyager 2 (a) and New Horizons (b) are
planetary probe missions that performed
flybys of outer solar system objects.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Consider the following activities:
1. Spraying pesticides on a crop field
2. Inspecting the craters of active volcanoes
3. Collecting breath samples from spouting
whales for DNA analysis.

At the present level of technology, which of the above
activities can be successfully carried out by using
drones?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Drones (Unmanned Aerial Vehicles or UAVs)
are versatile platforms that can be equipped
with various sensors and tools to perform
tasks that are dangerous, difficult, or
inefficient for humans.
• Spraying pesticides: This is a well-established
application. Agricultural drones are equipped
with tanks and spray nozzles to apply
pesticides, herbicides, or fertilizers to crop
fields with high precision (precision
agriculture).
• Inspecting active volcanoes: Drones are ideal
for safely flying into and over the craters of
active volcanoes. They can be equipped with
cameras, thermal sensors, and gas detectors
to monitor volcanic activity, map changes in
the crater, and collect data without risking
human lives.
• Collecting breath samples from whales: This
is an innovative scientific application.
Researchers have successfully developed
specialized drones that can fly through the
"spout" or exhaled breath of a whale to
collect samples. This "snot" contains DNA,
hormones, and microorganisms that can be
analyzed to assess the whale's health, diet,
and genetic information non-invasively.
• Since all three activities are currently and
successfully carried out using drones, the
correct option is (d).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. Which of the following statements are correct
regarding the general difference between plant and
animal cells?
1. Plant cells have cellulose cell walls whilst
animal cells do not.

2. Plant cells do not have plasma membranes
unlike animal cells which do.
3. Mature plant cell has one large vacuole whilst
an animal cell has many small vacuoles.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. A key distinguishing
feature is the presence of a rigid cell wall
outside the plasma membrane in plant cells.
This wall is primarily made of cellulose and
provides structural support. Animal cells lack
a cell wall.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. Both plant and
animal cells have a plasma membrane (also
called a cell membrane). It is a fundamental
component of all cells, controlling the passage
of substances in and out of the cell. In plant
cells, it is located just inside the cell wall.
• Statement 3 is correct. A mature plant cell
typically has a single, large central vacuole
that can take up a significant portion of the
cell's volume. It stores water, nutrients, and
waste products and helps maintain turgor
pressure. In contrast, animal cells, if they
have vacuoles at all, typically have several
small ones.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q10. In the context of recent advances in human
reproductive technology. “Pronuclear Transfer” is
used for
(a) Fertilisation of egg in vitro by the donor sperm
(b) Genetic modification of sperm producing cells
(c) Development of stem cells into functional embryos
(d) Prevention of mitochondrial diseases in offspring
The correct option is (d).
• Pronuclear Transfer is a specific technique of
mitochondrial replacement therapy,
sometimes referred to as "three-parent baby"
technology.

•

•

•

•

•

Its sole purpose is the prevention of
mitochondrial diseases being passed from a
mother to her child.
Mitochondria, which have their own DNA
(mtDNA), are inherited exclusively from the
mother via her egg cell. If the mother has a
disease caused by faulty mtDNA, she will pass
it on to all her children.
In pronuclear transfer, the mother's egg is
fertilized with the father's sperm, creating a
zygote. The pronuclei (which contain the
nuclear DNA from both parents) are then
removed from this zygote and transferred into
a healthy donor egg that has also been
fertilized and had its own pronuclei removed.
The resulting reconstructed embryo has the
nuclear DNA from its parents but the healthy
mitochondria (and mtDNA) from the donor,
thus preventing the transmission of the
mother's mitochondrial disease.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q11. With reference to solar water pumps, consider
the following statements:
1. Solar power can be used for running surface
pumps and not for submersible pumps.
2. Solar power can be used for running
centrifugal pumps and not the ones with
piston.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• Solar water pumping systems use electricity
generated by photovoltaic (PV) panels to run
a pump. The type of pump used can vary
depending on the application.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Solar power can be
used to run both surface pumps and
submersible pumps. Surface pumps are
placed on the ground and are used to move
water from sources like ponds, streams, or
shallow wells. Submersible pumps are placed

•

•
•

underwater in a deep well or borehole. Solarpowered submersible pumps are a very
common and critical application for providing
drinking water and irrigation in off-grid areas.
Statement 2 is incorrect. Solar power can be
used to run various types of pumps, including
both centrifugal pumps and piston pumps (a
type of positive displacement pump).
Centrifugal pumps are common for high-flow
applications. Piston pumps are often used in
solar applications for deep wells because they
can be more efficient at lifting water from
great depths.
Since both statements make incorrect
limiting claims, neither is correct.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q12. With reference to the current trends in the
cultivation of sugarcane in India, consider the
following statements:
1. A substantial saving in seed material is made
when ‘bud chip settings’ are raised in a
nursery and transplanted in the main field.
2. When direct planting of setts is done, the
germination percentage is better with singlebudded setts as compared to setts with many
buds.
3. If bad weather conditions prevail when setts
are directly planted, single – budded setts
have better survival as compared to large
setts.
4. Sugarcane can be cultivated using settlings
prepared from tissue culture.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1 and 4 only
(d) 2, 3 and 4 only
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. Traditional sugarcane
cultivation uses large sections of the cane
stalk ("setts") as planting material, which is
bulky and requires a large quantity (several
tonnes per hectare). The 'bud chip settlings'
method uses only a small chip of the cane

•

•

•

•
•

containing a single bud. These are raised into
small plants (settlings) in a nursery and then
transplanted. This technique results in a
substantial saving in seed material and
ensures a uniform plant population.
Statement 2 is incorrect. When planting setts
directly, multi-budded setts (typically with
three buds) generally have a better
germination percentage than single-budded
setts. The multiple buds provide insurance
against germination failure, and the larger
sett contains more stored nutrients and water
to support initial growth.
Statement 3 is incorrect. Under bad weather
conditions (like drought or excessive heat),
large setts have better survival than singlebudded setts. The larger piece of cane stalk
holds more water and nutrients, giving the
new shoot a better buffer and a higher chance
of survival until conditions improve.
Statement 4 is correct. Tissue culture is a
modern technique used for mass propagation
of disease-free planting material. In
sugarcane, this method is used to produce a
large number of genetically uniform and
healthy plantlets (settlings), which can then
be used for cultivation.
Since statements 1 and 4 are correct, the
correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2021

Q1. Bollgard I and Bollgard II technologies are
mentioned in the context of
(a) Clonal propagation of crop plants
(b) Developing genetically modified crop plants
(c) Production of plant growth substances
(d) Production of biofertilizers
The correct option is (b).
• Bollgard I and Bollgard II are trademarked
brand names for specific genetic traits
developed by Monsanto (now Bayer) for
cotton.

•

•

•

These technologies involve the insertion of
genes from the soil bacterium Bacillus
thuringiensis (Bt) into the cotton plant's
genome. This makes them genetically
modified (GM) crop plants.
The inserted genes cause the plant to produce
proteins that are toxic to certain insect pests,
particularly the bollworm complex.
Bollgard I was a single-gene technology, while
Bollgard II is a more advanced double-gene
technology providing broader protection.
They are not related to clonal propagation,
plant growth substances, or biofertilizers.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. In the context of hereditary diseases, consider
the following statements:
1. Passing on mitochondrial diseases from
parent to child can be prevented by
mitochondrial replacement therapy either
before or after in vitro fertilization of the egg.
2. A child inherits mitochondrial diseases
entirely from mother and not from father.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 2 is correct. Mitochondria, the
cell's powerhouses, contain their own small
set of DNA (mtDNA). During fertilization, the
sperm contributes its nucleus but very few, if
any, of its mitochondria to the resulting
zygote. The egg cell, however, contributes all
of the zygote's mitochondria. Therefore, a
child inherits mitochondrial DNA and any
associated diseases entirely from the mother.
• Statement 1 is correct. Mitochondrial
Replacement Therapy (MRT) is a set of
techniques designed to prevent the
transmission of mitochondrial diseases. This
can be done using methods like pronuclear
transfer (performed after fertilization) or
spindle transfer (performed before

•

fertilization). In both cases, the parents'
nuclear DNA is transferred to a donor egg
that has healthy mitochondria. This allows
the resulting child to be free of the mother's
mitochondrial disease.
Since both statements are factually correct,
the correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q3. The term ‘ACE2’ is talked about in the context of:
(a) Genes introduced in the genetically modified
plants
(b) The development of India’s own satellite
navigation system
(c) Radio collars for wildlife tracking
(d) The spread of viral diseases
The correct option is (d).
• ACE2 stands for Angiotensin-Converting
Enzyme 2. It is an enzyme attached to the cell
membranes of cells located in the lungs,
arteries, heart, kidney, and intestines.
• The term gained widespread public attention
during the COVID-19 pandemic because the
ACE2 enzyme acts as the primary receptor for
the SARS-CoV-2 virus.
• The spike protein on the surface of the
coronavirus binds to the ACE2 receptor on a
human cell, which allows the virus to enter
the cell and begin replicating. Therefore, ACE2
is a key factor in the context of the spread of
this viral disease.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. Consider the following statements:
1. Adenoviruses have single stranded DNA
genomes whereas retroviruses have doublestranded DNA genomes.
2. Common cold is sometimes caused by an
adenovirus whereas AIDS is caused by a
retrovirus.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2

The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. This statement has
the genetic material types reversed and also
contains an error.
• Adenoviruses are a family of viruses that have
double-stranded DNA (dsDNA) genomes.
• Retroviruses are a family of viruses that have
single-stranded RNA (ssRNA) genomes. They
are unique because they use an enzyme called
reverse transcriptase to convert their RNA
into DNA after entering a host cell.
• Statement 2 is correct. Adenoviruses are a
common cause of respiratory illnesses,
including bronchitis, pneumonia, and some
cases of the common cold.
• AIDS (Acquired Immunodeficiency
Syndrome) is caused by the Human
Immunodeficiency Virus (HIV), which is a
classic example of a retrovirus.
• Since only the second statement is correct,
the correct option is (b).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. With reference to recent developments
regarding ‘Recombinant Vector Vaccine’, consider
the following statements:
1. Genetic engineering is applied in the
development of these vaccines.
2. Bacteria and viruses are used as vectors.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• A Recombinant Vector Vaccine uses a
harmless or weakened virus or bacterium
(the "vector") to deliver genetic material
from a different, disease-causing pathogen
into the body.
• Statement 1 is correct. The process of
creating such a vaccine fundamentally relies
on genetic engineering. Scientists take a gene
from the target pathogen (e.g., the gene for
the spike protein of the coronavirus) and

•

•

insert it into the genome of the harmless
vector virus. This is a core application of
recombinant DNA technology.
Statement 2 is correct. Both viruses and
bacteria can be used as vectors.
o Viral vectors are more common. For
example, the Oxford-AstraZeneca and
Johnson & Johnson COVID-19
vaccines use a modified, harmless
adenovirus as a vector.
o Bacterial vectors are also used. For
instance, a weakened form of the
Salmonella bacterium can be
engineered to deliver antigens for
other diseases.
Since both statements are correct, the
correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. Which one of the following is a reason why
astronomical distances are measured in light-years?
(a) Distances among stellar bodies do not change.
(b) Gravity of stellar bodies does not change.
(c) Light always travels in a straight line.
(d) Speed of light is always the same.
The correct option is (d).
• A light-year is a unit of distance, not time. It is
the distance that light travels in a vacuum in
one Julian year.
• The reason it is used as a standard for
measuring vast astronomical distances is
because the speed of light in a vacuum is a
universal constant. Its value is the same for
all observers, regardless of their motion or
the motion of the light source.
• This constant and incredibly high speed
makes it a reliable and convenient yardstick
for the immense scales of the cosmos.
• The other options are incorrect: Distances
between stellar bodies do change (a), gravity
certainly changes depending on mass and
distance (b), and while light travels in a
straight line in a uniform medium, its path is
bent by gravity (c). The key is the constancy

of its speed.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q7. In a pressure cooker, the temperature at which
the food is cooked depends mainly upon which of
the following?
1. Area of the hole in the lid
2. Temperature of the flame
3. Weight of the lid
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• A pressure cooker works by increasing the
boiling point of water. At normal atmospheric
pressure, water boils at 100°C. By sealing the
pot, steam is trapped, which increases the
pressure inside. This higher pressure raises
the boiling point of water, allowing food to
cook at a higher temperature and thus faster.
• 1. Area of the hole in the lid: The hole (vent)
works in conjunction with the weight on top
of it. The pressure inside is determined by the
force (from the weight) divided by the area of
the hole (Pressure = Force/Area). Therefore,
the area of the hole is a crucial factor in
determining the internal pressure and
cooking temperature.
• 2. Temperature of the flame: The
temperature of the flame only affects how
quickly the cooker reaches its operating
pressure and temperature. Once the
operating pressure is reached (and the weight
starts releasing excess steam), the internal
temperature stabilizes at the boiling point
corresponding to that pressure. A higher
flame will just cause steam to be released
faster but will not increase the internal
cooking temperature further.
• 3. Weight of the lid: The weight of the lid
(specifically, the weight placed on the vent) is
the primary factor that determines the
maximum pressure that can build up inside. A

•

heavier weight requires more steam pressure
to lift it, resulting in a higher internal pressure
and a higher cooking temperature.
Since the cooking temperature depends on
the internal pressure, which is set by the
weight and the area of the hole, the correct
option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q8. Consider the following:
1. Bacteria
2. Fungi
3. Virus
Which of the above can be cultured in
artificial/synthetic medium?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• An artificial or synthetic medium is a
nutrient-rich substance (like nutrient agar or
broth) prepared in a laboratory that does not
contain any living cells. It provides all the
necessary nutrients for microorganisms to
grow.
• 1. Bacteria: Most bacteria are free-living
organisms and can be easily grown (cultured)
on an artificial medium that provides the
necessary nutrients.
• 2. Fungi: Most fungi, such as yeasts and
molds, can also be readily cultured on
artificial media like potato dextrose agar.
• 3. Virus: Viruses are obligate intracellular
parasites. This means they lack their own
metabolic machinery and can only replicate
inside a living host cell. They cannot be
cultured on a non-living artificial/synthetic
medium. To grow viruses in a lab, one must
first grow a culture of living host cells (like
animal cells or bacteria) and then infect those
cells with the virus.
• Therefore, only bacteria and fungi can be
cultured in an artificial medium.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q9. With reference to street – lighting, how do
sodium lamps differ from LED lamps?
1. Sodium lamps produce light in 360 degrees
but it is not so in the case of LED lamps.
2. As street -lights, sodium lamps have a longer
lifespan than LED lamps.
3. The spectrum of visible light from sodium
lamps is almost monochromatic while LED
lamps offer significant colour advantages in
street lighting.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 3 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. Sodium lamps are
omnidirectional, meaning they emit light in
360 degrees. For street lighting, this is
inefficient, as a significant portion of the light
has to be reflected downwards by the fixture.
LEDs are directional light sources, typically
emitting light in a 180-degree pattern. This
directs more light to the target area without
the need for reflectors, increasing efficiency.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. LED lamps have a
significantly longer lifespan than sodium
lamps. A typical high-pressure sodium (HPS)
lamp has a lifespan of around 24,000 hours,
whereas a modern LED street light can last for
50,000 to 100,000 hours.
• Statement 3 is correct. Low-pressure sodium
lamps produce an almost monochromatic
yellow-orange light, which makes it very
difficult to distinguish colors. High-pressure
sodium lamps have a slightly broader
spectrum but still offer poor color rendering.
LED lamps, on the other hand, can be
designed to produce white light with a high
Color Rendering Index (CRI), offering
significant color advantages that improve
visibility and safety in street lighting.

•

Since statements 1 and 3 are correct, the
correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

•

•

Q10. Bisphenol A (BPA), a cause of concern, is a
structural/ key component in the manufacture of
which of the following kinds of plastics?
(a) Low-density polyethylene
(b) Polycarbonate
(c) Polyethylene terephthalate
(d) Polyvinyl chloride
The correct option is (b).
• Bisphenol A (BPA) is a chemical monomer
used in the synthesis of certain plastics and
resins.
• Its primary application is as a key building
block in the manufacture of polycarbonate
plastic.
• Polycarbonate is a strong, shatter-resistant,
and often transparent thermoplastic. It is
used to make a wide variety of products,
including reusable water bottles, food
storage containers, eyewear, and electronic
device casings.
• BPA is a cause of concern because it is an
endocrine disruptor that can leach from
polycarbonate products, leading to potential
health risks. It is not a key component in the
manufacture of LDPE, PET, or PVC.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q11. ‘Triclosan’, considered harmful when exposed
to high levels for a long time, is most likely present in
which of the following?
(a) Food preservatives
(b) Fruit-ripening substances
(c) Reused plastic containers
(d) Toiletries
The correct option is (d).
• Triclosan is a synthetic chemical with
antibacterial and antifungal properties.
• For decades, it has been widely added as an
antimicrobial agent to a variety of consumer
products, particularly personal care products
and toiletries.

It is most likely to be found in products such
as antibacterial soaps, body washes,
toothpastes, and some cosmetics.
Concerns have been raised about its potential
to disrupt hormones and contribute to
antibiotic resistance, leading to its ban or
restriction in certain products in some
countries. It is not used as a food
preservative, fruit-ripener, or in the
manufacture of plastic containers.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q12. Water can dissolve more substances than any
other liquid because
(a) It is dipolar in nature
(b) It is a good conductor of heat
(c) It has high value of specific heat
(d) It is an oxide of hydrogen
The correct option is (a).
• Water is known as the "universal solvent"
because of its remarkable ability to dissolve a
wide variety of substances.
• This ability stems from the polar nature of the
water molecule (H₂O). The oxygen atom in
the molecule has a slight negative charge,
while the two hydrogen atoms have a slight
positive charge.
• This charge separation creates a dipole. The
positive end of the water molecule is
attracted to negatively charged ions (anions),
and the negative end is attracted to positively
charged ions (cations). This attraction is
strong enough to pull apart the ionic bonds in
many salts and surround the individual ions,
causing the substance to dissolve. This same
polarity allows it to dissolve other polar
molecules (like sugars) through hydrogen
bonding.
• Its thermal properties (b, c) and its chemical
