composition (d) do not explain its solvent
ability.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2022
Q1. “Biorock technology” is talked about in which
one of the following situations?
(a) Clonal propagation of crop plants
(b) Development of building materials using plant
residues
(c) Identification of areas for exploration/extraction of
shale gas
(d) Providing salt licks for wild animals in
forests/protected areas
The correct option is (a).
• Biorock technology, also known as mineral
accretion technology, is a method used for
coral reef restoration.
• The process involves passing a low-voltage
electrical current through a submerged
conductive structure, typically made of steel.
• This electrolysis causes minerals naturally
dissolved in seawater (like calcium carbonate
and magnesium hydroxide) to precipitate and
coat the structure, forming a limestone-like
substrate.
• Fragments of damaged coral are then
attached to this structure. The Biorock
material provides a solid base for the corals to
grow on, and the electrical field is believed to
stimulate their growth rate, making them
more resilient to environmental stresses like
warming waters.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. Consider the following statements:
DNA Barcoding can be a tool to:
1. assess the age of a plant or animal.
2. distinguish among species that look alike.
3. identify undesirable animal or plant materials
in processed foods.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1 and 2
(d) 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).

•

•

•

•

•

DNA barcoding is a method of species
identification that uses a short, standardized
section of DNA from an organism's genome.
Statement 1 is incorrect. The age of a plant or
animal is typically assessed using methods like
radiocarbon dating or dendrochronology.
DNA barcoding identifies what a species is,
not how old it is.
Statement 2 is correct. This is a primary
function of DNA barcoding. It is particularly
useful for distinguishing between cryptic
species—species that are morphologically
very similar but are genetically distinct.
Statement 3 is correct. DNA barcoding is a
powerful tool for food authentication and
safety. By sequencing a DNA barcode from a
processed food product, scientists can identify
the species present and detect substitutions
or undeclared materials.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q3. Consider the following statements in respect of
probiotics:
1. Probiotics are made of both bacteria and
yeast.
2. The organisms in probiotics are found in food
we ingest but they do not naturally occur in
our gut.
3. Probiotics help in the digestion of milk sugars.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. Probiotics are live
microorganisms that, when administered in
adequate amounts, confer a health benefit on
the host. They are most commonly bacteria
(e.g., Lactobacillus, Bifidobacterium), but can
also include certain types of yeast (e.g.,
Saccharomyces boulardii).
• Statement 2 is incorrect. While we ingest
probiotic organisms in food, many of the
same or similar types of beneficial microbes

•

•

do naturally occur in our gut. The human gut
microbiota includes many probiotic species.
Statement 3 is correct. Many probiotic
bacteria, particularly those found in yogurt
and other fermented dairy products (like
Lactobacillus species), produce the enzyme
lactase (β-galactosidase). This enzyme helps
in the digestion of lactose, the primary sugar
found in milk.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. Which one of the following statements best
describes the role of B cells and T cells in the human
body?
(a) They protect the body from environmental
allergens.
(b) They alleviate the body’s pain and inflammation.
(c) They act as immune suppressants in the body.
(d) They protect the body from the diseases caused by
pathogens.
The correct option is (d).
• B cells and T cells are the two main types of
lymphocytes, which are a type of white blood
cell. They are key players in the adaptive
immune system.
• The primary role of the adaptive immune
system is to recognize and mount a specific
defense against foreign invaders, or
pathogens, such as viruses, bacteria, and
fungi.
• B cells are responsible for the humoral
immune response. When activated, they
differentiate into plasma cells that produce
antibodies. These antibodies circulate and
bind to specific pathogens, neutralizing them
or marking them for destruction.
• T cells are responsible for the cell-mediated
immune response. Helper T cells coordinate
the immune response, while cytotoxic T cells
directly identify and kill infected host cells.
• Therefore, their fundamental role is to
protect the body from diseases caused by
pathogens.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q5. With reference to “Software as a Service (SaaS)”,
consider the following statements:
1. SaaS buyers can customise the user interface
and can change data fields.
2. SaaS users can access their data through their
mobile devices.
3. Outlook, Hotmail and Yahoo Mail are forms of
SaaS.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Software as a Service (SaaS) is a cloud
computing model where software is licensed
on a subscription basis and is centrally
hosted. Users access the software over the
internet.
• Statement 1 is correct. Modern SaaS
applications are typically configurable. While
users cannot change the core code, they can
often customize the user interface (UI) and
configure settings such as adding or hiding
data fields.
• Statement 2 is correct. A key benefit of SaaS
is accessibility. Since the application is
accessed via the internet, users can access
their data from any internet-connected
device, including mobile devices.
• Statement 3 is correct. Web-based email
services like Outlook, Hotmail, and Yahoo!
Mail are classic examples of SaaS. The
provider manages the backend infrastructure
and users access the service via a web
browser.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. With reference to Web 3.0, consider the
following statements:
1. Web 3.0 technology enables people to control
their own data.
2. In Web 3.0 world, there can be blockchain
based social networks.

3. Web 3.0 is operated by users collectively
rather than a corporation.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Web 3.0 (Web3) represents the next
evolution of the internet, envisioned as a
decentralized internet built on blockchain
technology.
• Statement 1 is correct. A core principle of
Web 3.0 is decentralization and user
empowerment. Unlike Web 2.0 where large
corporations control user data, Web 3.0 aims
to give people control over their own data,
often via crypto wallets that manage digital
identity and assets.
• Statement 2 is correct. Because Web 3.0 is
built on decentralized technologies, it allows
for the creation of decentralized applications
(dApps), including blockchain-based social
networks where users have greater
ownership over content and the social graph.
• Statement 3 is correct. The decentralized
architecture of Web 3.0 means platforms and
services can be governed and operated by
users collectively through mechanisms like
Decentralized Autonomous Organizations
(DAOs), rather than being controlled by a
single corporation.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q7. Which one of the following is the context in
which the term “qubit” is mentioned?
(a) Cloud Services
(b) Quantum Computing
(c) Visible Light Communication Technologies
(d) Wireless Communication Technologies
The correct option is (b).
• The term "qubit" is short for quantum bit.
• It is the basic unit of quantum information in
Quantum Computing.

•

•

Unlike a classical bit, which can only be in one
of two states (0 or 1), a qubit can exist in a
superposition of both states simultaneously.
This property, along with quantum
entanglement, allows quantum computers to
process a vast number of calculations at once,
giving them the potential to solve certain
complex problems that are intractable for
classical computers.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q8. Consider the following communication
technologies:
1. Closed-circuit Television
2. Radio Frequency Identification
3. Wireless Local Area Network
Which of the above are considered Short-Range
devices/technologies?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Short-Range Devices (SRDs) are radio devices
that offer a low risk of interference with other
radio services, usually because their
transmitted power, and hence their range, is
low.
• 1. Closed-circuit Television (CCTV): Modern
wireless CCTV systems use radio frequencies
(like the 2.4 GHz or 5.8 GHz bands) to
transmit video signals from the camera to a
recorder over a limited distance, typically
within a single property. This fits the
definition of a short-range technology.
• 2. Radio Frequency Identification (RFID):
RFID technology uses radio waves to identify
objects. Passive RFID tags have a very short
range (from centimeters to a few meters),
while active tags can have a slightly longer
range, but it is still fundamentally a shortrange technology used for applications like
access control and inventory tracking.
• 3. Wireless Local Area Network (WLAN):
Commonly known as Wi-Fi, WLAN is designed

•

to provide wireless network connectivity
within a localized area, such as a home, office,
or public hotspot. Its range is typically limited
to tens of meters, clearly classifying it as a
short-range technology.
Since all three technologies operate over
limited distances, they are all considered
short-range.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q9. Consider the following statements:
1. Other than those made by humans,
nanoparticles do not exist in nature.
2. Nanoparticles of some metallic oxides are
used in the manufacture of some cosmetics.
3. Nanoparticles of some commercial products
which enter the environment are unsafe for
humans.
Which of the above statements is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1 and 2 only
(d) 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Nanoparticles do
exist in nature. They are produced by
numerous natural processes, such as volcanic
eruptions, forest fires, weathering of rocks,
and even by some biological processes. These
are known as natural or incidental
nanoparticles.
• Statement 2 is correct. Nanoparticles of
certain metallic oxides, particularly zinc oxide
(ZnO) and titanium dioxide (TiO₂), are widely
used in the manufacture of cosmetics,
especially sunscreens. In their nanoparticle
form, they are transparent on the skin while
still being highly effective at blocking harmful
UV radiation.
• Statement 3 is correct. There are significant
concerns about the safety of some
engineered nanoparticles. When
nanoparticles from commercial products (like
cosmetics, paints, or clothing) enter the
environment, their small size and high

•

reactivity can pose risks. They can be toxic to
some organisms, accumulate in the food
chain, and their potential long-term effects
on human health are an active area of
research and concern (nanotoxicology).
Since statements 2 and 3 are correct, the
correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q10. If a major solar storm (solar flare) reaches the
Earth, which of the following are the possible effects
on the Earth?
1. GPS and navigation systems could fail.
2. Tsunamis could occur at equatorial regions.
3. Power grids could be damaged.
4. Intense auroras could occur over much of the
Earth
5. Forest fires could take place over much of the
planet.
6. Orbits of the satellites could be disturbed.
7. Shortwave radio communication of the
aircraft flying over polar regions could be
interrupted.
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1, 2, 4 and 5 only
(b) 2, 3, 5, 6 and 7 only
(c) 1, 3, 4, 6 and 7 only
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7
The correct option is (c).
• A major solar storm involves the ejection of
charged particles and electromagnetic
radiation from the Sun, which can interact
with the Earth's magnetic field and
atmosphere.
• 1. GPS and navigation systems could fail:
Correct. The storm can disturb the Earth's
ionosphere, which can delay, distort, and
scintillate the radio signals from GPS
satellites, leading to inaccurate positioning or
complete failure.
• 2. Tsunamis could occur: Incorrect. Tsunamis
are giant sea waves caused by underwater
earthquakes, volcanic eruptions, or
landslides, not by solar storms.

•

•

•

•

•

•

3. Power grids could be damaged: Correct.
The storm can induce powerful
geomagnetically induced currents (GICs) in
long conductors on the ground, such as power
lines. These currents can overload and
damage large transformers in the power
grids, potentially causing widespread
blackouts.
4. Intense auroras could occur: Correct. The
interaction of the storm's charged particles
with the Earth's magnetosphere energizes
particles in the upper atmosphere, causing
them to glow. A major storm can create
intense auroras visible over a much wider
area than usual.
5. Forest fires could take place: Incorrect.
Solar flares are bursts of radiation, not fire.
They do not have the ability to ignite fires on
Earth.
6. Orbits of the satellites could be disturbed:
Correct. The storm heats and expands the
Earth's upper atmosphere, increasing
atmospheric drag on satellites in low-Earth
orbit. This increased drag can cause their
orbits to decay more quickly; satellite
electronics can also be damaged.
7. Shortwave radio communication could be
interrupted: Correct. The storm heavily
disrupts the ionosphere, which is used to
reflect shortwave radio signals for longdistance communication. This can cause a
complete blackout of high-frequency radio
communications, particularly affecting
aircraft flying over polar regions.
Therefore, the possible effects are 1, 3, 4, 6,
and 7.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q11. Which one of the following statements best
reflects the idea behind the “Fractional Orbital
Bombardment System” often talked about in the
media?
(a) A hypersonic missile is launched into space to
counter the asteroid approaching the Earth and
explode it in space.

(b) A spacecraft lands on another planet after making
several orbital motions.
(c) A missile is put into a stable orbit around the Earth
and deorbits over a target on the Earth.
(d) A spacecraft moves along a comet with the same
speed and places a probe on its surface
The correct option is (c).
• A Fractional Orbital Bombardment System
(FOBS) is a type of warhead delivery system.
• The core idea is to launch a payload (a missile
or warhead) into a low Earth orbit.
• The payload travels in this stable orbit for a
portion (a "fraction") of a full orbital period.
• Just before it reaches the intended target, it
fires a retrograde engine to deorbit, causing it
to re-enter the atmosphere and strike the
target from an unpredictable direction.
• The main strategic advantage is that its
trajectory does not reveal the final target
until the very end, making it difficult to track
and intercept compared to a traditional
ballistic missile, which follows a predictable
arc.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q12. Consider the following statements:
1. Biofilms can form on medical implants within
human tissues.
2. Biofilms can form on food and food
processing surfaces.
3. Biofilms can exhibit antibiotic resistance.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• A biofilm is a structured community of
microorganisms that adhere to a surface and
are encased in a self-produced slimy matrix.
• Statement 1 is correct. Biofilms are a major
problem in medicine as they can form on the
surfaces of medical implants, such as
catheters, artificial joints, and heart valves.

•

•

•

These biofilm-related infections are very
difficult to treat.
Statement 2 is correct. Biofilms can readily
form on surfaces in the food industry, such as
pipes, tanks, and cutting boards. They can be
a persistent source of contamination, leading
to food spoilage and the transmission of
foodborne illnesses.
Statement 3 is correct. One of the most
significant characteristics of biofilms is their
high level of antibiotic resistance. The slimy
matrix acts as a physical barrier, preventing
antibiotics from reaching the bacteria inside.
Furthermore, bacteria within a biofilm are in a
different physiological state and can
exchange resistance genes, making them
much more resistant to antimicrobial agents
than their free-floating counterparts.
Since all three statements are correct, the
correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2023

Q1. Consider the following heavy industries:
1. Fertilizer plants
2. Oil refineries
3. Steel plants
Green hydrogen is expected to play a significant role
in decarbonizing how many of the above industries?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All three
(d) None
The correct option is (c).
• Green hydrogen is produced through the
electrolysis of water using renewable energy,
making it a carbon-free energy carrier and
feedstock.
• It is seen as a key solution for decarbonizing
"hard-to-abate" heavy industries.
• Fertilizer plants: The production of ammonia,
a key component of nitrogen-based fertilizers,
is currently a very carbon-intensive process
that uses natural gas (grey hydrogen). Green

•

•

•

•

hydrogen can directly replace grey hydrogen
in the Haber-Bosch process to produce green
ammonia, thus decarbonizing the fertilizer
industry.
Oil refineries: Refineries use large amounts of
hydrogen (typically grey hydrogen) for
processes like hydrotreating (to remove
sulfur) and hydrocracking (to break down
heavy hydrocarbons). Green hydrogen can be
used as a direct substitute to decarbonize
these refining processes.
Steel plants: Traditional steelmaking uses
coke (derived from coal) as a reducing agent
in a blast furnace, which is a major source of
CO₂ emissions. Green hydrogen can be used
as a clean reducing agent in a Direct Reduced
Iron (DRI) process, producing "green steel"
with significantly lower or zero carbon
emissions.
Since green hydrogen has a clear and
significant role in decarbonizing all three
listed industries, the correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. ‘Wolbachia method’ is sometimes talked about
with reference to which one of the following?
(a) Controlling the viral diseases spread by mosquitoes
(b) Converting crop residues into packing material
(c) Producing biodegradable plastics
(d) Producing biochar from thermochemical
conversion of biomass
The correct option is (a).
• The Wolbachia method is an innovative
biological control strategy used to combat
mosquito-borne viral diseases.
• Wolbachia is a naturally occurring bacterium
that is harmless to humans but affects the
ability of mosquitoes to transmit viruses.
• The method involves releasing male
mosquitoes that have been infected with the
Wolbachia bacterium into the wild. When
these males mate with wild female
mosquitoes, the resulting eggs do not hatch,
which helps to suppress the mosquito
population.

•

•

Alternatively, when female mosquitoes
carrying Wolbachia are released, they pass
the bacterium to their offspring. Mosquitoes
with Wolbachia have a reduced ability to
transmit viruses like dengue, Zika, and
chikungunya. This makes the Wolbachia
method a powerful tool for controlling the
spread of these viral diseases.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. Consider the following activities:
1. Spreading finely ground basalt rock on
farmlands extensively.
2. Increasing the alkalinity of oceans by adding
lime.
3. Capturing carbon dioxide released by various
industries and pumping it into abandoned
subterranean mines in the form of carbonated
waters.
How many of the above activities are often
considered and discussed for carbon capture and
sequestration?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All three
(d) None
The correct option is (c).
• The question asks about methods discussed
for carbon capture and sequestration (CCS),
which are techniques designed to remove CO₂
from the atmosphere and store it long-term.
• 1. Enhanced Weathering: Spreading finely
ground silicate rocks like basalt on farmlands
is a method known as enhanced weathering.
The minerals in the rock react with CO₂ in the
atmosphere and soil, converting it into stable
carbonate minerals, thereby sequestering the
carbon. This is a widely discussed carbon
removal technique.
• 2. Ocean Alkalinity Enhancement: Adding
alkaline substances like lime (calcium oxide)
or olivine to the oceans increases their
alkalinity. This enhances the ocean's natural
capacity to absorb and store atmospheric CO₂
by converting it into stable bicarbonate and

•

•

•

carbonate ions. This is another major area of
research for carbon sequestration.
3. Carbon Capture and Storage (CCS): This
involves capturing CO₂ emissions directly from
industrial sources (like power plants or
cement factories) and injecting it into deep
geological formations for long-term storage.
Abandoned subterranean mines, saline
aquifers, and depleted oil and gas reservoirs
are considered potential storage sites.
Injecting it as carbonated water is one of the
methods to ensure it remains trapped.
Since all three activities are prominent and
frequently discussed methods for carbon
capture and sequestration, the correct option
is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. ‘Aerial metagenomics’ best refers to which one
of the following situations?
(a) Collecting DNA samples from air in a habitat at one
go
(b) Understanding the genetic makeup of avian
species of a habitat
(c) Using air-borne devices to collect samples from
moving animals
(d) Sending drones to inaccessible areas to collect
plant and animal samples from land surfaces and
water bodies
The correct option is (a).
• Metagenomics is the study of genetic
material recovered directly from
environmental samples. It allows scientists to
study the community of microorganisms in a
habitat without needing to culture them in a
lab.
• Aerial metagenomics applies this concept to
the air. It involves using air samplers (which
can be on the ground or on drones/aircraft) to
collect environmental DNA (eDNA)—
fragments of genetic material shed by
organisms—that is suspended in the air of a
particular habitat.
• By sequencing this collected DNA, scientists
can identify the various species of plants,

•

animals, fungi, and bacteria present in that
habitat, all from a single air sample.
Therefore, it refers to collecting DNA samples
from the air in a habitat at one go.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. ‘Microsatellite DNA’ is used in the case of which
one of the following?
(a) Studying the evolutionary relationships among
various species of fauna
(b) Stimulating ‘stem cells’ to transform into diverse
functional tissues
(c) Promoting clonal propagation of horticultural
plants
(d) Assessing the efficacy of drugs by conducting
series of drug trials in a population
The correct option is (a).
• Microsatellite DNA, also known as Short
Tandem Repeats (STRs), are short, repetitive
sequences of DNA that are found throughout
an organism's genome.
• The number of repeats at a specific
microsatellite locus can vary greatly between
individuals and species. This high level of
variation makes them excellent genetic
markers.
• By comparing the patterns and lengths of
microsatellites across different individuals,
populations, or species, geneticists can
determine levels of genetic relatedness.
• This makes them a powerful tool for studying
evolutionary relationships, as well as for
applications like population genetics,
conservation biology, and DNA fingerprinting
for paternity testing and forensics. They are
not used for stimulating stem cells, clonal
propagation, or assessing drug efficacy.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. Consider the following statements:
1. Carbon fibres are used in the manufacture of
components used in automobiles and
aircrafts.
2. Carbon fibres, once used, cannot be recycled.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. Carbon fibres are
known for their exceptional strength-toweight ratio, high stiffness, and temperature
tolerance. These properties make them an
ideal material for manufacturing highperformance, lightweight components in the
automotive (especially for racing and
supercars) and aerospace (for fuselage, wings,
and other structural parts of aircrafts)
industries, where reducing weight is critical
for fuel efficiency and performance.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. While recycling
carbon fibre composites is more complex and
energy-intensive than recycling materials like
aluminum, it is technically possible and
increasingly being done. Methods like
pyrolysis (heating in the absence of oxygen)
can be used to burn off the polymer matrix,
allowing the valuable carbon fibres to be
recovered and reused in new composite
materials. Therefore, the statement that they
"cannot be recycled" is false.
• Since only the first statement is correct, the
correct option is (a).
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. Consider the following actions:
1. Detection of car crash/collision, which result
in the deployment of airbags almost
instantaneously.
2. Detection of accidental free fall of a laptop
towards the ground which results in the
immediate turning off of the hard drive.
3. Detection of the tilt of the smart-phone which
results in the rotation of display between
portrait and landscape mode.
In how many of the above actions is the
function of the accelerometer required?
(a) Only one

(b) Only two
(c) All three
(d) None
The correct option is (c).
• An accelerometer is a sensor that measures
proper acceleration.
• This includes the static force of gravity and
the dynamic forces caused by movement or
vibration.
• 1. Car crash detection: In a car, an
accelerometer detects the sudden, large
negative acceleration (deceleration) that
occurs during a crash. This signal is sent to the
airbag control unit, triggering the deployment
of the airbags. This is a critical function of the
accelerometer.
• 2. Laptop free fall detection: Many laptops
are equipped with an accelerometer for hard
drive protection. It continuously monitors the
device's orientation relative to gravity. If it
detects a sudden change corresponding to
free fall, it signals the system to park the hard
drive's read/write heads, preventing damage
upon impact.
• 3. Smartphone screen rotation: Smartphones
use an accelerometer to detect changes in
their orientation. When you tilt the phone,
the accelerometer measures the change in
the direction of the force of gravity and
signals the operating system to rotate the
display between portrait and landscape
modes.
• Since the accelerometer is required for all
three described actions, the correct option is
(c).
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. With reference to the role of biofilters in
Recirculating Aquaculture System, consider the
following statements:
1. Biofilters provide waste treatment by
removing uneaten fish feed.
2. Biofilters convert ammonia present in fish
waste to nitrate.

3. Biofilters increase phosphorus as a nutrient
for fish in water.
How many of the statements given above are correct?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All three
(d) None
The correct option is (b).
• A Recirculating Aquaculture System (RAS) is a
system for farming fish where water is
treated and reused. Biofilters are a critical
component of the water treatment process.
• Statement 1 is correct. While the primary role
of a biofilter is biological nitrification, the
overall filtration system in a RAS, which
includes mechanical filters often placed
before the biofilter, is designed to remove
solid waste. Biofilters themselves, with their
large surface area, can trap and break down
solid organic waste like uneaten fish feed and
feces, thus contributing to waste treatment.
• Statement 2 is correct. This is the main and
most crucial function of a biofilter in RAS. Fish
excrete toxic ammonia as a waste product.
The biofilter provides a large surface area for
beneficial bacteria to grow. These bacteria
perform nitrification, a two-step process that
converts toxic ammonia first into nitrite (also
toxic) and then into much less harmful
nitrate.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. High levels of
phosphorus in the water can lead to problems
like eutrophication and are generally
undesirable. The goal of the filtration system
is to manage or remove excess nutrients, not
increase them. Biofilters do not increase
phosphorus; in some advanced systems,
components are added specifically to remove
phosphorus.
• Since statements 1 and 2 are correct, the
correct option is (b).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q9. Consider the following pairs:
Objects in space — Description

1. Cepheids : Giant clouds of dust and gas in
space
2. Nebulae : Stars which brighten and dim
periodically
3. Pulsars : Neutron stars that are formed when
massive stars run out of fuel and collapse
How many of the above pairs are correctly matched?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All three
(d) None
The correct option is (a).
• Pair 1 is incorrectly matched. Cepheids (or
Cepheid Variables) are stars that brighten
and dim periodically. They are not clouds of
dust and gas. Their period of pulsation is
directly related to their intrinsic luminosity,
making them crucial "standard candles" for
measuring cosmic distances. The description
given actually fits a nebula.
• Pair 2 is incorrectly matched. Nebulae are
giant clouds of dust and gas in space. They
are often "star nurseries" where new stars
are born, or they can be the remnants of dead
or dying stars. The description given ("Stars
which brighten and dim periodically")
actually fits a Cepheid.
• Pair 3 is correctly matched. Pulsars are a type
of rapidly rotating neutron star. Neutron stars
are the extremely dense remnants that are
formed when a massive star runs out of fuel
and collapses in a supernova explosion. As
they spin, they emit beams of radiation that
sweep across space, which are observed as
regular pulses from Earth.
• Since only one pair (Pair 3) is correctly
matched, the correct option is (a).
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q10. Which one of the following countries has its
own Satellite Navigation System?
(a) Australia
(b) Canada
(c) Israel
(d) Japan

The correct option is (d).
• A global satellite navigation system (GNSS)
provides autonomous geospatial positioning
with global coverage. Several countries have
developed their own global or regional
systems.
• (a) Australia: Australia does not have its own
satellite navigation system. It relies on other
systems, primarily the US GPS, and is
developing a ground-based augmentation
system (SBAS) to improve accuracy.
• (b) Canada: Canada does not have its own
satellite navigation system and is a partner in
the US GPS system.
• (c) Israel: Israel does not have its own
independent satellite navigation system. It
relies on GPS and other global systems.
• (d) Japan: Japan has developed and operates
the Quasi-Zenith Satellite System (QZSS), also
known as Michibiki. While it is designed to be
a regional system that augments and
improves the performance of GPS in the AsiaOceania region, it is an independent,
operational satellite navigation system
owned by Japan.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q11. Consider the following statements:
1. Ballistic missiles are jet-propelled at subsonic
speeds throughout their flights, while cruise
missiles are rocket-powered only in the initial
phase of flight.
2. Agni-V is a medium-range supersonic cruise
missile, while BrahMos is a solid – fuelled
intercontinental ballistic missile.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. This statement has
the descriptions of ballistic and cruise
missiles completely reversed.

o

•

•
•

Ballistic missiles are rocket-powered
during the initial boost phase of their
flight. They then follow a high, arcing,
unpowered trajectory (a ballistic
path) to the target.
o Cruise missiles are jet-propelled and
fly at low altitudes within the
atmosphere, much like an airplane,
often at subsonic or supersonic
speeds, throughout their flight.
Statement 2 is incorrect. This statement
mischaracterizes both missiles.
o Agni-V is a solid-fueled
intercontinental ballistic missile
(ICBM), not a medium-range cruise
missile.
o BrahMos is a medium-range ramjetpowered supersonic cruise missile,
not a solid-fueled ICBM.
Since both statements are factually incorrect,
the correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q12. With reference to green hydrogen, consider the
following statements:
1. It can be used directly as a fuel for internal
combustion.
2. It can be blended with natural gas and used as
fuel for heat or power generation.
3. It can be used in the hydrogen fuel cell to run
vehicles.
How many of the above statements are correct?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All three
(d) None
The correct option is (c).
• Green hydrogen is hydrogen produced using
renewable energy. It is a versatile energy
carrier with multiple applications.
• Statement 1 is correct. Hydrogen can be used
as a fuel in a modified internal combustion
engine (ICE). Instead of gasoline, the engine
burns hydrogen with oxygen from the air. The

•

•

•
•

primary emission is water vapor, making it a
low-carbon option.
Statement 2 is correct. Hydrogen can be
blended with natural gas and injected into
existing gas pipelines. This blend can then be
burned in power plants or for
industrial/residential heat generation,
reducing the carbon intensity of the natural
gas supply.
Statement 3 is correct. This is a primary
application for green hydrogen in
transportation. In a hydrogen fuel cell,
hydrogen reacts electrochemically with
oxygen to produce electricity, which then
powers an electric motor to run a vehicle. The
only emission from the tailpipe is water.
Since all three are valid uses for green
hydrogen, the correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2024

Q1. Which one of the following words/phrases is
most appropriately used to denote “an interoperable
network of 3D virtual worlds that can be accessed
simultaneously by millions of users, who can exert
property rights over virtual items”?
(a) Big data analytics
(b) Cryptography
(c) Metaverse
(d) Virtual matrix
The correct option is (c).
• The phrase describes a persistent, shared, 3D
virtual space where users can interact with
each other and with digital objects.
• Key elements mentioned are interoperability,
simultaneous access by many users, and
property rights over virtual items.
• This entire concept defines the Metaverse,
envisioned as the next evolution of the
internet — a persistent virtual universe.
• Big data analytics (a) refers to examining
large datasets.
• Cryptography (b) relates to secure
communication.

•
•

Virtual matrix (d) is not a recognized term for
this concept.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. With reference to radioisotope thermoelectric
generators (RTGs), consider the following
statements:
1. RTGs are miniature fission reactors.
2. RTGs are used for powering the onboard
systems of spacecrafts.
3. RTGs can use Plutonium-238, which is a byproduct of weapons developments.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. RTGs are not fission
reactors. They do not use a chain reaction but
generate electricity from the natural
radioactive decay of a radioisotope.
• Statement 2 is correct. RTGs are long-lasting,
reliable power sources ideal for deep space
missions (e.g., Voyager, Cassini, New
Horizons) where solar power is insufficient.
• Statement 3 is correct. Plutonium-238 is
commonly used as RTG fuel. It is a by-product
of weapons-grade plutonium production and
is specifically suited for steady heat output.
• Since statements 2 and 3 are correct, the
correct option is (b).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following statements:
Statement-I: Giant stars live much longer than dwarf
stars.
Statement-II: Compared to dwarf stars, giant stars
have a greater rate of nuclear reactions.
Which one of the following is correct in respect of the
above statements?
(a) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II explains Statement-I
(b) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct, but
Statement-II does not explain Statement-I

(c) Statement-I is correct, but Statement-II is incorrect
(d) Statement-I is incorrect, but Statement-II is correct
The correct option is (d).
• Statement-II is correct. Giant stars are much
more massive than dwarf stars, causing
higher core pressure and temperature. This
leads to a faster nuclear fusion rate.
• Statement-I is incorrect. Despite having more
fuel, giant stars burn through it much faster,
so their lifespans are shorter. Dwarf stars,
including stars like our Sun, live for billions of
years, while massive giants live only millions
of years.
• Therefore, Statement-I is incorrect but
Statement-II is correct.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following is synthesised in the
human body that dilates blood vessels and increases
blood flow?
(a) Nitric oxide
(b) Nitrous oxide
(c) Nitrogen dioxide
(d) Nitrogen pentoxide
The correct option is (a).
• The human body produces Nitric oxide (NO),
a gaseous signaling molecule.
• It acts as a vasodilator, synthesized by
endothelial cells lining blood vessels.
• When released, it causes smooth muscle
relaxation, leading to vessel dilation and
increased blood flow.
• Nitrous oxide (b) is an anesthetic gas,
Nitrogen dioxide (c) is a pollutant, and
Nitrogen pentoxide (d) is a chemical
compound not produced in the body.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. Consider the following activities:
1. Identification of narcotics on passengers at
airports or in aircraft
2. Monitoring of precipitation
3. Tracking the migration of animals
In how many of the above activities can the radars be
used?

(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All three
(d) None
The correct option is (b).
• Radar (Radio Detection and Ranging) uses
radio waves to detect and track objects.
• Activity 1 – Incorrect: Radar cannot identify
narcotics; this is done using X-ray scanners,
trace detection, or sniffer dogs.
• Activity 2 – Correct: Weather radars detect
raindrops, snow, hail, etc., to monitor
precipitation and storm movement.
• Activity 3 – Correct: Radar systems are used
in biological studies to track animal
migration, including birds, bats, and insects,
based on radar reflections.
• Therefore, radar is used in two of the listed
activities.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. Consider the following aircraft:
1. Rafael
2. MiG-29
3. Tejas MK-1
How many of the above are considered fifthgeneration fighter aircraft?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All three
(d) None
The correct option is (d).
• Fifth-generation fighters feature all-aspect
stealth, advanced avionics, supercruise, and
sensor fusion.
• Rafale: A 4.5-generation multirole fighter
with partial stealth and advanced sensors, not
fifth-generation.
• MiG-29: A 4th-generation fighter from the
Soviet era.
• Tejas MK-1: An Indian 4.5-generation light
combat aircraft.
• None of these aircraft meet the full fifthgeneration criteria (e.g., F-22 Raptor, F-35
Lightning II).

•

Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q7. In which of the following are hydrogels used?
1. Controlled drug delivery in patients
2. Mobile air-conditioning systems
3. Preparation of industrial lubricants
Select the correct answer using the code given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Hydrogels are three-dimensional networks of
hydrophilic polymers that can absorb and
retain large amounts of water.
• 1. Controlled drug delivery: This is a major
biomedical application. Drugs can be
encapsulated within the hydrogel matrix. The
hydrogel can then be designed to release the
drug slowly over time, providing controlled
drug delivery in patients.
• 2. Mobile air-conditioning systems:
Hydrogels are used in evaporative cooling
systems. A hydrogel layer can absorb water
and then, as air passes over it, the water
evaporates, creating a cooling effect. This
principle can be applied to create low-power
personal or mobile air-conditioning or
cooling devices.
• 3. Preparation of industrial lubricants: Due to
their high water content and specific polymer
structure, hydrogels can exhibit excellent
low-friction properties. They are being
developed and used as water-based,
environmentally friendly industrial
lubricants, especially in applications where
conventional oil-based lubricants are not
ideal.
• Since hydrogels have applications in all three
areas, the correct option is (d).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Which one of the following is the exhaust pipe
emission from Fuel Cell Electric Vehicles, powered by
hydrogen?

(a) Hydrogen peroxide
(b) Hydronium
(c) Oxygen
(d) Water vapour
The correct option is (d).
• A Hydrogen Fuel Cell Electric Vehicle (FCEV)
uses a fuel cell to generate electricity to
power its motor.
• The fuel cell works by electrochemically
combining hydrogen (H₂) from a tank with
oxygen (O₂) from the ambient air.
• The chemical reaction is: 2H₂ + O₂ → 2H₂O +
Electricity + Heat.
• The only product that comes out of the
exhaust pipe is water, which is typically in the
form of water vapour. This makes FCEVs zeroemission vehicles at the point of use.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. Recently, the term “pumped-storage
hydropower” is actually and appropriately discussed
in the context of which one of the following?
(a) Irrigation of terraced crop fields
(b) Lift irrigation of cereal crops
(c) Long duration energy storage
(d) Rainwater harvesting system
The correct option is (c).
• Pumped-storage hydropower (PSH) is a type
of energy storage system. It consists of two
water reservoirs at different elevations.
• When there is excess electricity on the grid
(e.g., during periods of high solar or wind
generation), the system uses this power to
pump water from the lower reservoir to the
upper reservoir, effectively storing the energy
as gravitational potential energy.
• When electricity is needed, the water is
released from the upper reservoir back down
to the lower one, flowing through a turbine to
generate electricity.
• Because it can store large amounts of energy
and release it over many hours or even days,
PSH is a leading technology for long-duration
energy storage, which is crucial for stabilizing

•

power grids with high levels of intermittent
renewable energy.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q10. “Membrane Bioreactors” are often discussed in
the context of:
(a) Assisted reproductive technologies
(b) Drug delivery nanotechnologies
(c) Vaccine production technologies
(d) Wastewater treatment technologies
The correct option is (d).
• A Membrane Bioreactor (MBR) is an
advanced technology used for treating
wastewater.
• It combines a biological treatment process
(the "bioreactor," where microorganisms
break down organic waste) with a membrane
filtration process.
• Instead of using a traditional sedimentation
tank to separate the treated water from the
biological sludge, an MBR uses microfiltration
or ultrafiltration membranes.
• This combination allows for a more compact
system that produces a very high-quality
effluent, making it highly effective for both
municipal and industrial wastewater
treatment technologies.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2025
Q1. Consider the following types of vehicles:
I. Full battery electric vehicles
II. Hydrogen fuel cell vehicles
III. Fuel cell-electric hybrid vehicles
How many of the above are considered as alternative
powertrain vehicles?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All the three
(d) None
The correct option is (c).
• An alternative powertrain is any vehicle
propulsion system that does not rely solely on

•

•

•

•

•

a traditional internal combustion engine (ICE)
burning gasoline or diesel.
I. Full battery electric vehicles (BEVs): These
vehicles use an electric motor powered
exclusively by a rechargeable battery pack.
This is a classic example of an alternative
powertrain as it completely replaces the ICE.
II. Hydrogen fuel cell vehicles (FCEVs): These
vehicles use a fuel cell to generate electricity
from hydrogen, which then powers an electric
motor. This is also an alternative powertrain,
as it uses a fuel cell and electric motor
instead of an ICE.
III. Fuel cell-electric hybrid vehicles: These
vehicles combine a hydrogen fuel cell with a
battery. The fuel cell can charge the battery
and/or directly power the motor, while the
battery provides power for acceleration and
regenerative braking. This hybrid system is
also a non-conventional, alternative
powertrain.
Since all three use propulsion systems that
differ from a standard ICE, they are all
considered alternative powertrain vehicles.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. With reference to Unmanned Aerial Vehicles
(UAVs), consider the following statements:
I. All types of UAVs can do vertical landing.
II. All types of UAVs can do automated hovering.
III. All types of UAVs can use battery only as a source
of power supply.
How many of the statements given above are correct?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All the three
(d) None
The correct option is (d).
• Statement I is incorrect. Not all UAVs can
perform vertical landing. Fixed-wing UAVs,
which are designed like conventional
airplanes, require a runway or net for landing.
They cannot land vertically like multirotor
drones.

•

•

•

•

Statement II is incorrect. Automated
hovering is possible for multirotor UAVs and
VTOL hybrids, but fixed-wing UAVs cannot
hover—they must maintain forward motion
to stay aloft.
Statement III is incorrect. Many consumer
UAVs use batteries, but larger, longendurance UAVs, like the MQ-9 Predator, use
internal combustion or jet engines because
liquid fuels have higher energy density than
batteries.
Since all three statements make absolute
claims (“All types”) and are factually
incorrect, the correct answer is none.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q3. In the context of electric vehicle batteries,
consider the following elements:
I. Cobalt
II. Graphite
III. Lithium
IV. Nickel
How many of the above usually make up battery
cathodes?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) Only three
(d) All the four
The correct option is (c).
• A lithium-ion battery consists of a cathode
(positive electrode), anode (negative
electrode), and electrolyte.
• I. Cobalt: Used in cathodes such as Lithium
Cobalt Oxide (LCO) and Nickel Manganese
Cobalt (NMC). It stabilizes structure and
improves energy density.
• II. Graphite: Used as the anode (negative
electrode), not the cathode.
• III. Lithium: A key component of all lithiumion cathodes, forming compounds like LiCoO₂
or LiFePO₄.
• IV. Nickel: Used in high-energy-density
cathodes such as NMC and NCA, improving
energy capacity.

•
•

Therefore, Cobalt, Lithium, and Nickel are
used in cathodes — total three elements.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. What is the common characteristic of the
chemical substances generally known as CL-20, HMX
and LLM-105, which are sometimes talked about in
media?
(a) These are alternatives to hydrofluorocarbon
refrigerants
(b) These are explosives in military weapons
(c) These are high energy fuels for cruise missiles
(d) These are fuels for rocket propulsion
The correct option is (b).
• CL-20 (Hexanitrohexaazaisowurtzitane), HMX
(Octogen), and LLM-105 are all advanced
high-performance explosives.
• They are classified as energetic materials
used in military munitions and warheads.
• CL-20 is one of the most powerful nonnuclear explosives known.
• HMX is a widely used military explosive.
• LLM-105 is a modern “insensitive” high
explosive, safer but still powerful.
• They are not refrigerants, not cruise missile
fuels, and not rocket propellants.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Consider the following statements:
I. It is expected that Majorana l chip will enable
quantum computing.
II. Majorana l chip has been introduced by Amazon
Web Services (AWS).
III. Deep learning is a subset of machine learning.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) I and II only
(b) II and III only
(c) I and III only
(d) I, II and III
The correct option is (c).
• Statement I is correct. Majorana fermions
are exotic particles that are their own
antiparticles. Qubits built using them
(“Majorana-based qubits”) are topologically

•

•

•
•

protected from noise, potentially enabling
stable quantum computing.
Statement II is incorrect. Research on
Majorana-based quantum chips is primarily
led by Microsoft (Station Q lab), not AWS.
Statement III is correct. Deep learning is a
subset of machine learning that uses multilayered artificial neural networks for complex
data analysis.
Thus, statements I and III are correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. With reference to monoclonal antibodies, often
mentioned in news, consider the following
statements:
I. They are man-made proteins.
II. They stimulate immunological function due to their
ability to bind to specific antigens.
III. They are used in treating viral infections like that of
Nipah virus.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) I and II only
(b) II and III only
(c) I and III only
(d) I, II and III
The correct option is (d).
• Statement I is correct. Monoclonal
antibodies (mAbs) are laboratory-made
proteins designed to mimic natural
antibodies.
• Statement II is correct. mAbs bind to specific
antigens (like viral proteins or cancer cell
markers), stimulating immune responses or
neutralizing pathogens.
• Statement III is correct. mAbs are used to
treat diseases like COVID-19, Ebola, and
Nipah virus infections (experimentally).

Q7. Consider the following statements:
I. No virus can survive in ocean waters.
II. No virus can infect bacteria.
III. No virus can change the cellular transcriptional

activity in host cells.
How many of the statements given above are correct?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All the three
(d) None
The correct option is (d).
• Statement I is incorrect. Viruses are the most
abundant biological entities in ocean waters.
Marine viruses, particularly those that infect
bacteria (bacteriophages), play a critical role
in marine ecosystems and nutrient cycling.
• Statement II is incorrect. Viruses that
specifically infect bacteria are called
bacteriophages or phages; they are
ubiquitous and a major area of scientific study
(including phage therapy).
• Statement III is incorrect. Altering the host
cell's functions is fundamental to viral
infection: viruses hijack the host cell's
machinery for replication and change cellular
transcriptional activity to prioritise viral
production.
• Since all three statements are factually
incorrect, the correct option is (d).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Consider the following pairs:
Plant — Description

V. Papaya : Woody shrub

(b) Only three
(c) Only four
(d) All the five
The correct option is (b).

Q9. Consider the following statements:

Which one of the following is correct in respect of the

(b) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct but

(c) Statement I is correct but Statement II is not
correct
(d) Statement I is not correct but Statement II is

Q10. Consider the following statements:
I. Indian Railways have prepared a National Rail Plan
(NRP) to create a future ready railway system by
2028.
II. ‘Kavach’, an Automatic Train Protection system,
developed in collaboration with Germany.
III. ‘Kavach’ system consists of RFID tags fitted on
track in station section.

(a) I and II only
(b) II and III only
(c) I and III only
(d) I, II and III
The correct option is (a).
• Statement I is not correct. The National Rail

railway system by 2030, not 2028.
• Statement II is not correct. Kavach is an

Protection (ATP) system developed by RDSO
with Indian industry — not developed in
collaboration with Germany.
• Statement III is correct. The Kavach system
includes RFID tags on the track in station and
block sections to provide precise location
information to onboard units.
• The statements that are not correct are I and
II.

Q11. Consider the following space missions :
I. Axiom-4
II. SpaDeX
III. Gaganyaan
How many of the space missions given above
encourage and support micro-gravity research?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two

(c) All the three
(d) None
The correct option is (c).
• Microgravity research studies phenomena in
low-gravity (near-zero g) environments — a
primary purpose of missions to space stations
and human spaceflight.
• I. Axiom-4: Axiom missions are private
astronaut missions to the International Space
Station (ISS) and include scientific
experiments and technology demonstrations
in microgravity.
• II. SpaDeX (Space Docking Experiment): A
planned mission demonstrating autonomous
docking — missions operating in orbit provide
opportunities for microgravity research even
if not the primary goal.
• III. Gaganyaan: India’s human spaceflight
programme; human missions typically carry
life-science, materials science, and other
microgravity experiments.
• All three missions involve orbital operations
and encourage or provide opportunities for
microgravity research.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
