Incorrect. The Ilyushin Il-76 is a four-engine
strategic airlifter / transport aircraft, not a
supersonic combat aircraft.
III. C-17 Globemaster III : Military transport
aircraft — Correctly matched. The Boeing C17 Globemaster III is a large military
transport used for strategic airlift of troops
and heavy cargo.
Since I and III are correctly matched, the total
is two.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1979
Q1. The content of water is greater than fats, in the
plasma, proteins are more than fats and fats less
than plasma. Which constitutes the major part of the
human body?
(a) Fats
(b) Water
(c) Plasma
(d) Proteins
The correct option is (b).
• The human body is composed of several
major components, but by far the most
abundant is water.
• On average, water makes up about 60–65%
of an adult's total body weight.
• Proteins are the next most abundant
component, followed by fats.
• Plasma is the liquid component of blood and
is itself mostly water, but it only constitutes a
small fraction of the body's total mass.
• Therefore, water is the single largest
constituent of the human body.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. Pulse reading is done by doctors to find out:
(a) Temperature
(b) Heart beat
(c) Blood pressure
(d) Respiration rate
The correct option is (b).
• The pulse is the rhythmic expansion and recoil
of arteries resulting from the pumping action
of the heart.
• Each time the heart beats (contracts), it
pushes a wave of blood through the arteries.
• This pressure wave can be felt at points like
the wrist (radial artery) or neck (carotid
artery).
• By counting these pulsations per minute, a
doctor measures the heart beat or heart rate.
• Temperature is measured with a
thermometer, blood pressure with a
sphygmomanometer, and respiration rate by

counting breaths.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. After how many days does a rash appear on the
body after the attack of Measles?
(a) One day
(b) Four days
(c) Six days
(d) One week
The correct option is (b).
• The incubation period of measles is about 10–
12 days, starting with fever, cough, and runny
nose.
• After 2–3 days, Koplik’s spots may appear
inside the mouth.
• The measles rash appears 3–5 days after the
start of symptoms (fever).
• It begins on the face and spreads downward.
• Therefore, “four days” is the most accurate
answer among the choices.
Hence, option (b) is the most clinically
accurate answer.
Q4. The Vitamin responsible for anti-sterile activity
is:
(a) Vitamin A
(b) Vitamin B
(c) Vitamin C
(d) Vitamin E
The correct option is (d).
• Vitamin E is a fat-soluble vitamin functioning
as an antioxidant.
• It is called the “anti-sterility” vitamin because
deficiency causes reproductive failure in
animals.
• It is essential for protecting cells from
damage, crucial for reproduction and overall
health.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q5. It is not advisable to sleep under a tree at night
because of the:
(a) Release of oxygen in lesser amount
(b) Release of oxygen in larger amount
(c) Release of carbon monoxide

(d) Release of carbon dioxide
The correct option is (d).
• Photosynthesis occurs only in light, during
which plants take in CO₂ and release O₂.
• Respiration occurs day and night, where
plants take in O₂ and release CO₂.
• At night, only respiration continues, so CO₂
concentration rises near trees.
• This can displace oxygen and make breathing
difficult.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. Higher plants take up nitrogen as:
(a) Nitrites only
(b) Nitrates only
(c) Nitrates and ammonia
(d) Urea
The correct option is (c).
• Plants cannot use atmospheric nitrogen (N₂)
directly.
• Soil bacteria convert nitrogen into usable
forms.
• Plants absorb nitrate ions (NO₃⁻) and
ammonium ions (NH₄⁺).
• These are used to make amino acids,
proteins, and nucleic acids.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. Green plants in the sea are useful for the
respiration of fish because:
(a) They give out oxygen
(b) They give out carbon dioxide
(c) They give out oxygen and carbon dioxide
simultaneously
(d) They take oxygen and give out carbon dioxide
The correct option is (a).
• Fish require dissolved oxygen (DO) for
respiration.
• Green plants (phytoplankton and algae)
perform photosynthesis and release oxygen.
• This oxygen becomes dissolved in water,
helping fish survive.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q8. Stripes on the back of the animals are the
indication of:
(a) Mating habit
(b) Food habit
(c) Cave dwelling
(d) Matching of skin colour with surroundings
The correct option is (d).
• Stripes on animals are a form of camouflage.
• They help in concealment or confusion in the
surroundings.
• Tigers use it for hunting, zebras for confusing
predators.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. Which of the following is a balanced fertiliser for
plants?
(a) Urea
(b) Ammonia sulphate
(c) Nitrates
(d) Compost
The correct option is (d).
• Balanced fertilizer provides all essential
nutrients.
• Compost contains N, P, K and micronutrients.
• It also improves soil structure and microbial
health.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q10. Which of the following is not immunised by
'Triple Antigen'?
(a) Typhoid
(b) Whooping cough
(c) Tetanus
(d) Diphtheria
The correct option is (a).
• Triple Antigen (DTP) protects against
Diphtheria, Tetanus, and Pertussis
(Whooping Cough).
• Typhoid is caused by Salmonella Typhi and
needs a separate vaccine.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q11. Milk fever in cows occurs due to the lack of:
(a) Phosphates
(b) Calcium

(c) Iron
(d) Iodine
The correct option is (b).
• Milk fever (hypocalcemia) is caused by
calcium deficiency after calving.
• Calcium demand spikes during milk
production.
• Leads to muscle weakness and paralysis if
untreated.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q12. After hatching by the hen, the young chicks
come out of eggs within:
(a) One week
(b) Two weeks
(c) Three weeks
(d) Four weeks
The correct option is (c).
• The incubation period for hen’s eggs is 21
days (3 weeks).
• The hen maintains warmth and humidity
during brooding.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q13. Which one of the following is a fast growing
tree?
(a) Teak
(b) Eucalyptus
(c) Banyan
(d) Coconut
The correct option is (b).
• Eucalyptus is known for rapid growth and
high timber yield.
• Teak, Banyan, Coconut grow much slower.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q14. Decrease in white blood cells results in:
(a) Decrease in Antibodies
(b) Increase in Antigens
(c) Increase in Antibodies
(d) No change
The correct option is (a).
• White blood cells (WBCs) are responsible for
immunity.
• B-lymphocytes produce antibodies.

•

Decrease in WBCs (leukopenia) → decrease
in antibody production → weak immune
system.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q15. In India people suffer from Anaemia due to lack
of:
(a) Iron
(b) Iodine
(c) Calcium
(d) Potassium
The correct option is (a).
• Anaemia results from low hemoglobin or red
blood cells.
• Iron deficiency is the main cause in India.
• Hemoglobin synthesis requires iron.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q16. Reserpine derived from the plant 'Serpentina' is
used to:
(a) Alleviate pains
(b) Alleviate high blood pressure
(c) Alleviate low blood pressure
(d) Cure rickets
The correct option is (b).
• Reserpine, from Rauwolfia serpentina, treats
hypertension.
• It lowers blood pressure by relaxing blood
vessels.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q17. In India it is found recently that incidence of
Malaria is increasing because:
(a) The mosquitoes have become DDT resistant
(b) Of poverty in villages
(c) Of poor sanitary conditions
(d) On account of population increase
The correct option is (a).
• Anopheles mosquitoes developed resistance
to DDT.
• This reduced effectiveness of malaria control.
• Though poverty and sanitation contribute,
resistance is the key cause.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q18. Less dew is formed on cloudy nights because:
(a) Clouds absorb the falling dew
(b) Clouds scatter moisture
(c) In cloudy nights the radiation takes place very
slowly
(d) In cloudy nights the radiation takes place very
quickly
The correct option is (c).
• Dew forms when ground cools below dew
point.
• On clear nights, heat radiates quickly →
cooling and dew.
• On cloudy nights, clouds trap heat, slowing
radiative cooling.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q19. Feeding of milk cattle with cotton seeds:
(a) Increases fat content temporarily
(b) Decreases fat content temporarily
(c) May decrease or increase fat content
(d) Causes no change
The correct option is (a).
• Cottonseed provides protein, fiber, and fat.
• It increases milk fat temporarily by affecting
rumen fermentation.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q20. Mouth and foot diseases in cattle are caused
due to:
(a) Bacteria
(b) Virus
(c) Fungi
(d) Penicillium
The correct option is (b).
• Foot-and-mouth disease (FMD) is caused by a
virus (Aphthovirus).
• It affects cloven-hoofed animals and spreads
rapidly.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q21. A body partially floats in water when:
(a) The volume of the displaced liquid is equal to the
volume of the body
(b) The volume of the displaced liquid is greater than
the volume of the body

(c) The weight of the displaced water is equal to the
weight of the body
(d) The weight of the displaced water is greater than
the weight of the body
The correct option is (c).
• This question relates to Archimedes' principle
of buoyancy.
• The principle states that the buoyant force on
a submerged object is equal to the weight of
the fluid displaced by the object.
• An object floats (partially or fully submerged)
when the upward buoyant force exactly
balances the downward force of the object's
own weight.
• Therefore, a body will float (partially or fully)
when the weight of the displaced water is
equal to the weight of the body.
• Option (a) describes a fully submerged object,
which might sink, float, or rise depending on
its weight.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q22. Permanent hardness of water cannot be
removed by:
(a) Boiling
(b) Adding caustic soda
(c) Distillation
(d) Adding soda
The correct option is (a).
• Water hardness is caused by dissolved
calcium and magnesium salts.
• Temporary hardness is caused by hydrogen
carbonates (bicarbonates) of calcium and
magnesium and can be removed by boiling,
which precipitates them as carbonates.
• Permanent hardness is caused by sulfates
and chlorides of calcium and magnesium;
these do not precipitate upon heating.
• Therefore, permanent hardness cannot be
removed by boiling.
• It can be removed by other methods like
adding washing soda (sodium carbonate),
using ion-exchange resins, or by distillation.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q23. In summer, a man with excess perspiration
feels weak, because of the:
(a) Loss of more water through evaporation
(b) Loss of salts through evaporation
(c) Loss of carbohydrates through evaporation
(d) All factors mentioned above
The correct option is (a).
• Perspiration (sweating) is the body's primary
mechanism for cooling down.
• Sweat is composed mostly of water, with
small amounts of dissolved salts
(electrolytes).
• Excessive perspiration leads to a significant
loss of water from the body, which can cause
dehydration.
• Dehydration is the primary reason for feeling
weak, dizzy, and fatigued, as it reduces blood
volume and impairs cellular function.
• While there is also a loss of salts, the sheer
volume of water loss is the main cause of
weakness. Carbohydrates are not lost through
sweat.
• The provided answer key says (d), but the
most significant and direct cause is water loss.
Hence, option (a) is the most accurate
primary reason.
Q24. The two branches of a plant give two different
fruits: tomatoes and brinjals. This can be explained:
(a) By hybridisation
(b) By grafting one with the other
(c) By nature's freak
(d) By (a) or (c) mentioned above
The correct option is (b).
• Grafting is a horticultural technique where
tissues of plants are joined so as to continue
their growth together.
• This is possible only between closely related
species. Tomato (Solanum lycopersicum) and
Brinjal/Eggplant (Solanum melongena) both
belong to the same genus, Solanum.
• Because they are closely related, it is possible
to graft a branch of a tomato plant onto the
rootstock of a brinjal plant (or vice versa). The
grafted branch (scion) will continue to grow

•

and produce its own fruit (tomatoes), while
the original plant (rootstock) will produce
brinjals, resulting in one plant with two
different types of fruit.
Hybridization involves cross-pollination to
create a new plant with mixed genetic traits,
not two different fruits on one plant.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q25. The function of the liver is to:
(a) Promote digestion of food
(b) Promote respiration
(c) Store glucose as glycogen
(d) None of these
The correct option is (c).
• The liver has numerous vital functions, but
among the options provided, the most
accurate is its role in glucose metabolism.
• After a meal, when blood glucose levels are
high, the liver takes up excess glucose from
the blood and converts it into glycogen, a
storage polymer.
• The liver then stores this glycogen. Later,
when blood glucose levels fall, the liver breaks
down the stored glycogen back into glucose
(glycogenolysis) and releases it into the
bloodstream to maintain stable blood sugar
levels.
• While the liver produces bile which aids in fat
digestion (a), its primary function is not to
"promote digestion" in general. It has no
direct role in respiration (b).
Hence, option (c) is a correct and major
function of the liver.
Q26. Which of the following can be said as the
"Theory of Darwin"?
(a) Survival of the fittest and struggle for existence
(b) Weak and strong always maintain a fixed
proportion
(c) Different species do not arise by genetic mutation
(d) None of these
The correct option is (a).

•

•

•

•

•

Charles Darwin's theory of evolution by
natural selection is built on several key
observations and inferences.
He observed that organisms produce more
offspring than can survive, leading to a
"struggle for existence."
He also observed that there is variation
among individuals within a species, and these
variations are heritable.
He inferred that individuals with variations
better suited to their environment are more
likely to survive and reproduce. This concept
is summarized by the phrase "survival of the
fittest" (a term coined by Herbert Spencer but
widely associated with Darwin's theory).
This differential survival and reproduction
(natural selection) leads to gradual evolution
of populations over time.
Hence, option (a) accurately summarizes key
concepts of Darwin's theory.

Q27. The term 'Test Tube Baby' implies:
(a) Fertilisation of ovum takes place in the test tube
but it develops in the uterus
(b) Fertilisation of ovum takes place in the test tube
and develops in the test tube itself
(c) Fertilisation of the ovum takes place in the uterus
but develops in the test tube
(d) Fertilisation takes place in uterus and embryo
develops in uterus
The correct option is (a).
• 'Test Tube Baby' is a non-scientific term for a
baby conceived through In Vitro Fertilization
(IVF).
• "In vitro" means "in glass." The process
involves retrieving an ovum (egg) from the
mother and sperm from the father.
• Fertilization of the ovum by the sperm then
takes place outside the body in a laboratory
dish (not literally a test tube).
• The resulting embryo is allowed to develop
for a few days in the lab and is then
transferred into the mother's uterus to
implant and develop naturally for the
remainder of the pregnancy.

•

A baby cannot develop fully in a test tube (b).
Hence, option (a) correctly describes the
process.

Q28. Decomposition of organic matter is due to:
(a) Virus
(b) Fungi
(c) Bacteria
(d) None of these
The correct option is (c).
• Decomposition is the process by which dead
organic substances are broken down into
simpler organic or inorganic matter.
• This process is carried out by decomposers.
The primary decomposers in most ecosystems
are bacteria and fungi.
• Bacteria are particularly important and are
responsible for a vast amount of
decomposition, breaking down a wide variety
of organic materials.
• Fungi are also major decomposers, especially
of complex plant material like lignin.
• Viruses are not decomposers; they are
obligate parasites that replicate inside living
cells.
• Between fungi and bacteria, bacteria are
often considered the most numerous and
significant group of decomposers overall.
Given the options, both (b) and (c) are correct,
but bacteria are arguably the most
fundamental answer.
Hence, option (c) is the best choice.
Q29. Carbohydrates, proteins and vitamins which are
responsible for energy, growth and vitality are
obtained respectively from:
(a) Cereals, milk and vegetables
(b) Milk, pulses and cereals
(c) Milk, pulses and vegetables
(d) Pulses, vegetables and cereals
The correct option is (a).
• This question asks to match the
macronutrient/micronutrient with its primary
food source in the given order:
Carbohydrates, Proteins, Vitamins.

•

•

•

•

Carbohydrates (for energy): Cereals like rice,
wheat, and corn are staple foods rich in
carbohydrates.
Proteins (for growth): While pulses are a
good source, milk is an excellent source of
high-quality protein.
Vitamins (for vitality): Vegetables are among
the best and most diverse sources of essential
vitamins.
Therefore, the order Cereals (Carbohydrates),
Milk (Proteins), and Vegetables (Vitamins)
correctly matches the nutrient with a primary
source.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q30. The growth of seedling plants after
transplantation will not always be proper because:
(a) New soil may not contain the required minerals
(b) During transplantation root hairs get damaged
(c) Roots cannot penetrate deep into the soil
(d) Of all factors stated above
The correct option is (d).
• Transplanting a seedling is a stressful event,
and failure to thrive can be caused by multiple
factors — known as transplant shock.
• (a) New soil may not contain the required
minerals: A drastic change in soil type, pH, or
nutrient content can shock the plant.
• (b) During transplantation root hairs get
damaged: The delicate root hairs are
responsible for the vast majority of water and
nutrient absorption; damage impairs uptake
immediately after moving.
• (c) Roots cannot penetrate deep into the soil:
If the new soil is heavily compacted, roots
may struggle to establish and access water
and nutrients.
• Since all the factors listed can contribute to
poor growth after transplantation, the most
comprehensive answer is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q31. BCG vaccination is to be given to a new born
child:
(a) Immediately after birth

(b) Within 48 hours
(c) Within seven days
(d) Within six months
The correct option is (a).
• The BCG (Bacille Calmette-Guérin) vaccine
protects against severe forms of tuberculosis
(TB), particularly in children.
• In countries where TB is prevalent, WHO and
national guidelines recommend that the BCG
vaccine be given to all healthy babies as soon
as possible after birth.
• Administering it "immediately after birth" or
at least before discharge from the hospital is
the standard and ideal practice to provide
early protection. The other options are
acceptable timeframes, but (a) reflects the
ideal protocol.
Hence, option (a) is the most accurate
description of the ideal timing.
Q32. Which of the following cows gives maximum
milk yield?
(a) Jersey
(b) Holstein
(c) Red Sindhi
(d) Sahiwal
The correct option is (b).
• The Holstein-Friesian (Holstein) is a breed of
dairy cattle renowned for the highest milk
production.
• Holsteins dominate dairy industries globally
because of their ability to produce large
volumes of milk.
• Jersey cows produce milk with high butterfat
content but lower total volume. Red Sindhi
and Sahiwal are heat-tolerant breeds from
the Indian subcontinent but have lower yields
compared to Holsteins.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1980
Q1. Another name for Vitamin C is:
(a) Folic acid
(b) Ascorbic acid
(c) Niacin
(d) Acetic acid
The correct option is (b).
• Vitamin C is an essential water-soluble
vitamin.
• Its chemical name is L-ascorbic acid, or simply
ascorbic acid.
• Humans cannot synthesize Vitamin C, so it
must be obtained from the diet.
• It is crucial for collagen synthesis, immune
function, and acts as an antioxidant.
• A deficiency causes scurvy.
• Folic acid is Vitamin B9, Niacin is Vitamin B3,
and acetic acid is the main component of
vinegar.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. Dialysis is used for a patient suffering from:
(a) Kidney trouble
(b) Liver trouble
(c) Lung trouble
(d) Heart trouble
The correct option is (a).
• Dialysis is a medical procedure that artificially
performs the functions of the kidneys when
they fail.
• This condition is known as renal failure or
kidney trouble.
• The kidneys normally filter waste products
(like urea) and excess fluid from the blood to
form urine.
• When the kidneys fail, these wastes
accumulate to toxic levels in the body.
• Dialysis uses a machine to filter the blood,
removing waste and excess water, thus
keeping the patient alive.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. The green colour of plants is due to the presence
of:

(a) Chlorophyll
(b) Sugar
(c) Mitochondria
(d) Xylem
The correct option is (a).
• The green color of leaves and stems in plants
is due to a pigment called chlorophyll.
• Chlorophyll is located in organelles called
chloroplasts.
• Its function is to absorb light energy for
photosynthesis.
• Chlorophyll primarily absorbs red and blue
light, but reflects green light, making plants
appear green.
• Sugar is a product of photosynthesis,
mitochondria are for respiration, and xylem
is for water transport.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. The deficiency of Vitamin D causes:
(a) Rickets
(b) Night blindness
(c) Pellagra
(d) Scurvy
The correct option is (a).
• Vitamin D is essential for calcium and
phosphorus absorption and bone strength.
• A deficiency leads to Rickets in children —
bones become soft, weak, and deformed
(e.g., bowed legs).
• In adults, a similar condition is called
osteomalacia.
• Night blindness → Vitamin A deficiency;
Pellagra → Vitamin B3 (Niacin) deficiency;
Scurvy → Vitamin C deficiency.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. During processing and cooking which part of
food is mostly destroyed?
(a) Proteins
(b) Vitamins
(c) Carbohydrates
(d) Fats
The correct option is (b).
• Vitamins are the most heat-sensitive and
easily destroyed nutrients during processing
and cooking.
• Water-soluble vitamins like Vitamin C and Bvitamins (thiamine, folate, etc.) are especially
sensitive to heat, light, and oxygen.
• Boiling, frying, or prolonged cooking can
destroy a significant portion of these
nutrients.
• Proteins may be denatured by heat but
usually become more digestible, not
destroyed.
• Carbohydrates and fats are relatively heatstable.
• The provided answer key says (a), but
scientifically, vitamins are the most labile
nutrients.
Hence, option (b) is the most accurate
answer.
Q6. Which one of the following is secreted by the
Pancreas and regulates the amount of sugar in the
body?
(a) Renin
(b) Creatin
(c) Vitamin
(d) Insulin
The correct option is (d).
• The pancreas has both exocrine (digestive)
and endocrine (hormonal) functions.
• Its endocrine function is carried out by islets
of Langerhans.
• These cells produce hormones that regulate
blood glucose levels — the most important
being insulin.
• When blood sugar rises (after a meal), insulin
helps transport glucose into body cells and

•

signals the liver to store excess glucose as
glycogen.
This lowers blood sugar levels, maintaining
balance.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1981

Q1. Identical twins are born when:
(a) Two sperms fertilise one ovum
(b) Two sperms fertilise two ovums simultaneously
(c) One sperm fertilises the ovum and zygote divides
into two separate cells developing independently
(d) One sperm fertilises two ovums
The correct option is (c).
• Identical (or monozygotic) twins originate
from a single reproductive event.
• One sperm fertilizes one ovum to create a
single zygote, just as in a normal singleton
pregnancy.
• Very early in development, this single zygote
splits into two separate, identical cell
masses.
• Each of these masses then develops
independently into an embryo.
• Because they originate from the same single
zygote, identical twins share the exact same
genetic material (genome) and are always the
same sex.
• Fraternal (dizygotic) twins occur when two
separate eggs are fertilized by two separate
sperm.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. Conception generally takes place at the time of:
(a) Menses
(b) Before menses
(c) Immediately after menses
(d) Two weeks after menses
The correct option is (d).
• Conception (fertilization) can only occur if a
sperm meets a viable egg; this is tied to
ovulation.
• Ovulation is the release of an egg from the
ovary.

•
•

•

In a typical 28-day menstrual cycle, ovulation
occurs around day 14.
Day 1 of the cycle is the first day of menses
(the period); therefore day 14 is
approximately two weeks after the start of
menses.
The egg is viable for about 12–24 hours, and
sperm can survive up to 5 days in the female
tract, so the fertile window is centered
around two weeks after the start of the last
menstrual period.
Hence, option (d) is the most accurate
answer.

Q3. Why do mothers feed the child with Vitamin A?
(a) To prevent rickets
(b) To prevent beri-beri
(c) To prevent night blindness
(d) To prevent polio
The correct option is (c).
• Vitamin A is a fat-soluble vitamin crucial for
vision, immune function, and cellular growth.
• It is a component of rhodopsin, a pigment in
the retina essential for low-light vision.
• Deficiency of Vitamin A impairs rhodopsin
production, leading to night blindness
(nyctalopia).
• Therefore, adequate Vitamin A intake in
children prevents night blindness and
maintains healthy vision.
• Rickets → Vitamin D deficiency; Beri-beri →
Vitamin B1 deficiency.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. Growing children require comparatively more:
(a) Proteins
(b) Carbohydrates
(c) Vitamins
(d) All of these
The correct option is (a).
• While children need all nutrients, their
requirement for protein per unit body weight
is significantly higher than adults.
• Protein is the fundamental building block for
the body.

•

•
•

During childhood, there is rapid growth and
development, requiring synthesis of tissues,
muscles, bones, enzymes, and hormones.
This synthesis needs a constant supply of
amino acids from dietary protein.
Carbohydrates provide energy and vitamins
are essential, but the structural requirement
for protein is paramount during growth.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. Heart patients should avoid taking excess of:
(a) Proteins
(b) Fats
(c) Vitamins
(d) Carbohydrates
The correct option is (b).
• Dietary management is crucial for
cardiovascular health.
• Heart patients are advised to limit intake of
certain types of fats, especially saturated fats
and trans fats.
• These fats raise LDL ("bad") cholesterol,
contributing to atherosclerosis (plaque
buildup in arteries).
• Atherosclerosis narrows arteries, increases
blood pressure, and raises risk of heart attack
and stroke.
• While excess calories from any source can
cause obesity (a risk factor), the direct link
between specific fats and heart disease makes
them primary to avoid.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. How does dehydration help in the preservation
of food?
(a) Growth of micro-organism is checked
(b) Nutrients are concentrated
(c) Surface of food stuffs becomes hard
(d) Chemical reactions stop in the absence of water
The correct option is (a).
• Dehydration (drying) is an ancient method of
food preservation.
• Microorganisms (bacteria, yeasts, molds)
require water to grow and reproduce.

•

•
•

Removing water reduces the food’s water
activity to levels where these microorganisms
cannot survive or multiply.
By inhibiting microbial growth, dehydration
effectively prevents spoilage.
Though nutrients are concentrated and
chemical reactions slow, the primary
preservative effect is inhibiting microbial
growth.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q7. How is the body temperature maintained?
(a) Due to temperature regulating mechanism located
in the base of the brain
(b) Due to excessive intake of water
(c) Due to regular intake of foods
(d) Due to the constant flow of blood in the capillaries
The correct option is (a).
• Humans are endotherms, maintaining a
constant internal body temperature (~37°C).
• Thermoregulation is controlled by the
hypothalamus, a region at the base of the
brain.
• The hypothalamus acts as the body’s
thermostat, receiving temperature signals
from skin and blood receptors.
• If too hot, it triggers sweating and
vasodilation; if too cold, it triggers shivering
and vasoconstriction.
• This central mechanism maintains stable
body temperature.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8. Why are countries scared to import fruits?
(a) Because they become rotten easily and spread
diseases
(b) Because they are not easily adjustable to new
climate
(c) Because insects carried through fruits are
dangerous
(d) All of the above
The correct option is (c).
• Biosecurity is the main reason for strict
regulations on importing fresh fruits.

•
•
•

•

Fruits can transport agricultural pests and
diseases not present in the importing country.
This includes invasive insects (e.g., fruit flies),
fungal diseases, and plant viruses.
Introduction of such pests can devastate
native agriculture and ecosystems, causing
major economic and environmental damage.
This threat is far greater than concerns about
fruit rotting.
Hence, option (c) is the most significant
reason.

Q9. A person with which blood group will be a
universal donor?
(a) A
(b) B
(c) O
(d) AB
The correct option is (c).
• A "universal donor" is a blood type
transfusable to patients of any ABO type with
minimal reaction risk.
• Blood group O red cells lack A and B antigens
on their surface.
• Because they lack these antigens, recipients
of types A, B, or AB will not recognize O-type
cells as foreign.
• O-negative is the true universal red cell donor
because it also lacks the Rh antigen.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q10. Dysentery and Influenza are caused by:
(a) Bacteria
(b) Viruses
(c) Bacteria and Viruses respectively
(d) Viruses and Bacteria respectively
The correct option is (c).
• Dysentery is an intestinal inflammation with
severe diarrhea, blood, and mucus; commonly
caused by bacteria (e.g., Shigella) or protozoa
(Entamoeba histolytica).
• Influenza (flu) is a contagious respiratory
illness caused by influenza viruses
(Orthomyxoviridae).
• Therefore, the correct pairing is Bacteria
(dysentery) and Viruses (influenza),
respectively.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q11. Which organ is associated with the production
of insulin in the human body?
(a) Pancreas
(b) Liver
(c) Kidney
(d) Heart
The correct option is (a).
• Insulin is a hormone regulating blood glucose.
• It is produced by beta cells in the islets of
Langerhans within the pancreas.
• The pancreas releases insulin into the
bloodstream in response to rising blood
glucose (e.g., after eating), signaling cells to
take up glucose.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q12. When the rice is boiled, it expands because:
(a) The starch gelatinises and swells
(b) The starch gets polymerised
(c) The grains absorb moisture and swell
(d) Sugars break down in the grains
The correct option is (a).
• Expansion of rice during boiling is called
gelatinization.
• Rice grains contain starch granules that are
insoluble in cold water.

•

•

•

When heated, the starch granules absorb
water and swell; their crystalline structure
breaks down and they soften.
This gelatinization—starch absorbing water
and swelling on heating—is the specific
mechanism causing expansion.
Option (c) is true in general, but (a) gives the
specific scientific term and mechanism.
Hence, option (a) is the most complete and
accurate answer.

Q13. Which of the following is the most common
wheat disease in India?
(a) Green ear
(b) Smut
(c) Loose smut
(d) Rust
The correct option is (d).
• Wheat rust is a widespread and economically
damaging fungal disease in India and
worldwide.
• Caused by species of the genus Puccinia, rust
appears as pustules on leaves and stems.
• There are three main types: stem rust (black
rust), leaf rust (brown rust), and stripe rust
(yellow rust).
• Rust reduces photosynthetic ability and
causes significant yield losses.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q14. The sex of the offspring is determined, when:
(a) The conception takes place
(b) The sperm fertilises the ovum
(c) The embryo is partially developed
(d) The end of gestation period is near
The correct option is (b).
• Genetic sex is determined at the moment of
fertilization.
• The ovum always carries an X chromosome;
the sperm carries X or Y.
• X-sperm + X-ovum → XX (female); Y-sperm +
X-ovum → XY (male).
• Thus the sex is set when the sperm fertilises
the ovum.

•

Conception (a) is another term for this event,
but (b) specifies the biological action.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q15. Colour blindness is:
(a) More common among women
(b) Very rare in women
(c) Very rare in men
(d) Found to the same extent in both the sexes
The correct option is (b).
• Most common red–green color blindness is Xlinked recessive.
• Genes for red and green photopigments are
on the X chromosome.
• Males (XY) with a defective X will express
color blindness; females (XX) need defects on
both Xs to be affected.
• Hence, color blindness is far more common in
men (≈1 in 12) and very rare in women (≈1 in
200).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q16. What happens when alcohol is taken in excess?
(a) It destroys the liver
(b) It produces inflammation of the stomach
(c) It causes the heart to become weak
(d) All of the above
The correct option is (d).
• Excessive alcohol (acute or chronic) damages
multiple organs.
• (a) Chronic abuse leads to liver disease: fatty
liver → alcoholic hepatitis → cirrhosis.
• (b) Alcohol irritates the stomach lining,
causing gastritis, pain, nausea, and ulcers.
• (c) Chronic heavy drinking can cause alcoholic
cardiomyopathy, weakening the heart
muscle.
• Since all are documented consequences, all of
the above is correct.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q17. The deficiency of _______ causes goitre.
(a) Sulphur
(b) Iodine
(c) Calcium

(d) Vitamin A
The correct option is (b).
• Goitre is enlargement of the thyroid gland,
visible as a neck swelling.
• The most common cause worldwide is dietary
iodine deficiency.
• The thyroid needs iodine to make T3 and T4
hormones; low iodine → low hormones →
TSH rises → thyroid hypertrophy (goitre).
• The provided answer key saying (a) is
incorrect; iodine deficiency is the classical
cause.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q18. Which of the following crops has the highest
photosynthetic activity?
(a) Cotton
(b) Sugarcane
(c) Rice
(d) Wheat
The correct option is (b).
• Photosynthetic efficiency varies by pathway:
C3 vs C4 plants.
• C4 plants (e.g., sugarcane, maize, sorghum)
fix carbon more efficiently in hot, sunny
conditions, reducing photorespiration.
• Sugarcane is a C4 plant and among the most
photosynthetically efficient crops, producing
large biomass per unit area.
• Cotton, rice, and wheat are C3 plants and
generally less efficient under high light and
temperature.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1982
Q1. Flood-hit areas are to be immediately given
medical cover. Which of the following is the most
appropriate step?
(a) BCG injection
(b) Inoculation against Cholera
(c) Smallpox vaccination
(d) None of these
The correct option is (b).

•
•
•

•

•

•

Floods lead to the contamination of drinking
water sources with sewage and other waste.
This creates a high risk for the outbreak of
waterborne diseases.
Cholera is a severe diarrheal disease caused
by the bacterium Vibrio cholerae, transmitted
through contaminated water and food.
It can spread rapidly in post-disaster
situations like floods and cause lifethreatening dehydration.
Therefore, a primary public health response in
flood-hit areas is to prevent cholera
outbreaks, which includes providing clean
water, sanitation, and, where appropriate,
inoculation (vaccination) against Cholera.
BCG is for tuberculosis, and smallpox has
been eradicated.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. The rich source of iron is:
(a) Eggs
(b) Green vegetables
(c) Pulses
(d) Milk
The correct option is (b).
• Iron is an essential mineral required for the
production of hemoglobin.
• While many foods contain iron, some are
particularly rich sources.
• Green leafy vegetables, such as spinach, kale,
and broccoli, are well-known for being rich
sources of iron (non-heme iron).
• While eggs and pulses also contain iron, green
vegetables are generally considered a
superior plant-based source.
• Milk is a very poor source of iron.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. Which among the following acts as a resistance
against disease in the body?
(a) Vitamins
(b) Carbohydrates
(c) Red Corpuscles
(d) White Corpuscles
The correct option is (d).

•
•

•

•

The body's defense system against disease is
the immune system.
The primary cells of the immune system are
the White Corpuscles, also known as white
blood cells or leukocytes.
These cells are responsible for identifying and
destroying foreign invaders like bacteria,
viruses, and fungi through mechanisms
including phagocytosis and producing
antibodies.
Red corpuscles carry oxygen, carbohydrates
provide energy, and vitamins are co-factors
for metabolic processes.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q4. Vitamins do not act as a/an:
(a) Source of energy
(b) Stimulant in the growth of the body
(c) Agent in regulating the chemical processes in the
body metabolism
(d) All of the above
The correct option is (a).
• Vitamins are organic compounds required by
the body in small amounts for various
metabolic functions.
• They act as coenzymes and cofactors,
regulating chemical processes in metabolism
(c), and are essential for proper growth and
development (b).
• However, vitamins themselves are not
broken down to provide calories and
therefore do not act as a source of energy.
• The body's energy comes from the
macronutrients: carbohydrates, fats, and
proteins.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. Which of the following enters the human body
through the skin?
(a) Tape worm
(b) Hook worm
(c) Ring worm
(d) Thread worm
The correct option is (b).

•
•

•

•
•

This question asks about parasitic worms that
can penetrate the skin.
Hookworm larvae live in contaminated soil
and can burrow through bare skin (typically
the feet) when a person walks on the soil.
Once inside, they travel through the
bloodstream to the lungs and then to the
intestines, where they mature and cause
infection.
Ringworm is a fungal infection of the skin and
does not enter through the skin into the body.
Tapeworms and threadworms are typically
ingested as eggs or larvae in contaminated
food or water.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q6. Honey bees are called social insects because:
(a) They visit all flowers to collect nectar
(b) They live among men
(c) They depend for their food upon other members
(d) They move socially with other insects
The correct option is (c).
• The term "social insect" refers to species that
live in large, organized colonies with
cooperative brood care, overlapping
generations, and division of labor into
reproductive and non-reproductive castes.
• In a honey bee colony, there is a clear
division of labor: the queen lays eggs, drones
mate, and sterile female workers perform
tasks like foraging, building comb, and caring
for young.
• This system means individuals are
interdependent; for example, the queen and
the young depend for their food upon other
members (worker bees).
• This cooperative structure and division of
labor is the essence of being a social insect.
Hence, option (c) best captures the meaning
of "social insect."
Q7. Milk of animals fed on grass contains large
amounts of fat in:
(a) Summer
(b) Winter

(c) Spring
(d) Autumn
The correct option is (b).
• The composition of milk, particularly fat and
protein content, varies seasonally.
• For dairy cows, the concentration of milk fat
and protein tends to be highest during the
winter months and lowest during the
summer months.
• This is due to factors including changes in diet
(fresh pasture vs stored forage),
environmental temperature stress (heat
stress in summer reduces feed intake and milk
fat), and the cow's lactation cycle.
• The provided answer key says (a), which is
incorrect. Heat stress in summer typically
depresses milk fat content.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q8. A universal donor has the blood group belonging
to:
(a) A
(b) B
(c) O
(d) AB
The correct option is (c).
• A universal donor is a person whose blood
can be transfused to recipients of any other
ABO type with the lowest risk of reaction.
• Group O red blood cells lack A and B antigens
on their surface.
• Because there are no antigens to trigger an
immune response, this blood can be given to
people with A, B, AB, or O blood.
• O-negative is the true universal red cell donor
because it also lacks the RhD antigen.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9. The retina in the eyes acts as a _______ in the
camera.
(a) Lens
(b) Shutter
(c) Film
(d) None of these
The correct option is (c).

•
•

•

•
•

This question uses an analogy between the
human eye and a camera.
The retina is the light-sensitive layer at the
back of the eye that detects light focused by
the lens and converts it into neural signals.
In a traditional camera, the film is the lightsensitive material that captures the focused
image projected by the lens.
In a digital camera, the equivalent is the
image sensor (CCD or CMOS).
The lens of the eye corresponds to the
camera's lens, and the iris/pupil acts like the
camera's aperture.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q10. Chlorophyll is a/an:
(a) Halogen acid
(b) A green pigment found in the plants
(c) A chloro-organic compound
(d) Element
The correct option is (b).
• Chlorophyll is the molecule responsible for
photosynthesis in plants, algae, and
cyanobacteria.
• It is a green pigment that absorbs light
energy, primarily in the blue and red parts of
the spectrum, and reflects green light, making
plants appear green.
• It is a complex organic molecule (a chlorin
pigment), not a halogen acid, a simple chloroorganic compound, or an element.

•

Its structure contains a magnesium ion at its
center.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q11. Health hazard floods are responsible for the
spread of:
(a) Cholera
(b) Malaria
(c) Influenza
(d) Diarrhoea
The correct option is (a).
• Flooding causes widespread contamination of
water supplies with sewage and fecal matter,
creating ideal conditions for waterborne
diseases.
• Cholera is a classic and severe waterborne
disease caused by Vibrio cholerae and
spreads rapidly through contaminated water.
• It can cause major epidemics with high
mortality in post-disaster scenarios like
floods.
• While diarrhoea (d) is a symptom of cholera
and other waterborne illnesses, cholera is the
specific disease that is a major health hazard
after floods.
• Malaria is spread by mosquitoes, and
influenza is an airborne respiratory virus.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q12. Hydroponics means:
(a) Cultivation of plants without the use of soil
(b) Detection of sound in water
(c) Animals living in water
(d) A Japanese way of flowering
The correct option is (a).
• Hydroponics is a method of growing plants
where the roots are supplied with a mineral
nutrient solution instead of being planted in
soil.
• The term comes from Greek: hydro (water)
and ponos (labor).
• In hydroponic systems, plant roots can be
suspended in nutrient solution or supported
by an inert medium like perlite or gravel.

•

The key feature is cultivation without the use
of soil.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q13. During cardiac failure, which of the following is
advised as a first aid?
(a) Mouth to mouth resuscitation
(b) Giving external cardiac massage
(c) Giving cool water to drink
(d) Giving complete body massage
The correct option is (b).
• The question likely refers to cardiac arrest
(heart stops beating), an extreme form of
cardiac failure.
• Immediate first aid is Cardiopulmonary
Resuscitation (CPR).
• A key component of CPR is external cardiac
massage (chest compressions), which
manually pumps the heart to circulate
oxygenated blood to the brain and vital
organs.
• Mouth-to-mouth is the rescue breathing part
of CPR, but chest compressions are the most
critical first step.
• Giving water or a body massage is
inappropriate and potentially harmful.
Hence, option (b) is the most appropriate
action.
Q14. Pearls are formed inside:
(a) Squids
(b) Snails
(c) Oysters
(d) Molluscs
The correct option is (c).
• Pearls are hard, lustrous objects produced
within the soft tissue of shelled mollusks.
• The most famous and commercially significant
pearls are formed inside oysters.
• A pearl forms when an irritant (parasite or
shell fragment) gets trapped inside the
oyster's mantle; the oyster secretes layers of
nacre (mother-of-pearl) around it, forming a
pearl over time.

•

While oysters are a type of mollusk, (c) is the
more specific and common answer.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q15. Dehydration in the human body results due to
loss of:
(a) Salts
(b) Water
(c) Vitamins
(d) Hormones
The correct option is (b).
• Dehydration occurs when the loss of body
water exceeds intake.
• The body loses water through breathing,
sweating, urination, and defecation.
• If lost water is not adequately replaced, the
body becomes dehydrated.
• While loss of salts (electrolytes) often
accompanies water loss and can worsen the
condition, the fundamental deficit is water.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q16. A ready source of energy is available for
athletes in:
(a) Vitamins
(b) Fats
(c) Proteins
(d) Carbohydrates
The correct option is (d).
• The body's quickest and most easily accessible
source of energy is carbohydrates.
• Carbohydrates are broken down into glucose,
which enters the bloodstream and is readily
used by muscles for fuel.
• The body also stores carbohydrates as
glycogen in muscles and liver for rapid energy
release during exercise.
• Fats are more concentrated but metabolized
more slowly; proteins are primarily for repair,
and vitamins do not provide energy.
• Athletes often consume simple
carbohydrates (sports drinks or gels) for a
quick energy boost.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q17. The easiest way of finding out the nutritional
status for a large number of children in a short time
is by:
(a) Studying their behaviour
(b) Studying their hair, face and eyes
(c) Measuring their height and weight
(d) All of the above
The correct option is (c).
• In public health and large-scale screenings,
anthropometry is the standard method for
quickly assessing nutritional status.
• This involves measuring height and weight
and comparing them to standardized growth
charts for age and sex.
• Metrics like height-for-age (stunting), weightfor-height (wasting), and weight-for-age
(underweight) are simple, objective, and
quick to measure.
• Studying behavior or physical signs is more
subjective and time-consuming.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q18. To keep the seeds in good condition, we should
keep them in a place which is:
(a) Warm and dry
(b) Warm and wet
(c) Cool and dry
(d) Cool and wet
The correct option is (c).
• Seeds are living organisms in a dormant state;
to preserve viability their metabolic rate must
remain low.
• Heat and moisture increase metabolic activity
and encourage germination or spoilage.
• Therefore, the ideal storage for most seeds is
cool and dry, which minimizes metabolic
activity and prevents mold or fungal growth.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q19. The best conceiving season for cows and
buffaloes is:
(a) Summer
(b) Winter
(c) Spring
(d) There is no definite relationship between

conceiving and season
The correct option is (d).
• Cows and domestic buffaloes are polyestrous,
meaning they can cycle and conceive
throughout the year.
• Unlike some wild animals with specific
breeding seasons, domestic cattle aren’t
strictly tied to a particular season.
• Extreme heat stress may negatively affect
fertility in summer, but with proper
management, they can conceive any time.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q20. The most preferential poultry feed is:
(a) Maize
(b) Barley
(c) Wheat
(d) Rice husk
The correct option is (a).
• Modern poultry feed emphasizes a highenergy grain.
• Maize (corn) is the most widely used and
preferred grain globally for poultry diets.
• It has high energy content, is highly
digestible, palatable, and contains pigments
(xanthophylls) that give a desirable yellow
color to egg yolks and chicken skin.
• Barley and wheat are used but maize is
typically the benchmark; rice husk is a lownutrient fiber and not a primary feed.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1983
Q1. The age of a tree can be estimated by:
(a) Counting the number of concentric rings
(b) Counting the nodes and internodes of the trunk
(c) Measuring the girth of the tree trunk
(d) Counting the number of branches
The correct option is (a).
• The most accurate method for determining
the age of a tree is called dendrochronology.
• In temperate climates, trees grow at different
rates throughout the year, forming distinct
growth layers.

•
•
•
•

•

In spring, growth is rapid, creating a wide,
light-coloured ring.
In summer and autumn, growth slows,
creating a thin, dark-coloured ring.
One light ring plus one dark ring together
represent one year of growth.
By counting the number of these concentric
rings in a cross-section of the trunk, one can
precisely determine the tree's age.
Measuring the girth (c) can give a very rough
estimate, but it is highly inaccurate as growth
rate varies greatly depending on species,
location, and environmental conditions.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2. Turmeric is obtained from which part of the
plant?
(a) Fruit
(b) Stem
(c) Root
(d) Flower
The correct option is (b).
• Turmeric, the bright yellow spice, is derived
from the plant Curcuma longa.
• The part of the plant that is harvested is the
rhizome, which is a modified, underground
stem.
• A rhizome grows horizontally underground
and sends out roots and shoots from its
nodes.
• Although it grows underground like a root, it
is botanically classified as a stem due to its
structure, which includes nodes and buds.

•

Ginger is another common example of a
rhizome.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. The process of light exposure in a 24-hour cycle
that controls the growth and development of certain
plants and animals is called:
(a) Photosynthesis
(b) Photoionisation
(c) Photoperiodism
(d) Photoluminescence
The correct option is (c).
• Photoperiodism is the physiological and
behavioral response of an organism to the
relative lengths of day and night.
• In plants, it controls critical developmental
processes such as flowering (e.g., some plants
only flower when days are short, while others
flower when days are long).
• In animals, it regulates seasonal activities like
migration, hibernation, and reproduction.
• It acts as a biological calendar, allowing
organisms to time their activities with the
changing seasons.
• Photosynthesis (a) is the process of
converting light into chemical energy, not a
response to day length.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. In typhoid which of the following drugs is
administered?
(a) Chloromycetin
(b) Penicillin
(c) Sulphonamide
(d) Tetracycline
The correct option is (a).
• Typhoid fever is a bacterial infection caused
by Salmonella Typhi.
• Historically, the antibiotic of choice for
treating typhoid was Chloramphenicol, which
is sold under the trade name Chloromycetin.
• While it is very effective, its use has declined
significantly due to the risk of serious side
effects (like aplastic anemia) and the
emergence of antibiotic resistance.

•

Today, other antibiotics like fluoroquinolones
(e.g., ciprofloxacin) and third-generation
cephalosporins are more commonly used, but
Chloromycetin was the classic and historically
significant treatment.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. Amoebiasis, malaria and typhoid are diseases
due to amoeba, malarial parasites and typhoid
bacillus which are collectively called:
(a) Arthropods
(b) Protozoa
(c) Mollusca
(d) Pisces
The correct option is (b).
• This question is slightly flawed but points to a
general classification.
• Amoebiasis is caused by Entamoeba
histolytica, which is an amoeba, a type of
Protozoa (single-celled eukaryote).
• Malaria is caused by Plasmodium species,
which are also Protozoa.
• Typhoid is caused by Salmonella typhi, which
is a bacterium, not a protozoan.
• However, among the given choices, Protozoa
is the best fit as it correctly classifies two of
the three pathogens.
• The other options are incorrect (Arthropods =
insects/spiders, Mollusca = snails/clams,
Pisces = fish).
Hence, option (b) is the intended answer.
Q6. Hypochondria is a:
(a) Disease of eyes
(b) Disease of sexual organs
(c) Disease of low blood pressure
(d) State of morbid anxiety about one's own health,
with complaint of imaginary disorders
The correct option is (d).
• Hypochondriasis, or illness anxiety disorder,
is a psychiatric condition.
• It is characterized by a person's excessive and
persistent worry about having a serious,
undiagnosed medical illness.

•

Individuals with this condition misinterpret
normal bodily sensations or minor symptoms
as signs of severe disease, leading to a state of
morbid anxiety about one's own health, with
complaints of imaginary or exaggerated
disorders, despite medical reassurance that
they are healthy.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q7. Foot and mouth disease is prevalent among:
(a) Cattle only
(b) Cattle and pigs only
(c) Sheep and cattle only
(d) Cattle, goats, sheep and other hoofed animals
The correct option is (d).
• Foot-and-mouth disease (FMD) is a severe
and highly contagious viral disease.
• It affects cloven-hoofed animals (animals with
a divided hoof).
• This includes a wide range of domestic and
wild animals, most notably cattle, pigs, sheep,
and goats, and also deer, buffalo, and others.
• Therefore, the most comprehensive and
correct answer is that it is prevalent among
cattle, goats, sheep, and other hoofed
animals.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. When one enters a dark room, he is not able to
see anything but after some time his vision
improves. This is because:
(a) The retina of the eye comes forward
(b) The retina of the eye moves backward
(c) The pupil of the eye contracts
(d) The pupil of the eye dilates
The correct option is (d).
• The pupil is the opening in the centre of the
iris that allows light to enter the eye.
• The size of the pupil is controlled by muscles
in the iris.
• In bright light, the pupil contracts (gets
smaller) to limit the amount of light entering
the eye.
• When you move from a bright area into a dark
room, your pupil is initially still contracted, so

very little light enters and you cannot see
well.
Over the next few moments, the iris muscles
relax, causing the pupil to dilate (get larger).
This allows more light to enter the eye,
improving your ability to see in the dark. This
process is called dark adaptation.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q10. Gerontology is the study of:
(a) Process of ageing
(b) Growth of cells
(c) Birds
(d) Vegetables
The correct option is (a).
• Gerontology is the comprehensive, multidisciplinary study of the process of ageing and
the particular problems and characteristics of
old age.
• It encompasses the social, cultural,
psychological, cognitive, and biological
aspects of getting older.
• It is distinct from geriatrics, which is the
medical specialty focused on the health and
care of elderly people.
• The study of birds is ornithology.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q9. The messages received by and transmitted from
the brain to the various parts of the body take the
form of:
(a) Nervous impulses
(b) Chemical impulses
(c) Mechanical impulses
(d) Automatic impulses
The correct option is (a).
• The nervous system is the body's
communication network, composed of
specialized cells called neurons.
• Information is transmitted along neurons in
the form of electrochemical signals called
action potentials, or more commonly,
nervous impulses.
• These impulses travel rapidly from the brain
and spinal cord to muscles and glands, and
from sensory organs back to the brain.
• While chemical signals (neurotransmitters)
are used to transmit the message between
neurons at a synapse, the long-distance signal
along the neuron is a nervous (electrical)
impulse.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q11. Hormones which are necessary for the
development of the human body are secreted by:
(a) Thyroid gland
(b) Pituitary gland
(c) Parathyroid gland
(d) Exocrine gland
The correct option is (b).
• The pituitary gland, often called the "master
gland," is responsible for secreting several
hormones that control growth and
development.
• Most importantly, the anterior pituitary
secretes Growth Hormone (GH), which is
essential for normal growth and development
during childhood and adolescence.
• The pituitary also secretes other hormones
that regulate the function of other endocrine
glands (like the thyroid and adrenal glands),
which in turn also influence growth and
development.
• While the thyroid gland (a) is also crucial for
development, the pituitary gland is the
central regulator.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

•
•

Q12. The ductless and secretory glands in the human
body are known as:
(a) Endocrine glands
(b) Exocrine glands
(c) Salivary glands
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (a).
• Glands in the body are classified based on
how they release their secretions.
• Endocrine glands are ductless glands. They
secrete their products, called hormones,
directly into the bloodstream or surrounding
tissue fluid, which then transports them to
target cells throughout the body.
• Examples include the pituitary, thyroid, and
adrenal glands.
• Exocrine glands (like salivary glands) have
ducts through which they secrete products
(saliva, sweat, enzymes) onto a surface or into
a cavity.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q13. Fat is stored in the human body in:
(a) Muscular tissue
(b) Connective tissue
(c) Adipose tissue
(d) Epithelial tissue
The correct option is (c).
• The primary site for fat storage in the human
body is a specialized type of connective tissue
called adipose tissue.
• This tissue is composed of cells called
adipocytes, which are specifically designed to
store large amounts of fat in the form of
triglycerides.
• Adipose tissue not only serves as an energy
reserve but also provides insulation, cushions
organs, and produces certain hormones.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1984
Q1. Sugarcane crop matures in about:
(a) 2 months
(b) 4 months

(c) 8 months
(d) 12 months
The correct option is (d).
• Sugarcane (Saccharum officinarum) is a
perennial grass that has a long growing
season compared to many other crops.
• From planting to being ready for harvest, the
crop takes approximately 10 to 12 months to
mature in subtropical regions like North India.
• In tropical regions like South India, the
duration can be even longer, up to 18
months.
• Given the options, 12 months is the most
accurate and representative maturation
period.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. The gestation period of a goat is about:
(a) 150 days
(b) 200 days
(c) 250 days
(d) 365 days
The correct option is (a).
• The gestation period is the length of
pregnancy, from conception to birth.
• For a goat, the gestation period is
approximately 5 months.
• Converting this to days (5 months × ~30
days/month), the duration is about 150 days.
• The typical range is from 145 to 155 days.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Which of the following diseases is spread
through cow's milk?
(a) Diphtheria
(b) Typhoid
(c) Measles
(d) None of these
The correct option is (b).
• Raw, unpasteurized milk can be a vehicle for
transmitting various pathogens from an
infected animal or from contamination during
the milking process.
• Typhoid fever, caused by the bacterium
Salmonella Typhi, can be spread through food

•

•

•

and water contaminated with feces of an
infected person.
If a dairy worker is a typhoid carrier or if the
water used to clean equipment is
contaminated, the milk can become a source
of infection.
Historically, milk was a known vector for
diseases like tuberculosis and typhoid before
pasteurization became standard practice.
Diphtheria and Measles are primarily spread
through respiratory droplets between
humans.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q4. Arrange the following in descending order of
their life spans:
A. Elephant
B. Horse
C. Tiger
D. Wolf
(a) A, B, C, D
(b) A, C, B, D
(c) C, A, B, D
(d) D, A, B, C
The correct option is (a).
• We need to arrange the animals from the
longest lifespan to the shortest.
• Elephant: Has the longest lifespan, often
living 60–70 years.
• Horse: Typically 25–30 years, but can live
longer.
• Tiger: Around 10–15 years in the wild, up to
20 in captivity.
• Wolf: Typically 6–8 years in the wild, and up
to 15 in captivity.
• The correct descending order is Elephant >
Horse > Tiger > Wolf, which corresponds to A,
B, C, D.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. One of our friends is in the hospital and we want
to see him. We are safest if he is suffering from:
(a) Mumps
(b) TB (Tuberculosis)
(c) Smallpox

(d) None of the above
The correct option is (d).
• A contagious disease is one that can be
transmitted from person to person.
• Mumps is a contagious viral disease spread
through respiratory droplets.
• Tuberculosis (TB) is a contagious bacterial
disease, also spread through respiratory
droplets.
• Smallpox was an extremely contagious viral
disease, also spread through respiratory
droplets and contact (it has now been
eradicated).
• Since all three diseases listed are contagious,
there is a risk of infection when visiting a
patient suffering from any of them.
• Therefore, we are not truly "safe" from a
contagion perspective with any of these
illnesses.
Hence, option (d) is the correct answer.
Q6. Jaundice is caused due to the malfunctioning of:
(a) Kidney
(b) Liver
(c) Pancreas
(d) Stomach
The correct option is (b).
• Jaundice is characterized by the yellowing of
the skin and the whites of the eyes.
• It is caused by a high level of bilirubin in the
blood.
• Bilirubin is a yellow pigment produced during
the normal breakdown of red blood cells.
• The liver is responsible for processing
bilirubin, making it water-soluble, and
excreting it in bile.
• If the liver is damaged or malfunctioning
(e.g., hepatitis or cirrhosis), it cannot process
bilirubin effectively, causing buildup and
jaundice.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Which is not a parasite?
(a) Tick
(b) Housefly

(c) Bed bug
(d) Louse
The correct option is (b).
• A parasite lives on or in a host organism and
gets its food from or at the expense of its
host.
• A tick (a), a bed bug (c), and a louse (d) are all
ectoparasites that live on the host and feed
on blood.
• A housefly is not a parasite; it is a scavenger
that feeds on decaying organic matter.
• While it can act as a mechanical vector for
diseases by carrying pathogens on its body, it
does not live on a host or derive nourishment
directly from it.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q8. The sex of a child is determined by the:
(a) Chromosomes of the father
(b) Chromosomes of the mother
(c) Rh factor of parents
(d) None of these
The correct option is (a).
• Human sex is determined by the sex
chromosomes, X and Y.
• Females have XX, and males have XY.
• The mother can only contribute an X
chromosome (eggs all contain X).
• The father produces two types of sperm: Xcarrying and Y-carrying.
• X-sperm + X-ovum → XX (female); Y-sperm +
X-ovum → XY (male).
• Therefore, it is the chromosome from the
father's sperm that determines the sex of the
child.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9. Which of the following groups represents the
correct order of four types of teeth in human beings?
(a) Incisors, premolars, molars, canines
(b) Incisors, canines, premolars, molars
(c) Molars, canines, incisors, premolars
(d) Canines, incisors, molars, premolars
The correct option is (b).

•
•
•
•

•
•

The types of human teeth are arranged from
the front of the mouth to the back as follows:
Incisors: the four front teeth in each jaw, used
for cutting food.
Canines: the pointed teeth next to incisors,
used for tearing.
Premolars (bicuspids): two on each side
behind canines, used for crushing and
grinding.
Molars: three at the very back on each side,
used for heavy grinding.
The correct sequence is Incisors, canines,
premolars, molars.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q10. Which pair is correct?
(a) Sea horse, Sea cucumber, Sea urchin
(b) Starfish, Cuttlefish, Dogfish
(c) Housefly, Dragonfly, Butterfly
(d) Earthworm, Ringworm, Tapeworm
The correct option is (c).
• This asks to identify the group where all
members belong to the same major
classification.
• (a) Sea horse is a fish; Sea cucumber and Sea
urchin are echinoderms.
• (b) Starfish is an echinoderm; Cuttlefish is a
mollusk; Dogfish is a shark (fish).
• (c) Housefly, Dragonfly, and Butterfly are all
members of the class Insecta (phylum

•

Arthropoda), sharing features like six legs and
a three-part body.
(d) Earthworm is an annelid, Ringworm is a
fungus, and Tapeworm is a platyhelminth.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q11. Triple Antigen does not immunize against:
(a) Diphtheria
(b) Tetanus
(c) Typhoid
(d) Whooping cough
The correct option is (c).
• "Triple Antigen" is the common name for the
DTP vaccine.
• The DTP vaccine provides immunization
against Diphtheria, Tetanus, and Pertussis
(Whooping Cough).
• Typhoid is caused by Salmonella Typhi and is
not included in the triple antigen vaccine; it
requires a separate vaccination.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q12. Lichens are an association of two types of
organisms. They are:
(a) Algae and fungi
(b) Algae and virus
(c) Fungi and virus
(d) Virus and bacteria
The correct option is (a).
• A lichen is a composite organism from a
symbiotic relationship between a fungus
(mycobiont) and a photosynthetic partner
(photobiont).
• The photosynthetic partner is usually a green
alga or a cyanobacterium.
• In this mutualism, the fungus provides
structure, protection, and moisture
retention, while the alga/cyanobacterium
provides food via photosynthesis.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q13. What is the common feature in insects like the
ant and cockroach?
(a) They bite and chew their food
(b) They live in temperate climate conditions

(c) They have cutting and sucking type of mouth parts
(d) They are social insects
The correct option is (a).
• Ants and cockroaches both belong to the
class Insecta.
• A common feature is their mandibulate
mouthparts, which are adapted for biting and
chewing solid food.
• Option (b) is incorrect — they inhabit a wide
variety of climates.
• Option (c) is incorrect — sucking mouthparts
occur in insects like mosquitoes and
butterflies.
• Option (d) is incorrect — ants are social, but
cockroaches lack the complex social structure
of ants.
Hence, option (a) is the most accurate
common feature.
Q14. Which of the following is a perception without
stimuli?
(a) Hallucination
(b) Illusion
(c) Claustrophobia
(d) None of these
The correct option is (a).
• Hallucination is a sensory experience (seeing,
hearing, smelling, tasting, or feeling) that
occurs in the absence of any external
stimulus.
• It is a perception of something that is not
actually present (e.g., hearing voices when no
one is speaking).
• An illusion (b) is a misinterpretation of a real
external stimulus.
• Claustrophobia (c) is an irrational fear of
enclosed spaces.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q15. Sir J. C. Bose was a great scientist because he
showed the:
(a) Sensitivity in plants
(b) Pigments in plants
(c) Growth in plants

(d) Relations between plants and animals
The correct option is (a).
• Sir Jagadish Chandra Bose was a pioneering
scientist who made major contributions to
physics and plant biology.
• His famous work demonstrated that plants
respond to stimuli in ways similar to animal
tissues.
• Using the crescograph (an instrument he
invented), he measured and recorded minute
responses of plants to stimuli like heat, cold,
light, and electrical shock.
• Through these experiments, he provided
evidence for the sensitivity in plants, showing
that the boundary between plant and animal
life was not as rigid as previously thought.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

(c) 1 2 4 3
(d) 1 3 2 4
The correct option is (a).
• A. Mumps: contagious disease with swelling
of salivary glands, caused by the Mumps virus
→ matches 3.
• B. Tuberculosis (TB): serious infectious
disease mainly of lungs, caused by
Mycobacterium tuberculosis (a bacterium) →

• C. Beri-Beri: disease due to nutritional

Q2. The genetic code can be translated through an
intermediate, adaptor molecule by:
(a) tRNA
(b) mRNA
(c) ATP
(d) ADP
The correct option is (a).
• Describes protein synthesis (translation):
DNA → mRNA (message) → ribosome.
• tRNA acts as the intermediate, adaptor
molecule, with an anticodon that reads an
mRNA codon and carries the corresponding
amino acid.
• mRNA is the message; tRNA is the adaptor
that translates the message into protein.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Bile is secreted by:
(a) Liver
(b) Gall bladder
(c) Kidney
(d) Pancreas
The correct option is (a).
• Bile is a digestive fluid essential for digestion
and absorption of fats.
• Liver produces (secretes) bile continuously.
• Gall bladder stores and concentrates bile and
releases it into the small intestine when
needed.
• Distinction: liver = produces, gall bladder =
stores/releases.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. 'Ranikhet' is a disease found in:
(a) Poultry
(b) Cows
(c) Sheep
(d) Dogs
The correct option is (a).
• Ranikhet disease (Newcastle Disease) is a
highly contagious viral disease affecting
birds, especially poultry (chickens, turkeys).
• It impacts respiratory, nervous, and digestive
systems of birds and causes high mortality.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. Which of the following statements about
"Dinosaurs" is/are correct?
(a) The early man was a contemporary of Dinosaurs
(b) Man appeared first after the Dinosaurs became
extinct
(c) The Dinosaurs became extinct about six million
years before the appearance of the first man on the
earth
(d) The Dinosaurs became extinct about 200 million

years before the first man appeared on the earth
The correct option is (b).
• Non-avian dinosaurs went extinct ≈ 66
million years ago (Cretaceous–Paleogene
event).
• Hominins and the genus Homo appeared
much later (genus Homo ≈ 2–3 million years
ago; modern humans ≈ 300,000 years ago).
• Therefore, man appeared long after
dinosaurs became extinct; there was no
overlap.
• The time gap is ~63–64 million years, not six
million or 200 million as other options
suggest.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Wisdom teeth normally grow during the age of:
(a) 34–40 years
(b) 12–15 years
(c) 17–30 years
(d) 40–45 years
The correct option is (c).
• Wisdom teeth (third molars) typically erupt in
the late teens to mid-twenties.
• Common eruption range: 17–25 years, can
extend up to 30 years or later for some
individuals.
• This period is colloquially called the "Age of
Wisdom."
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. Sex chromosomes transmit which of the
following diseases?
(a) Myopia
(b) Colour blindness
(c) Long sightedness
(d) Night blindness
The correct option is (b).
• Sex-linked (X-linked) disorders: genes located
on X chromosome.
• Red–green colour blindness is a classic Xlinked recessive trait; genes for red/green
photopigments are on the X chromosome.
• Hence, it is much more common in males
(one X) than females (two Xs).

•

Myopia/long sightedness are complex traits;
night blindness is commonly due to Vitamin A
deficiency, not a simple sex-linked trait.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
