Q9. 'Acupuncture' is a medical system of treatment
prominently used in China in which:
(a) Herbs are used for the pain relief
(b) Drugs are used for the pain relief
(c) Special needles are inserted into particular parts of
the body for the relief
(d) Electric shocks are applied for the relief of pain
The correct option is (c).
• Acupuncture (Traditional Chinese Medicine)
involves insertion of very thin needles into
specific points on the body (meridians).
• Intended to rebalance the flow of qi (vital
energy); in modern terms, it stimulates
nerves, muscles, and connective tissue,
promoting pain relief and release of natural
painkillers.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q10. Poliomyelitis is an epidemic virus infection.
Which of the following is responsible for it?
(a) Rhabdo virus
(b) Entero virus
(c) Dengue virus
(d) Mumps virus
The correct option is (b).
• Poliomyelitis (polio) is caused by the
poliovirus, which is a species of Enterovirus.
• Enteroviruses typically enter via the
gastrointestinal tract (fecal–oral route) and
can invade the nervous system, causing
paralysis in polio.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q11. Snake bite first affects the:
(a) Nervous system
(b) Blood circulation
(c) Brain
(d) Lungs
The correct option is (b).

•

Snake venom is a complex mix of toxins that
must enter the blood circulation (or
lymphatics) to be transported throughout the
body.
Venom effects depend on species: hemotoxic
venoms affect blood and blood vessels
(clotting, bleeding, tissue breakdown);
neurotoxic venoms affect the nervous
system.
Regardless, the circulatory system is the
initial pathway for systemic dissemination of
venom.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q12. Assertion (A): Photosynthesis and vision are the
phenomenon of visible light.
Reason (R): The visible light is absorbed by small
particles containing chlorophylls as well as the retina
of the eye.
(a) if A and R both are correct and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) if A and R both are correct but R is not the correct
explanation of A
(c) if A is incorrect but R is correct
(d) if A is correct but R is incorrect
The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A): True — both photosynthesis
and vision fundamentally depend on visible
light.
• Reason (R): True — both processes rely on
absorption of visible light by specialized
pigments: chlorophyll (photosynthesis) and
photoreceptor pigments (e.g., rhodopsin) in
the retina (vision).
• R correctly explains A: the presence of lightabsorbing molecules tuned to visible
wavelengths is why both phenomena use
visible light.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1986
Q1. Match the columns:
List I

List II

A. Vitamin A 1. Beriberi
B. Vitamin B 2. Rickets
C. Vitamin C 3. Scurvy
D. Vitamin D 4. Sterility
E. Vitamin E 5. Night Blindness
Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
ABCDE
(a) 5 4 3 2 1
(b) 5 1 3 2 4
(c) 1 2 3 4 5
(d) 3 2 1 5 4
The correct option is (b).
• A. Vitamin A deficiency is a leading cause of
preventable blindness in children. One of the
first signs is Night Blindness. So, A matches
with 5.
• B. Vitamin B (specifically Vitamin B1, or
Thiamine) deficiency causes the disease
Beriberi, which affects the nervous and
cardiovascular systems. So, B matches with 1.
• C. Vitamin C (Ascorbic Acid) is essential for
collagen synthesis. A severe deficiency leads
to Scurvy, characterized by bleeding gums,
poor wound healing, and weakness. So, C
matches with 3.
• D. Vitamin D is crucial for calcium absorption.
A deficiency in children causes Rickets,
leading to soft and deformed bones. So, D
matches with 2.
• E. Vitamin E is an antioxidant that is
important for reproductive health. A
deficiency can lead to neurological problems
and, in animal studies, has been linked to
Sterility. So, E matches with 4.
• The correct matching is A-5, B-1, C-3, D-2, E-4.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. A person walking barefooted in a field feels a
sharp sting and on examination two puncture marks
are found on his leg. He is most probably bitten by a:

(a) Poisonous snake
(b) Non-poisonous snake
(c) Scorpion
(d) Rat
The correct option is (a).
• The key clue is the presence of two distinct
puncture marks.
• Many venomous snakes, particularly vipers
and elapids (like cobras), have a pair of long,
hollow teeth called fangs at the front of their
mouth, which they use to inject venom.
• A bite from such a snake typically leaves two
clear puncture wounds corresponding to
these two fangs.
• Non-poisonous snakes usually have rows of
smaller, solid teeth and leave a U-shaped
series of scratches or smaller punctures. A
scorpion sting would typically leave a single
puncture mark.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. The influence of genes in controlling the activity
of living organisms is through:
(a) Photosynthesis
(b) Protein synthesis
(c) Vitamins
(d) Hormones
The correct option is (b).
• The central dogma of molecular biology
describes how genetic information flows
within a biological system.
• A gene is a segment of DNA that contains the
instructions for building a specific molecule,
which is usually a protein.
• This information is first transcribed from DNA
into messenger RNA (mRNA). The mRNA then
travels to a ribosome, where the information
is translated into a specific sequence of amino
acids, creating a protein. This entire process is
protein synthesis.
• These proteins (which include enzymes,
structural components, and signaling
molecules) are the "workhorses" of the cell
and carry out virtually all cellular activities.
Therefore, genes control the organism by

dictating which proteins are made.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
•

Q4. Triple antigen vaccine is given to children to
protect them against:
(a) Polio
(b) Whooping cough
(c) Contagious diseases
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (b).
• "Triple Antigen" is the common name for the
DTP vaccine.
• It is a combination vaccine that protects
against three serious bacterial diseases:
Diphtheria, Tetanus, Pertussis (Whooping
Cough).
• The question asks which disease the vaccine
protects against from the given options. Since
Whooping Cough is one of the three
components, it is the correct answer. Polio
requires a separate vaccine. While the
diseases are contagious, that general term is
not the specific answer.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. AIDS is:
1. Caused by a virus
2. Carried by body fluid, blood and serum
3. Found mostly in males
4. Found mostly in homosexuals
Of these Statements:
(a) 1 and 4 are correct
(b) 1, 2 and 4 are correct
(c) 2 and 3 are correct
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4 are correct
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is correct. AIDS (Acquired
Immunodeficiency Syndrome) is the final
stage of an infection caused by a virus, the
Human Immunodeficiency Virus (HIV).
• Statement 2 is correct. HIV is transmitted
through direct contact with certain body
fluids from an infected person, most notably
blood, semen, vaginal fluids, and breast milk.

•

•

Serum is the fluid component of blood, so this
is also correct.
Statement 3 is incorrect. AIDS affects both
males and females; women and girls make up
more than half of people living with HIV
globally.
Statement 4 is correct (in a historical and
epidemiological context). In early years of the
epidemic in some regions, spread was rapid
among men who have sex with men, and
they remain a key affected population.
Therefore, statements 1, 2, and 4 are
considered correct in the context of the
question.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q6. The final product of photosynthesis is:
(a) Carbohydrates
(b) Carbon dioxide
(c) Oxygen
(d) Water
The correct option is (a).
• The overall balanced chemical equation for
photosynthesis is: 6CO₂ + 6H₂O + Light Energy
→ C₆H₁₂O₆ (Glucose) + 6O₂.
• The primary purpose of photosynthesis for
the plant is to convert light energy into
chemical energy stored in the bonds of sugar
molecules.
• The main product created and used by the
plant for energy and growth is glucose
(C₆H₁₂O₆), which is a type of carbohydrate.
• Oxygen is an important byproduct released
into the atmosphere, but the useful final
product for the plant is the carbohydrate.
Carbon dioxide and water are reactants
(inputs).
• (Note: the provided answer key says (c),
which is incorrect.)
Hence, option (a) is the correct answer.
Q7. Soybeans are rich in:
(a) Proteins
(b) Vitamin A
(c) Minerals

(d) Carbohydrates
The correct option is (a).
• Soybeans are a legume highly valued for its
nutritional content.
• They are particularly well-known for being an
excellent source of high-quality plant-based
protein.
• Soybeans contain all the essential amino
acids, making them a complete protein,
which is rare for plant foods. This is why they
are staple foods in many vegetarian and
vegan diets and are used to make products
like tofu and soy milk.
• While they contain other nutrients, their
protein content is their most defining feature.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8. Which of the following pairs is incorrect?
(a) Kiwi – Bird
(b) Alligator – Crocodile
(c) Bat – Mammal
(d) Fish – Pisces
The correct option is (b).
• Identify the incorrect classification:
• (a) Kiwi – Bird: Correct. The kiwi is a flightless
bird from New Zealand.
• (b) Alligator – Crocodile: Incorrect. Alligators
and crocodiles are both reptiles in order
Crocodilia but belong to different families; an
alligator is not a crocodile.
• (c) Bat – Mammal: Correct. Bats are mammals
capable of true flight.
• (d) Fish – Pisces: Correct. Pisces is the
traditional class for fish.
Hence, option (b) is incorrect.
Q9. Which of the following pairs is/are correct?
A. Main root – Storage
B. Lateral root – Absorption
C. Root hair – Conduction
Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
(a) Only A
(b) A and C
(c) All are correct

(d) None of the above
The correct option is (a).
• A. Main root – Storage: Correct. In many
plants with a taproot system, the main root
(taproot) is modified for food storage (e.g.,
carrot, radish).
• B. Lateral root – Absorption: Partially correct
but not the primary distinction; lateral roots
anchor the plant and explore soil; the primary
site of absorption is root hairs.
• C. Root hair – Conduction: Incorrect. Root
hairs primarily function in absorption,
increasing surface area for water and mineral
uptake. Conduction (transport) is performed
by xylem and phloem within the root.
• Based on the primary and most accurate
functions, only statement A is unequivocally
correct.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1987
1. Match the columns:
List I

List II

A. Otitis

1. Throat

B. Encephalitis 2. Ear
C. Laryngitis

3. Liver

D. Hepatitis

4. Brain
5. Kidney

Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
ABCD
(a) 1 2 3 4
(b) 4 3 1 2
(c) 2 4 1 3
(d) 3 4 1 2
The correct option is (c).
• The suffix "-itis" means inflammation of a
particular body part.
• A. Otitis: "Oto-" refers to the ear. Otitis is an
inflammation of the ear. So, A matches with 2.
• B. Encephalitis: "Encephalo-" refers to the
brain. Encephalitis is an inflammation of the
brain. So, B matches with 4.

•

•

C. Laryngitis: Larynx refers to the voice box,
which is located in the throat. Laryngitis is an
inflammation of the larynx. So, C matches
with 1.
D. Hepatitis: "Hepa-" refers to the liver.
Hepatitis is an inflammation of the liver. So, D
matches with 3.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. Which of the following is/are found in plant cells
but not in those of animals?
(a) Plastid & Cellular wall
(b) Chromosome
(c) Mitochondria & Golgi apparatus
(d) Cell wall & lysozyme
The correct option is (a).
• Plant and animal cells are both eukaryotic
but have several key differences.
• Cellular wall: Plant cells have a rigid cell wall
made of cellulose, which provides structural
support. Animal cells lack a cell wall.
• Plastids: a group of organelles found in plant
cells. The most well-known plastid is the
chloroplast, the site of photosynthesis.
Animal cells do not have plastids.
• Chromosomes (b), Mitochondria, and Golgi
apparatus (c) are found in both plant and
animal cells. Lysosomes (part of d) are
typically found in animal cells but are rare in
plant cells.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Which of the following diseases is hereditary?
(a) Haemophilia
(b) Tuberculosis
(c) Cancer
(d) Dysentery
The correct option is (a).
• A hereditary disease is one caused by a
genetic mutation and passed down from
parents to offspring.
• Haemophilia is a classic example of a
hereditary, X-linked recessive disorder. It is
caused by a mutation in genes producing

•

blood clotting factors, leading to inability of
blood to clot properly.
Tuberculosis (b) and Dysentery (d) are
infectious diseases caused by pathogens.
While some cancers (c) have a genetic
predisposition, most are not directly inherited
in the same way as haemophilia.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q4. Ultrafiltering units of the kidney are known as:
(a) Nephron
(b) Glomerulus
(c) Vena Cava
(d) Tubule
The correct option is (a).
• The kidneys filter waste from the blood
through ultrafiltration.
• The structural and functional unit responsible
for this process in the kidney is the nephron.
Each kidney contains about a million
nephrons.
• Each nephron consists of a glomerulus (b), a
tiny ball of capillaries where the initial
filtration occurs, and a tubule (d), where
substances are reabsorbed or secreted.
• While the glomerulus is the specific site of
ultrafiltration, the nephron is the complete
"ultrafiltering unit." The Vena Cava (c) is a
major vein to the heart.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. The human body is immune to which of the
following diseases?
(a) Smallpox
(b) Diabetes
(c) Tuberculosis
(d) Jaundice
The correct option is (a).
• This question concerns acquired immunity,
particularly at a population level.
• Smallpox is the only human disease to have
been completely eradicated worldwide
through vaccination. Because the virus no
longer exists in nature, the human population
as a whole is now effectively "immune" in the

•

sense that no one can naturally contract it
anymore.
The body does not have innate immunity to
diabetes (a metabolic disorder), tuberculosis
(a bacterial infection), or jaundice (a
symptom of liver disease).
Hence, option (a) is the best answer.

Q6. Temperature in human beings is controlled by:
(a) Pituitary gland
(b) Thyroid gland
(c) Hypothalamus gland
(d) Adrenal gland
The correct option is (c).
• Regulation of body temperature
(thermoregulation) is a critical homeostatic
function.
• The body's primary thermostat and control
center for temperature is the hypothalamus,
a small region at the base of the brain.
• The hypothalamus monitors blood
temperature and receives signals from skin
receptors, then triggers responses like
shivering, sweating, vasodilation, or
vasoconstriction to maintain core
temperature (~37°C).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. 'Blight' - the disease of potato can be cured by:
(a) Diathane
(b) Bordeaux mixture
(c) Dionine T-4
(d) Agrimycin and Ceresan
The correct option is (b).
• Potato blight (Late Blight) is caused by the
oomycete Phytophthora infestans and caused
the Irish Potato Famine.
• One classic treatment for fungal and
oomycete diseases is the Bordeaux mixture, a
combination of copper sulfate and slaked
lime, which acts as a protective fungicide.
• The provided answer key says (c), but
Bordeaux mixture is the classic and correct
answer for treating blight.
Hence, option (b) is the correct answer.

Q8. India's indigenous pigs generally give birth to
how many young ones at a time?
(a) 2–4
(b) 4–6
(c) 6–8
(d) 8–10
The correct option is (b).
• Litter size varies by breed. Improved/exotic
breeds often have 10–14 piglets.
• India's indigenous pig breeds are generally
less prolific and have smaller litters.
• The average litter size for most indigenous
Indian pig breeds is typically 4 to 6 piglets.
• The provided answer key says (d), which is
more typical of commercial, non-indigenous
breeds.
Hence, option (b) is the more accurate
answer for indigenous breeds.
Q9. Blood grouping was discovered by:
(a) Landsteiner
(b) Robert Koch
(c) William Harvey
(d) Louis Pasteur
The correct option is (a).
• The discovery of the main human blood
groups is credited to Karl Landsteiner.
• In 1900–1901, he found that blood from
different people would agglutinate when
mixed and identified groups A, B, and O,
enabling safe blood transfusions. He received
the Nobel Prize in 1930 for this work.
• Robert Koch discovered bacteria for TB and
cholera; William Harvey described blood
circulation; Louis Pasteur developed
pasteurization and vaccines.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q10. A good source of Vitamin A is:
(a) Carrot
(b) Potato
(c) Rice
(d) Tomato
The correct option is (a).

•

•

•

Vitamin A exists as preformed retinol in
animal products and provitamin A
carotenoids (like beta-carotene) in plants.
Carrots are rich in beta-carotene, which the
body converts to Vitamin A; the orange color
indicates this pigment.
Potatoes and rice are mainly carbohydrate
sources. Tomatoes contain some Vitamin A
but far less than carrots.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q11. Which of the following is not a seed-borne
disease?
(a) Brown leaf spot of rice
(b) Black arm of cotton
(c) Red rot of sugarcane
(d) Potato mosaic
The correct option is (d).
• A seed-borne disease is transmitted on or
within true seeds, infecting new plants as
they grow.
• Brown leaf spot of rice and Black arm of
cotton are known to be seed-borne.
• Red rot of sugarcane is primarily transmitted
through infected seed canes (setts), which are
vegetative planting material, not true
botanical seeds.
• Potato mosaic is a viral disease; potatoes are
propagated vegetatively using tubers (seed
potatoes), not true seeds. The virus is
transmitted through infected tubers and by
aphids, so it is not a true seed-borne disease.
• The provided answer key says (c), which is
also a candidate because sugarcane is planted
by setts, not seeds. However, potato mosaic
is a classic example of a disease not
transmitted by true seeds.
• Therefore, both (c) and (d) are technically
correct; in agricultural context, (d) is a slightly
better answer as sugarcane "seed" refers to
cuttings.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q12. Which of the following is necessary for the
improvement of the quality of crops?

1. Introduction
2. Hybridisation
3. Selection
4. Segregation
Codes:
(a) 2, 3 and 4
(b) 1, 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Crop improvement uses techniques to
develop varieties with desirable traits.
• 1. Introduction: bringing in new plant
varieties from other regions provides new
genetic material — a valid and fundamental
step.
• 2. Hybridisation: cross-breeding genetically
different parent plants to combine desirable
traits is essential.
• 3. Selection: after hybridisation, breeders
select offspring showing desired trait
combinations — critical.
• 4. Segregation: a Mendelian principle
describing allele separation during gamete
formation; it's a genetic phenomenon
breeders must understand, but it is not an
active technique breeders perform.
• Thus, Introduction, Hybridisation, and
Selection are the necessary techniques. The
provided key (a) excludes Introduction;
however, Introduction is a fundamental part
of crop improvement programs.
Hence, option (c) is the most complete
answer.
Q13. Which of the following fertilisers leaves an
acidic residue?
(a) Ammonium phosphate
(b) Ammonium sulphate
(c) Urea
(d) Sulphate of potash
The correct option is (b).
• The effect of a fertilizer on soil pH depends
on its chemistry.

•

•

•

Fertilisers containing ammonium (NH₄⁺) tend
to leave an acidic residue because
nitrification (conversion of NH₄⁺ → NO₃⁻)
releases H⁺ ions.
Ammonium sulphate ((NH₄)₂SO₄) contains
ammonium and sulfate; both contribute to
acidity, making it one of the most acidifying
common fertilizers.
Ammonium phosphate (a) is also acidifying,
urea (c) becomes acidic after conversion,
while sulphate of potash (d) is generally
neutral. Between (a) and (b), ammonium
sulphate has the stronger acidifying effect.
Hence, option (b) is the best answer.

Q14. UP-308 is a variety of:
(a) Millet
(b) Rice
(c) Cotton
(d) Wheat
The correct option is (d).
• UP-308 is a well-known high-yielding, semidwarf variety of wheat, developed in India
during the Green Revolution era to increase
wheat production.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q15. Foetus development in a woman's womb can
be ascertained by:
(a) CAT scanning
(b) Ultrasound
(c) PTT scanning
(d) Co-27 experiment
The correct option is (b).
• The standard, safe, and most common
method for monitoring fetal development is
ultrasound imaging (sonography).
• Ultrasound uses high-frequency sound waves
to create real-time images of the fetus and
surrounding structures; it checks growth,
anatomy, and position.
• CAT scanning (a) uses X-rays and is generally
avoided during pregnancy due to radiation
risk. PTT scanning and Co-27 are not standard

techniques for this purpose.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q16. Plants take nitrogen in the form of:
(a) Nitrites
(b) Ammonia
(c) Nitrates
(d) Element
The correct option is (c).
• Plants require nitrogen but cannot use
atmospheric N₂ directly.
• Soil bacteria convert atmospheric nitrogen
and organic matter into usable forms.
• The primary form absorbed by most higher
plants is nitrate (NO₃⁻).
• While plants can also absorb ammonium
(NH₄⁺), nitrate is generally the principal
source taken up from soil. Nitrites are
intermediate and can be toxic at high
concentrations.
Hence, option (c) is the most correct answer.
Q17. Opium is obtained from:
(a) Tablet type latex
(b) Latex juice
(c) Seed capsule of opium poppy
(d) Poppy leaves
The correct option is (c).
• Opium is derived from the opium poppy
(Papaver somniferum).
• It is obtained from the unripe seed capsule
(seed pod). After the petals fall, the seed
capsule is scored, causing it to exude a milky
latex juice (b).
• This latex dries on the capsule surface and
coagulates into a brownish mass — the raw
opium — which is scraped off.
• While (b) describes part of the process, the
opium is obtained from the seed capsule,
making (c) most accurate. The provided key
says (d), which is incorrect.
Hence, option (c) is the correct answer.
Q18. The cure for which of the following diseases
exists?

(a) Blood cancer
(b) Hepatitis-B
(c) Breast Cancer
(d) Lung Cancer
The correct option is (b).
• This asks about existence of a definitive
"cure" in the sense of prevention or
eradication.
• Hepatitis B is a viral infection for which a
highly effective vaccine exists that can
prevent infection. For chronic cases, antiviral
treatments can suppress the virus to
undetectable levels — considered a
functional cure in many contexts.
• Cancers (a, c, d) are often treatable and
sometimes curable, especially if caught early,
but there is no single guaranteed cure
comparable to a vaccine that prevents a viral
disease.
Hence, option (b) is the best answer.
Q19. ELISA test is prescribed for:
(a) AIDS
(b) Typhoid
(c) Polio
(d) Cancer
The correct option is (a).
• ELISA (Enzyme-Linked Immunosorbent Assay)
detects antibodies or antigens in samples.
• It is widely used as the initial screening test
for HIV (the virus that causes AIDS), detecting
antibodies produced in response to infection.
Positive ELISA results are confirmed with
more specific tests (e.g., Western blot).
• Although ELISA can be adapted for many
diseases, it is most commonly associated with
HIV/AIDS screening.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q20. Which of the following is a living fossil?
(a) Blue green algae
(b) Green algae
(c) Fungus
(d) Ginkgo
The correct option is (d).

•

•

•

A "living fossil" is a living species virtually
unchanged from ancient fossil relatives and
lacking close living relatives.
Ginkgo (Ginkgo biloba) is a classic example:
the last surviving species of a division that was
widespread during the time of dinosaurs, with
leaf form virtually unchanged for >200 million
years.
Blue-green algae (cyanobacteria) are ancient
but not a single unchanged species; they are
diverse.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q22. Photosynthesis generally occurs in which
portions of the plant?
(a) Leaf and other chloroplast bearing parts
(b) Stem and leaf
(c) Roots and chloroplast bearing parts

(d) Bark and leaf
The correct option is (a).
• Photosynthesis uses sunlight, water, and CO₂
to produce food in chloroplasts containing
chlorophyll.
• Chloroplasts are most abundant in leaves,
making them the primary site. Any green part
(young stems, unripe fruit, sepals) with
chloroplasts can photosynthesize to some
extent.
• Therefore, photosynthesis occurs in the leaf
and other chloroplast-bearing parts.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q23. Which of the following varieties are correctly

Q24. 'Khaira' is a plant disease caused due to the
deficiency of:
(a) Iron
(b) Carbon
(c) Zinc

(d) None of the above
The correct option is (c).
• Khaira disease affects rice, especially nursery
beds and transplanted fields.
• It is a physiological disorder caused by Zinc
(Zn) deficiency in soil.
• Symptoms: yellowish-brown patches, stunted
growth, reduced tillering; treatable with zinc
sulfate soil or foliar application.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q25. Azolla gives a good biofertilizer when mixed
with:
(a) Blue-green algae
(b) Cow dung
(c) Bone-meal
(d) Urea
The correct option is (a).
• Azolla is a small free-floating aquatic fern
that has a symbiotic relationship with a
nitrogen-fixing cyanobacterium (Anabaena
azollae), a blue-green alga.
• Anabaena fixes atmospheric nitrogen into
ammonia, enriching Azolla with nitrogen.
When incorporated into soil (e.g., rice
paddies), Azolla decomposes and releases this
nitrogen, acting as an excellent biofertilizer.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1988
Q1. The type of crop which is able to fix nitrogen
from the air is known as:
(a) Tuber
(b) Coffee
(c) Legume
(d) Wheat
The correct option is (c).
• Biological nitrogen fixation is the process by
which atmospheric nitrogen gas (N₂) is
converted into ammonia (NH₃), a form usable
by plants.
• This process is primarily carried out by
certain types of bacteria.

•

•

•

Legumes (plants in the pea family, such as
beans, lentils, clover, and alfalfa) have a
unique symbiotic relationship with nitrogenfixing bacteria called Rhizobia.
These bacteria live in nodules on the roots of
the legume plants. The plant provides the
bacteria with carbohydrates for energy, and
in return, the bacteria "fix" atmospheric
nitrogen into ammonia, which the plant can
then use to grow. This makes legumes
excellent for enriching the soil with nitrogen.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

•
•

•

•

Q2. Saliva helps in the digestion of:
(a) Starch
(b) Proteins
(c) Fibres
(d) Fats
The correct option is (a).
• Saliva contains a digestive enzyme called
salivary amylase (also known as ptyalin).
• The function of amylase is to begin the
chemical digestion of carbohydrates,
specifically starch.
• It breaks down the large, complex starch
molecules into smaller, simpler sugars like
maltose. This is the first step in carbohydrate
digestion, which is then completed in the
small intestine.
• The digestion of proteins and fats begins
later in the stomach and small intestine,
respectively.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Amniocentesis is a method for:
(a) Determination of foetal sex
(b) Separation of amino acids
(c) Determination of the sequence of amino acids in a
protein
(d) Inducing abortion
The correct option is (a).
• Amniocentesis is a prenatal diagnostic
procedure. It involves inserting a needle
through the mother's abdomen into the

•

amniotic sac to withdraw a small amount of
amniotic fluid.
This fluid contains fetal cells, which can be
analyzed for several purposes.
The primary medical reason for
amniocentesis is to detect chromosomal
abnormalities (like Down syndrome) and
genetic disorders.
However, because the fetal chromosomes
are analyzed, the procedure also reveals the
sex of the fetus (by checking for XX or XY
chromosomes).
While it is not the primary purpose and is
often ethically controversial, determination
of fetal sex is a direct outcome of the test. It
is a diagnostic tool, not a method for
inducing abortion (d).
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q4. Water hyacinth is related to:
(a) Lemna
(b) Pistia
(c) Eichhornia
(d) None of these
The correct option is (c).
• Water hyacinth is the common name for a
group of free-floating aquatic plants.
• Its scientific (generic) name is Eichhornia. The
most common species is Eichhornia crassipes.
• It is notorious for being a highly invasive
species in many parts of the world, including
India, where it is known as the "Terror of
Bengal" for its ability to rapidly cover water
bodies, blocking waterways and depleting
oxygen.
• Lemna (duckweed) and Pistia (water lettuce)
are other types of floating aquatic plants but
belong to different genera.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. Assertion (A): Earthworms are useful to man.
Reason (R): Earthworms help in Nitrogen Fixation in
plants.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A

(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true. Earthworms are
extremely useful to humans, particularly in
agriculture and gardening. They are known as
"farmer's friends" because their burrowing
aerates the soil and improves water drainage.
They also break down organic matter, and
their castings (excrement) are a rich natural
fertilizer.
• Reason (R) is false. Earthworms do not
perform nitrogen fixation. Nitrogen fixation
is a biochemical process carried out by
specific bacteria (like Rhizobium in legume
roots or free-living bacteria in the soil).
• Earthworms contribute to soil fertility by
decomposing organic matter, which releases
existing nitrogen, but they do not fix new
nitrogen from the atmosphere.
• Since A is true and R is false, the correct
option is (c).
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. Acid rain is caused by the pollution of the
environment by:
(a) Carbon dioxide and nitrogen
(b) Carbon monoxide and carbon dioxide
(c) Ozone and carbon dioxide
(d) Nitrous oxide and sulphur dioxide
The correct option is (d).
• Acid rain is precipitation that is unusually
acidic.
• It is primarily caused by the emission of two
main pollutants into the atmosphere: sulfur
dioxide (SO₂) and oxides of nitrogen (NOx),
which includes nitrous oxide.
• These gases, released from burning fossil
fuels in power plants and vehicles, react with
water, oxygen, and other chemicals in the
atmosphere to form sulfuric acid (H₂SO₄) and
nitric acid (HNO₃).

•

•

These strong acids then dissolve in
rainwater, making it acidic. While carbon
dioxide does dissolve in water to form weak
carbonic acid, it is not the major cause of the
"acid rain" problem.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q7. Which one of the following is a breed of cattle
famous for high milk yield as well as draught
capacity?
(a) The Kankrej
(b) The Tharparkar
(c) The Haryana Breed
(d) The Rathi breed
The correct option is (b).
• This question asks for a dual-purpose breed
of cattle, good for both milk and work
(draught).
• The Tharparkar breed, originating from the
Thar Desert region, is a well-known dualpurpose Zebu breed. The cows are good milk
producers, and the bullocks are strong and
suitable for ploughing and carting.
• The Kankrej (a) and Haryana (c) are also
considered good dual-purpose breeds.
However, Tharparkar is particularly noted for
its combination of good milk yield and
hardiness.
• The Rathi breed (d) is primarily a milch (dairy)
breed.
• Among the given options, Tharparkar is a
classic example of a breed excelling in both
milk and draught capabilities.
• Hence, option (b) is a strong correct answer.
(Note: This can be subjective, as several
Indian breeds are dual-purpose).
Q8. Which of the following is not an element of the
bone?
(a) Calcium
(b) Phosphorus
(c) Carbon
(d) Oxygen
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•

•

•

•

This question is slightly ambiguous. Bone is a
complex tissue made of both mineral and
organic components.
The mineral component is primarily calcium
phosphate, in the form of hydroxyapatite
(Ca₁₀(PO₄)₆(OH)₂). This means bones are rich
in Calcium (Ca), Phosphorus (P), and Oxygen
(O).
The organic component of bone is mostly
collagen, which is a protein. Proteins are
organic molecules containing Carbon (C),
Hydrogen, Oxygen, and Nitrogen.
Therefore, all four elements listed are
present in bone tissue. However, if the
question intends the primary mineral
elements, Calcium and Phosphorus are the
defining mineral elements. The provided
answer key says (c), but that is problematic
because the organic matrix of bone is carbonbased.
There is an error or poor framing in the
question; if forced to choose under the
assumption the question seeks the "nonmineral" element, Carbon would represent
the organic part.
Hence, option (c) is given as per the provided
key, though the question is flawed.

Q9. Inspection and dissection of a body after death
in human beings for determination of the cause of
death is called:
(a) Autopsy
(b) Autotomy
(c) Autoecism
(d) Autograft
The correct option is (a).
• An autopsy, also known as a post-mortem
examination, is a surgical procedure that
involves the thorough examination of a corpse
to determine the cause, manner, and
mechanism of death.
• It is performed by a pathologist and is a
critical tool in forensic medicine and for
understanding disease processes.

•

•

Autotomy (b) is the self-amputation of a body
part (e.g., a lizard dropping its tail). Autoecism
(c) is a term in parasitology. An autograft (d)
is a tissue graft from one's own body.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1989

Q1. There are more anti-bacterial drugs than antiviral drugs because:
(a) Bacterial diseases are intra-cellular.
(b) Viral diseases are intra-cellular.
(c) Viruses are smaller than bacteria.
(d) Drugs can penetrate bacteria more effectively.
The correct option is (b).
• The fundamental challenge in developing
antiviral drugs is that viruses are obligate
intracellular parasites.
• This means viral diseases are intra-cellular;
the virus replicates inside the host’s own
cells, hijacking the cellular machinery.
• An effective antiviral drug must target the
virus without harming the host cell, which is
difficult.
• Bacteria are mostly extracellular and have
their own cell walls and metabolic pathways,
making them easier to target with antibiotics.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. A Dinosaur is:
(a) A reptile
(b) An amphibian
(c) A mammal
(d) A bird
The correct option is (a).
• Dinosaurs were a diverse group of reptiles
that ruled the earth for over 160 million
years.
• In the Linnaean classification, they belong to
the superorder Dinosauria under the class
Reptilia.
• While they were reptiles, modern birds
evolved from theropod dinosaurs.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. Chlorophyll contains:
(a) Beryllium
(b) Calcium
(c) Magnesium
(d) Strontium
The correct option is (c).
• Chlorophyll is the green pigment essential for
photosynthesis.
• It is a porphyrin compound with a central
magnesium (Mg) ion.
• This magnesium atom helps in absorbing light
energy during photosynthesis.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. The characteristic feature of a virus is that:
(a) It is made of fats.
(b) It multiplies only on dead animals.
(c) It multiplies only in hosts.
(d) It lacks chlorophyll.
The correct option is (c).
• A virus is an obligate intracellular parasite.
• It can replicate only inside living host cells
using their machinery.
• Outside a host, it remains inert and nonliving.
• Its structure is composed of nucleic acid (DNA
or RNA) and a protein coat.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. The fertility of soil can be improved by:
(a) Removing dead earthworms
(b) Adding dead earthworms
(c) Adding living earthworms
(d) Removing living earthworms and adding dead
earthworms
The correct option is (c).
• Living earthworms are called “farmer’s
friends” for their role in improving soil
fertility.
• Their burrowing aerates the soil and
enhances water drainage.
• Their castings are rich in nutrients available to
plants.

•

•

Dead earthworms only add minor organic
matter, but living ones sustain fertility
continuously.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. Which of the following is true about Bats?
1. Bats are mammals.
2. Bats have wings which are actually the
modified forelimbs.
3. Bats are nocturnal in habit.
The correct options are:
(a) 1, 2 & 3
(b) 2 & 3
(c) 1 & 3
(d) 1 & 2
The correct option is (a).
• Bats are mammals — they are warmblooded, give birth to live young, and
produce milk.
• Their wings are modified forelimbs with skin
stretched over elongated fingers.
• They are nocturnal, active at night and resting
during the day.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. A population of cells derived from a single parent
cell is called:
(a) Haploid
(b) Diploid
(c) Symmetrical cell
(d) Clone
The correct option is (d).
• A clone is a genetically identical group of cells
or individuals derived asexually from a single
parent.
• All daughter cells share the same genetic
makeup as the parent.
• Haploid and diploid refer to chromosome
numbers, not identical cell populations.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Which of the following plants is biennial?
(a) Banana
(b) Pineapple
(c) Jackfruit

(d) Carrot
The correct option is (d).
• A biennial plant completes its life cycle in two
years.
• In the first year, it grows leaves and roots; in
the second year, it flowers and produces
seeds.
• Carrot is a classic biennial — it stores food in
its taproot in year one and flowers in year
two.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. Which of the following are fed to cattle?
1. Sugarcane tops
2. Sugarcane leaves
3. Sugarcane bagasse
4. Sugarcane jaggery
The correct options are:
(a) 1, 2, 3 & 4
(b) 1 & 3
(c) 2 & 3
(d) 1, 2 & 3
The correct option is (a).
• Sugarcane tops and leaves provide roughage
for livestock.
• Bagasse is a fibrous residue used as lowquality filler in cattle feed.
• Jaggery and molasses provide energy and
sweetness to improve palatability.
• Hence, all four are useful as cattle feed
components.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q10. Which of the following has the highest calories
per gram?
(a) Proteins
(b) Fats
(c) Sugar
(d) Glucose
The correct option is (b).
• Fats provide about 9 calories per gram, the
highest among macronutrients.
• Proteins and carbohydrates provide about 4
calories per gram each.

•
•

Thus, fats are over twice as energy-dense as
proteins or sugars.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q11. Guinea pigs used in medical research are:
(a) Rodents
(b) Black swine
(c) Stocky and brown in colour
(d) Found in Asia only
The correct option is (a).
• Guinea pigs (Cavia porcellus) are rodents
belonging to the family Caviidae.
• They are not pigs and not from Guinea, but
native to South America.
• They are used widely in biomedical research
as model organisms.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q12. 'Lathyrism' is a crippling disease caused by:
(a) Tinned fish
(b) Chick pea and pulses
(c) Meat
(d) Adulterated oil
The correct option is (b).
• Lathyrism (neurolathyrism) causes paralysis
of the lower limbs.
• It is due to consumption of Lathyrus sativus
(grass pea), which contains a neurotoxin.
• The disease occurs during famines when grass
pea becomes a staple.
• The pulse is sometimes used to adulterate
other expensive pulses.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q13. If a bacteria culture doubles exponentially in
every 30 minutes, then after 5 hours they become:
(a) 10 fold
(b) 100 fold
(c) 1000 fold
(d) 10000 fold
The correct option is (c).
• Total time = 5 hours = 300 minutes.
• Doubling time = 30 minutes, so 10 doublings
occur.
• Growth factor = 2¹⁰ = 1024 ≈ 1000-fold.

•
•

Hence, the population becomes roughly 1000
times larger.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q14. Match the following:
List I
List II
A. Vitamin E – 1. Scurvy
B. Vitamin K – 2. Rickets
C. Vitamin C – 3. Sterility
D. Vitamin D – 4. Blood coagulation
Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
ABCD
(a) 1 3 4 2
(b) 3 4 1 2
(c) 4 3 1 2
(d) 2 1 3 4
The correct option is (b).
• Vitamin E: prevents sterility; acts as an
antioxidant.
• Vitamin K: essential for blood clotting.
• Vitamin C: deficiency causes scurvy (bleeding
gums, poor healing).
• Vitamin D: deficiency causes rickets (soft

Q15. Assertion (A): Insects are not affected by
pesticides.
Reason (R): Insects are killed by pesticides.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true but R is not the correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is false — the purpose of
pesticides is to kill insects.
• Reason (R) is true — pesticides are toxic to
pests, including insects.
• Hence, since A is false and R is true, the
correct choice is (d).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1990
Q1. Ringworm is caused by:
(a) Bacterium
(b) Fungus
(c) Virus
(d) Worm
The correct option is (b).
• Despite its name, ringworm is not caused by a
worm.
• It is a common skin infection caused by a type
of fungus called a dermatophyte.
• The infection gets its name from the
characteristic rash it often creates: a red,
itchy, circular or ring-shaped patch on the
skin. The fungi feed on keratin, the protein
found in skin, hair, and nails.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. Match List I with List II and select the correct
answer using the codes given below them:
List I (Vectors)

List II (Diseases)

A. Tsetse fly

1. Yellow fever

B. Aedes mosquito 2. Sleeping sickness
C. Water snail

3. Bilharzia

D. Sandflies

4. Typhoid

5. Kala-azar
Select the correct answer from the codes given
below:
ABCD
(a) 1 2 3 4
(b) 3 1 5 2
(c) 2 1 3 5
(d) 5 2 3 1
The correct option is (c).
• A. Tsetse fly: Is the biological vector for
trypanosomes, the protozoan parasites that
cause Sleeping sickness (African
Trypanosomiasis). So, A matches with 2.
• B. Aedes mosquito: Specifically Aedes
aegypti, is the primary vector for several viral
diseases, including Dengue fever, Zika,
Chikungunya, and Yellow fever. So, B matches
with 1.

•

C. Water snail: Certain species of freshwater
snails act as the intermediate host for
parasitic flatworms called schistosomes.
These parasites cause the disease
schistosomiasis, also known as Bilharzia. So, C
matches with 3.
• D. Sandflies: Are the vectors for the

as Kala-azar. So, D matches with 5.
• The correct matching is A-2, B-1, C-3, D-5.
(Note: The provided answer key says (c) but
has a typo, listing D as 2 instead of 5. Based
on the correct biological matches, A-2, B-1, C3, D-5 is the right sequence).
Hence, the intended answer is (c), despite the typo in
the key's explanation.
Q3. Consider the following statements regarding
bones in the human body:
1. They consist of 80% solid matter and 20% water.
2. Solid matter in the bones is made mainly of
cartilage.
3. Impregnation of salts including carbonates and
phosphates of lime imparts hardness to the bones.
4. In old age, the proportion of lime decreases and
bones become brittle and break easily.
Of these statements:
(a) 1, 2 and 3 are correct
(b) 2 and 4 are correct
(c) 2 and 3 are correct
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4 are correct
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1: Broadly correct. Bone is a
composite material. By weight, it is roughly
70% mineral (solid matter) and also contains
water, collagen, and other proteins. The
80/20 split is a reasonable approximation for
this question.
• Statement 2: Incorrect. The solid matter is
mainly a mineral matrix of calcium phosphate
(hydroxyapatite), not cartilage. The organic
part is mainly collagen, which provides
flexibility.

•

Statement 3: Correct. The hardness and
rigidity of bone come from the deposition of
mineral salts, primarily calcium
phosphate and calcium
carbonate (phosphates and carbonates of
lime).
• Statement 4: Correct. With ageing, there is
often a net loss of bone mineral density, a
condition known as osteoporosis. This
decrease in "lime" (calcium salts) makes the
bones weaker, more porous, and brittle,
increasing the risk of fractures.
• The provided answer key says (a), which
includes the incorrect statement 2. Based on a
strict analysis, statements 3 and 4 are
definitely correct. Statement 1 is a plausible
approximation. Statement 2 is definitively
wrong. There appears to be an error in the
question's options or the provided key. If we
assume "solid matter" in statement 2 refers to
the organic matrix, it's still collagen, not
cartilage. If we must choose, the combination
of correct statements is not available.
However, if we ignore the error in statement
2, the combination of 1, 3, and 4 might be
intended.
Given the flaws, a definitive correct answer from the
options is difficult. However, statements 3 and 4 are
the most accurate.
Q5. The human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) is a
living entity because it can:
(a) Excrete in human serum
(b) Disturb host cell respiration
(c) Undergo autoreproduction
(d) Move from one cell to another
The correct option is (c).
• The status of viruses as "living" is a topic of
scientific debate. They exist at the boundary
of living and non-living.
• The primary characteristic that viruses share
with living organisms is their ability
to reproduce.
• A virus cannot reproduce on its own; it must
infect a host cell and hijack its machinery to

make copies of itself. This process of making
copies of its genetic material and components
can be described as autoreproduction (selfreplication).
• This ability to replicate and evolve is the
strongest argument for considering viruses as
living entities. The other options describe
effects or movements, but reproduction is a
core definition of life.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. Mycorrhiza is a symbiotic association between:
(a) Bacteria and roots of higher plants
(b) Algae and roofs of orchids
(c) Fungi and roots of higher plants
(d) Protozoa and roots of higher plants
The correct option is (c).
• Mycorrhiza (from the Greek for "fungusroot") is a mutualistic symbiotic association
between a fungus and the roots of a vascular
plant.
• In this relationship, the fungus colonizes the
plant's root system, extending the plant's
reach for water and nutrients (especially
phosphorus) from the soil. In return, the plant
provides the fungus with carbohydrates
produced through photosynthesis.
• This association is extremely common and
vital for the health of most terrestrial
ecosystems.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

The correct option is (c).
• This question requires knowledge of the

• C. Soybean: Is famously high in protein, often

Q8. Consider the following statements regarding
blood pressure:
1. It is the pressure exerted by the blood on the walls
of any vessel.
2. It decreases in the arteries as the distance from
the heart increases.
3. It is lower in the capillaries than in the arteries. 4.
It is usually lower in women than in men.
Of these statements:
(a) 1 and 4 are correct
(b) 2, 3 and 4 are correct
(c) 1, 2 and 3 are correct
(d) 1, 2 and 4 are correct
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1: Incorrect. The term "blood
pressure" specifically refers to the pressure in
the large arteries, not "any vessel." Pressure
in veins, for example, is much lower.

•

Statement 2: Correct. As blood flows away
from the heart through the arterial system,
pressure is lost due to friction against the
vessel walls. Therefore, pressure decreases
with distance from the heart.
• Statement 3: Correct. The pressure drops
significantly as blood moves from the large
arteries into the vast network of smaller
arterioles and then into the capillaries. So,
pressure is much lower in capillaries than in
arteries.
• Statement 4: Correct. On average, before
menopause, adult women tend to have
slightly lower blood pressure than men of the
same age.
• Since statements 2, 3, and 4 are correct, the
answer is (b).
• The provided answer key says (d), which
includes the imprecise statement 1 and
excludes the correct statement 3.
Hence, option (b) is the most accurate choice.
Q9. Match List I with List II and select the correct
answer using the codes given below them:
List I (Plant tissue) List II (Function)
A. Sclerenchyma 1. Conduction of water
B. Xylem

2. Transport of food

C. Phloem

3. Mechanical strength

D. Meristem

4. Cell division

5. Carbon assimilation
Select the correct answer from the codes given
below:
ABCD
(a) 3 1 2 4
(b) 1 4 3 5
(c) 4 2 5 3
(d) 2 5 4 1
The correct option is (a).
• A. Sclerenchyma: Is a type of ground tissue
with thick, lignified secondary walls. Its
primary function is to provide mechanical
strength and support to the plant. So, A
matches with 3.

•

B. Xylem: Is the primary water-conducting
vascular tissue in plants. It is responsible for
the conduction of water and minerals from
the roots to the rest of the plant. So, B
matches with 1.
• C. Phloem: Is the other vascular tissue,
responsible for the transport of food (sugars
produced during photosynthesis) from the
leaves to other parts of the plant where it is
needed. So, C matches with 2.
• D. Meristem: Is a region of undifferentiated
plant cells that are capable of active cell
division. Meristematic tissues are found at
the growing tips of roots and shoots and are
responsible for plant growth. So, D matches
with 4.
• The correct matching is A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q10. Consider the following statements regarding
hemoglobin: 1. It contains iron. 2. It is a carrier of
oxygen in the blood. 3. It imparts red colour to the
blood. 4. It provides immunity against certain
diseases.
Of these statements:
(a) 1, 2 and 4 are correct
(b) 1, 2 and 3 are correct
(c) 1, 3 and 4 are correct
(d) 2, 3 and 4 are correct
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is correct. Hemoglobin is a
protein that contains a non-protein
component called heme. At the center of each
heme group is an atom of iron (Fe), which is
what actually binds to oxygen.
• Statement 2 is correct. The primary function
of hemoglobin, which is located in red blood
cells, is to act as the carrier of oxygen,
transporting it from the lungs to the body's
tissues.
• Statement 3 is correct. The interaction of iron
and oxygen within the heme group is what
gives blood its characteristic red colour.
• Statement 4 is incorrect. Immunity against
diseases is the function of the immune

system, primarily the white blood cells and
antibodies. Hemoglobin has no role in
immunity.
• Since statements 1, 2, and 3 are correct, the
answer is (b).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q11. When compared to mammals, birds are less
sensitive to temporary deprivation of water,
because:
(a) The water requirement per unit weight is less in
birds than in mammals.
(b) Absorption of water from the gut is more
efficient in birds than in mammals.
(c) Urea excretion in mammals entails a greater loss
of water than that in birds.
(d) None of these
The correct option is (c).
• This question is about water conservation
strategies in different animals.
• Mammals excrete their nitrogenous waste
primarily in the form of urea. Urea is highly
soluble in water and requires a significant
amount of water to be flushed out of the
body in urine. This results in a substantial
water loss.
• Birds, on the other hand, excrete their
nitrogenous waste primarily as uric acid. Uric
acid is largely insoluble in water and is
excreted as a semi-solid white paste (the
white part of bird droppings). This method of
excretion is extremely water-efficient.
• Because urea excretion in mammals entails a
greater loss of water than uric acid excretion
in birds, birds are much better at conserving
water and are therefore less sensitive to
temporary water deprivation.
• The provided answer key says (a), which may
also be true to some extent, but (c) describes
the specific physiological mechanism that
makes birds so water-efficient.
Hence, option (c) is the most precise and explanatory
answer.

Q12. The main difference between raw soybeans and
cooked soybeans is in respect of:
(a) Amino acid composition
(b) Biological value of proteins
(c) Protein content
(d) Calorific value
The correct option is (b).
• Raw soybeans contain several "antinutritional factors," most notably trypsin
inhibitors. Trypsin is a key digestive enzyme
that breaks down proteins in the small
intestine.
• These inhibitors block the action of trypsin,
which means the protein in the raw soybeans
cannot be properly digested and absorbed by
the body.
• Cooking destroys these trypsin inhibitors.
• Therefore, while the total protein content (c)
and amino acid composition (a) are essentially
the same, the biological value of the
proteins—a measure of how efficiently the
body can absorb and utilize them—is
dramatically higher in cooked soybeans
compared to raw soybeans. Cooking makes
the protein bioavailable.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1991
Q1. Rabies is caused by a:
(a) Virus and it affects the nervous system
(b) Virus and it affects the cardiovascular system
(c) Bacteria and it affects the nervous system
(d) Bacteria and it affects the cardiovascular system
The correct option is (a).
• Rabies is an infectious disease caused by the
Rabies virus.
• The virus is transmitted through the saliva of
infected animals, typically via a bite.
• Once in the body, it travels along the nerves
to the central nervous system (brain and
spinal cord).
• It causes encephalitis, a severe and almost
always fatal inflammation of the brain.

•

Therefore, it is a virus that affects the nervous
system.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

•

Q2. The thermoregulatory centre of the human body
is:
(a) Pituitary
(b) Skin
(c) Hypothalamus
(d) Thyroid
The correct option is (c).
• Thermoregulation maintains the body’s core
internal temperature at about 37°C (98.6°F).
• The control centre is the hypothalamus,
acting as the body’s thermostat.
• It receives temperature data from blood and
skin receptors.
• It triggers sweating (to cool down) or
shivering (to warm up) to restore balance.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

•

Q3. Where does the exchange of gases (O₂ and CO₂)
take place in our lungs?
(a) Alveoli
(b) Bronchi
(c) Pleura
(d) Trachea
The correct option is (a).
• The lungs’ primary function is gas exchange.
• Air reaches the alveoli, tiny balloon-like air
sacs at the end of bronchioles.
• Alveoli are surrounded by capillaries with
thin walls (one cell thick).
• Oxygen diffuses into blood, and carbon
dioxide diffuses out into the alveoli.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. The resinous substance, shellac is obtained from:
(a) Crude oil
(b) The bark of a tree
(c) An insect
(d) Distillation of wood
The correct option is (c).
• Shellac is a natural resin derived from the
female lac insect (Kerria lacca).

•

The insects feed on tree sap and secrete a
resinous coating on branches.
The resin (sticklac) is processed into refined
shellac flakes.
The shellac is an insect secretion, not a tree
product.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. Which one of the following is associated with
AIDS?
(a) GIFT
(b) HIV
(c) IVF
(d) MAC
The correct option is (b).
• AIDS (Acquired Immunodeficiency
Syndrome) is caused by HIV (Human
Immunodeficiency Virus).
• HIV attacks CD4 T-helper cells, weakening the
immune system.
• GIFT and IVF are fertility treatments; MAC is
a bacterial infection in AIDS patients.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. Mitochondrial DNA in an individual comes from:
(a) Male parent
(b) Female parent
(c) Both parents
(d) Neither of the parents
The correct option is (b).
• Mitochondria contain their own DNA
(mtDNA), separate from nuclear DNA.
• During fertilization, the sperm’s
mitochondria are destroyed.
• The ovum (egg) contributes all mitochondria
to the zygote.
• Thus, mtDNA is maternally inherited (from
the mother).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Which one of the following diseases is least
infectious?
(a) Leprosy
(b) Tuberculosis
(c) Conjunctivitis

(d) Hepatitis
The correct option is (a).
• Leprosy (Hansen’s disease) is caused by
Mycobacterium leprae.
• It requires prolonged, close contact for
transmission.
• Over 95% of people are naturally immune.
• TB, conjunctivitis, and hepatitis spread more
easily.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q9. Which one of the following oils has the

(a) Soyabean oil

(c) Coconut oil

Cottonseed oil is moderate, coconut oil is

Q10. Protective action against harmful household

(a) Infrasonic waves
(b) Microwaves
(c) Ultrasonic waves
(d) Low frequency radio waves
The correct option is (c).
• Ultrasonic waves are high-frequency sounds
beyond human hearing.
• Pest control devices emit these to repel
insects and rodents.
• The sound is inaudible to humans but
irritating to pests.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q14. A significantly higher level of urea in the blood
is the indication of:
(a) Liver damage
(b) Kidney damage
(c) Heart attack
(d) Spleen damage
The correct option is (b).

kidneys.
• Kidney damage reduces filtration, causing
urea buildup (uremia).
• High Blood Urea Nitrogen (BUN) signals renal
failure.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q15. The medicines which are used to treat diseases

(d) Allergens
The correct option is (b).

body.

•
•

Antihistamines block histamine and relieve
itching, sneezing, hives.
Analgesics reduce pain; antipyretics lower
fever; allergens trigger allergies.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q16. Malaria fever is caused:
(a) Immediately after mosquito bite
(b) Upon disintegration and bursting of red blood cells
(c) When the malaria causing germs reach the liver
(d) Upon release of toxins into blood
The correct option is (b).
• Plasmodium parasite invades red blood cells
after liver stage.
• After 48–72 hours, cells burst, releasing
parasites and toxins.
• This causes fever and chills in cycles.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q17. Pyrilla is a:

content.
• It produces honeydew, leading to sooty mold
growth.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1992
Q1. Which one of the following plants can fix
nitrogen from the air?
(a) Rice
(b) Wheat
(c) Pea
(d) Maize
The correct option is (c).
• Nitrogen fixation converts atmospheric
nitrogen (N₂) into ammonia, a usable form for
plants.
• This is done by nitrogen-fixing bacteria that
form symbiotic relationships with plants.
• Such bacteria live in root nodules of legume
family plants like pea.
• Rice, wheat, and maize (grasses) lack this
symbiosis and depend on soil nitrogen.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. Chlorophyll is a naturally occurring chelate
compound in which the central metal is:
(a) Magnesium
(b) Copper
(c) Calcium
(d) Iron
The correct option is (a).
• Chlorophyll is a porphyrin ring structure, a
type of chelate compound.
• It has a central magnesium (Mg) ion bonded
to a large organic ligand.
• This Mg ion is essential for light absorption in
photosynthesis.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Match List I with List II and select the correct
answer using the codes given below the lists:
List I

List II

A. Islets of Langerhans 4. Insulin
B. Pituitary gland

3. Growth hormone

C. Thyroid gland

1. Calcitonin

D. Adrenal gland

2. Epinephrine

The correct option is (b).
• Islets of Langerhans → produce Insulin in the
pancreas.
• Pituitary gland → secretes Growth hormone
(GH), regulating growth and metabolism.
• Thyroid gland → produces Calcitonin,
maintaining blood calcium levels.
• Adrenal gland → secretes Epinephrine
(adrenaline), initiating the fight-or-flight
response.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. The two richest known sources of edible protein
are:
(a) Meat and eggs
(b) Some algae and other micro-organisms
(c) Soyabean and groundnut
(d) Milk and leafy vegetables
The correct option is (c).
• Soybean has 36–40% protein and is a
complete plant protein.

•
•

•

Groundnut (peanut) contains around 25%
protein.
Meat and eggs are high in quality, but
soybean and groundnut are higher in
percentage.
Algae and milk have lower protein
concentration as edible sources.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. Bats are able to fly in the dark since their wings
produce:
(a) Infrared rays
(b) Ultrasonic waves
(c) Sound waves
(d) Ultraviolet rays
The correct option is (b).
• Bats use echolocation, a form of biological
sonar.
• They emit ultrasonic waves (above 20 kHz)
from the mouth or nose.
• These waves bounce off objects and return as
echoes, helping navigation in darkness.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. Which one of the following diseases is caused
due to defective genes?
(a) Ulcer
(b) Haemophilia
(c) Diabetes
(d) Cancer
The correct option is (b).
• Haemophilia is a genetic disorder caused by a
mutation in the X-linked gene for a clotting
factor.
• It leads to impaired blood clotting and
excessive bleeding.
• Ulcers result from bacterial infection (H.
pylori).
• Diabetes and cancer have genetic and
environmental components, but are not
single-gene defects.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Consider the Food Pyramid given below showing
the interdependence of plants and animals in the

food chain. Which one of the following in the
pyramid is most delicately balanced in the chain?
(a) Herbivore
(b) Plant
(c) Top Carnivore
(d) Small Carnivore

The correct option is (c).
• In an ecological pyramid, energy decreases at
each trophic level.
• Top carnivores occupy the highest level with
least energy and population.
• Their survival depends on the stability of all
levels below (plants → herbivores → small
carnivores).
• Thus, they are the most delicately balanced
group.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. Consider the figure given below:

The above figure contains calories required per day
by boys and girls. It can be inferred from the graph
that in general calorie requirement for boys and
girls:
(a) Reaches a peak value at about the same age
(b) Increases nearly at the same rate till the age of 13

(c) Is quite different at all ages
(d) Is similar till the age of 17
The correct option is (b).
• Up to about age 13, boys and girls show a
similar calorie requirement trend.
• After puberty, boys’ calorie needs rise faster
due to muscle growth.
• Girls’ needs level off earlier due to earlier
puberty onset.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q9. Man cannot digest cellulose whereas cows can
do so because:
(a) Their gut contains certain bacteria capable of
digesting cellulose
(b) They have a many-chambered stomach
(c) They have efficient grinding molars
(d) They produce an enzyme cellulase which can
digest cellulose
The correct option is (a).
• Cellulose is a complex carbohydrate with βacetal bonds that humans cannot digest.
• Cows’ rumen hosts bacteria and protozoa
producing cellulase enzyme.
• These microorganisms break cellulose into
digestible compounds.
• The many-chambered stomach provides
space, but the bacteria perform digestion.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1993
Q1. A camel uses its hump for:
(a) storing water
(b) storing fat
(c) balancing the body during walking in desert sand
(d) temperature regulation
The correct option is (b).
• It is a common misconception that a camel's
hump is used for storing water.
• In reality, the hump is a large reservoir of
adipose tissue, meaning it is used for storing
fat.
• This concentrated fat storage serves two main
purposes.

•

•

•

First, it acts as a vital energy reserve for the
camel during long periods when food is
scarce.
Second, by localizing the fat in the hump
rather than distributing it under the skin all
over the body, it minimizes insulation and
helps the camel stay cool in the hot desert
climate.
When this fat is metabolized, it does produce
a small amount of metabolic water, but its
primary function is fat storage.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. The theory of 'jumping genes' was propounded
by:
(a) Gregor Johann Mendel
(b) Thomas Hunt Morgan
(c) Barbara McClintock
(d) Watson and Crick
The correct option is (c).
• The discovery of "jumping genes," more
formally known as transposable elements
(TEs), was made by the American geneticist
Barbara McClintock.
• Through her meticulous research on maize
(corn) in the 1940s and 1950s, she observed
that certain genetic elements could move
from one location on a chromosome to
another.
• Her work was initially met with skepticism but
was eventually recognized as a
groundbreaking discovery, for which she was
awarded the Nobel Prize in Physiology or
Medicine in 1983.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Sucrose content in sugarcane decreases:
(a) if high rainfall occurs during the period of growth
of the plant
(b) if frost occurs during the period of ripening
(c) if there is fluctuation in temperature during the
period of growth of the plant
(d) if there is high temperature during the time of
ripening
The correct option is (b).

•
•

•
•

•

•

•

Sugarcane accumulates sucrose in its stalk as
it matures.
The ideal conditions for ripening involve
bright sunny days and cool nights, which
promote sugar storage and reduce vegetative
growth.
If frost occurs during the period of ripening, it
damages the plant's cells.
This damage can trigger the conversion of
stored sucrose into glucose and fructose, a
process called inversion.
This significantly reduces the purity and
overall sucrose content of the cane, making it
less suitable for sugar production.
High temperatures during ripening can also
reduce sucrose by increasing respiration,
where the plant uses up stored sugar for
energy.
However, frost has a more direct and
damaging effect on the stored sucrose itself.
Hence, option (b) is the most accurate
answer.

Q4. Which one of the following is a "man-made"
cereal NOT found in nature?
(a) Dwarf wheat
(b) Hybrid maize
(c) Triticale
(d) Soya bean
The correct option is (c).
• Triticale is a hybrid cereal that was created by
humans through intergeneric hybridization.
• It is a cross between wheat (Triticum) and rye
(Secale).
• The goal was to combine the high yield and
quality of wheat with the disease resistance
and hardiness of rye.
• This cross does not occur naturally.
• It was first bred in laboratories in the late
19th century.
• Dwarf wheat and hybrid maize are varieties
developed through selective breeding and
hybridization within a species, but the species
themselves are natural.

•

Soya bean is a natural legume species.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. Sugarcane, sugarbeet, sweet pea, chickpea,
pigeon pea and French bean belong to:
(a) two plant families
(b) three plant families
(c) four plant families
(d) five plant families
The correct option is (b).
• Sugarcane: Belongs to the grass family,
Poaceae.
• Sugarbeet: Belongs to the amaranth family,
Amaranthaceae (formerly Chenopodiaceae).
• Sweet pea, chickpea, pigeon pea, and French
bean: These are all legumes and belong to the
Fabaceae (Leguminosae) family.
• Therefore, the plants listed belong to a total
of three plant families: Poaceae,
Amaranthaceae, and Fabaceae.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. A fertile soil, suitable for growing common crops,
is likely to have a pH value of:
(a) three
(b) four
(c) six to seven
(d) nine to ten
The correct option is (c).
• Soil pH measures acidity or alkalinity and
affects nutrient availability.
• Most common agricultural crops thrive in
slightly acidic to neutral soil.
• The optimal pH range for most plant nutrients
is between 6.0 and 7.0.
• A pH of three or four is extremely acidic; nine
or ten is extremely alkaline, both toxic for
most crops.
• Therefore, a pH value of six to seven indicates
a fertile soil.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. Poison glands of snakes are homologous to:
(a) electric organs of fishes
(b) stings of rays

(c) sebaceous glands of mammals
(d) salivary glands of vertebrates
The correct option is (d).
• Homologous structures share a common
evolutionary origin even if functions differ.
• The venom glands of snakes are a type of
modified salivary gland.
• Specifically, they are homologous to the
parotid salivary gland found in other
vertebrates.
• In snakes, this gland evolved to produce a
complex mixture of toxins (venom) for prey
subduing and defense, instead of just
digestive enzymes.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

A. Wingless insect: Silverfish are small,

Q9. Three communicable diseases prevalent in
developing countries caused by unsafe drinking
water and bad sanitation are:
(a) acute diarrhoea, cancer and gout
(b) malaria, acute diarrhoea and schistosomiasis
(c) onchocerciasis, leukaemia and arthritis
(d) rheumatism, malaria and AIDS
The correct option is (b).
• This question asks for communicable
(infectious) diseases linked to poor water and
sanitation.
• Acute diarrhoea: Caused by pathogens like E.
coli, Vibrio cholerae, and Rotavirus,
transmitted through contaminated water and
food.
• Malaria: Transmitted by mosquitoes that
often breed in stagnant water, common
where sanitation is poor.
• Schistosomiasis (Bilharzia): A parasitic
disease whose life cycle involves freshwater
snails; infection occurs via contact with
contaminated freshwater.
• Therefore, malaria, acute diarrhoea, and
schistosomiasis are all linked to water and
sanitation issues.
• Other options list non-communicable
diseases (cancer, gout, leukaemia, arthritis,
rheumatism) or diseases not transmitted by

water (AIDS).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
