UPSC PYQs - 1994

•

Q1. The heart of a human embryo starts beating:
(a) in the first week of its development
(b) in the third week of its development
(c) in the fourth week of its development
(d) in the sixth week of its development
The correct option is (c).
• The development of the human heart is a
rapid and early process.
• The embryonic heart begins to form and beat
very early in gestation. The first heartbeat
occurs at approximately 22-23 days after
conception.
• Since development is measured in weeks, this
falls squarely in the fourth week of its
development (weeks are counted from day 1,
so week 1 is days 1-7, week 2 is days 8-14,
week 3 is days 15-21, and week 4 is days 2228).
• The provided answer key says (b), which is
slightly too early. The heart begins to form in
the third week, but the first beat typically
occurs at the beginning of the fourth week.
• Hence, option (c) is the most precise answer.
Q2. If a father has blood group A and a mother has
blood group O, then which one of the following
blood groups may be found in their son?
(a) B
(b) AB
(c) O
(d) B, AB, or O
The correct option is (c).
• Let's look at the genetics of blood types.
Blood type is determined by alleles. 'A' and 'B'
are dominant, and 'O' is recessive.
• A person with blood group A can have the
genotype AA or AO.
• A person with blood group O must have the
genotype OO.
• Case 1: Father is AO, Mother is OO.

•
•

•

•
•

The child can inherit 'A' from the father and
'O' from the mother (genotype AO, blood type
A).
The child can inherit 'O' from the father and
'O' from the mother (genotype OO, blood
type O).
Case 2: Father is AA, Mother is OO.
The child must inherit 'A' from the father and
'O' from the mother (genotype AO, blood type
A).
In either case, the child can only have blood
group A or blood group O. They cannot have B
or AB.
From the options provided, O is a possible
blood group for the son.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q3. In the case of a 'Test-tube baby':
(a) fertilization takes place inside the test-tube
(b) development of the baby takes place inside the
test-tube
(c) fertilization takes place outside the mother's body
(d) unfertilized egg develops inside the test-tube
The correct option is (c).
• The term "test-tube baby" is a non-scientific
term for a baby conceived via In Vitro
Fertilization (IVF).
• "In vitro" is Latin for "in glass." The core of
the IVF process is that fertilization takes place
outside the mother's body in a laboratory
setting (e.g., in a petri dish, not literally a test
tube).
• Eggs are retrieved from the mother's ovaries
and are combined with sperm from the father
in the lab.
• Once fertilization occurs and the resulting
embryo begins to develop, it is then
transferred into the mother's uterus to
continue its development.
• Therefore, statement (c) is the most accurate
and encompassing description. Statement (a)
is also true, but (c) is more general and
correct. The development of the baby (b)
does not happen in a test tube.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. Match List I with List II and select the correct
answer from the codes given below the lists:
List I (Crops) — List II (Crop Pests)
A. Rice — 1. Aphid
B. Wheat — 2. Gundhi bug
C. Sugarcane — 3. Greasy cutworm
D. Gram — 4. Top shoot borer moth
5. Bollworm
Codes:
ABCD
(a) 2 3 4 5
(b) 3 1 2 4
(c) 2 1 4 3
(d) 5 4 1 3
The correct option is (c).
• A. Rice: Is notoriously affected by the Gundhi
bug (Leptocorisa acuta), which sucks the sap
from developing grains, causing them to be
empty or "chaffy." So, A matches with 2.
• B. Wheat: Is a host for many pests, including
Aphids, which suck sap from the plant,
weakening it and potentially transmitting
viruses. So, B matches with 1.
• C. Sugarcane: A major pest for this crop is the
Top shoot borer moth (Scirpophaga
excerptalis), whose larvae bore into the
growing point of the cane, killing it. So, C
matches with 4.
• D. Gram (Chickpea): Is often attacked by
various types of cutworms, including the
Greasy cutworm (Agrotis ipsilon), which cuts
young seedlings at the soil level. It is also
attacked by the gram pod borer (bollworm).
However, cutworm is a good match. So, D
matches with 3.
• The correct matching is A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. What is the correct sequence of the following in
a heart attack?
1. Narrowing of the inner orifice of the vessel
2. 'Plaque' from fibrous tissue and high
cholesterol
3. Inadequate supply of blood and oxygen

4. Clots of blood carried into the coronary
arteries
Choose the answer from the codes given below:
(a) 1, 2, 3, 4
(b) 2, 4, 1, 3
(c) 2, 3, 1, 4
(d) 4, 2, 1, 3
The correct option is (b).
• Let's trace the progression of events leading
to a heart attack (myocardial infarction).
• Step 1 (Long-term process): The process
begins with atherosclerosis, which is the
buildup of 'Plaque' from fibrous tissue and
high cholesterol on the inner walls of the
coronary arteries. So, the sequence starts
with 2.
• Step 2 (The acute event): A heart attack
typically occurs when one of these plaques
ruptures. The body's response to this rupture
is to form a blood clot at the site to try and
heal the vessel wall. This clot can grow large
enough to block the artery. So, the next step
is 4. (Note: The wording "carried into" is
slightly inaccurate; the clot usually forms at
the site of the plaque).
• Step 3 (The immediate result): The formation
of the clot leads to a severe narrowing or
complete blockage of the inner orifice of the
vessel. So, the next step is 1.
• Step 4 (The consequence): This blockage
prevents blood from flowing to the section of
the heart muscle supplied by that artery,
leading to an inadequate supply of blood and
oxygen. This is ischemia, and if it lasts long
enough, the heart muscle begins to die, which
is the heart attack itself. So, the final step is 3.
• The correct sequence is 2 → 4 → 1 → 3.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. Which of the following are characteristics shown
by a breastfed baby when compared to a bottle-fed
baby?
1. It is less obese.
2. It shows a greater capacity to resist diseases.
3. It gets more vitamins and proteins.

4. Its growth in height is abnormal.
Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 1, 2 and 4
(c) 1, 3 and 4
(d) 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. Studies have shown a
correlation between breastfeeding and a
lower risk of childhood obesity. Breastfed
infants may be better at self-regulating their
intake.
• Statement 2 is correct. Breast milk is rich in
antibodies (especially colostrum, the first
milk) and other immune factors that are
passed from the mother to the baby. This
provides passive immunity and helps the
baby resist diseases and infections.
• Statement 3 is correct. Breast milk is
considered the perfect food for a baby. It
contains a unique and ideal balance of
vitamins, proteins, fats, and other nutrients
that are easily digestible and perfectly suited
for a baby's developmental needs. While
formula is designed to be nutritious, breast
milk is the gold standard.
• Statement 4 is incorrect. Breastfeeding
promotes healthy, normal growth. There is
no evidence that it causes "abnormal" growth
in height.
• Since statements 1, 2, and 3 are correct, the
answer is (a).
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. Which of the following is/are used as
biofertilizers?
1. Azolla
2. Blue-green algae
3. Alfalfa
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 2 alone
(b) 1 and 2
(c) 1 and 3

(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Biofertilizers are substances that contain
living microorganisms which, when applied to
seeds, plant surfaces, or soil, colonize the
rhizosphere or the interior of the plant and
promote growth by increasing the supply or
availability of primary nutrients to the host
plant.
• 1. Azolla: Is an aquatic fern that has a
symbiotic relationship with a nitrogen-fixing
cyanobacterium (Anabaena). When
incorporated into soil (especially in rice
paddies), it acts as an excellent nitrogen-rich
biofertilizer.
• 2. Blue-green algae (Cyanobacteria): Many
free-living species of blue-green algae are
capable of fixing atmospheric nitrogen and
are used as biofertilizers, particularly in rice
cultivation.
• 3. Alfalfa: Is a legume. While not a
biofertilizer itself, it is a "green manure" crop.
It fixes nitrogen via its symbiotic relationship
with Rhizobium bacteria. When the alfalfa
crop is ploughed back into the soil, it
decomposes and enriches the soil with
nitrogen and organic matter, effectively
acting as a biofertilizer for the next crop.
• Since all three are used to enrich the soil with
biologically fixed nitrogen, all can be
considered part of the biofertilizer system.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Even though an animal is fed with a
carbohydrate-rich diet, its blood sugar concentration
tends to remain constant. This is on account of the
fact that in the case of an animal:
(a) hormones of pituitary glands control metabolic
process
(b) hormones of pancreas cause such a condition
(c) blood sugar is readily absorbed by liver
(d) glucose undergoes autolysis
The correct option is (b).
• The regulation of blood sugar (glucose) levels
is a critical homeostatic process.

•

•
•

•

•

•

After a carbohydrate-rich meal, glucose is
absorbed into the bloodstream, causing blood
sugar levels to rise.
In response to this rise, the beta cells in the
pancreas secrete the hormone insulin.
Insulin signals the body's cells (especially in
the liver, muscle, and fat tissue) to take up
glucose from the blood to be used for energy
or stored for later. The liver stores excess
glucose as glycogen.
This process effectively lowers blood sugar
back to its normal range. Conversely, if blood
sugar drops too low, the pancreas secretes
another hormone, glucagon, which tells the
liver to release stored glucose.
Therefore, the hormones of the pancreas
(insulin and glucagon) are directly responsible
for maintaining a constant blood sugar
concentration.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q9. Which one of the following crops enriches the
nitrogen content in the soil?
(a) Potato
(b) Sorghum
(c) Sunflower
(d) Pea
The correct option is (d).
• Crops that enrich the soil with nitrogen are
typically those that can "fix" nitrogen from
the atmosphere.
• This ability is characteristic of plants in the
legume family, which form a symbiotic
relationship with nitrogen-fixing bacteria
(Rhizobia) in their root nodules.
• The pea plant is a legume. By fixing
atmospheric nitrogen, it not only provides for
its own needs but also leaves residual
nitrogen in the soil when the plant
decomposes, enriching it for subsequent
crops.
• Potato, sorghum, and sunflower are not
legumes and do not fix nitrogen; they deplete
nitrogen from the soil as they grow.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q10. In the balanced dietaries for a day of an adult
working man, the weights of carbohydrate food and
total protein food (both superior and inferior) should
be in the order of:
(a) 600 gm and 420 gm respectively
(b) 600 gm and 600 gm respectively
(c) 420 gm and 600 gm respectively
(d) 420 gm and 420 gm respectively
The correct option is (c).
• In a balanced dietaries of a day of an adult
working man, weights of carbohydrate food
and total protein food should be in order of
600gm and 400gm respectively.
Q11. Which one of the following is caused by the
expression of a recessive gene present on the sex
chromosome?
(a) Rheumatism
(b) Nervous shock
(c) Muscular dystrophy
(d) Cerebral hemorrhage
The correct option is (c).
• A disease caused by a recessive gene on a sex
chromosome is known as a sex-linked
recessive disorder. The most common type is
X-linked recessive.
• Duchenne Muscular Dystrophy is a wellknown and severe X-linked recessive genetic
disorder. It is caused by a mutation in the
gene for the protein dystrophin. Because it is
X-linked recessive, it almost exclusively
affects males.
• Rheumatism (a) is a general term for joint
problems. Nervous shock (b) is a physiological
state. Cerebral hemorrhage (d) is a type of
stroke. None of these are simple sex-linked
genetic disorders.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q12. Which one of the following animals does not
store water in the intestine?
(a) Moloch
(b) Camel
(c) Zebra

(d) Uromastix
The correct option is (b).
• This question addresses a common
misconception.
• It was once believed that camels store water
in their stomach or intestines, but this is not
true. As established earlier, their humps store
fat, not water. They conserve water very
efficiently through physiological means
(producing dry feces and concentrated urine,
and tolerating dehydration), but they do not
have a specific water storage organ in their
gut.
• Other desert animals have developed
different strategies. The Moloch (a lizard) can
collect dew on its skin and channel it to its
mouth. The Uromastix (another lizard) can
store water in its bladder. Zebras need to
drink water regularly.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q13. The only anthropoid ape to be found in India is
the:
(a) hanuman monkey
(b) lion-tailed macaque of Western Ghats
(c) slow loris of Assam
(d) white-browed gibbon of Assam
The correct option is (d).
• "Anthropoid ape" refers to the superfamily
Hominoidea, which includes gibbons (lesser
apes) and the great apes (orangutans, gorillas,
chimpanzees, and humans).
• Monkeys (like the hanuman monkey and
macaque) and prosimians (like the slow loris)
are primates, but they are not apes.
• The only ape species found naturally in India
is the Hoolock Gibbon.
• The Hoolock Gibbon is also known as the
white-browed gibbon due to the distinct
white bands of hair above its eyes. It is found
in the forests of Northeast India, particularly
Assam.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q14. The highest milk producing breed of goat in
India is:
(a) Barbari
(b) Jamunapari
(c) Black Bengal
(d) Beetal
The correct option is (b).
• This question asks to identify the best dairy
goat breed in India based on milk yield.
• The Jamunapari (Jamnapari) breed,
originating from the Etawah district of Uttar
Pradesh, is renowned for being the best and
highest milk-producing goat breed in India. It
is a large-sized goat known for its good milk
yield and is often used for dual purposes
(milk and meat).
• The Beetal (d) is also a good dairy breed. The
Barbari (a) is a smaller, more prolific breed.
The Black Bengal (c) is primarily known for its
high-quality meat and skin, not for high milk
yield.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q15. Assertion (A): A lock of Einstein's hair, if
scientists could locate it and extract its DNA, could
help in producing another Einstein, by cloning.
Reason (R): The DNA extracted from the cell of an
embryo at an early stage of development can be
transferred to individual eggs which in turn can be
implanted into the uterus of a surrogate mother to
give birth to an identical offspring.
In the context of the above two statements, which
one of the following is correct?
(a) Both A and R are true, and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true, but R is not a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true, but R is false
(d) A is false, but R is true
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is false. This statement
oversimplifies what makes a person who they
are. While cloning could theoretically produce
a genetically identical individual, "Einstein"
was the product of far more than just his

•

•
•

•

genes. His intelligence, creativity, and
contributions were shaped by his unique
upbringing, education, environment,
experiences, and the historical context in
which he lived. A genetic clone would not be
"another Einstein." Furthermore, cloning a
mammal from a non-living source like a lock
of hair is currently beyond our technological
capabilities.
Reason (R) is true. This statement accurately
describes the general process of reproductive
cloning (somatic cell nuclear transfer) and
embryo splitting. The process involves
transferring the nucleus (containing DNA)
from a somatic cell (or using an early-stage
embryo) into an enucleated egg, which is
then stimulated to develop and implanted
into a surrogate mother.
Since the Assertion is false and the Reason is
true, the correct option is (d).
The provided answer key says (a), which is
incorrect. R does not explain A, and A is a
false premise.
Hence, option (d) is the correct answer.

Q16. Which one of the following vitamins is
considered to be a hormone?
(a) A
(b) B
(c) C
(d) D
The correct option is (d).
• A vitamin is an essential nutrient that the
body cannot synthesize and must obtain from
the diet. A hormone is a signaling molecule
produced by the body that regulates
physiology and behavior.
• Vitamin D is unique because it functions as a
hormone, and the body can synthesize it.
• When the skin is exposed to sunlight, it
produces a precursor that is then converted
by the liver and kidneys into the active form
of Vitamin D, called calcitriol.
• Calcitriol is a steroid hormone that circulates
in the blood and regulates calcium and

•

phosphate levels by acting on the intestines,
kidneys, and bones. Because the body can
make it and it acts as a signaling molecule, it
fits the definition of a hormone.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q17. Rennin and lactase, the enzymes required to
digest milk, disappear in the human body by the age
of:
(a) two
(b) three
(c) five
(d) eight
The correct option is (b).
• Rennin (or chymosin) is an enzyme found in
the stomachs of infant mammals that curdles
milk, allowing it to stay in the stomach longer
for better digestion. Its production
significantly decreases after infancy.
• Lactase is the enzyme that digests lactose, the
sugar in milk. In most human populations
(with the notable exception of Northern
Europeans), the production of lactase
dramatically decreases after weaning,
typically between the ages of 2 and 5. This
leads to lactose intolerance in adulthood.
• Given the options, the age of three is a
reasonable average age by which the levels of
these enzymes have significantly declined in
most individuals.
• The provided answer key says (d), but the
decline happens much earlier than age eight
for the majority of the world's population.
• Hence, option (b) is the most accurate
answer.
Q18. Haemoglobin is dissolved in the plasma of:
(a) frog
(b) fish
(c) man
(d) earthworm
The correct option is (d).
• In vertebrates like frogs, fish, and man,
hemoglobin is a protein that is contained
within specialized cells called red blood cells

•

•

•

(erythrocytes). It is not dissolved freely in the
blood plasma.
In some invertebrates, the respiratory
pigment is not contained within cells but is
instead dissolved directly in the blood plasma
(or hemolymph).
The earthworm is a classic example of this. It
has a circulatory system with blood that is red
because it contains hemoglobin, but this
hemoglobin is dissolved in the plasma, not in
red blood cells.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q19. Which of the following fish are bred exclusively
in the cold regions of India?
1. Catla
2. Tinca
3. Bhekti
4. Trout
Choose the correct answer from the codes given
below:
(a) 1 and 4
(b) 1, 2, and 4
(c) 2 and 4
(d) 3 and 4
The correct option is (c).
• This question asks to identify cold-water fish
species bred in India.
• 1. Catla: Is a major Indian carp, a warm-water
species widely cultivated in the plains of India.
It is not a cold-water fish.
• 2. Tinca (Tench): Is a freshwater fish native to
Europe and Asia. It is a cyprinid fish that can
tolerate a range of temperatures but is often
found and bred in cooler waters.
• 3. Bhekti (Barramundi): Is a species of catfish
found in the Indo-West Pacific. It is a warmwater, euryhaline species.
• 4. Trout: (Both Brown Trout and Rainbow
Trout) are classic cold-water fish. They were
introduced to India by the British and are now
bred in the cold, clear streams and lakes of
the Himalayan states like Jammu & Kashmir,
Himachal Pradesh, and Uttarakhand.

•
•
•

Therefore, Tinca and Trout are the cold-water
species from the list.
The provided answer key says (b), which
incorrectly includes Catla.
Hence, option (c) is the correct answer.

Q20. Phenylketonuria is an example of an in-born
error of metabolism. This "error" refers to:
(a) hormonal overproduction
(b) atrophy of endocrine glands
(c) inherited lack of an enzyme
(d) non-disjunction
The correct option is (c).
• Phenylketonuria (PKU) is a classic example of
an "inborn error of metabolism."
• These are genetic disorders that result from a
defect in a single gene that codes for a
specific enzyme.
• In the case of PKU, the "error" is the inherited
lack of a functional enzyme called
phenylalanine hydroxylase.
• This enzyme is necessary to metabolize the
amino acid phenylalanine into another amino
acid, tyrosine. Without the enzyme,
phenylalanine builds up in the blood and
body tissues to toxic levels, causing severe
intellectual disability and other problems if
not treated with a special diet from birth.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1995
Q1. Which one of the following sets is correctly
matched?
(a) Diphtheria, Pneumonia and Leprosy : Hereditary
(b) AIDS, Syphilis and Gonorrhoea : Bacterial
(c) Colour blindness, Haemophilia and Sickle cell
anaemia : Sex linked
(d) Polio, Japanese B encephalitis and plague : Viral
The correct option is (c).
• Let's analyze each set.
• (a) is incorrect. Diphtheria, Pneumonia, and
Leprosy are all infectious diseases caused by
bacteria, not hereditary conditions.

•

•

•

•

•

(b) is incorrect. AIDS is caused by a virus
(HIV), while Syphilis and Gonorrhoea are
bacterial infections. So, the set is not
uniformly bacterial.
(c) is mostly correct in the context of the
question. Colour blindness and Haemophilia
are classic examples of sex-linked (X-linked)
recessive disorders. Sickle cell anaemia is a
hereditary disorder, but it is an autosomal
recessive disorder, not sex-linked. However,
compared to the other options which are
definitively wrong, this is the "best fit" as all
three are genetic/hereditary, and two are
sex-linked. This is a flawed question.
(d) is incorrect. Polio and Japanese B
encephalitis are viral diseases, but Plague is
caused by a bacterium (Yersinia pestis).
Given the clear errors in (a), (b), and (d),
option (c) is the intended answer, despite the
inaccuracy regarding sickle cell anaemia.
Hence, option (c) is the intended correct
answer.

Q2. A person with 'AB' blood group is sometimes
called a universal recipient because of the:
(a) lack of antigens in his blood
(b) lack of antibodies in his blood
(c) lack of both antigens and antibodies in his blood
(d) presence of antibodies in his blood
The correct option is (b).
• The ABO blood group system is based on the
presence or absence of A and B antigens on
the surface of red blood cells and A and B
antibodies in the plasma.
• A person with blood group AB has both A and
B antigens on their red blood cells.
• Because their body recognizes both A and B
antigens as "self," their plasma does not
contain anti-A or anti-B antibodies.
• This lack of antibodies means their immune
system will not attack red blood cells from a
donor with A, B, or O blood type. Therefore,
they can receive blood from any ABO group,
making them "universal recipients."

•

•

The provided answer key explanation is
incorrect; they have a lack of antibodies, not
antigens.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. Which one of the following is a membrane that
protects the developing embryo from desiccation?
(a) Amnion
(b) Allantois
(c) Chorion
(d) Yolk sac
The correct option is (a).
• The developing embryo of amniotes (reptiles,
birds, and mammals) is surrounded by several
extraembryonic membranes.
• The Amnion is the innermost membrane that
encloses the embryo in a fluid-filled cavity
called the amniotic sac.
• This amniotic fluid provides a watery,
cushioned environment that protects the
embryo from mechanical shock and, crucially,
prevents it from drying out (desiccation).
• This adaptation was key to allowing
vertebrates to reproduce on land.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following antimicrobial drugs is
suitable for the treatment of both tuberculosis and
leprosy?
(a) Isoniazid
(b) p-aminosalicylic acid
(c) Streptomycin
(d) Rifampicin
The correct option is (d).
• Both Tuberculosis (TB) and Leprosy are
chronic infectious diseases caused by bacteria
of the genus Mycobacterium (M. tuberculosis
and M. leprae, respectively).
• Treatment for both diseases involves a long
course of multi-drug therapy to prevent the
development of resistance.
• Rifampicin (also known as Rifampin) is a
powerful, broad-spectrum antibiotic that is a
cornerstone of treatment for both
tuberculosis and leprosy. It is a key

•

•

component in the standard multi-drug
regimens for both conditions.
Isoniazid (a) is a primary drug for TB but not
typically for leprosy. Streptomycin (c) is a
second-line drug for TB.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q5. Most of the desert plants bloom during night
time because:
(a) their blooming is controlled by low temperature
(b) they are sensitive to the phases of the moon
(c) the desert insects eat away flowers during day
time
(d) the desert insects are active during night time
The correct option is (d).
• Many desert plants, such as certain species of
cacti, have flowers that open at night. This is
an adaptation for pollination.
• The primary reason for nocturnal blooming is
to attract nocturnal pollinators. In the desert,
many potential pollinators, such as moths and
bats, are most active during the cooler night
time hours to avoid the extreme heat of the
day.
• By opening their large, often white and
fragrant flowers at night, these plants
maximize their chances of being pollinated by
these nocturnal creatures.
• While low temperature (a) is a factor that
makes insects active, the direct cause for the
plant's blooming is to coincide with the
activity of its pollinators.
• Hence, option (d) is the most direct and
accurate reason.
Q6. The only snake that builds a nest is:
(a) Chain viper
(b) King Cobra
(c) Krait
(d) Saw-scaled viper
The correct option is (b).
• Most snakes do not exhibit parental care and
simply lay their eggs in a suitable location and
abandon them.

•

•

•

The King Cobra (Ophiophagus hannah) is
unique among snakes for its nest-building
behavior.
The female King Cobra gathers leaves and
other vegetation, using her body to push
them into a mound. She then lays her eggs
inside this mound and will often guard the
nest until the eggs are close to hatching. The
decaying vegetation in the nest also generates
heat, which helps incubate the eggs.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q7. Which of the following elements are present in
all proteins?
1. Carbon
2. Hydrogen
3. Oxygen
4. Nitrogen
Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
(a) 2 and 3
(b) 1, 2 and 4
(c) 1, 3 and 4
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (d).
• Proteins are polymers made up of monomer
units called amino acids.
• Every amino acid has a central carbon atom
bonded to four groups: an amino group (NH₂), a carboxyl group (-COOH), a hydrogen
atom (-H), and a variable side chain (R group).
• Because of this fundamental structure, all
amino acids, and therefore all proteins,
contain the elements Carbon (C), Hydrogen
(H), Oxygen (O), and Nitrogen (N).
• Some amino acids (like methionine and
cysteine) also contain sulfur, but this is not
present in all proteins.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Which one of the following hormones contains
iodine?
(a) Thyroxine
(b) Testosterone
(c) Insulin

(d) Adrenaline
The correct option is (a).
• The thyroid gland, located in the neck,
produces hormones that regulate
metabolism.
• The primary hormones produced by the
thyroid are Thyroxine (T4) and
Triiodothyronine (T3).
• The synthesis of these hormones is critically
dependent on the element iodine. The "4" in
T4 and "3" in T3 refer to the number of iodine
atoms attached to the hormone molecule.
• A dietary deficiency of iodine prevents the
thyroid from producing these hormones,
leading to conditions like goiter and
hypothyroidism.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9. "By weaving threads of physics, chemistry and
biology into a rich tapestry, this remarkable scientist
provided a unifying molecular view of the world... He
touted the wonderful properties of Vitamin C first as
a cure of common cold and later as a preventive
agent against cancer." The scientist referred to is:
(a) G.N. Lewis
(b) Linus Carl Pauling
(c) Fritz London
(d) Walter Heitler
The correct option is (b).
• The description perfectly fits Linus Carl
Pauling.
• Pauling was a towering figure of 20th-century
science, unique in winning two unshared
Nobel Prizes (one in Chemistry for his work
on the nature of the chemical bond, and one
for Peace for his activism against nuclear
weapons testing). His work bridged physics,
chemistry, and biology.
• In his later life, he became a famous and
controversial advocate for the health benefits
of taking very large doses of Vitamin C, which
he believed could prevent the common cold
and treat cancer.
• The provided answer key says (c), which is
incorrect. Fritz London was a theoretical

•

physicist known for his work on chemical
bonding and superconductivity; he was not
associated with Vitamin C advocacy.
Hence, option (b) is the correct answer.

Q10. "It begins as a single cell and grows into a
merciless disease that claims millions of lives year
after year. But scientists are steadily unlocking its
mysteries, and the fight against it may now have
reached a dramatic turning point. New discoveries
promise better therapies and hope in the war
against..." The disease referred to is:
(a) Cancer
(b) AIDS
(c) Tuberculosis
(d) Alzheimer's disease
The correct option is (a).
• The description points directly to Cancer.
• The phrase "begins as a single cell" is the
classic definition of cancer, which is a disease
of uncontrolled cell growth originating from a
single mutated cell.
• "Grows into a merciless disease that claims
millions of lives" accurately describes the
nature and impact of cancer.
• The reference to "unlocking its mysteries"
and "new discoveries promise better
therapies" reflects the ongoing, intensive
research into the molecular basis of cancer
and the development of targeted therapies,
immunotherapy, etc.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q11. The normal temperature of the human body on
the Kelvin scale is:
(a) 280
(b) 290
(c) 300
(d) 310
The correct option is (d).
• The normal human body temperature is
approximately 37° Celsius.
• The formula to convert Celsius (°C) to Kelvin
(K) is: K = °C + 273.15.

•
•
•

Therefore, the normal body temperature in
Kelvin is: 37 + 273.15 = 310.15 K.
Of the given options, 310 is the closest value.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1996

Q1. According to the World Health Organisation, the
disease which causes the death of the largest
number of people today is
(a) AIDS
(b) Tuberculosis
(c) Malaria
(d) Ebola
The correct option is (b).
• According to the World Health Organization
(WHO), Tuberculosis (TB) is one of the top
infectious killers worldwide.
• While the global effort to combat TB has
saved millions of lives, it remains a major
public health crisis.
• AIDS (Acquired Immunodeficiency Syndrome),
caused by the HIV virus, is also a major global
health issue, but global efforts have
significantly reduced AIDS-related deaths. TB
is the leading cause of death among people
living with HIV.
• Malaria is a life-threatening disease caused by
parasites transmitted through mosquito bites.
While it causes a significant number of
deaths, particularly in sub-Saharan Africa, its
total mortality is lower than that of
Tuberculosis.
• Ebola is a severe and often fatal viral
hemorrhagic fever. It occurs in outbreaks and,
while having a very high fatality rate, does not
cause the largest number of deaths globally
on an ongoing basis compared to TB.
• Hence option b correct
Q2. Which one of the following is a modified stem?
(a) Carrot
(b) Sweet Potato
(c) Coconut

(d) Potato
The correct option is (d).
• A modified stem is a plant stem that has been
adapted to perform special functions such as
food storage, propagation, or survival
through unfavorable conditions.
• The potato is a tuber, which is a type of
modified stem that grows underground.
• It is swollen and fleshy, serving as a storage
organ for the plant's food reserves (starch).
• The "eyes" of the potato are actually nodes
with buds from which new plants can grow.
• A carrot is a taproot, which is a modified
root, not a stem.
• A sweet potato is also a tuberous root,
meaning it is a modified root specialized for
storage, not a stem.
• A coconut is the fruit of the coconut palm
tree, specifically a type of fruit known as a
drupe.
• Hence option d correct
Q3. Fat present below the skin surface in our body,
acts as a barrier against
(a) loss of heat from the body
(b) loss of essential body fluids
(c) loss of salts from the body
(d) entry of harmful micro-organisms from the
environment
The correct option is (a).
• The layer of fat located just beneath the skin
is known as subcutaneous fat.
• This tissue serves several functions, one of the
most important being insulation.
• Fat is a poor conductor of heat. This property
means that the subcutaneous fat layer acts as
a thermal barrier, slowing down the rate at
which heat is lost from the body to the colder
external environment.
• This helps in maintaining a stable internal
body temperature (thermoregulation).
• The skin itself (specifically the epidermis) acts
as the primary barrier against the entry of
harmful microorganisms and the loss of body
fluids.

•
•

While the fat layer provides cushioning, its
main role in this context is thermal insulation.
Hence option correct

Q4. Which of the following professional(s) are more
likely to run the risk of a permanent change in their
cell's DNA?
1. Researchers using carbon-14 isotope
2. X-ray technician
3. Coal miner
4. Dyer and Painter
Select the correct Answer by using the codes given
below:
(a) 2 alone
(b) 1 and 2
(c) 1, 2 and 4
(d) 1, 3 and 4
The correct option is (b).
• Permanent changes in a cell's DNA are known
as mutations. These can be caused by
exposure to mutagens, which are physical or
chemical agents that alter genetic material.
• X-rays (used by X-ray technicians) and
radiation from radioactive isotopes like
Carbon-14 are forms of ionizing radiation.
This type of radiation has enough energy to
knock electrons out of atoms and molecules,
which can directly damage the DNA structure,
leading to permanent mutations.
• Professionals with repeated or prolonged
exposure are at a higher risk.
• Coal miners are exposed to coal dust, which
can lead to serious lung diseases like
pneumoconiosis, but coal dust itself is not
considered a primary mutagen that causes
permanent, widespread DNA changes in the
same way ionizing radiation does.
• Dyers and painters may be exposed to various
chemicals, some of which can be toxic or
carcinogenic. However, the direct DNAdamaging potential of ionizing radiation from
X-rays and C-14 is a well-established and highrisk factor.
• Hence option b correct

Q5. Which one of the following is not an essential
micronutrient for plants?
(a) Boron
(b) Zinc
(c) Sodium
(d) Copper
The correct option is (c).
• Essential plant nutrients are elements
required by plants for normal growth,
development, and reproduction. They are
categorized into macronutrients (needed in
larger amounts) and micronutrients (needed
in smaller amounts).
• The seven universally recognized essential
micronutrients for plants are Boron (B), Zinc
(Zn), Manganese (Mn), Iron (Fe), Copper (Cu),
Molybdenum (Mo), and Chlorine (Cl). Nickel
(Ni) is also now considered essential.
• Sodium (Na) is not considered an essential
micronutrient for all plants. While it can be
beneficial for certain types of plants
(especially C4 plants like sugarcane) by aiding
in metabolism and synthesis of chlorophyll, it
is not universally required for all plants to
complete their life cycle.
• Boron, Zinc, and Copper are all established
essential micronutrients, playing critical roles
in various enzymatic and metabolic processes
within the plant.
• Hence option c correct
Q6. Of the four landmarks in medical history given
below, which one was the first to take place?
(a) Organ transplant
(b) Bypass surgery
(c) Test-tube baby
(d) Plastic surgery
The correct option is (d).
• Plastic Surgery (1917): Sir Harold Gillies is
considered the "father of modern plastic
surgery." During World War I, he developed
pioneering techniques for facial
reconstruction on soldiers with severe
injuries. He performed a landmark skin graft
procedure on Walter Yeo in 1917.

•

•

•

•
•

Organ Transplant (1954): The first successful
living-related kidney transplant was
performed by Dr. Joseph Murray in Boston in
1954, involving identical twin brothers.
Bypass Surgery (1960): The first successful
coronary artery bypass surgery was
performed in 1960 at the Albert Einstein
College of Medicine in the United States.
Test-Tube Baby (1978): The world's first baby
conceived through in vitro fertilization (IVF),
Louise Brown, was born in England in 1978.
Based on this timeline, plastic surgery was the
first of these four landmarks to occur.
Hence option d correct

Q7. The nutritional deficiency condition that needs
to be given top priority for remedial action in India
today is
(a) Scurvy
(b) Rickets
(c) Xerophthalmia
(d) Pellagra
The correct option is (c).
• Xerophthalmia is a progressive eye disease
caused by a severe Vitamin A deficiency. It
can lead to night blindness and, if untreated,
can progress to permanent blindness.
• In many developing countries, including India,
Vitamin A deficiency is a major public health
problem, especially among young children
and pregnant women, making it a top priority
for remedial action.
• Rickets is caused by Vitamin D deficiency,
leading to soft and weak bones in children.
While it is a concern in India, the prevalence
and severity of blindness caused by
Xerophthalmia often place it higher on the
priority list for public health interventions.
• Scurvy is caused by Vitamin C deficiency and
Pellagra is caused by Niacin (Vitamin B3)
deficiency. While these conditions exist, they
are generally less widespread and have less
severe public health consequences in India
compared to Vitamin A deficiency.
• Hence option c correct

Q8. Living organisms require at least 27 elements of
which 15 are metals. Among these, those required in
major quantities include
(a) Potassium, Manganese, Molybdenum & Calcium
(b) Potassium, Molybdenum, Copper & Calcium
(c) Potassium, Sodium, Magnesium & Calcium
(d) Sodium, Magnesium, Copper & Manganese
The correct option is (c).
• Elements required by living organisms in large
amounts are called macronutrients or major
elements. For humans and most animals,
these include elements that play crucial roles
in nerve function, bone structure, and fluid
balance.
• The elements Potassium (K), Sodium (Na),
Magnesium (Mg), and Calcium (Ca) are all
essential metals required in major quantities.
• Potassium and Sodium are key electrolytes
for nerve impulse transmission and
maintaining fluid balance.
• Calcium is the primary component of bones
and teeth and is vital for muscle contraction.
• Magnesium is involved in over 300 enzymatic
reactions, including energy metabolism and
protein synthesis.
• Molybdenum (Mo), Copper (Cu), and
Manganese (Mn) are also essential metals,
but they are required in much smaller
amounts and are classified as micronutrients
or trace elements.
• Hence option c correct
Q9. Match List I with List II and select the correct
Answer by using codes given below the lists:
List I
A. Vitamin
B. Enzyme
C. Hormone
D. Protein
List II
1. Pepsin
2. Carotene
3. Keratin
4. Progesterone

Codes:
ABCD
(a) 1 2 3 4
(b) 2 1 4 3
(c) 2 1 3 4
(d) 1 2 4 3
The correct option is (b).
• A. Vitamin: Carotene (specifically betacarotene) is a precursor to Vitamin A. The
body converts carotene from foods like
carrots into Vitamin A. So, A matches with 2.
• B. Enzyme: Pepsin is a digestive enzyme
found in the stomach that breaks down
proteins into smaller peptides. So, B matches
with 1.
• C. Hormone: Progesterone is a steroid
hormone involved in the menstrual cycle,
pregnancy, and embryogenesis. So, C
matches with 4.
• D. Protein: Keratin is a fibrous structural

feathers, horns, claws, and the outer layer of
the skin in vertebrates. So, D matches with 3.

Q10. Physico-chemical characteristics of water in
water sources undergo changes due to
(a) aquatic macrophytes
(b) aquatic fungi
(c) effluents
(d) evapotranspiration
The correct option is (c).
• The physico-chemical characteristics of water
include properties like pH, temperature,
turbidity (clarity), dissolved oxygen, and the
concentration of various chemical
compounds.
• Effluents are liquid waste or sewage
discharged into a body of water from sources
like industrial factories or sewage treatment
plants.
• These discharges can drastically alter the
water's characteristics by introducing

•

•

•

pollutants, chemicals, heavy metals, and
organic matter.
This directly changes the water's chemical
composition, pH, temperature, and oxygen
levels, causing the most significant and often
harmful changes.
Aquatic macrophytes (plants) and fungi can
influence water characteristics, but typically
to a lesser extent than industrial or municipal
effluents. Evapotranspiration primarily
affects the water volume, not its core
chemical properties.
hence option c correct

Q11. Ecologists have so far known, found and
certified a large number of species in the plant and
animal kingdom. In terms of members, the largest
found and identified so far are from among the
(a) Fungi
(b) Plants
(c) Insects
(d) Bacteria
The correct option is (c).
• The animal kingdom is divided into several
phyla. The phylum Arthropoda, which
includes insects, spiders, crustaceans, and
others, is by far the largest phylum in the
animal kingdom.
• Within the arthropods, the class Insecta
represents the vast majority of species. It is
estimated that insects account for over 80%
of all known animal life on Earth.
• While bacteria are incredibly numerous in
terms of individual organisms and are
omnipresent, the question refers to the
number of certified species. In terms of
described and named species, insects far
outnumber bacteria, fungi, and plants.
• The sheer diversity and adaptability of insects
have allowed them to colonize nearly every
terrestrial and freshwater habitat, leading to
the evolution of an enormous number of
distinct species.
Hence option correct (c).

Q12. It is possible to produce seedless tomato fruits
by
(a) applying trace elements in tomato fields
(b) spraying mineral solution on plants
(c) spraying hormones on flowers
(d) applying fertilizers containing radioactive elements
The correct option is (c).
• The development of fruit without prior
fertilization is known as parthenocarpy. This
process results in seedless fruits.
• Plant hormones, particularly a class of
hormones called auxins, can be used to
artificially induce parthenocarpy.
• When auxins are sprayed onto the flowers of
plants like tomatoes, they stimulate the ovary
to develop into a fruit, even though
fertilization (and therefore seed
development) has not occurred.
• This bypasses the natural process that
requires pollination and fertilization to
trigger fruit growth.
• Trace elements, mineral solutions, and
fertilizers are essential for general plant
growth but do not specifically induce the
formation of seedless fruit. Radioactive
elements are not used for this purpose.
Hence option correct (c).
Q13. Which of the following are associated with
diabetes mellitus, a common disease in adults?
1. Higher sugar level in blood
2. Lower sugar level in blood
3. Lower insulin level in blood
4. Higher insulin level in blood
Select the correct Answer by using the codes
given below:
(a) 2 and 4
(b) 1 and 2
(c) 2 and 3
(d) 1 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Diabetes mellitus is a metabolic disorder
characterized by high blood sugar levels over
a prolonged period. This condition is known as

•

•

•

•

hyperglycemia. Therefore, statement 1,
"Higher sugar level in blood," is correct.
This high blood sugar occurs because the body
either does not produce enough insulin or
cannot effectively use the insulin it does
produce. Insulin is the hormone responsible
for allowing glucose (sugar) to enter cells to
be used for energy.
In Type 1 diabetes, the pancreas fails to
produce enough insulin. This directly leads to
a "Lower insulin level in blood."
In Type 2 diabetes, the body's cells become
resistant to insulin's effects. While the
pancreas might initially produce more insulin
to compensate, the underlying and common
cause, especially in Type 1, is a deficiency.
Combining the most definitive characteristics
from the options, high blood sugar (1) and
low insulin (3) are the correct pair.
Hence option correct (d).

Q14. Consider the following statements: AIDS is
transmitted
1. by sexual intercourse.
2. by blood transfusion.
3. by mosquitoes and other blood sucking
insects.
4. across the placenta.
Of these statements:
(a) 1, 2 and 3 are correct
(b) 1, 2 and 4 are correct
(c) 1, 3 and 4 are correct
(d) 1 and 3 are correct
The correct option is (b).
• AIDS (Acquired Immunodeficiency Syndrome)
is caused by the Human Immunodeficiency
Virus (HIV). The virus is transmitted through
specific body fluids.
• Sexual intercourse (1) is a primary mode of
transmission, as HIV is present in semen and
vaginal fluids.
• Blood transfusion (2) is another major route.
If blood from an infected person is transfused
to another, the virus can be transmitted
directly into the bloodstream. This also

•

•

•

includes sharing contaminated needles
among intravenous drug users.
Across the placenta (4), from an infected
mother to her child during pregnancy,
childbirth, or breastfeeding, is a welldocumented mode of transmission known as
vertical or perinatal transmission.
Mosquitoes and other blood-sucking insects
(3) do not transmit HIV. Although they ingest
blood, the virus does not survive or replicate
within the mosquito's gut and is not injected
into the next person bitten. The insect's
biology is not conducive to HIV transmission.
Therefore, statements 1, 2, and 4 are correct
modes of transmission.
Hence option correct (b).

Q15. Even though an animal is fed with
carbohydrate-rich diet, its blood sugar concentration
tends to remain constant. This is on account of the
fact that in the case of an animal
(a) hormones of pituitary glands control metabolic
process
(b) hormones of pancreas cause such a condition
(c) blood sugar is readily absorbed by liver
(d) glucose undergoes autolysis
The correct option is (b).
• The regulation of blood sugar (glucose)
concentration is a critical homeostatic process
controlled primarily by hormones produced in
the pancreas.
• After a carbohydrate-rich meal, glucose is
absorbed into the bloodstream, causing blood
sugar levels to rise. In response, the beta cells
of the pancreas release the hormone insulin.
• Insulin acts as a key, signaling cells in the
liver, muscles, and fat tissue to take up
glucose from the blood. The liver converts
excess glucose into glycogen for storage. This
process lowers blood sugar levels back to a
normal range.
• If blood sugar drops too low, the alpha cells of
the pancreas release glucagon, which signals
the liver to break down stored glycogen and
release glucose back into the blood. This dual-

•

hormone system keeps blood sugar levels
remarkably constant.
While pituitary hormones are involved in
overall metabolism, the direct, moment-tomoment control of blood glucose is the
responsibility of the pancreatic hormones,
insulin and glucagon.
Hence option correct (b).

Q16. People drinking water from a shallow hand
pump are likely to suffer from all of the following
diseases except
(a) Cholera
(b) Typhoid
(c) Jaundice
(d) Fluorosis
The correct option is (d).
• Cholera, Typhoid, and Jaundice (specifically
Hepatitis A and E) are all water-borne
diseases. They are caused by pathogenic
microorganisms (bacteria and viruses) that
are transmitted through water contaminated
with fecal matter. Shallow hand pumps are
highly susceptible to such contamination from
surface runoff or nearby septic systems.
• Fluorosis, however, is a condition caused by
the excessive intake of fluoride, a chemical
element. It is a geo-chemical disease, not an
infectious one.
• High concentrations of fluoride occur
naturally in the groundwater of certain
geological regions. While a shallow hand
pump can draw this contaminated water, the
origin of the disease is the chemical
composition of the water source itself, not
microbial contamination from waste.
• Therefore, while one might get fluoride from
the pump, fluorosis is distinct from the
infectious diseases listed.
Hence option correct (d).
Q17. Which of the following would lead to
'malnutrition'?
1. Over nutrition
2. Under nutrition

3. Imbalanced nutrition.
Select the correct Answer by using the codes
gives below:
(a) 2 alone
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Malnutrition is a broad term that refers to
deficiencies, excesses, or imbalances in a
person's intake of energy and/or nutrients. It
covers all three conditions mentioned.
• Under-nutrition (2) is the most common form
of malnutrition, where there is an insufficient
intake of energy and nutrients to meet an
individual's needs. This leads to conditions like
stunting, wasting, and being underweight.
• Over-nutrition (1) is also a form of
malnutrition. It results from an excessive
intake of nutrients, particularly calories
(energy), leading to overweight, obesity, and
an increased risk of non-communicable
diseases like heart disease and diabetes.
• Imbalanced nutrition (3) occurs when the diet
lacks a proper proportion of essential
nutrients, even if the total calorie intake is
adequate. For example, a diet high in calories
but low in vitamins and minerals is a form of
malnutrition.
• Since malnutrition encompasses all three
states—too little, too much, or the wrong
balance of nutrients—all three statements are
correct.
Hence option correct (d).
Q18. Besides proteins and carbohydrates, other
elements of nutritional value found in milk include
(a) calcium, potassium and iron
(b) calcium and potassium
(c) potassium and iron
(d) calcium and iron
The correct option is (b).
• Milk is known as a nearly complete food
because it is rich in many essential nutrients.

•

•

•

•

It is an excellent source of Calcium (Ca), which
is vital for bone and teeth health, and
Potassium (K), an important electrolyte for
nerve function and blood pressure
regulation.
Milk is also a good source of other nutrients
like phosphorus, vitamin D (if fortified),
vitamin B12, and riboflavin.
However, milk is notably a very poor source
of Iron (Fe). This is why infants who are
exclusively fed milk after 6 months of age are
at risk for iron-deficiency anemia if not given
iron-fortified foods or supplements.
Therefore, any option including iron as a key
nutritional element in milk is incorrect. The
option containing only calcium and potassium
is the correct one.
Hence option correct (b).

Q19. Match List I (Physiological processes) with List II
(Cell organelles) and select the correct Answer by
using the codes given below the lists:
List I
A. Photosynthesis
B. Mineral uptake
C. Respiration
D. Protein Synthesis
List II
1. Plasma Membrane
2. Chloroplast
3. Mitochondria
4. Ribosomes
Codes:
(a) 1 2 3 4
(b) 1 2 4 3
(c) 2 1 3 4
(d) 2 1 4 3
The correct option is (c).
• A. Photosynthesis: This is the process by
which plants convert light energy into
chemical energy. It occurs in the chloroplasts
(2), which contain the chlorophyll pigment.
So, A matches with 2.
• B. Mineral uptake: The uptake of mineral
ions from the soil into the root cells is

•

•

•

controlled by the plasma membrane (1),
which selectively regulates the passage of
substances into and out of the cell. So, B
matches with 1.
C. Respiration: Cellular respiration is the
process of breaking down glucose to produce
ATP (energy). This occurs primarily in the
mitochondria (3), often called the
"powerhouse" of the cell. So, C matches with
3.
D. Protein Synthesis: The synthesis of
proteins from amino acids is carried out by
ribosomes (4), which read the genetic code
from messenger RNA (mRNA) to assemble
the protein chain. So, D matches with 4.
Therefore, the correct matching is A-2, B-1, C3, D-4.
Hence option correct (c).
UPSC PYQs - 1997

Q1. The characteristic odour of garlic is due to
(a) a chloro compound
(b) a sulphur compound
(c) a fluorine compound
(d) acetic acid
The correct option is (b).
• The distinctive and pungent odor of garlic is
primarily due to a variety of organosulfur
compounds.
• When a garlic clove is crushed or cut, an
enzyme called alliinase converts a compound
named alliin into allicin. Allicin is a volatile
and unstable sulfur-containing compound
that is responsible for the immediate, sharp
smell.
• Allicin then quickly breaks down into other
sulfur compounds, such as diallyl disulfide,
which contribute to the lingering garlic odor.
• Chloro compounds, fluorine compounds, and
acetic acid are not responsible for the
characteristic smell of garlic.
Hence option correct (b).

Q2. Which one of the following techniques can be
used to establish the paternity of a child?
(a) Protein analysis
(b) Chromosome counting
(c) Quantitative analysis of DNA
(d) DNA finger-printing
The correct option is (d).
• DNA fingerprinting (also known as DNA
profiling) is a laboratory technique used to
determine the probable identity of a person
by comparing their DNA sequence to that of
another person or a sample.
• A child inherits half of their DNA from their
biological mother and the other half from
their biological father.
• By comparing the child's DNA profile with
that of the alleged father, a paternity test can
be performed. If the child has DNA markers
that are not found in the mother's DNA, they
must have been inherited from the biological
father. If the alleged father's DNA contains
these specific markers, it provides strong
evidence of paternity. If he does not have
them, he can be excluded as the biological
father.
• Protein analysis is not specific enough for this
purpose. Chromosome counting can identify
genetic disorders like Down syndrome but
cannot establish paternity. Quantitative
analysis of DNA (measuring the amount) is
also not used for this purpose.
Hence option correct (d).
Q3. Which one of the following is an active
component of oil of clove?
(a) Menthol
(b) Eugenol
(c) Methanol
(d) Benzaldehyde
The correct option is (b).
• Oil of clove is an essential oil extracted from
the clove plant. Its primary active component,
making up 80–95% of the oil, is Eugenol.
• Eugenol is responsible for the oil's
characteristic aroma and its medicinal

•

properties. It has been used for centuries in
dentistry as a local anesthetic and antiseptic.
It helps to numb pain (analgesic effect) and
kill germs, which is why it is often used to
provide temporary relief from toothaches.
Menthol is the active component of
peppermint oil. Methanol is a type of alcohol.
Benzaldehyde is the compound responsible
for the smell of almonds.
Hence option correct (b).

Q4. The pituitary gland by virtue of its tropic
hormones controls the secretory activity of other
endocrine glands. Which one of the following
endocrine glands can function independent of the
pituitary gland?
(a) Thyroid
(b) Gonads
(c) Adrenals
(d) Parathyroid
The correct option is (d).
• The pituitary gland is often called the "master
gland" because it produces tropic hormones
that regulate the function of many other
endocrine glands, including the thyroid,
adrenal glands, and gonads (testes and
ovaries).
• The Parathyroid glands, however, function
largely independently of the pituitary gland.
Their primary role is to regulate calcium levels
in the blood.
• The parathyroid glands monitor blood
calcium levels directly. When calcium levels
fall, they secrete Parathyroid Hormone (PTH).
When calcium levels rise, they reduce PTH
secretion. This regulation is a direct feedback
loop based on blood calcium concentration
and is not controlled by hormones from the
pituitary.
Hence option correct (d).
Q5. Oxygen transportation in a human body takes
place through
1. Blood
2. Lungs

3. Tissue
The correct sequence of transportation is
(a) 1, 2, 3
(b) 3, 1, 2
(c) 2, 1, 3
(d) 1, 3, 2
The correct option is (c).
• The process of oxygen transportation begins
with inhalation. Air enters the Lungs (2) and
reaches the tiny air sacs called alveoli.
• In the alveoli, oxygen diffuses across the thin
membrane into the surrounding capillaries
and enters the Blood (1). Here, it binds to
hemoglobin in red blood cells.
• The oxygen-rich blood is then pumped by the
heart throughout the body. When it reaches
the body's Tissues (3), the oxygen is released
from the hemoglobin and diffuses from the
blood into the tissue cells, where it is used for
cellular respiration.
• Therefore, the correct sequence of oxygen
transport is from the Lungs, into the Blood,
and finally to the Tissues.
Hence option correct (c).
Q6. Corpus luteum is a mass of cells found in
(a) brain
(b) ovary
(c) pancreas
(d) spleen
The correct option is (b).
• The Corpus luteum (Latin for "yellow body")
is a temporary endocrine structure that
develops in the ovary after an egg has been
released from the ovarian follicle during
ovulation.
• Its primary function is to secrete hormones,
most importantly progesterone, and also
some estrogen.
• Progesterone is crucial for preparing the
uterus for pregnancy by causing the uterine
lining (endometrium) to thicken. If
fertilization and implantation occur, the
corpus luteum continues to produce
progesterone to maintain the pregnancy until

the placenta takes over this function. If
pregnancy does not occur, the corpus luteum
degenerates, leading to a drop in
progesterone levels and the onset of
menstruation.
Hence option correct (b).
Q7. Which one of the following organisms is likely to
show the highest- concentration of DDT once it has
been introduced into the ecosystem?
(a) Grasshopper
(b) Toad
(c) Snake
(d) Cattle
The correct option is (c).
• This question describes the process of
biomagnification (or bioaccumulation),
where the concentration of a persistent toxin
like DDT increases at successively higher
levels in a food chain.
• DDT is fat-soluble and is not easily broken
down by organisms. When a lower-level
organism consumes DDT, it gets stored in its
fatty tissues. When a higher-level organism
eats many of these lower-level organisms, it
accumulates the DDT from all of them.
• Considering a typical food chain: Plants →
Grasshopper → Toad → Snake.
• Grasshoppers eat plants that may have DDT.
Toads eat many grasshoppers, concentrating
the DDT. A snake eats many toads, further
concentrating the DDT to the highest level
among these options.
• Cattle are herbivores and would be at a low
level in the food chain, thus having a lower
concentration of DDT compared to the top
predator in this list.
Hence option correct (c).
Q8. Alpha-keratin is a protein present in
(a) blood
(b) skin
(c) wool
(d) eggs
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•

•

Keratin is a type of fibrous structural protein.
It is the key material making up hair, feathers,
horns, claws, hooves, and the outer layer of
the skin among vertebrates.
Alpha-keratin (α-keratin) is the specific type
of keratin found in all mammals. It is the
primary component of mammalian hair,
which includes wool. Wool is the textile fiber
obtained from sheep and other animals, and it
is almost entirely composed of α-keratin.
The outer layer of the skin also contains
keratin, but wool is a more direct and
concentrated example of α-keratin.
Blood's primary protein is hemoglobin. Eggs
are rich in proteins like albumin, but not
keratin.
Hence option correct (c).

Q9. Which one of the following is cultivated by
transplanting seedlings?
(a) Maize
(b) Sorghum
(c) Onion
(d) Soyabean
The correct option is (c).
• Transplanting is the agricultural practice of
starting plants from seed in a protected
environment (like a nursery or greenhouse)
and then moving the young plants, or
seedlings, to their permanent growing
location in the field.
• This technique is commonly used for crops
like onion. Onion seeds are very small and the
young seedlings are delicate. Growing them in
a nursery first allows for better germination
rates, protection from pests and adverse
weather, and ensures a more uniform and
healthy crop when they are transplanted.
• Crops like Maize (corn), Sorghum, and
Soyabean are typically direct-seeded,
meaning their seeds are planted directly into
the field where they will grow to maturity.
Their seedlings are generally robust and do
not benefit from or tolerate transplanting as

well as onions do.
Hence option correct (c).
•

Q10. What is the average fat content of buffalo milk?
(a) 7.2%
(b) 4.5%
(c) 9.0%
(d) 10.0%
The correct option is (a).
• The composition of milk varies significantly
between different mammal species. Buffalo
milk is known for being much richer in fat
compared to cow's milk.
• The average fat content of buffalo milk is
approximately 7–8%. This is substantially
higher than cow's milk, which typically has a
fat content of about 3–4%.
• This high fat content gives buffalo milk its
characteristic creamy texture and makes it
ideal for producing high-fat dairy products like
butter, ghee, and certain types of cheese.
• Based on the options provided, 7.2% is the
most accurate figure for the average fat
content of buffalo milk.
Hence option correct (a).
Q11. The major component of honey is
(a) glucose
(b) sucrose
(c) maltose
(d) fructose
The correct option is (d).
• Honey is a supersaturated sugar solution,
with carbohydrates being its main
constituents (over 80%).
• The two primary sugars in honey are
monosaccharides: fructose and glucose.
• Of these two, fructose is the most abundant
sugar, typically making up about 38% of
honey by weight. Glucose is the second most
abundant, at around 31%.
• The high concentration of fructose is what
makes honey sweeter than table sugar
(sucrose) and gives it its unique physical

properties, such as its ability to remain liquid
for a long time.
Sucrose and maltose are disaccharides and
are present in honey, but in much smaller
quantities compared to fructose and glucose.
Hence option correct (d).

Q12. Arteries supplying blood to the heart are called
(a) carotid arteries
(b) hepatic arteries
(c) coronary arteries
(d) pulmonary arteries
The correct option is (c).
• The heart muscle (myocardium) requires its
own continuous supply of oxygen-rich blood
to function. This blood is delivered by the
coronary arteries.
• The coronary arteries are the very first
branches off the aorta, the main artery
leaving the heart. They wrap around the
outside of the heart, supplying it with the
blood it needs to keep pumping. Blockage of
these arteries leads to a heart attack.
• Carotid arteries supply blood to the brain,
neck, and face.
• Hepatic arteries supply blood to the liver.
• Pulmonary arteries carry deoxygenated
blood from the heart to the lungs.
Hence option correct (c).
Q13. Recommended daily intake of proteins for a
moderately active woman is
(a) 30 g
(b) 37 g
(c) 40 g
(d) 46 g
The correct option is (d).
• The Recommended Dietary Allowance (RDA)
for protein for a healthy adult is 0.8 grams
per kilogram of body weight. This is the
minimum amount needed to meet basic
nutritional requirements.
• To calculate the recommended intake for an
"average" woman, we can use an average
body weight. For example, if we assume an

•

average weight of around 57–60 kg, the
calculation would be:
o 57.5 kg × 0.8 g/kg = 46 grams of
protein per day.
This value is for a sedentary individual. A
"moderately active" woman would have
slightly higher needs, but 46 g is the standard
baseline recommendation and the closest
correct answer among the choices. The other
values are too low to meet the basic
requirements.
Hence option correct (d).

Q14. Match List I with List II and select the correct
answer by using the codes given below the lists:
List I
A. Malaria
B. Filaria
C. Encephalitis
D. Leukaemia
List II
1. Bone marrow
2. Brain
3. Muscle
4. Lymph node
5. Blood Cells
Codes:
ABCD
(a) 5 3 2 1
(b) 5 4 2 1
(c) 4 3 5 1
(d) 5 4 1 2
The correct option is (b).
• A. Malaria: This disease is caused by the
Plasmodium parasite, which infects and
destroys red blood cells (5). So, A matches
with 5.
• B. Filaria (Filariasis): This is caused by
parasitic worms that live in the lymphatic
system. They can cause severe swelling and
damage to the lymph nodes (4) and lymphatic
vessels, leading to conditions like
elephantiasis. So, B matches with 4.

•

•

•

C. Encephalitis: This is an inflammation of the
brain (2), often caused by a viral infection. So,
C matches with 2.
D. Leukaemia: This is a type of cancer that
affects the blood and bone marrow (1). It is
characterized by the rapid production of
abnormal white blood cells. So, D matches
with 1.
Therefore, the correct matching is A-5, B-4, C2, D-1.
Hence option correct (b).

Q15. Antigen is a substance which
(a) lowers body temperature
(b) destroys harmful bacteria
(c) triggers the immune system
(d) is used as an antidote to poison
The correct option is (c).
• An antigen is any molecule, usually a protein
or polysaccharide, that can be recognized by
the adaptive immune system and triggers an
immune response.
• When the immune system detects an antigen
(which it identifies as foreign or "non-self"), it
activates lymphocytes (like B-cells and T-cells)
to attack the substance directly or to produce
antibodies against it.
• Antigens can be parts of bacteria, viruses,
fungi, or other pathogens. They can also be
non-living substances like pollen, toxins, or
chemicals. The key characteristic is its ability
to be recognized by and provoke a response
from the immune system.
• It does not inherently lower body
temperature (that's an antipyretic) or act as
an antidote. While some antigens are parts of
harmful bacteria, the antigen itself is the
trigger, not the destroyer.
Hence option correct (c).

UPSC PYQs - 2000
Q1. At which stage in its life-cycle does the silkworm
yield the fibre of commerce?
(a) Egg
(b) Larva
(c) Pupa
(d) Imago
The correct option is (c).
• The life cycle of a silkworm consists of four
stages: egg, larva (caterpillar), pupa, and
imago (adult moth).
• The commercially valuable silk fibre is
produced by the larva to create a protective
casing around itself before it transforms into
the next stage.
• This casing is called a cocoon, which is spun
from a single, continuous thread of raw silk
produced by its salivary glands.
• The silkworm is in the pupa stage while inside
the cocoon. To harvest silk, the cocoon is
boiled in water, killing the pupa and allowing
the silk filament to be unwound.
• If left to develop, the adult moth (imago)
would break the silk fibre.
• Hence, correct option is (c).

Q2. Match List I (Diseases) with List II (Types of
disease) and select the correct answer using the
codes given below the Lists:

List I
A. Haemophilia
B. Diabetes
C. Rickets
D. Ringworm
List II
1. Deficiency disease
2. Genetic disease
3. Hormonal disorder
4. Fungal infection
Codes:
(a) 2 3 4 1
(b) 2 3 1 4
(c) 3 2 1 4
(d) 3 2 4 1
The correct option is (b).
• Haemophilia: A genetic disease where blood
doesn't clot normally due to lack of clotting
factors. → A–2
• Diabetes: A hormonal disorder caused by
insulin deficiency or insulin resistance. → B–3
• Rickets: A deficiency disease caused by lack
of Vitamin D, leading to soft bones. → C–1
• Ringworm: A fungal infection of the skin, not
caused by a worm. → D–4
• Therefore, the correct matching is A–2, B–3,
C–1, D–4.
• Hence, correct option is (b).
Q3. The blood glucose level is commonly expressed
as
(a) mm of Hg
(b) millimoles per litre
(c) parts per million
(d) grams per litre
The correct option is (b).
• The standard international unit for measuring
blood glucose concentration is millimoles per
litre (mmol/L).
• It reflects the number of glucose molecules in
a given volume of blood.
• In some countries like the United States, it is
measured in mg/dL, but mmol/L is globally
accepted.

•
•
•
•

mm of Hg is a pressure unit (used for blood
pressure).
ppm is used for very low concentrations (like
pollutants).
g/L is a mass concentration unit, not standard
for glucose measurement.
Hence, correct option is (b).

Q4. Phytotron is a facility to
(a) grow plants under disease-free conditions
(b) conserve endangered species of plants
(c) grow plants under controlled conditions
(d) induce mutations
The correct option is (c).
• A phytotron is a research facility for studying
plant physiology.
• It allows precise control over temperature,
light, humidity, air composition, and
nutrients.
• Researchers use it to study how
environmental factors affect plant growth
and development.
• It enables repeatable experiments that are
not possible in natural environments.
• Though it can grow disease-free plants, its
primary purpose is environmental control for
research.
• Hence, correct option is (c).
Q5. Match List I (Endocrine glands) with List II
(Hormones secreted) and select the correct answer
using the codes given below the Lists:
List I
A. Gonads
B. Pituitary
C. Pancreas
D. Adrenal
List II
1. Insulin
2. Progesterone
3. Growth hormones
4. Cortisone
Codes:
(a) 3 2 4 1
(b) 2 3 4 1

(c) 2 3 1 4
(d) 3 2 1 4
The correct option is (c).
• Gonads: Reproductive glands (testes/ovaries)
secrete Progesterone (2). → A–2
• Pituitary: The “master gland” secretes
Growth hormone (3). → B–3
• Pancreas: Secretes Insulin (1) through Islets
of Langerhans. → C–1
• Adrenal: Produces Cortisone (4) from the
adrenal cortex. → D–4
• Therefore, the correct matching is A–2, B–3,
C–1, D–4.
• Hence, correct option is (c).
Q6. The sensation of fatigue in the muscles after
prolonged strenuous physical work is caused by
(a) a decrease in the supply of oxygen
(b) minor wear and tear of muscle fibres
(c) the depletion of glucose
(d) the accumulation of lactic acid
The correct option is (d).
• During intense exercise, oxygen supply
becomes insufficient for aerobic respiration.
• Muscles switch to anaerobic respiration,
breaking down glucose without oxygen.
• This produces lactic acid as a byproduct.
• Lactic acid accumulation lowers pH,
disrupting enzyme activity and muscle
contraction, causing fatigue.
• While oxygen decrease and glucose depletion
contribute, lactic acid build-up is the direct
cause.
• Hence, correct option is (d).
UPSC PYQs – 2001
Q1. Which of the following cell organelles play the
most significant role in protein synthesis?
(a) Lysosome and Centrosome
(b) Endoplasmic reticulum and Ribosome
(c) Golgi apparatus and Mitochondria
(d) Lysosome and Mitochondria
The correct option is (b).

•

•

•

•
•

•

Protein synthesis is a fundamental process
involving the creation of proteins by cellular
organelles.
Ribosomes are the actual sites of protein
synthesis, assembling amino acids into
polypeptide chains using mRNA instructions.
The Rough Endoplasmic Reticulum (RER),
studded with ribosomes, helps in folding,
modifying, and transporting newly
synthesized proteins.
Thus, ribosomes perform synthesis, and ER
performs processing and transport.
Lysosomes handle waste breakdown, and
mitochondria produce energy, while Golgi
apparatus packages proteins.
Hence, correct option is (b).

Q2. Assertion (A): Scientists can cut apart and paste
together DNA molecules at will, regardless of the
source of the molecules.
Reason (R): DNA fragments can be manipulated
using restriction endonucleases and DNA ligases.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is NOT a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true because recombinant
DNA technology enables scientists to cut and
join DNA from any organism.
• Reason (R) is true as restriction
endonucleases act as molecular scissors, and
DNA ligase acts as molecular glue.
• These enzymes enable cutting and pasting of
DNA fragments precisely.
• Therefore, R correctly explains A by
describing the mechanism behind genetic
engineering.
• Hence, correct option is (a).
Q3. The cellular and molecular control of
programmed cell death is known as
(a) Apoptosis

(b) Ageing
(c) Degeneration
(d) Necrosis
The correct option is (a).
• Apoptosis is a programmed cell death
process vital for development and tissue
homeostasis.
• It is a controlled mechanism eliminating
unwanted or damaged cells without
inflammation.
• Necrosis is uncontrolled cell death due to
injury, leading to inflammation.
• Ageing and degeneration are broader
phenomena that may involve apoptosis.
• Hence, correct option is (a).
Q4. Which organelle in the cell, other than nucleus,
contains DNA?
(a) Centriole
(b) Golgi apparatus
(c) Lysosome
(d) Mitochondrion
The correct option is (d).
• Mitochondria contain their own circular DNA,
known as mitochondrial DNA (mtDNA).
• This supports the endosymbiotic theory,
suggesting mitochondria were once freeliving prokaryotes.
• Chloroplasts in plants also contain DNA.
• Golgi apparatus, lysosomes, and centrioles
lack DNA.
• Hence, correct option is (d).
Q5. Epiphytes are plants which depend on other
plants for
(a) Food
(b) Mechanical support
(c) Shade
(d) Water
The correct option is (b).
• Epiphytes grow on other plants for
mechanical support but are non-parasitic.
• They derive moisture and nutrients from the
air and rain, not from the host plant.

•
•

Growing higher provides better sunlight and
less competition.
Hence, correct option is (b).

Q6. Antigen is a substance which
(a) Destroys harmful bacteria
(b) Is used to treat poisoning
(c) Lowers body-temperature
(d) Stimulates formation of antibody
The correct option is (d).
• An antigen is any foreign molecule that
triggers an immune response.
• It induces B-lymphocytes to produce
antibodies specific to it.
• The term “antigen” means antibody
generator.
• Antigens do not directly destroy pathogens;
they trigger immune defense.
• Hence, correct option is (d).
Q7. Which of the following features of DNA makes it
uniquely suited to store and transmit genetic
information from generation to generation?
(a) Complementarity of the two strands
(b) Double helix
(c) Number of base-pairs per turn
(d) Sugar-phosphate backbone
The correct option is (a).
• Complementary base pairing enables
accurate DNA replication.
• Adenine (A) pairs with Thymine (T) and
Guanine (G) pairs with Cytosine (C).
• Each strand serves as a template for the
formation of its complementary strand.
• This ensures precise transmission of genetic
information.
• While other features are important,
complementarity ensures fidelity of
inheritance.
• Hence, correct option is (a).
Q8. The American multinational company, Monsanto
has produced an insect-resistant cotton variety that
is undergoing field trials in India. A toxin gene from
which ONE of the following bacteria has been

transferred to this transgenic cotton?
(a) Bacillus subtilis
(b) Bacillus thuringiensis
(c) Bacillus amyloliquifaciens
(d) Bacillus globii
The correct option is (b).
• The insect-resistant variety is known as Bt
cotton.
• “Bt” stands for Bacillus thuringiensis, a
bacterium producing Bt toxin.
• The toxin gene is inserted into cotton DNA,
making the plant resistant to bollworms.
• The plant produces the toxin itself, killing the
pest larvae.
• Hence, correct option is (b).

Q10. Consider the following statements:
1. Tapeworm is a hermaphrodite.
2. Round-worm has separate sexes.

3. Filaria is caused by a nematode.

Which of these are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 1, 2 and 3
(c) 3 and 4
(d) 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (b).
• Tapeworms are hermaphrodites, having both
reproductive organs.
• Roundworms (Ascaris) are dioecious, having
separate sexes.
• Filaria is caused by filarial worms, which are
nematodes.
• Guinea-worm is also a nematode, not an

• Thus, 1, 2, and 3 are correct.
• Hence, correct option is (b).
Q11. Match List I with List II and select the correct

Q12. "Athlete's Foot" is a disease caused by
(a) Bacteria
(b) Fungus
(c) Protozoan
(d) Nematode
The correct option is (b).
• Athlete’s Foot (Tinea pedis) is a fungal
infection of the feet.
• It is caused by dermatophyte fungi that thrive
in warm, moist areas.
• It causes itching, redness, and peeling
between the toes.
• It is not caused by bacteria, protozoa, or
nematodes.
• Hence, correct option is (b).
Q13.In the eye donation, which part of the eye is
transplanted from the donor?
Options:
(a) Cornea
(b) Lens
(c) Retina
(d) The whole eye
Correct Option: (a)
• Only the cornea (transparent front part of the
eye) can be successfully transplanted.
• The cornea helps to focus light on the retina
for vision.
• Whole eye transplantation is not possible
because the optic nerve cannot be
reconnected to the brain.
• Thus, when an “eye transplant” is performed,
it means a corneal transplant.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q14.A man whose blood group is not known meets
with a serious accident and needs blood transfusion
immediately. Which one of the following blood groups
will be safe for transfusion?

(a) O, Rh–
(b) O, Rh+
(c) AB, Rh–
(d) AB, Rh+
Correct Option: (a)
• O, Rh– (O negative) is called the universal
donor blood group.
• It contains no A or B antigens on red blood
cells and no Rh antigen, so it can be given
safely to any recipient.
• O, Rh+ can only be given to Rh-positive
individuals.
• AB group is the universal recipient, not
donor.
• Therefore, in emergency transfusions where
blood type is unknown, O, Rh– is used.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q15.“Metastasis” is the process by which —
(a) Cells divide rapidly under the influence of drugs
(b) Cancer cells spread through the blood or lymphatic
system to other sites or organs
(c) The chromosomes in cell nuclei attach to the
spindle before moving to poles
(d) Cancer cells are successfully inhibited to divide
further
Correct Option: (b)
Explanation:
• Metastasis is the process of cancer cell
spread from the primary site to secondary
sites.
• Cancer cells travel via the bloodstream or
lymphatic system.
• The new sites often include bones, liver,
lungs, or brain.
• This leads to the formation of secondary
tumors — a hallmark of malignant cancer.
• The spread makes treatment more difficult
and is a key reason for cancer-related deaths.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q16. Match List I with List II and select the correct
code:

List I
(Substance)

List II (Physiological Role)

A. Ptyalin

1. Converts angioten-sinogen in
blood into angiotensin

B. Pepsin

2. Digests starch

C. Renin

3. Digests proteins
4. Hydrolyses fats

D. Oxytocin

Codes:
ABCD

(b) 3 4 2 5
(c) 2 3 5 1
(d) 3 1 2 4
Correct Option: (a)
• Ptyalin (Salivary amylase): Enzyme in saliva;
