UPSC PYQs – 2002
Q1. Among living organisms, which one of the
following is the most responsible factor for bringing
about the origin of a new species?
(a) Isolation
(b) Mutation
(c) Natural Selection
(d) Sexual Reproduction
The correct option is (b).
• The origin of a new species (speciation)
requires the introduction of new genetic
variation.

•

•

•

•

•

•

Mutation is the ultimate source of all new
genetic variation. A mutation is a permanent
change in the DNA sequence of an organism.
While Natural Selection (c) is the primary
mechanism that drives evolution by favoring
advantageous traits, it can only act on the
variation that already exists.
Isolation (a) (geographic or reproductive) is
crucial to prevent gene flow and allow
divergence, but isolation itself does not
create genetic differences; it allows
differences created by mutation and sorted
by natural selection to accumulate.
Sexual Reproduction (d) shuffles existing
genes into new combinations
(recombination), creating variation among
individuals, but it does not create entirely
new genes or alleles.
Therefore, mutation is the most fundamental
factor because it creates the raw material
(new genetic information) upon which all
other evolutionary forces act to bring about
new species.
Hence, correct option is (b).

Q2. Ticks and mites are actually
(a) Arachnids
(b) Crustaceans
(c) Insects
(d) Myriapods
The correct option is (a).
• Ticks and mites belong to the class Arachnida.
• Arachnids (adults) characteristically have
eight legs and a body divided into
cephalothorax and abdomen, and no
antennae.
• Insects (c) have six legs, three body parts, and
usually antennae.
• Crustaceans (b) (crabs, lobsters, shrimp) are
mostly aquatic and have two pairs of
antennae.
• Myriapods (d) (centipedes, millipedes) have
many segments with one or two pairs of legs
per segment.
• Hence, correct option is (a).

Q3. With reference to the blood in a normal person,
which one of the following statements is correct?
(a) Compared to arteries, veins are less numerous and
hold less of the body's blood at any given time
(b) Blood cells constitute about 70 per cent of the
total volume of the blood
(c) White Blood Cells (WBC) are made by lymph nodes
only
(d) The blood has more platelets than WBC
The correct option is (d).
• Statement (d) is correct: a normal platelet
count (150,000–400,000/µL) is far greater
than normal WBC count (4,500–11,000/µL).
• Statement (a) is incorrect: the venous system
holds the majority (~60–70%) of the body's
blood; veins act as a blood reservoir.
• Statement (b) is incorrect: blood cells make
up about 45% of blood volume (the rest is
plasma ~55%).
• Statement (c) is incorrect: WBCs are
produced primarily in the bone marrow;
lymph nodes are sites of
maturation/proliferation for some WBC types
but not the sole site of production.
• Hence, correct option is (d).
Q4. Consider the following plants:
1. Bougainvillea
2. Carnations
3. Cocoa
4. Grapes
Which of these plants are propagated by stem
cuttings?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1, 3 and 4
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (d).
• Propagation by stem cutting is a common
method where a piece of stem develops roots
to form a new plant.
• Bougainvillea (1) is commonly propagated
from stem cuttings.
• Carnations (2) are frequently propagated by
stem cuttings.

•
•

•
•

Cocoa (3) can be propagated by stem
cuttings, though it may be more challenging.
Grapes (4) are almost universally propagated
by dormant hardwood stem cuttings
(standard commercial practice).
Since all four can be propagated this way, all
statements are correct.
Hence, correct option is (d).

Q5. In the context of the organic evolution, the loss
of limbs of snakes is explained by the phenomenon
of
(a) use and disuse of organs
(b) adaptation to living in burrows
(c) natural selection
(d) inheritance of acquired characters
The correct option is (a).
• The question refers to Lamarck's evolutionary
theory, whose central idea is the "Law of Use
and Disuse."
• Lamarck proposed that organs used
frequently become stronger/developed,
while those not used (disuse) shrink and
disappear over generations.
• The classic example is the loss of limbs in
snakes: ancestors that stopped using legs
(e.g., adopted burrowing/slithering) lost them
by disuse over generations.
• Modern evolutionary theory (Darwinian
natural selection) offers a different
mechanism (random mutations selected for),
but the question's phenomenon is a textbook
example of the Lamarckian use and disuse
idea.
• "Inheritance of acquired characters" (d) is
another part of Lamarck's theory, but "use
and disuse" (a) is the specific phenomenon
explaining the loss itself.
• Hence, correct option is (a).
Q6. When one gene controls two or more different
characters simultaneously, the phenomenon is called
(a) Apomixis
(b) Pleiotropy

(c) Polyploidy
(d) Polyteny
The correct option is (b).
• Pleiotropy is when a single gene influences
multiple, seemingly unrelated phenotypic
traits.
• Example: Phenylketonuria (PKU) — a
mutation in one gene leads to intellectual
disability, fair skin/hair, seizures, illustrating
multiple effects from one gene. Marfan
syndrome is another example where one
defective gene affects skeleton, eyes,
cardiovascular system.
• Apomixis (a): asexual seed production
without fertilization.
• Polyploidy (c): organism has >2 complete sets
of chromosomes.
• Polyteny (d): giant chromosomes from
repeated DNA replication without cell division
(in certain insect cells).
• Hence, correct option is (b).
Q7. With reference to the human body consider the
following statements:
1. The production of somatotropin goes up
when a person exercises
2. Men's testes produce progesterone
3. Women's adrenal glands secrete testosterone
4. Stress causes the adrenals to release very less
amount of cortisol than usual
Which of these statements are correct?
(a) 1, 2, 3 and 4
(b) 1, 2 and 4
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 2 and 4
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. Somatotropin
(Human Growth Hormone, HGH) is released
by the pituitary gland; secretion is stimulated
by factors including strenuous exercise, deep
sleep, and fasting.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. Testes primarily
produce testosterone; progesterone is mainly
a female sex hormone produced by ovaries.

•

•

•
•

Statement 3 is correct. Adrenal glands in
both sexes secrete small amounts of
androgens, including testosterone, so women
do produce some testosterone via the
adrenals.
Statement 4 is incorrect. Cortisol is a "stress
hormone"; stress increases adrenal cortisol
release, not decreases it.
Therefore, only 1 and 3 are correct.
Hence, correct option is (c).

Q8. Which one of the following is monogamous?
(a) Wolf
(b) Walrus
(c) Seal
(d) Deer
The correct option is (a).
• Monogamy: an individual has one partner for
life or at a time (serial monogamy).
• Wolves (gray wolves) typically form longterm pair bonds; a pack is often a family unit
led by a mated pair, usually bonded for life.
• Walruses, seals, deer are generally
polygynous (one male mates with multiple
females); males compete for access to groups
of females and do not form long-term pair
bonds.
• Hence, correct option is (a).
Q9. In a bisexual flower, if androecium and
gynoecium mature at different times, the
phenomenon is known as
(a) Dichogamy
(b) Herkogamy
(c) Heterogamy
(d) Monogamy
The correct option is (a).
• Dichogamy: temporal separation of
maturation of male (androecium) and female
(gynoecium) parts to prevent self-pollination
and encourage cross-pollination.
• Types: Protandry (male matures first) and
Protogyny (female matures first).
• Herkogamy (b): spatial separation of anthers
and stigma (not temporal).

•

•

Heterogamy/Monogamy are terms more
related to animal mating systems, not floral
maturation timing.
Hence, correct option is (a).

Q10. Foot-and-Mouth disease in animals, a current
epidemic in some parts of the world, is caused by
(a) Bacterium
(b) Fungus
(c) Protozoan
(d) Virus
The correct option is (d).
• Foot-and-Mouth Disease (FMD) affects
cloven-hoofed animals (cattle, pigs, sheep,
goats).
• It is caused by a virus: an Aphthovirus in the
Picornaviridae family, with multiple
serotypes.
• FMD is highly contagious and economically
devastating due to trade restrictions and
livestock losses.
• It is not caused by bacteria, fungi, or
protozoa.
• Hence, correct option is (d).
Q11. Which one of the following statements is
correct?
(a) All echinoderms are viviparous
(b) Round worm has no circulatory system
(c) In bony fishes, swim bladder is usually present
(d) In cartilaginous fishes, fertilisation is internal
The correct option is (c).
• Statement (c) is correct: most bony fishes
(Osteichthyes) possess a swim bladder, a gasfilled organ that controls buoyancy and helps
maintain depth without constant swimming.
• Statement (a) is incorrect: echinoderms
(starfish, sea urchins) typically use external
fertilization and are not viviparous.
• Statement (b) is incorrect as phrased:
roundworms (nematodes) have a
pseudocoelom that facilitates circulation of
nutrients (a simple circulatory arrangement),
though not a closed circulatory system like
higher animals.

•

•

Statement (d): many cartilaginous fishes
(Chondrichthyes) do exhibit internal
fertilization, but among the choices the most
universally correct defining statement is (c)
about swim bladder presence in bony fishes.
Hence, correct option is (c).

Q12.Which one of the following statements is
correct?
(a) Prions are the smallest free-living cells
(b) The cell wall of Mycoplasmas is made up of amino
sugars
(c) Viroids consist of a single-stranded RNA molecule
(d) Rickettsias lack cell wall
Correct Option: (c)
• Viroids are among the smallest known
infectious agents, smaller than viruses.
• They consist of a short, circular, singlestranded RNA molecule without any protein
coat (capsid).
• They infect plants, causing diseases like
potato spindle tuber disease.
• Prions are infectious proteins, not living cells.
• Mycoplasmas have no cell wall at all.
• Rickettsias do possess a cell wall, though they
are obligate intracellular bacteria.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q13.Assertion (A): Drinking of whisky increases the
frequency of urination.
Reason (R): Alcohol intake speeds up the secretion of
vasopressin in the body.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
Correct Option: (c)
• Assertion (A) is true — alcohol consumption
increases urination frequency (diuretic
effect).
• Reason (R) is false — alcohol actually inhibits
the secretion of vasopressin (ADH), not
increases it.

•

Vasopressin helps the kidneys reabsorb
water; when its secretion decreases, the
kidneys excrete more urine.
• Therefore, alcohol causes dehydration and
increased urination due to suppression of
vasopressin.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q14.Assertion (A): Human diet should compulsorily
contain Glycine, Serine, and Tyrosine.
Reason (R): Essential amino acids cannot be
synthesised in the human body.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
Correct Option: (d)
• Glycine, Serine, and Tyrosine are nonessential amino acids — they can be
synthesised by the human body.
• Therefore, the assertion (A) is false — they
are not compulsory in the diet.
• The reason (R) is true — essential amino
acids cannot be synthesized by the body and
must be obtained from food.
• Examples of essential amino acids:
o Arginine (for children), Histidine,
Isoleucine, Leucine, Lysine,
Methionine, Phenylalanine,
Threonine, Tryptophan, Valine.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2003
Q1. With reference to normal human beings,
consider the following statements:
1. In response to the presence of HCL, secretin is
produced from the duodenum.
2. Enterogastrone is produced in the small
intestine in response to the presence of fatty
acids.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) Only 1

(b) Only 2
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. When acidic chyme
(partially digested food mixed with
hydrochloric acid, HCl) passes from the
stomach into the duodenum (the first part of
the small intestine), it stimulates specialized
cells (S cells) in the duodenal wall to produce
the hormone secretin.
• Secretin then signals the pancreas to release
bicarbonate, which neutralizes the acid.
• Statement 2 is correct. Enterogastrone is a
general term for any hormone secreted by
the small intestine that inhibits stomach
motility and secretion.
• The presence of fatty acids and lipids in the
small intestine is a primary trigger for the
release of these hormones (like
cholecystokinin or CCK, and Gastric Inhibitory
Peptide or GIP).
• This mechanism slows down the emptying of
the stomach, allowing more time for the
effective digestion of fats in the intestine.
• Since both statements accurately describe
physiological processes in the digestive
system, both are correct.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. With reference to the work of human kidney,
consider the following statements:
1. After the waste is removed in the kidney, the
cleaner blood is sent back through renal
artery.
2. From Bowman's capsule, the filtered liquid
passes through tiny tubes where much of the
glucose is reabsorbed and sent back to the
blood in the renal vein.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) Only 1
(b) Only 2
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).

•

•

•

•

•

•

•

•

Statement 1 is incorrect. The renal artery is
the vessel that carries "dirty" or unfiltered
blood to the kidney.
After the kidney filters this blood, the "clean"
blood exits the kidney through the renal
vein, not the renal artery.
Statement 2 is correct. The filtration process
begins in the glomerulus, where blood plasma
is forced into the Bowman's capsule.
This filtered liquid (filtrate) then passes
through a series of tiny tubes (the renal
tubule).
In these tubes, essential substances that the
body needs to keep, such as glucose, amino
acids, and most of the water, are reabsorbed
from the filtrate back into the blood.
This reabsorbed blood then collects in the
renal vein to be returned to general
circulation.
Therefore, only the second statement
correctly describes the process of
reabsorption in the kidney.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. Consider the following statements:
1. Molasses is a by-product of sugar production
process.
2. Bagasse obtained in the sugar mills is used as
a fuel in the boilers to generate steam for the
sugar factories.
3. Sugar can only be produced from sugarcane
as the raw material.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. Molasses is the thick,
dark, syrupy liquid that remains after sugar
crystals have been removed from sugarcane
or sugar beet juice.
• It is a key by-product of the sugar refining
process.

•

•

•

•
•

Statement 2 is correct. Bagasse is the fibrous
pulp left over after sugarcane stalks are
crushed to extract their juice.
This material is rich in cellulose and is
commonly used as a biofuel; in sugar mills it is
burned in boilers to produce steam,
generating electricity and heat to power the
factory.
Statement 3 is incorrect. While sugarcane is a
major source, sugar is also commercially
produced from sugar beets. Sugar can also be
derived from other sources like maple sap
and palms.
Therefore, only statements 1 and 2 are
correct.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q4. Octopus is:
(a) an arthropod
(b) an echinoderm
(c) a hemichordate
(d) a mollusc
The correct option is (d).
• An Octopus belongs to the phylum Mollusca
and the class Cephalopoda.
• This class also includes squid, cuttlefish, and
nautilus.
• Key characteristics of molluscs include a soft
body, which in many species is protected by a
hard shell; cephalopods like the octopus have
a highly developed nervous system and have
lost their external shell through evolution.
• Arthropods (a) have an exoskeleton and
jointed legs (insects, spiders).
• Echinoderms (b) are marine animals like
starfish and sea urchins with radial symmetry
and spiny skin.
• Hemichordates (c) are marine invertebrates
like acorn worms.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q5. Consider the following conditions of a sick
human body:
1. Swollen lymph nodes
2. Sweating at night

3. Loss of memory
4. Loss of weight
Which of these are the symptoms of AIDS?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1, 3 and 4
(d) 1, 2, 4
The correct option is (d).
• AIDS (Acquired Immunodeficiency
Syndrome) is the late stage of HIV infection,
where the immune system is severely
damaged.
• Swollen lymph nodes (1), particularly in the
neck, armpits, and groin, are a very common
and early symptom of HIV infection as the
immune system begins to fight the virus.
• Sweating at night (2), often drenching, is
another common symptom associated with
HIV/AIDS and opportunistic infections (e.g.,
tuberculosis).
• Loss of weight (4), often called "wasting
syndrome," is a defining characteristic of
advanced HIV/AIDS, where a person loses at
least 10% of their body weight
unintentionally.
• Loss of memory (3) can occur in very
advanced stages (AIDS dementia complex),
but it is not considered one of the most
common or primary symptoms compared to
the others.
• The other three symptoms (1, 2, and 4) are
much more characteristic and frequently
seen.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. Consider the following statements:
1. The common blue green algae Spirogyra and
Ulothrix are found in both fresh water ponds
and oceans.
2. The chameleon can look ahead with one eye,
and at the same time look behind with
another.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) Only 1
(b) Only 2

(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Spirogyra and
Ulothrix are types of green algae, not bluegreen algae (which are cyanobacteria).
• Furthermore, Spirogyra and Ulothrix are
found almost exclusively in freshwater
environments like ponds and slow-moving
streams; they are not typically found in
oceans.
• Statement 2 is correct. Chameleons have
remarkable eyes set in conical turrets; each
eye can move independently of the other,
giving a full 360-degree field of vision.
• This allows a chameleon to scan for prey with
one eye while watching for predators with
the other, e.g., looking forward and
backward at the same time.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Which one of the following statements is not
correct?
Options:
(a) Milk contains none of the B-vitamins
(b) Vitamin A (retinal) deficiency leads to dry and scaly
skin
(c) One of the symptoms of scurvy is pain in the joints
(d) Vitamin B1 (thiamine) deficiency can lead to heart
failure
Correct Option: (a)
• (a) Milk contains none of the B-vitamins —
Incorrect statement
o Milk is a rich source of several Bvitamins, including:
▪ Vitamin B1 (Thiamine)
▪ Vitamin B2 (Riboflavin)
▪ Vitamin B3 (Niacin)
▪ Vitamin B5 (Pantothenic
acid)
▪ Vitamin B6 (Pyridoxine)
▪ Vitamin B12 (Cobalamin)
▪ Folate
o Therefore, statement (a) is not
correct.

•

(b) Vitamin A deficiency → causes dry, scaly
skin and night blindness — Correct.
• (c) Scurvy (Vitamin C deficiency) → causes
joint pain, gum bleeding, and poor wound
healing — Correct.
• (d) Vitamin B1 deficiency → causes beriberi,
which can lead to heart failure (wet beriberi)
— Correct.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2004
Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. Non-function of lachrymal gland is an
important symptom of deficiency of Vitamin
A.
2. Deficiency of Vitamin B1 can lead to
indigestion and heart enlargement.
3. Vitamin C deficiency can lead to pain in the
muscles.
4. Deficiency of Vitamin D causes increased loss
of Ca++ in urine.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1, 3 and 4
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is correct. Vitamin A is crucial for
maintaining healthy epithelial tissues,
including the conjunctiva of the eye. A severe
deficiency leads to a condition called
xerophthalmia, where the lachrymal glands
(tear glands) fail to produce tears, causing
extreme dryness of the eyes.
• Statement 2 is correct. Vitamin B1
(Thiamine) deficiency causes the disease
beriberi. "Wet beriberi" specifically affects
the cardiovascular system, leading to
symptoms like an enlarged heart (heart
enlargement) and can impair nerve function
related to the digestive tract, causing
indigestion.
• Statement 3 is correct. Vitamin C (Ascorbic
acid) is essential for the formation of

•

•
•

collagen. A deficiency leads to scurvy, one of
the symptoms of which is pain in the muscles
and joints due to bleeding within these
tissues.
Statement 4 is correct. Vitamin D is essential
for the absorption of calcium (Ca++) from the
gut. When Vitamin D is deficient, the body
cannot absorb calcium properly. To maintain
blood calcium levels, it starts to break down
bone, and the kidneys are unable to reabsorb
calcium effectively, leading to increased loss
of Ca++ in the urine.
Since all four statements are correct, the
correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q2. The hormone insulin is a:
(a) Glycolipid
(b) Fatty acid
(c) Peptide
(d) Sterol
The correct option is (c).
• Hormones can be classified based on their
chemical structure. Insulin is a protein-based
hormone.
• Specifically, it is a peptide hormone,
composed of 51 amino acids arranged in two
chains (an A chain and a B chain) linked by
disulfide bonds.
• Glycolipids (a) are lipids with a carbohydrate
attached.
• Fatty acids (b) are building blocks of fats.
• Sterols (d) (or steroid hormones like
testosterone and estrogen) are derived from
cholesterol and have a characteristic four-ring
carbon structure. Insulin's structure is
fundamentally different from these.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. In which organ of the human body are the
lymphocyte cells formed?
(a) Liver
(b) Bone marrow
(c) Pancreas
(d) Spleen

The correct option is (b).
• Lymphocytes are a type of white blood cell
that is a fundamental part of the immune
system.
• All blood cells, including red cells, white cells,
and platelets, originate from hematopoietic
stem cells located in the bone marrow. This
process is called hematopoiesis.
• Therefore, lymphocytes are formed in the
primary lymphoid organ, the bone marrow.
• While lymphocytes mature and become
activated in other organs (T-cells mature in
the Thymus, and both B and T cells are
activated in secondary lymphoid organs like
the spleen and lymph nodes), their initial site
of formation is the bone marrow.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Assertion (A): Fatty acids should be a part of the
balanced human diet.
Reason (R): The cells of the human body cannot
synthesize any fatty acids.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true. Fatty acids are a crucial
component of a balanced diet. They are a
major source of energy and are essential for
absorbing fat-soluble vitamins (A, D, E, K).
Certain types of fatty acids, known as
essential fatty acids, are also required for
brain function, controlling inflammation, and
other vital processes.
• Reason (R) is false. The human body can
synthesize most of the fatty acids it needs
from carbohydrates and proteins. For
example, it can produce saturated and
monounsaturated fatty acids. However, it
cannot synthesize certain polyunsaturated
fatty acids, namely omega-3 (alpha-linolenic
acid) and omega-6 (linoleic acid). These are

•
•

called "essential fatty acids" precisely
because they must be obtained from the diet.
The statement that the body "cannot
synthesize any fatty acids" is incorrect.
Since the Assertion is true and the Reason is
false, the correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. Consider the following statements:
1. Femur is the longest bone in the human body.
2. Cholera is a disease caused by bacteria.
3. 'Athlete's foot' is a disease caused by virus.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. The femur, or thigh
bone, is indeed the longest, heaviest, and
strongest bone in the human body.
• Statement 2 is correct. Cholera is an acute
diarrhoeal infection caused by ingestion of
food or water contaminated with the
bacterium Vibrio cholerae.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. 'Athlete's foot'
(tinea pedis) is not caused by a virus. It is a
common skin infection caused by a fungus (a
type of dermatophyte).
• Therefore, only statements 1 and 2 are
correct.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

•

Pneumoconiosis is a group of occupational
lung diseases caused by inhalation of dust
particles and the lung’s reaction to them.
It is most commonly associated with workers
in coal mining, known as “black lung
disease.”
Other forms include silicosis (from silica dust)
and asbestosis (from asbestos fibers).
Workers in tanneries, distilleries, or glass
industries face other hazards, not
pneumoconiosis.
Hence, correct option is (b).

Q2. Assertion (A): All the proteins in our food are
digested in small intestine only.
Reason (R): The protein-digesting enzymes from
pancreas are released into small intestine.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is false: Protein digestion begins
in the stomach by pepsin, not only in the
small intestine.
• Reason (R) is true: The pancreas releases
proteolytic enzymes (trypsin, chymotrypsin)
into the small intestine for further digestion.
• Hence, digestion occurs in both stomach and
small intestine.
• Therefore, Assertion is false, Reason is true.
• Hence, correct option is (d).

Q3. Assertion (A): Amoeba reproduces by fission.
Reason (R): All unicellular organisms reproduce by
asexual methods.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•
•

Assertion (A) is true: Amoeba reproduces
asexually by binary fission, producing two
identical daughter cells.
Reason (R) is false: Not all unicellular
organisms reproduce asexually. Yeast and
Paramecium can also undergo sexual
reproduction (e.g., conjugation).
Hence, A is true but R is false.
Therefore, correct option is (c).

Q4. Assertion (A): The person with diabetes insipidus
feels thirsty.
Reason (R): A person with diabetes insipidus suffers
from excess secretion of vasopressin.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true: Diabetes insipidus
causes excessive urination (polyuria) and
intense thirst (polydipsia).
• Reason (R) is false: It is caused by a deficiency
or ineffectiveness of vasopressin (antidiuretic
hormone), not excess.
• Lack of vasopressin leads to failure of water
reabsorption in kidneys, causing dehydration.
• Hence, A is true, R is false.
• Therefore, correct option is (c).

UPSC PYQs – 2006

Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. Caffeine, a constituent of tea and coffee is a
diuretic.
2. Citric acid is used in soft drinks.
3. Ascorbic acid is essential for the formation of
bones and teeth.
4. Citric acid is a good substitution for ascorbic
acid in our nutrition.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2, only
(b) 1, 2 and 3, only
(c) 3 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. Caffeine is a wellknown central nervous system stimulant and
has a mild diuretic effect, causing the kidneys
to excrete more sodium and water.
• Statement 2 is correct. Citric acid is widely
used in the food and beverage industry,
including soft drinks, for tartness and as a
preservative.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. Ascorbic acid
(Vitamin C) is essential for collagen synthesis,
not directly for structural formation of bones
and teeth (those rely primarily on calcium and
phosphorus, and Vitamin D for absorption).
• Statement 4 is incorrect. Citric acid and
ascorbic acid are chemically different; citric
acid cannot substitute for the essential
biological functions of ascorbic acid.
Hence correct option is (a).

Q2. What is the name of the vessel that delivers the
nutrient rich blood from the stomach & small
intestine to the liver?
(a) Left hepatic artery
(b) Hepatic vein
(c) Right hepatic artery
(d) Hepatic portal vein
The correct option is (d).
• After digestion, nutrient-rich but oxygen-poor
blood from the digestive organs is collected
into the Hepatic Portal Vein.
• The hepatic portal vein transports this blood
directly to the liver for processing and
detoxification before release into general
circulation.
• Hepatic arteries supply oxygen-rich blood to
the liver, while the hepatic vein carries
processed, deoxygenated blood away from
the liver.
Hence correct option is (d).
Q3. Consider the following statements with
reference to human body:
1. The common bile duct release its contents
into stomach.
2. The pancreatic duct releases its contents into
duodenum.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The common bile
duct carries bile from the liver and
gallbladder and releases it into the
duodenum, not the stomach.
• Statement 2 is correct. The pancreatic duct
carries digestive enzymes from the pancreas
and releases them into the duodenum to aid
digestion.
• Since both ducts empty into the duodenum,
only statement 2 is correct.
Hence correct option is (b).

Q4. Consider the following statements:

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Meningococcal

meningitis include high fever, headache, stiff

while the remaining have Rh- blood.

(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. ELISA is primarily
used to detect antibodies or antigens (e.g.,
HIV screening). While some ELISA tests detect
tumor markers, ELISA is not the first or most
basic test for detecting cancer; biopsy and
imaging are more definitive.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The distribution of
Rh blood types is not 50/50; about 85% of

Q5. Which one among the following is not a good
source of nutritional calcium?
(a) Rice
(b) Ragi
(c) Skimmed milk
(d) Egg
The correct option is (a).
• Ragi (finger millet) is an exceptionally rich
source of calcium.
• Skimmed milk and other dairy products are
excellent sources of absorbable calcium.
• Eggs contain a moderate amount of calcium
(mainly in the yolk).
• Rice, especially polished white rice, is
primarily a carbohydrate source and contains
very little calcium, making it a poor source of
this mineral.
Hence correct option is (a).

Q7. Assertion (A): Cellulose is used in making shatter
proof glass.
Reason (R): Polysaccharides are not soluble in water.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A.
(c) A is True but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is false. Shatterproof (safety)
glass is made by laminating a plastic layer
(usually polyvinyl butyral, PVB) between glass
sheets; cellulose is not used for this purpose.
• Reason (R) is true. Many polysaccharides
(e.g., cellulose, starch) are insoluble or poorly
soluble in water due to their large size and
complex structure.
• Since A is false and R is true, the correct
option is (d).
Hence correct option is (d).

1. ELISA test is employed as the first and most
basic test for an individual to detect cancer.

UPSC PYQs – 2007
Q1. Which one of the following is the correct
sequence in the order of decreasing length of the
three structural parts given below of small intestine
in the human body?
(a) Jejunum - Duodenum- Ileum
(b) Ileum-Duodenum- Duodenum
(c) Jejunum-Ileum- Duodenum
(d) Ileum- Jejunum- Duodenum
The correct option is (d).
• The small intestine is composed of three
distinct parts. The order in which food passes
through them is Duodenum → Jejunum →
Ileum.
• Ileum: This is the final and longest section of
the small intestine, measuring approximately
3 meters (about 10 feet) in length.
• Jejunum: This is the middle section, located
between the duodenum and the ileum. It is
the second-longest part, measuring
approximately 2.5 meters (about 8 feet).
• Duodenum: This is the first and shortest
section of the small intestine. It is C-shaped
and measures only about 25–30 centimetres
(about 10–12 inches).
• Therefore, the correct sequence from longest
to shortest is Ileum – Jejunum – Duodenum.
• Hence correct option is (d).
Q2. In human body, which one of the following
hormones regulates blood calcium and Phosphate?
(a) Glucagon
(b) Growth hormone
(c) Parathyroid hormone
(d) Thyroxine
The correct option is (c).
• The regulation of calcium and phosphate
levels in the blood is primarily controlled by
the Parathyroid Hormone (PTH), which is
secreted by the parathyroid glands.
• When blood calcium levels drop, the
parathyroid glands release PTH. PTH acts in
three main ways to increase blood calcium:

•
•

•

It stimulates the release of calcium
from bones.
o It increases the reabsorption of
calcium in the kidneys, reducing its
loss in urine.
o It stimulates the production of active
Vitamin D in the kidneys, which in
turn increases calcium absorption
from the intestines.
PTH has the opposite effect on phosphate,
increasing its excretion by the kidneys.
Glucagon regulates blood sugar. Growth
hormone stimulates growth. Thyroxine
regulates metabolism.
Hence correct option is (c).

Q3. How do most insects respire?
(a) Through skin
(b) Through gills
(c) By lungs
(d) By tracheal system
The correct option is (d).
• Insects have a unique respiratory system that
is very different from that of vertebrates.
They do not have lungs.
• They respire through a network of small tubes
called tracheae. This network, known as the
tracheal system, branches throughout the
insect's entire body.
• Air enters this system through external
openings called spiracles. From the spiracles,
the air travels through the tracheae, which
branch into progressively smaller tubes called
tracheoles.
• These tracheoles deliver oxygen directly to
the body's cells and tissues and carry carbon
dioxide away. This direct gas exchange
system is highly efficient and is a defining
characteristic of insects.
• Respiration through skin is common in
amphibians. Respiration through gills is used
by fish. Respiration through lungs is used by
mammals, birds, and reptiles.
• Hence correct option is (d).

Q4. In human being, normally in which one of the
following parts, does the sperm fertilize the ovum?
(a) Cervix
(b) Fallopian tube
(c) Lower part of uterus
(d) Upper part of uterus
The correct option is (b).
• Fertilization in humans is the fusion of a
sperm with an ovum (egg) to form a zygote.
• After ovulation, the egg is released from the
ovary and travels into the Fallopian tube
(oviduct).
• Sperm travel from the vagina, through the
cervix and uterus, and into the fallopian
tubes.
• Fertilization typically occurs in the outer,
wider portion of the fallopian tube known as
the ampulla. The zygote then divides and
travels down the fallopian tube to implant in
the uterus.
• The cervix, lower uterus, and upper uterus
are parts of the pathway, but the actual event
of fertilization normally happens within the
fallopian tube.
• Hence correct option is (b).
Q5. Which one of the following parts of human brain
is the regulating centre for swallowing and vomiting?
(a) Cerebellum
(b) Cerebrum
(c) Medulla oblongata
(d) Pons
The correct option is (c).
• The Medulla Oblongata is the lower part of
the brainstem and is a crucial control center
for many of the body's vital autonomic
(involuntary) functions.
• It contains the reflex centers that control
actions such as vomiting, swallowing,
coughing, sneezing, and hiccupping.
• It also contains the cardiac, respiratory, and
vasomotor centers, which regulate heart
rate, breathing, and blood pressure,
respectively.

•

•

The cerebellum is primarily responsible for
coordinating voluntary movements, posture,
and balance. The cerebrum handles higher
functions like thought and action. The pons is
involved in functions like sleep, respiration,
and facial sensation, but the primary reflex
center for swallowing and vomiting is the
medulla.
Hence correct option is (c).

Q6. Production of which one of the following is a
function of the liver?
(a) Lipase
(b) Urea
(c) Mucus
(d) Hydrochloric acid
The correct option is (b).
• The liver plays a central role in protein
metabolism. When proteins are broken down,
they produce ammonia, which is highly toxic.
• The liver performs the urea cycle, converting
toxic ammonia into urea, a much less toxic
compound.
• This urea is released into the bloodstream,
transported to the kidneys, and excreted in
urine.
• Lipase is primarily produced by the pancreas.
Mucus is produced by mucous membranes.
Hydrochloric acid is produced by parietal cells
in the stomach.
• Hence correct option is (b).
Q7. Which one of the following is not a digestive
enzyme in the human systems?
(a) Trypsin
(b) Gastrin
(c) Ptyalin
(d) Pepsin
The correct option is (b).
• Gastrin is a hormone, not a digestive enzyme.
It is produced by G-cells in the stomach lining
and stimulates the stomach to secrete gastric
acid (HCl) and pepsin.

•

•
•
•

Trypsin is a digestive enzyme from the
pancreas that breaks down proteins in the
small intestine.
Ptyalin (salivary amylase) is an enzyme in
saliva that begins the digestion of starch.
Pepsin is a stomach enzyme that begins
protein breakdown.
Hence correct option is (b).

Q8. Which of the following types of light are strongly
absorbed by plants?
(a) Violet and orange
(b) Blue and red
(c) Indigo and yellow
(d) Yellow and violet
The correct option is (b).
• Plants perform photosynthesis using
pigments, primarily chlorophyll a and
chlorophyll b.
• These chlorophyll pigments have specific
absorption spectra; they strongly absorb light
in the blue–violet and red–orange parts of
the visible spectrum.
• They do not absorb green and yellow light
well; instead, they reflect it, which is why
plants appear green.
• Because plants absorb blue and red light most
strongly, these wavelengths are most
effective for driving photosynthesis.
• Hence correct option is (b).
Q9. In the human body, which structure is the
appendix attached to?
(a) The large intestine
(b) The small intestine
(c) The gall bladder
(d) The stomach
The correct option is (a).
• The appendix (vermiform appendix) is a
small, finger-like, blind-ended tube that is
attached to the cecum.
• The cecum is a pouch that forms the
beginning of the large intestine. Therefore,
the appendix is structurally an outpouching

•

•

of the large intestine (located at the junction
of the small and large intestine).
Though once considered vestigial, the
appendix may play a role in the immune
system and act as a storage for beneficial gut
bacteria.
Hence correct option is (a).

UPSC PYQs – 2008
Q1. Consider the following statements about
probiotic food:
1. Probiotic food contains live bacteria which are
considered beneficial to humans.
2. Probiotic food helps in maintaining gut flora.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 Only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. Probiotic foods
contain adequate amounts of live
microorganisms (primarily bacteria and some
yeasts) that, when consumed, confer a health
benefit on the host.
• Statement 2 is correct. One primary benefit
of probiotics is to help restore and maintain
the natural balance of microorganisms in the
digestive tract (gut flora/microbiota).

•

•

Since both statements accurately define key
characteristics and functions of probiotic
foods, both are correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. What is the pH level of blood of normal person?
(a) 4.5-4.5
(b) 6.45-6.55
(c) 7.35-7.45
(d) 8.25-8.35
The correct option is (c).
• The pH of human blood is tightly regulated
for homeostasis; the normal arterial blood pH
range is 7.35 to 7.45.
• Blood is therefore slightly alkaline (neutral pH
= 7.0).
• pH < 7.35 = acidosis and pH > 7.45 = alkalosis,
both potentially life-threatening.
• The other ranges listed are incorrect for
normal blood.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Assertion (A) : In Human body, liver has an
important role in fat digestion.
Reason (R): Liver produces two importance fatdigesting enzymes.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true and R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true. The liver plays a crucial,
albeit indirect, role in fat digestion by
producing bile, which emulsifies fats into
smaller droplets.
• Reason (R) is false. The liver does not
produce fat-digesting enzymes; lipases are
primarily produced by the pancreas (and
partly by stomach/intestine).
• The Reason therefore gives an incorrect
explanation for the Assertion.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. Among the following, which one lays eggs and
does not produce young ones directly?
(a) Echidna
(b) Kangaroo
(c) Porcupine
(d) Whale
The correct option is (a).
• The Echidna (and the platypus) are
monotremes, a group of mammals that lay
eggs instead of giving birth to live young.
• The female echidna lays a single soft-shelled
egg, incubates it in a pouch, and after
hatching the puggle feeds on milk secreted
from pores.
• Kangaroo, porcupine, and whale give birth to
live young (marsupial or placental mammals).
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. The release of which one of the following into
ponds and wells helps in controlling the mosquitoes?
(a) Crab
(b) Dogfish
(c) Gambusia fish
(d) Snail
The correct option is (c).
• Gambusia (mosquitofish) are small
freshwater fish that are voracious predators
of mosquito larvae.
• They have been widely introduced into
standing water bodies (ponds, wells) as
biological control to reduce mosquito
populations and vector-borne disease risk.
• Crabs, dogfish, and snails do not significantly
control mosquito larvae.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. Which one of the following is an insectivorous
plant?
(a) Passion flower plant
(b) Pitcher plant
(c) Night queen
(d) Flame of the forest
The correct option is (b).

•

•

•

•

Pitcher plants are classic insectivorous
(carnivorous) plants whose modified leaves
form a pitcher containing digestive fluid.
Insects are lured by nectar/colour, fall into
the pitcher, are digested, and their nutrients
(e.g., nitrogen) are absorbed.
Examples also include the Venus flytrap and
sundews; the other options are not
insectivorous.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q7. For which one of the following snakes is the diet
mainly composed of other snakes?
(a) Krait
(b) Russell's viper
(c) Rattlesnake
(d) King cobra
The correct option is (d).
• The King Cobra (Ophiophagus hannah) is
specialized as a snake-eater; its name
Ophiophagus literally means “snake-eating.”
• Its primary prey includes other snakes (both
non-venomous and venomous).
• Kraits, Russell’s viper, and rattlesnakes
primarily feed on rodents and other small
vertebrates.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. In which one of the following kinds of organisms
is the phenomenon found wherein the female kills
the male after copulation?
(a) Dragonfly
(b) Honeybee
(c) Spider
(d) Pit viper
The correct option is (c).
• The behavior of the female killing and
consuming the male (sexual cannibalism) is
well-documented in certain spider species
(e.g., black widow, some orb-weavers) and in
praying mantises.
• In honeybees, drones die after mating
because of reproductive organ rupture, not
because the female kills them.

•
•

This behavior is not characteristic of
dragonflies or pit vipers.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2009

Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. Sweet orange plant is propagated by grafting
technique.
2. Jasmine plant is propagated by layering
technique.
Which of the statements given above is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. Grafting is a standard
horticultural technique used to propagate
citrus trees, including sweet orange. The
scion (bud/shoot of desired variety) is joined
onto the rootstock of another tree, ensuring
fruit quality and a strong root system.
• Statement 2 is correct. Layering is a
propagation method where a stem attached
to the parent plant is made to form roots
while still attached. Jasmine plants, with
flexible stems, are commonly propagated this
way.
• Since both statements describe correct
propagation methods, both are correct.
• Hence correct option is (c).
Q2. In the context of genetic disorders, consider the
following:
A woman suffers from colour blindness while her
husband does not suffer from it. They have a son and
a daughter. In this context, which one of the following
statements is most probably correct?
(a) Both children suffer from colour blindness.
(b) Daughter suffers from colour blindness while son
does not suffer from it.
(c) Both children do not suffer from colour blindness.
(d) Son suffers from colour blindness while daughter
does not suffer from it.

The correct option is (d).
• Colour blindness is an X-linked recessive
disorder.
• The mother (colour-blind) genotype = XcXc,
and the father (normal) = XNY.
• Son: inherits Y from father and Xc from
mother → XcY → colour-blind.
• Daughter: inherits XN from father and Xc
from mother → XNXc → normal vision, but
carrier.
• Thus, son is colour-blind, daughter is not.
• Hence correct option is (d).

Q3. Consider the following:
1. Camphor
2. Chicory
3. Vanilla
Which of the above is/are plant product(s)?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Camphor: Obtained from Cinnamomum
camphora tree; used as an aromatic and
medicinal plant product.
• Chicory: A flowering plant whose roots are
used as a coffee substitute and leaves in
salads.
• Vanilla: Derived from vanilla orchids; the
flavouring comes from cured seed pods.
• All three are plant-derived products.
• Hence correct option is (d).
Q4. In making the saffron spice, which one of the
following parts of the plant is used?

(a) Leaf
(b) Petal
(c) Sepal
(d) Stigma
The correct option is (d).
• Saffron is obtained from the stigma and
styles of the Crocus sativus flower.
• The stigma (female reproductive part) is
collected and dried to make the spice.
• Each flower has only three stigmas, requiring
thousands of flowers for small quantities,
making it highly valuable.
• Hence correct option is (d).
Q5. With reference to the evolution of living
organisms, which one of the following sequences is
correct?
(a) Octopus - Dolphin - Shark
(b) Pangolin - Tortoise - Hawk
(c) Salamander - Python - Kangaroo
(d) Frog - Crab - Prawn
The correct option is (c).
• Salamander: Amphibian – first vertebrates
adapted to land.
• Python: Reptile – evolved from amphibians,
fully terrestrial.
• Kangaroo: Mammal – evolved later from
reptilian ancestors (synapsids).
• Sequence follows Amphibian → Reptile →
Mammal, matching evolutionary progression.
• Other options mix unrelated or incorrect
evolutionary groups.
• Hence correct option is (c).
UPSC PYQs – 2010
Q1. Widespread resistance of malarial parasite to
drugs like chloroquine has prompted attempts to
develop a malarial vaccine to combat malaria. Why is
it difficult to develop an effective malaria vaccine?
(a) Malaria is caused by several species of Plasmodium
(b) Man does not develop immunity to malaria during
natural infection
(c) Vaccines can be developed only against bacteria

(d) Man is only an intermediate host and not the
definitive host
The correct option is (a).
• The complexity of the Plasmodium parasite
makes vaccine development difficult.
• Malaria is caused by multiple species — P.
falciparum, P. vivax, P. malariae, P. ovale —
each presenting different antigens.
• The parasite’s life cycle includes multiple
stages (sporozoite, merozoite, gametocyte),
each expressing different surface antigens,
complicating vaccine targeting.
• The parasite also changes surface proteins to
evade immune detection.
• Other options are incorrect:
o Humans develop partial immunity (b).
o Vaccines exist for viruses and
parasites, not just bacteria (c).
o Humans being intermediate hosts (d)
is not the main issue.
• Hence, correct option is (a).
Q2. A pesticide which is a chlorinated hydrocarbon is
sprayed on a food crop. The food chain is: Food crop
- Rat - Snake - Hawk. In this food chain, the highest
concentration of the pesticide would accumulate in
which one of the following?
(a) Food crop
(b) Rat
(c) Snake
(d) Hawk
The correct option is (d).
• The process involved is biomagnification,
where persistent, fat-soluble chemicals (like
DDT) increase in concentration at higher
trophic levels.
• Food crop absorbs pesticide → rat eats
multiple crops and accumulates it → snake
eats many rats → hawk (top predator)
consumes many snakes.
• Thus, hawk has the highest concentration
due to cumulative accumulation through the
food chain.
• Hence, correct option is (d).

Q3. King Cobra is the only snake that makes its own
nest. Why does it make its nest?
(a) It is a snake-eater and the nest helps attract other
snakes
(b) It is a viviparous snake and needs a nest to give
birth to its offspring
(c) It is an oviparous snake and lays its eggs in the nest
and guards the nest until they are hatched
(d) It is a large, cold-blooded animal and needs a nest
to hibernate in the cold season
The correct option is (c).
• The King Cobra is oviparous — it lays eggs,
not live young.
• The female builds a nest of leaves and debris,
lays eggs inside, and guards the nest until
near hatching.
• The decaying vegetation provides heat for
incubation.
• This parental care is rare among snakes.
• Hence, correct option is (c).
Q4. Some species of plants are insectivorous. Why?
(a) Their growth in shady and dark places does not
allow sufficient photosynthesis
(b) They are adapted to grow in nitrogen-deficient
soils and thus depend on insects for sufficient
nitrogenous nutrition
(c) They cannot synthesize certain vitamins
themselves and depend on the insects digested by
them
(d) They have remained in that stage of evolution as
living fossils
The correct option is (b).
• Insectivorous plants (e.g., pitcher plant,
sundew, Venus flytrap) grow in nitrogen-poor
soils such as bogs and marshes.
• They perform photosynthesis for energy but
trap insects to obtain nitrogen and minerals
required for protein and DNA synthesis.
• It is an adaptation for nutrient deficiency, not
for energy or vitamin needs.
• Hence, correct option is (b).
Q5. Which one of the following processes in the
bodies of living organisms is a digestive process?

(a) Breakdown of proteins into amino acids
(b) Breakdown of glucose into CO₂ and H₂O
(c) Conversion of glucose into glycogen
(d) Conversion of amino acids into proteins
The correct option is (a).
• Digestion involves breaking down complex
food molecules into simpler absorbable
forms.
• Proteins → Amino acids is a catabolic
digestive process, occurring via enzymes like
pepsin and trypsin.
• Other options describe metabolic or synthetic
processes, not digestion.
• Hence, correct option is (a).
Q6. From the point of view of evolution of living
organisms, which one of the following is the correct
sequence of evolution?
(a) Otter - Tortoise – Shark
(b) Shark - Tortoise – Otter
(c) Tortoise - Shark – Otter
(d) Shark - Otter - Tortoise
The correct option is (b).
• Sharks (fish) → first vertebrates (~500 million
years ago).
• Tortoises (reptiles) → evolved from
amphibians (~315 million years ago).
• Otters (mammals) → evolved later from
reptilian ancestors (~225 million years ago).
• Thus, the evolutionary sequence is Fish →
Reptile → Mammal.
• Hence, correct option is (b).
Q7. Consider the following statements:
1. Hepatitis B is several times more infectious
than HIV/AIDS.
2. Hepatitis B can cause liver cancer.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•
•

Statement 1 is correct: Hepatitis B virus is 50–
100 times more infectious than HIV due to
higher viral load in blood and bodily fluids.
Statement 2 is correct: Chronic Hepatitis B
infection causes liver cirrhosis and can lead to
hepatocellular carcinoma (liver cancer).
Both statements are scientifically accurate.
Hence, correct option is (c).

Q8.Consider the following statements:
1. Every individual in the population is equally
susceptible host for Swine Flu.
2. Antibiotics have no role in the primary
treatment of Swine Flu.
3. To prevent the future spread of Swine Flu in
the epidemic area, the swine (pigs) must all be
culled.
Which of the statements given above, is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Correct Option: (a)
• (1) Every individual is equally susceptible
host —
o Swine Flu is caused by the H1N1
influenza virus, transmitted humanto-human, not just pig-to-human.
o Technically, all humans can be
infected if exposed, though children,
pregnant women, elderly, and
immunocompromised individuals are
at higher risk.
• (2) Antibiotics have no role — Correct
o Swine Flu is viral, not bacterial;
antibiotics do not kill viruses.
o Treatment involves rest, fluids,
antipyretics, and antiviral drugs like
oseltamivir (Tamiflu), not antibiotics.
• (3) Culling pigs is not necessary — Incorrect
o The H1N1 strain now spreads mainly
among humans.
o Mass culling of pigs is not a
preventive measure for human Swine
Flu epidemics.

Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9.With regard to the transmission of the Human
Immunodeficiency Virus (HIV), which one of the
following statements is not correct?
(a) The chances of transmission from female to male
are twice as likely as from male to female
(b) The chances of transmission are more if a person
suffers from other sexually transmitted infections
(c) An infected mother can transmit the infection to
her baby during pregnancy, at childbirth and by
breastfeeding
(d) The risk of contracting infection from transfusion
of infected blood is much higher than an exposure to
contaminated needle
Correct Option: (a)
• (a) Incorrect statement — Transmission rates
from male to female are actually higher than
from female to male due to larger exposure
surface and higher viral load in semen.
• (b) Correct — Presence of other STIs increases
mucosal damage and viral entry, thus
increasing HIV risk.
• (c) Correct — Mother-to-child transmission
can occur during pregnancy, delivery, or
breastfeeding.
• (d) Correct — Infected blood transfusion
carries a much higher risk than exposure
through a single contaminated needle.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2011
Q1. A married couple adopted a male child. A few
years later, twin boys were born to them. The blood
group of the couple is AB positive and O negative.
The blood group of the three sons is A positive, B
positive and O positive. The blood group of the
adopted son is
(a) O positive
(b) A positive
(c) B positive
(d) Cannot be determined on the basis of the given
data
The correct option is (a).

•

•
•

•

•

•

•

Let's analyze the genetics of the blood groups
of the biological parents and their children
first.
The parents' blood groups are AB (genotype
IᴬIᴮ) and O (genotype ii).
The possible blood groups of their biological
children can only be A (genotype Iᴬi) or B
(genotype Iᴮi). They cannot have a biological
child with blood group AB or O.
The problem states that the couple has three
sons with blood groups A positive, B positive,
and O positive.
Since the couple cannot have a biological
child with blood group O, the son with the O
positive blood group must be the adopted
son.
The twin boys born to them must be the ones
with blood groups A positive and B positive,
which are genetically possible outcomes for
this couple.
Hence, correct option is (a).

Q2. Aspartame is an artificial sweetener sold in the
market. It consists of amino acids and provides
calories like other amino acids. Yet, it is used as a
low-calorie sweetening agent in food items. What is
the basis of this use?
(a) Aspartame is as sweet as table sugar, but unlike
table sugar, it is not readily oxidized in human body
due to lack of requisite enzymes.
(b) When aspartame is used in food processing, the
sweet taste remains, but it becomes resistant to
oxidation.
(c) Aspartame is as sweet as sugar, but after ingestion
into the body, it is converted into metabolites that
yield no calories.
(d) Aspartame is several times sweeter than table
sugar, hence food items made with small quantities of
aspartame yield fewer calories on oxidation.
The correct option is (d).
• The key to using aspartame as a low-calorie
sweetener is its incredibly high sweetness
intensity.
• Aspartame is approximately 200 times
sweeter than sucrose (table sugar).

•

•

•

•

Because it is so sweet, only a very small
quantity is needed to achieve the same level
of sweetness as a much larger amount of
sugar.
While aspartame does contain calories (about
4 calories per gram, the same as sugar), the
amount used is so minuscule that the caloric
contribution to the food item is negligible.
Statement (a) is incorrect because it is much
sweeter than sugar. Statement (c) is incorrect
because its metabolites do yield calories.
Statement (d) correctly identifies that its high
sweetness intensity allows for the use of tiny,
low-calorie quantities.
Hence, correct option is (d).

Q3. At present, scientists can determine the
arrangement or relative positions of genes or DNA
sequences on a chromosome. How does this
knowledge benefit us?
1. It is possible to know the pedigree of
livestock.
2. It is possible to understand the causes of all
human diseases.
3. It is possible to develop disease-resistant
animal breeds.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. By mapping the genes
of livestock, scientists can identify genetic
markers associated with desirable traits (like
milk yield or meat quality) or undesirable
genetic diseases. This information allows for
the creation of detailed pedigrees and helps
in selective breeding programs.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The statement says
it's possible to understand the causes of "all"
human diseases. This is an overstatement.
While gene mapping is crucial for
understanding the genetic component of
many diseases, many diseases result from a

•

•
•

complex interplay of multiple genes and
environmental factors, or are caused purely
by environmental agents. We cannot
understand all diseases through gene
mapping alone.
Statement 3 is correct. By identifying the
specific genes that confer resistance to
certain diseases in animals, scientists can use
that knowledge to selectively breed animals
that carry these resistance genes or use
genetic engineering to develop diseaseresistant breeds.
Therefore, statements 1 and 3 are correct.
Hence, correct option is (c).

Q4. When the bark of a tree is removed in a circular
fashion all around near its base, it gradually dries up
and dies because
(a) Water from soil cannot rise to aerial parts.
(b) Roots are starved of energy.
(c) Tree is infected by soil microbes.
(d) Roots do not receive oxygen for respiration.
The correct option is (b).
• This process of removing a ring of bark is
called girdling. The bark of a tree contains a
crucial vascular tissue called the phloem.
• The function of the phloem is to transport
sugars (produced during photosynthesis in
the leaves) downwards to other parts of the
tree, including the roots. The roots need this
sugar for energy to carry out their functions,
like absorbing water and nutrients.
• When the phloem is removed in a complete
circle, the downward flow of this energy
source is cut off. The roots are starved of
energy (b) and can no longer function.
• Once the roots die, they can no longer absorb
water. The upper part of the tree then dies
from lack of water.
• Water transport upwards occurs in the
xylem, which is deeper inside the trunk, so
water can still rise for a short time (a is
incorrect). Infection (c) and lack of oxygen (d)
are not the primary cause of death from
girdling.

•

Hence, correct option is (b).
Q5. Regular intake of fresh fruits and vegetables is
recommended in the diet since they are a good
source of antioxidants. How do antioxidants help a
person maintain health and promote longevity?
(a) They activate the enzymes necessary for vitamin
synthesis in the body and help prevent vitamin
deficiency.
(b) They prevent excessive oxidation of
carbohydrates, fats and proteins in the body and help
avoid unnecessary wastage of energy.
(c) They neutralize the free radicals produced in the
body during metabolism.
(d) They activate certain genes in the cells of the body
and help delay the ageing process.
The correct option is (c).
• During normal metabolic processes, the body
produces unstable molecules called free
radicals. These molecules are highly reactive
because they have unpaired electrons.
• Free radicals can cause damage to cells,
proteins, and DNA by oxidizing them. This
cellular damage, known as oxidative stress, is
linked to aging and many chronic diseases.
• Antioxidants are molecules that can safely
donate an electron to a free radical, thereby
neutralizing it and stopping the chain reaction
of cellular damage.
• Fresh fruits and vegetables are rich sources of
antioxidants like Vitamin C, Vitamin E, and
various phytochemicals. By neutralizing free
radicals, antioxidants help protect cells from
damage, which helps maintain health and may
promote longevity.
• Hence, correct option is (c).
Q6. There is a concern over the increase in harmful
algal blooms in the sea water of India. What could be
the causative factors for this phenomenon?
1. Discharge of nutrients from the estuaries.
2. Run-off from the land during the monsoon
3. Upwelling in the seas.
Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only

(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, and 3.
The correct option is (d).
• Harmful Algal Blooms (HABs) are caused by
the rapid overgrowth of algae; the key trigger
is excess nutrients in the water
(eutrophication).
• 1. Discharge of nutrients from the estuaries:
Estuaries carry nitrogen and phosphorus from
inland sources (fertilisers, sewage) into
coastal waters, feeding algae.
• 2. Run-off from the land during the
monsoon: Heavy monsoon rains wash large
amounts of nutrients from land into the sea,
providing an influx that fuels blooms.
• 3. Upwelling in the seas: Upwelling brings
deep, nutrient-rich water to the surface,
supplying nutrients that can trigger massive
algal growth.
• Since all three phenomena supply nutrients to
coastal waters and can contribute to HABs,
the correct choice is (d).
• Hence, correct option is (d).
UPSC PYQs – 2012
Q1. Consider the following kinds of organisms:
1. Bat
2. Bee
3. Bird
Which of the above is/are pollinating agent/agents?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• A pollinating agent is any agent that transfers
pollen from the male anther of a flower to
the female stigma, enabling fertilization. This
can be an abiotic agent (like wind) or a biotic
agent (an animal).
• Bat (1): Many species of bats are important
pollinators, especially in tropical and desert
climates. They are attracted to large, pale,

•

•

•
•

night-blooming flowers with strong, musky
odors. This process is called chiropterophily.
Bee (2): Bees are the most well-known and
important group of pollinators worldwide.
They are attracted to bright, colorful, and
fragrant flowers and are highly efficient at
transferring pollen as they collect nectar and
pollen for food.
Bird (3): Many species of birds, such as
hummingbirds, sunbirds, and honeyeaters,
are also crucial pollinators. They are typically
attracted to large, brightly colored (often red)
flowers that are tubular in shape and produce
copious amounts of nectar. This process is
called ornithophily.
Since bats, bees, and birds all act as
pollinating agents, all three are correct.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

•

•
•

association with the roots of most plants. The
fungus helps the plant absorb phosphorus
and other micronutrients from the soil in
exchange for carbohydrates.
Flowering plants (3): Some flowering plants
are used as "green manure," which acts as a
form of biofertilizer. For example,
leguminous plants like clover or sunn hemp
are grown and then plowed back into the soil.
The nitrogen fixed in their root nodules is
then released, enriching the soil for the next
crop. The aquatic fern Azolla, which hosts a
nitrogen-fixing cyanobacterium, is also used
as a biofertilizer in rice paddies.
Since species from all three groups are used
as biofertilizers, all are correct.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2013

Q2. Consider the following kinds of organisms:
1. Bacteria
2. Fungi

Some species of which of the above kinds of
organisms are employed as bio fertilisers?

• Biofertilizers are substances that contain

seeds, plant surfaces, or soil, colonize the
