promote growth by increasing the supply or
availability of primary nutrients to the host
plant.
• Bacteria (1): Certain bacteria are widely used
as biofertilizers. The most famous are
Rhizobium, which forms a symbiotic
relationship with leguminous plants to fix
atmospheric nitrogen, and free-living
nitrogen-fixing bacteria like Azotobacter.
• Fungi (2): Certain types of fungi, known as
mycorrhizal fungi, form a symbiotic

Q1. With reference to the usefulness of the byproducts of sugar industry, which of the following
statements is/are correct?
1. Bagasse can be used as biomass fuel for the
generation of energy.
2. Molasses can be used as one of the
feedstocks for the production of synthetic
chemical fertilizers.
3. Molasses can be used for the production of
ethanol.
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. Bagasse, the fibrous
residue left after crushing sugarcane, is a
major by-product. It is rich in cellulose and is
widely used as a biomass fuel. Sugar mills
burn bagasse in their own boilers to generate
steam and electricity to power their
operations, often producing surplus electricity
that can be sold to the grid.

•

•

•
•

Statement 2 is incorrect. Molasses is used in
the production of ethanol and as a
supplement in animal feed. It is also used in
the fermentation industry. However, it is not
a primary feedstock for the production of
synthetic chemical fertilizers like urea or
DAP, which are produced through industrial
chemical processes (like the Haber-Bosch
process for ammonia).
Statement 3 is correct. Molasses is rich in
fermentable sugars. It is a primary and very
common feedstock for the industrial
production of ethanol through fermentation
by yeast. This ethanol can be used as a biofuel
or as an industrial solvent.
Therefore, statements 1 and 3 are correct.
Hence correct option is (c).

Q2. Improper handling and storage of cereal grains
and oil seeds result in the production of toxins
known as aflatoxins which are not generally
destroyed by normal cooking process. Aflatoxins are
produced by
(a) Bacteria
(b) Protozoa
(c) Moulds
(d) Viruses
The correct option is (c).
• Aflatoxins are a family of toxic compounds
that are classified as mycotoxins (toxins
produced by fungi).
• They are produced by certain species of
moulds (a type of fungus), primarily
Aspergillus flavus and Aspergillus parasiticus.
• These moulds grow on various agricultural
commodities such as maize (corn), peanuts,
cottonseed, and tree nuts, especially under
warm and humid conditions during storage.
• Aflatoxins are highly toxic and are known to
be potent carcinogens, primarily affecting the
liver. Because they are heat-stable, they are
not destroyed by normal cooking processes,
making contamination a serious food safety
concern.

•
•

They are not produced by bacteria, protozoa,
or viruses.
Hence correct option is (c).

Q3. In the grasslands, trees do not replace the
grasses as a part of an ecological succession because
of
(a) insects and fungi
(b) limited sunlight and paucity of nutrients
(c) water limits and fire
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (c).
• Ecological succession is the process by which
the structure of a biological community
evolves over time. In many ecosystems,
grasses are eventually replaced by shrubs and
then trees. However, in grasslands, this
succession is halted.
• The two primary limiting factors that prevent
trees from establishing dominance in
grasslands are water availability and frequent
fires.
• Water Limits: Grasslands are characterized by
moderate to low rainfall, which is often
insufficient to support the growth of large
forests. Grasses, with their deep and fibrous
root systems, are better adapted to these
conditions than trees.
• Fire: Grasslands are prone to frequent fires
(both natural and man-made). Grasses are
adapted to survive fires because their growing
points are located at or below the ground
surface. Trees, on the other hand, have their
growing points (buds) above ground and are
typically killed by fires when they are young
saplings.
• Insects and fungi (a) and limited sunlight (b)
are not the primary reasons. In fact,
grasslands have abundant sunlight, and while
nutrients can be a factor, water and fire are
the defining constraints.
• Hence correct option is (c).
Q4. Consider the following minerals
1. Calcium

2. Iron
3. Sodium
Which of the minerals given above is/are required by
human body for the contraction of muscles?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Muscle contraction is a complex process that
requires the coordinated action of several
minerals.
• Calcium (1): Calcium ions (Ca²⁺) play the most
direct and critical role. The nerve impulse
triggers the release of calcium from the
sarcoplasmic reticulum within the muscle
cell. This calcium then binds to a protein
complex (troponin), which causes a
conformational change that allows the muscle
proteins (actin and myosin) to slide past each
other, resulting in contraction.
• Sodium (3): Sodium ions (Na⁺) are essential
for the transmission of the nerve impulse
(action potential) that initiates the muscle
contraction. The flow of sodium ions into the
nerve and muscle cells is what carries the
electrical signal telling the muscle to contract.
• Iron (2): Iron's role is more indirect but still
essential. Iron is a key component of
hemoglobin (in red blood cells) and
myoglobin (in muscle cells), the proteins
responsible for transporting and storing
oxygen. Muscle cells require oxygen to
produce the ATP needed to power muscle
contractions. Without sufficient iron, oxygen
delivery is impaired, and muscle function
suffers.
• Since all three minerals are required for
proper muscle function, all are correct.
• Hence correct option is (d).
Q5. Many transplanted seedlings do not grow
because
(a) the new soil does not contain favourable minerals
(b) most of the root hairs grip the new soil too hard

(c) most of the root hairs are lost during
transplantation
(d) leaves get damaged during transplantation
The correct option is (c).
• The failure of transplanted seedlings to thrive
is often referred to as "transplant shock."
• The primary reason for this is damage to the
root system during the process of uprooting
and replanting.
• Root hairs are tiny, delicate, single-cell
extensions of the root's epidermal cells. They
are responsible for the vast majority of water
and mineral absorption from the soil.
• During transplantation, it is almost impossible
to move a seedling without tearing off or
severely damaging most of these delicate
root hairs.
• When the seedling is replanted, its ability to
absorb water is drastically reduced because
the primary absorption structures (the root
hairs) have been lost. The plant loses water
through its leaves (transpiration) faster than
its damaged root system can absorb it,
causing the seedling to wilt and often die
before it can grow new root hairs.
• While new soil (a) or damaged leaves (d) can
be factors, the loss of root hairs is the most
critical and common cause of transplant
failure.
• Hence correct option is (c).
Q6. Which of the following statements is / are
correct?
1. Viruses lack enzymes necessary for the
generation of energy.
2. Viruses can be cultured in any synthetic
medium.
3. Viruses are transmitted from one organism to
another by biological vectors only.
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3

The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. Viruses are obligate
intracellular parasites. They are metabolically
inert outside of a host cell and lack their own
enzymes for energy generation (like those for
cellular respiration) and protein synthesis.
They must hijack the host cell's machinery to
replicate.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. Because viruses lack
their own metabolic machinery, they cannot
be grown on a synthetic medium like bacteria
can. They can only be cultured inside of living
host cells (e.g., in cell cultures, embryonated
eggs, or living animals).
• Statement 3 is incorrect. The statement that
viruses are transmitted by biological vectors
only is false. While many viruses are
transmitted by biological vectors (like
mosquitoes transmitting dengue virus), many
others are transmitted through other means,
such as airborne droplets (influenza), direct
contact (herpes), contaminated food and
water (norovirus), or contaminated inanimate
objects (fomites).
• Therefore, only the first statement is correct.
• Hence correct option is (a).
Q7. Which of the following leaf modifications occurs/
occur in desert areas to inhibit water loss?
1. Hard and waxy leaves
2. Tiny leaves or no leaves
3. Thorns instead of leaves
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below.
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Plants that live in desert areas (xerophytes)
have evolved numerous adaptations to
minimize water loss (transpiration).
• 1. Hard and waxy leaves: Many desert plants
have leaves covered with a thick, waxy
cuticle. This waxy layer is waterproof and

•

•

•

•

significantly reduces the amount of water that
evaporates from the leaf surface.
2. Tiny leaves or no leaves: Reducing the total
surface area of leaves is a key strategy. Many
desert plants have very small leaves
(microphylls). Some have shed their true
leaves entirely, with photosynthesis occurring
in flattened stems (cladodes).
3. Thorns instead of leaves: In cacti and some
other succulents, the leaves have been
modified into sharp thorns or spines. This
drastically reduces the surface area available
for water loss and also deters herbivores.
Since all three are common leaf
modifications in desert plants to conserve
water, all are correct.
Hence correct option is (d).
UPSC PYQs – 2014

Q1. Consider the following pairs:
Vitamin Deficiency : Diseases
1. Vitamin C : Scurvy
2. Vitamin D : Rickets
3. Vitamin E : Night blindness
Which of the pairs above is/are correctly matched?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) None
The correct option is (a).
• Pair 1 is correctly matched: Deficiency of
Vitamin C (ascorbic acid) causes Scurvy,
characterized by fatigue, bleeding gums, and
poor wound healing due to impaired collagen
synthesis.
• Pair 2 is correctly matched: Deficiency of
Vitamin D impairs calcium absorption,
leading to soft and weak bones. In children,
this is known as Rickets.
• Pair 3 is incorrectly matched: Night blindness
results from Vitamin A deficiency, not
Vitamin E. Vitamin E deficiency causes
neurological problems, not visual disorders.

•
•

Therefore, only pairs 1 and 2 are correctly
matched.
Hence, correct option is (a).

Q2. Lichens, which are capable of initiating ecological
succession even on a bare rock, are actually a
symbiotic association of
(a) algae and bacteria
(b) algae and fungi
(c) bacteria and fungi
(d) fungi and mosses
The correct option is (b).
• Lichens are a symbiotic relationship between
algae or cyanobacteria and fungi.
• The fungus provides structure, protection,
and moisture absorption, while the alga or
cyanobacterium performs photosynthesis to
produce food (carbohydrates) shared with
the fungus.
• This partnership allows lichens to survive in
harsh environments (like bare rock), making
them pioneer species in ecological
succession.
• Hence, correct option is (b).
Q3. Which one of the following is the process
involved in photosynthesis?
(a) Potential energy is released to form free energy
(b) Free energy is converted into potential energy and
stored
(c) Food is oxidized to release carbon dioxide and
water
(d) Oxygen is taken, and carbon dioxide and water
vapour are given out
The correct option is (b).
• In photosynthesis, light energy (free energy)
from the Sun is converted into chemical
energy (potential energy) and stored in the
bonds of glucose molecules.
• The equation:
6CO₂ + 6H₂O + sunlight → C₆H₁₂O₆ + 6O₂
• The stored chemical energy can later be
released during cellular respiration.
• Option (c) refers to respiration, the opposite
of photosynthesis.

•

Hence, correct option is (b).

Q4. Which of the following statements is/are correct
regarding vegetative propagation of plants?
1. Vegetative propagation produces clonal
population.
2. Vegetative propagation helps in eliminating
the virus.
3. Vegetative propagation can be practiced most
of the year.
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: Vegetative
propagation is asexual reproduction,
producing clones (genetically identical
offspring).
• Statement 2 is incorrect: It does not
eliminate viruses; infected parent plants
transmit viruses to offspring.
• Statement 3 is correct: It can be practiced
most of the year under suitable conditions,
unlike sexual reproduction that depends on
specific seasons.
• Therefore, statements 1 and 3 are correct.
• Hence, correct option is (c).
Q5. Among the following organisms, which one does
not belong to the class of other three?
(a) Crab
(b) Mite
(c) Scorpion
(d) Spider
The correct option is (a).
• Mite, Scorpion, and Spider belong to the class
Arachnida (Arachnids). They have eight legs,
no antennae, and two body parts.
• Crab belongs to Crustacea, which have two
pairs of antennae and are typically aquatic.
• Thus, Crab does not belong to the same class
as the others.
• Hence, correct option is (a).

Q6. Consider the following:
1. Bats
2. Bears
3. Rodents
The phenomenon of hibernation can be observed in
which of the above kinds of animals?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) Hibernation cannot be observed in any of the
above
The correct option is (c).
• Hibernation is a state of dormancy and
metabolic slowdown during winter to
conserve energy when food is scarce.
• Bats (1): Many species enter true hibernation,
lowering body temperature and metabolism.
• Bears (2): Bears undergo a form of winter
sleep, with slower metabolism and no food or
water intake.
• Rodents (3): Many rodents (like hamsters,
ground squirrels, and woodchucks) are true
hibernators.
• Hence, all three show hibernation.
• Therefore, correct option is (c).

with reference to which one of the following
diseases?
(a) AIDS
(b) Bird flu
(c) Dengue
(d) Swine flu
The correct option is (d).

A virus.
• It became globally significant in 2009, when a
new strain of the virus emerged and caused a
pandemic.
• This strain was a novel reassortment of
human, avian (bird), and swine (pig)
influenza viruses.

•

•
•
•
•

Because it contained genetic material from
influenza viruses that circulate in pigs, it was
commonly referred to as “swine flu.”

Immunodeficiency Virus (HIV).
Bird flu (b) refers to influenza caused by avian
influenza viruses such as H5N1.
Dengue (c) is caused by the dengue virus,
which is transmitted by mosquitoes.
Hence, correct option is (d).
UPSC PYQs – 2016

Q1. In eye donation, which one of the following parts
of donor's eye is utilised?
(a) Iris
(b) Lens
(c) Cornea
(d) Retina
The correct option is (c).
• In eye donation, it is not the entire eyeball
that is transplanted — only the cornea is used
to restore sight.
• The cornea is the transparent, dome-shaped
outer layer at the front of the eye that
focuses light as it enters.
• A corneal transplant (keratoplasty) replaces a
damaged or cloudy cornea with a healthy one
from a donor.
• The iris (a), lens (b), and retina (d) are not
transplanted in standard eye donation
procedures.
• Hence, correct option is (c).
Q2. Which one of the following genetic diseases is
sex-linked?
(a) Royal haemophilia
(b) Tay-Sachs disease
(c) Cystic fibrosis
(d) Hypertension
The correct option is (a).
• Royal haemophilia (Haemophilia A) is a
classic X-linked recessive disorder caused by
a defective gene on the X chromosome.

•

•
•

•

Since males (XY) have only one X
chromosome, they are more likely to express
the disorder. Females (XX) usually act as
carriers.
Tay-Sachs disease (b) and Cystic fibrosis (c)
are autosomal recessive diseases.
Hypertension (d) is a multifactorial condition
influenced by both genes and environment,
not sex-linked.
Hence, correct option is (a).

Q3. Neem tree has acquired industrial importance as
a source of
(a) biopesticide and anti-fertility compound
(b) anti-fertility compound, biofertilizer and anticancer drug
(c) biofertilizer, biopesticide and anti-fertility
compound
(d) anti-cancer drug, biopesticide and biofertilizer
The correct option is (c).
• The Neem tree (Azadirachta indica) is
valuable for its bioactive compounds
extracted from leaves, seeds, and bark.
• Biopesticide: Contains azadirachtin, a
powerful insect repellent and growth
disruptor, used widely in organic farming.
• Biofertilizer: Neem cake (residue after oil
extraction) acts as an organic manure,
enriching soil and protecting roots from pests.
• Anti-fertility compound: Neem oil has shown
spermicidal and anti-fertility properties,
studied for natural contraceptive use.
• Hence, correct option is (c).
Q4. Lathyrism is caused by excessive consumption of
(a) Kesari dal
(b) Mustard oil
(c) Polished rice
(d) Mushrooms
The correct option is (a).
• Lathyrism is a neurological disorder causing
paralysis of the lower limbs.
• It results from consumption of Kesari dal
(Lathyrus sativus), which contains the

•

•

neurotoxin ODAP (β-N-Oxalyl-L-α,βdiaminopropionic acid).
Prolonged intake, especially during droughts
or famines, leads to irreversible nerve
damage.
Hence, correct option is (a).

Q5. Barium in a suitable form is administered to
patients before an X-ray examination of the
stomach, because
(a) barium allows X-rays to pass through the stomach
on account of its transparency to X-rays
(b) barium compound, like magnesium sulphate helps
in cleaning the stomach before X-ray examination
(c) barium is a good absorber of X-rays and this helps
the stomach to appear clearly in contrast with the
other regions in the picture
(d) barium salts are white in colour and this helps the
stomach to appear clearly in contrast with other
regions in the picture
The correct option is (c).
• Barium sulfate is used as a radiocontrast
agent in X-rays of the digestive tract.
• It is a good absorber (radiopaque) of X-rays
due to its high atomic number, making the
stomach and intestines visible.
• The barium meal coats the inner lining,
creating a white outline on X-rays for better
diagnosis.
• Hence, correct option is (c).

Q7. Which one of the following is a useful functional
association between fungi and the roots of higher
plants?
(a) Biofertilizer
(b) Coralloid root
(c) Lichen
(d) Mycorrhiza
The correct option is (d).
• Mycorrhiza is a mutualistic association
between fungi and plant roots.
• The fungus increases absorption of water and
minerals (especially phosphorus).
• The plant provides carbohydrates to the
fungus.
• Biofertilizer (a) is a general term; Coralloid
roots (b) associate with cyanobacteria, and
Lichen (c) involves fungus +
algae/cyanobacteria, not roots.
• Hence, correct option is (d).

•

•

A. Adrenaline → (1): Triggers “fight or flight”

UPSC PYQs – 2017

Q1. Biological Oxygen Demand (BOD) is a standard
criterion for
(a) Measuring oxygen levels in blood
(b) Computing oxygen levels in forest ecosystems
(c) Pollution assay in aquatic ecosystems
(d) Assessing oxygen levels in high altitude regions
The correct option is (c).
• Biological Oxygen Demand (BOD) measures
the amount of dissolved oxygen required by
aerobic microorganisms to decompose
organic matter in a water sample.
• It is a standard test used to assess the organic
pollution level in aquatic ecosystems.
• High BOD means high organic pollution,
indicating that microorganisms are consuming
more oxygen to break down waste, reducing
oxygen available for aquatic life.
• Thus, high BOD = poor water quality and low
BOD = cleaner water.
• It is not used for measuring oxygen in blood
(a), forest ecosystems (b), or high altitudes
(d).
• Hence, correct option is (c).
Q2. Consider the following statements:
1. In tropical regions, Zika virus disease is
transmitted by the same mosquito that
transmits dengue.
2. Sexual transmission of Zika virus disease is
possible.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: Zika virus is
transmitted primarily by the Aedes mosquito
(Aedes aegypti) — the same species
responsible for spreading dengue,
chikungunya, and yellow fever in tropical
regions.
• Statement 2 is correct: Apart from mosquito
transmission, Zika virus can also be
transmitted sexually, as the virus can persist
in semen for several weeks after infection.
• Both statements are scientifically verified
facts.
• Hence, correct option is (c).

Q1. Solar eclipse occurs when
(a) Earth comes between sun and moon
(b) Moon is at right angle to the earth
(c) Moon comes between sun and earth
(d) Sun comes between moon and earth
The correct option is (c).
• A solar eclipse occurs when the Moon passes
directly between the Sun and Earth.
• When this alignment happens, the Moon’s
shadow falls on the Earth, blocking sunlight
partially or completely.
• It can occur only during the New Moon
phase.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

•

Q2. When a man circles round the earth in a satellite,
then
(a) His mass becomes zero but weight remains
constant
(b) Mass remains constant but weight becomes zero
(c) Both mass and weight remain constant
(d) Both mass and weight remain zero
The correct option is (b).
• Mass is an intrinsic property and remains
constant regardless of location.
• Weight depends on gravitational pull (Weight
= mass × gravity).
• In a satellite, a man is in continuous freefall,
creating a state of weightlessness.
• His apparent weight becomes zero, even
though gravity still acts on him.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. The weight of an object at the poles is greater
than at the equator. This is because
(a) Of the shape of the earth
(b) The attraction of the moon is maximum at the
earth's surface
(c) The attraction of the sun is maximum at the earth's
surface
(d) Gravitational pull is more at the poles
The correct option is (d).

•
•
•

The Earth is an oblate spheroid, flattened at
poles and bulging at the equator.
Therefore, the distance to Earth's center is
smaller at poles.
By Newton’s law of gravitation, gravity
increases with decreasing distance.
Hence, gravitational pull is stronger at the
poles.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q4. The water in an open pond remains cool even in
hot summer because
(a) Of continuous evaporation of water
(b) Water radiates heat more rapidly than the
atmosphere
(c) Water absorbs heat less rapidly than the
atmosphere
(d) Water absorbs heat more rapidly than the
atmosphere
The correct option is (a).
• Evaporation is a cooling process where highenergy molecules escape as vapor.
• This removes heat from the remaining water,
lowering its temperature.
• Though water’s high specific heat slows
heating, evaporation is the dominant cooling
effect.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. You are travelling in a car and a thunderstorm
suddenly takes place. What will be your first step?
(a) Stop the car, get out of it and lie flat in the field
(b) Stand below a tree
(c) Go to a nearby wooden pole and stick to it
(d) Stay in the car
The correct option is (d).
• A car’s metal body acts as a Faraday cage,
conducting lightning around passengers
safely.
• Safest action: stay inside, pull over, and avoid
touching metal parts.
• Options (a), (b), and (c) increase lightning risk.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. In high mountain regions bleeding through the
nose occurs because
(a) The pressure of the blood capillaries is higher than
the outside pressure
(b) The pressure at high altitudes is greater than that
in the plains
(c) The blood pressure increases at high altitudes
(d) The blood pressure decreases at high altitudes
The correct option is (a).
• At high altitudes, atmospheric pressure is
lower.
• Internal blood pressure remains relatively
constant, creating a pressure difference.
• This causes capillaries to rupture, leading to
nosebleeds.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. When light enters a closed room through a small
hole in the door, the image of an outside building
appears inverted on the opposite wall. This is
because
(a) The hole acts as a convex lens
(b) Light takes a curvature at the edges of the hole
(c) Of rectilinear propagation of light
(d) The hole acts as a concave lens
The correct option is (c).
• This is the principle of a pinhole camera.
• Light travels in straight lines — called
rectilinear propagation.
• Light from the top of the object travels to the
bottom of the wall, and vice versa.
• This crossing of rays produces an inverted
image.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. An ordinary clock loses time in summer. This is
because
(a) The length of the pendulum increases and the time
period increases
(b) The length of the pendulum increases and the time
period decreases
(c) The length of the pendulum decreases and the
time period increases
(d) The length of the pendulum decreases and the
time period decreases

The correct option is (a).
• In summer, metal expands, increasing the
pendulum’s length.
• The time period (T ∝ √l) therefore increases.
• Each swing takes slightly longer, so the clock
runs slow.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9. A ball bounces higher at high altitudes than in
plains. This is because
(a) The air pressure on higher altitudes is lower than
that in plains
(b) Downward pull due to gravity is less at higher
altitudes
(c) The rarefied air offers less resistance to the ball
(d) You become more energetic at hills, so greater the
force applied, higher goes the ball
The correct option is (c).
• At high altitudes, air is rarefied and less
dense.
• Therefore, air resistance is lower, so the ball
loses less energy.
• It bounces higher compared to plains.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q10. The minor planets revolving between the orbits
of Mars and Jupiter are called
(a) Asteroids
(b) Comets
(c) Meteors
(d) Novas
The correct option is (a).
• Asteroids are rocky bodies orbiting the Sun,
mainly between Mars and Jupiter.
• Comets are icy bodies, meteors are burning
debris, and novas are stellar explosions.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1980
Q1. Range of Television Broadcasting is confined to a
limited distance because
(a) Long waves are used
(b) Short waves are absorbed by the atmosphere

(c) Energy of the waves is dissipated
(d) Earth is spherical in shape
The correct option is (d).
• Television broadcasting uses VHF (Very High
Frequency) and UHF (Ultra High Frequency)
waves.
• These waves travel in straight lines — known
as line-of-sight propagation.
• Because the Earth is spherical, the curvature
blocks these signals beyond the horizon.
• Thus, the broadcasting range is limited by
Earth's curvature.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Unit of distance used in navigation is
(a) Nautical mile
(b) Kilometre
(c) Light year
(d) Yard
The correct option is (a).
• Nautical mile is the standard unit used in
marine and air navigation.
• It is based on Earth’s circumference and
equals one minute of latitude.
• 1 nautical mile = 1,852 meters.
• Kilometre is used on land, light year for
space, and yard is an imperial unit.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. What type of mirror is used by motorists to see
the road behind them?
(a) Convex
(b) Concave
(c) Plane
(d) Concave-convex
The correct option is (a).
• Convex mirrors curve outward and produce
virtual, upright, and diminished images.
• They offer a wider field of view, helping
drivers see more area behind.
• Used in rear-view mirrors to minimize blind
spots.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q4. The escape velocity from the earth's surface is
about
(a) 30 km/sec
(b) 6 km/sec
(c) 11.2 km/sec
(d) 300 km/sec
The correct option is (c).
• Escape velocity is the minimum speed
needed to break free from Earth’s gravity.
• For Earth, it is approximately 11.2 km/s (≈
40,270 km/h).
• Objects launched at this speed will not fall
back to Earth.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. The radiant energy of the sun is due to
(a) Nuclear fission
(b) Nuclear fusion
(c) Sinking of the sun
(d) Violent explosions
The correct option is (b).
• The Sun’s energy comes from nuclear fusion
in its core.
• Hydrogen nuclei fuse to form helium,
releasing enormous energy.
• This follows Einstein’s equation E = mc².
• Nuclear fission occurs in reactors, not in the
Sun.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. In a microphone, transformation of energy takes
place from
(a) Sound into electrical energy
(b) Electrical into sound energy
(c) Sound into mechanical energy
(d) Mechanical into sound energy
The correct option is (a).
• A microphone is a transducer converting
sound waves into electrical signals.
• Sound vibrations move a diaphragm, creating
electric variations.
• This forms the basis of audio recording and
communication.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q7. Fuel used in a nuclear reactor is
(a) Uranium
(b) Heavy water
(c) Barium
(d) Cadmium
The correct option is (a).
• Uranium-235 is the most common fuel used
in nuclear reactors.
• It is a fissile material, splitting to release large
amounts of energy.
• Heavy water acts as a moderator, cadmium
as a control rod, and barium is a by-product.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8. Neil Armstrong was the first person to reach the
moon. While walking on the moon
(a) His mass remained the same but weight increased
(b) His mass remained the same but weight decreased
(c) His mass as well as weight decreased
(d) His mass increased but weight remained the same
The correct option is (b).
• Mass is the amount of matter, which remains
constant anywhere.
• Weight depends on gravitational force.
• The Moon’s gravity is about 1/6th of Earth’s,
so his weight decreased accordingly.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q9. Distance of stars is measured in
(a) Light years
(b) Kilometres per second
(c) Kilometres only
(d) Nautical miles
The correct option is (a).
• Light-year is the standard unit for measuring
astronomical distances.
• It is the distance light travels in one year =
9.46 trillion km.
• Kilometres or nautical miles are too small for
cosmic scales.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q10. Isotopes of the same element differ in the
number of
(a) Protons

(b) Neutrons
(c) Electrons
(d) Positrons
The correct option is (b).
• Isotopes have the same number of protons
but different neutrons.
• This causes a difference in mass number, not
in chemical properties.
• Example: Carbon-12 and Carbon-14 differ by
two neutrons.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q11. Stainless steel contains which of the following?
(a) Aluminium and Zinc
(b) Chromium and Carbon
(c) Zinc and Mercury
(d) Copper and Cadmium
The correct option is (b).
• Stainless steel is an iron alloy containing
carbon and at least 10.5% chromium.
• Chromium oxide layer prevents rusting and
corrosion.
• Nickel may be added to increase strength and
shine.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q12. Radar is used for
(a) Detecting objects by using light waves
(b) Reflecting sound waves to detect objects
(c) Determining the presence and location of objects
with radio waves
(d) Tracking rain-bearing clouds
The correct option is (c).
• RADAR stands for Radio Detection and
Ranging.
• It uses radio waves to detect distance, angle,
and speed of objects.
• It works by transmitting waves and receiving
their reflections (echoes).
• Weather radar is one of its applications.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1981
Q1. In a hydraulic device as shown in the figure, how
much weight should be put on the piston A to
balance the level of water when a weight of 40 gm is
put on the piston B?

(a) 10 g
(b) 5 g
(c) 20 g
(d) 40 g
The correct option is (a).
• This question is based on Pascal's Principle,
which states that pressure applied to an
enclosed fluid is transmitted undiminished to
every portion of the fluid and the walls of the
containing vessel.
• In a hydraulic system, the pressure on both
pistons is equal: P₁ = P₂.
• Pressure (P) is defined as Force (F) per unit
Area (A), so F₁/A₁ = F₂/A₂.
• Let's assume the diameter of piston A is 'd', so
its radius is r = d/2 and its area is A₁ = π(d/2)²
= πd²/4.
• The diameter of piston B is twice that of A, so
its diameter is '2d', its radius is r = d, and its
area is A₂ = π(d)² = πd².
• We are given the weight (force) on piston B,
F₂ = 40 g. We need to find the weight on
piston A, F₁.
• Using the formula: F₁ / (πd²/4) = 40 / (πd²).
• Simplifying, we get F₁ = (40 * πd²/4) / πd² = 40
/ 4 = 10 g. (Note: The provided answer key
says (c) 20g, which is incorrect based on the
principles of hydraulics. The force is
proportional to the area, and since the area

•

of B is 4 times the area of A, the force on A
should be 1/4 the force on B).
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2. To check a thermometer, the boiling point of
water should be recorded as shown in the figure:

(a) A
(b) B
(c) C
(d) D
The correct option is (c).
• To accurately measure the temperature of a
substance, the sensing part of the
thermometer (the bulb) must be fully
immersed in the substance being measured.
• In this case, to measure the boiling point of
water, the thermometer’s bulb must be
placed in the boiling water itself, not just in
the steam above it.
• In figure (c), the bulb of the thermometer is
correctly and fully immersed in the boiling
water, which will give the most accurate
reading of the boiling point.
• In (a) and (b), the bulb is only in the steam,
which might not be at the exact boiling
temperature. In (d), the bulb is touching the
bottom of the beaker, which could be hotter
than the water itself.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. To an astronaut in the spacecraft, the sky colour
appears to be
(a) Blue
(b) White
(c) Black
(d) Orange red
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•

•

The sky appears blue on Earth because of
Rayleigh scattering. The Earth's atmosphere
contains tiny molecules that scatter shortwavelength light (blue and violet) more
effectively than long-wavelength light.
In space, outside the Earth's atmosphere,
there are no atmospheric particles to scatter
the sunlight.
Without this scattering, an astronaut in a
spacecraft sees the Sun as a brilliant, white
star against the void of space, which appears
completely black.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. A new temperature scale has been devised
according to which water freezes at 40°X and boils at
240°X. The relationship between Xelsius and Celsius
scale is best expressed by the graph

(a) I
(b) II
(c) III
(d) IV
The correct option is (c).
• This question asks for the graphical
representation of the relationship between
two linear temperature scales: Celsius (°C)
and the new Xelsius (°X).
• We know the following corresponding points:
Freezing point of water: 0°C = 40°X; Boiling
point of water: 100°C = 240°X.
• Since the relationship between two linear
scales is a straight line, we can plot these two
points. The line must pass through (0, 40) and
(100, 240).
• Graph (I) shows a negative slope.
• Graph (II) starts from the origin (0,0).
• Graph (III) correctly shows a straight line that
starts at a positive value on the Y-axis (40°X)

•
•

when the X-axis (Celsius) is at 0, and then
rises linearly. This matches our points.
Graph (IV) shows a curve, which is incorrect
for a linear relationship.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. On the above scale, the average body
temperature of 98.4°F will be equivalent to
(a) 77°X
(b) 113.3°X
(c) 128.6°X
(d) 154.4°X
The correct option is (b).
• First, we need to establish the conversion
formula between Celsius (°C) and Xelsius (°X).
A general formula for converting between two
linear scales is: (Temp₂ - Point A₂) / (Point B₂ Point A₂) = (Temp₁ - Point A₁) / (Point B₁ Point A₁).
• Let's use the freezing and boiling points of
water: (°X - 40) / (240 - 40) = (°C - 0) / (100 0).
• (°X - 40) / 200 = °C / 100.
• °X - 40 = 2 * °C => °X = 2 * °C + 40.
• Next, convert the given Fahrenheit
temperature to Celsius: °C = (°F - 32) * 5/9.
• °C = (98.4 - 32) * 5/9 = 66.4 * 5/9 = 36.89°C
(approximately 37°C).
• Now, convert this Celsius temperature to
Xelsius using our formula: °X = 2 * (36.89) +
40 = 73.78 + 40 = 113.78°X.
• This value is closest to 113.3°X. (Note: The
calculation in the provided answer key is
flawed. It incorrectly assumes a direct
proportional relationship from 0°F, which is
not how temperature scales work).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. What are the primary colours?
(a) Blue, green, yellow
(b) Yellow, red, blue
(c) Red, blue, green
(d) Green, white, black
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•

•

•

The question is ambiguous as there are two
sets of primary colors: additive and
subtractive.
Additive primary colors are used when mixing
light (like on a computer screen or TV). These
are Red, Green, and Blue (RGB). Mixing them
produces white light. This is the standard
definition in physics.
Subtractive primary colors are used when
mixing pigments (like paint or ink). These are
traditionally taught as Red, Yellow, and Blue
(RYB), although the more accurate set used in
printing is Cyan, Magenta, and Yellow (CMY).
Mixing these absorbs light, producing black.
Given the common scientific context of these
questions, Red, Blue, and Green (RGB) is the
most appropriate answer for the primary
colors of light.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q7. If water contracts on freezing, which of the
following would happen?
(a) Icebergs will completely float on the surface of
water
(b) Ice would become more dense
(c) Lakes would freeze from top to bottom
(d) Rocks will crack due to freezing of internal water
The correct option is (b).
• This is a hypothetical question. In reality,
water expands when it freezes, making ice
less dense than liquid water.
• Density is mass per unit volume. If water
were to contract (decrease in volume) upon
freezing, its mass would be packed into a
smaller space.
• This would mean that ice would become
more dense than liquid water.
• As a consequence, ice would sink instead of
float (ruling out a), and lakes would freeze
from the bottom up instead of top-down
(ruling out c). Cracking rocks (d) is a result of
water expanding upon freezing, so this would
not happen.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q8. When the ice floating in a glass of water melts,
the level of water will
(a) Increase
(b) Decrease
(c) Remain constant
(d) First increase and then decrease
The correct option is (c).
• This is a classic physics problem based on
Archimedes' principle.
• A floating object displaces a volume of fluid
that has a weight equal to the object's own
weight.
• An ice cube is just frozen water. The volume
of water it displaces while floating has the
same weight as the ice cube itself.
• When the ice cube melts, it turns into a
volume of liquid water that has exactly the
same weight.
• Therefore, the melted water exactly fills the
volume that the ice cube was displacing, and
the overall water level will remain constant.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9. What is the function of a fuse wire?
(a) To prevent an unduly high electric current from
passing through a circuit
(b) To increase the current supply
(c) To decrease the current supply
(d) To stabilise the voltage
The correct option is (a).
• A fuse wire is a safety device designed to
protect an electrical circuit from excessive
current.
• It is made of a metal alloy with a low melting
point and is placed in series with the circuit it
protects.
• If the current in the circuit exceeds a safe
level (due to a short circuit or overload), the
fuse wire heats up, melts, and breaks the
circuit.
• This stops the flow of electricity and prevents
an unduly high electric current from
damaging the appliance or causing a fire.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q10. Why is it advisable not to draw current for
many appliances from a single point?
(a) Current will decrease
(b) Current will increase
(c) Current and voltage both will increase
(d) Voltage will decrease whereas current will increase
The correct option is (d).
• When you plug multiple appliances into a
single outlet or power strip, you are
connecting them in parallel to the main
power supply.
• In a parallel circuit, the total current drawn
from the source is the sum of the currents
drawn by each individual appliance (I_total =
I₁ + I₂ + I₃ + ...). This means the total current
will increase as you add more appliances.
• However, all electrical wiring has some
internal resistance. As the total current
increases, the voltage drop across the house
wiring (V = I * R_wiring) also increases. This
causes the voltage available at the outlet to
decrease.
• This combination of increased current and
decreased voltage can overload the circuit,
cause wires to overheat, and damage the
appliances.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q11. Why does a man weigh more at the poles than
at the equator?
(a) Because there is no atmosphere at the poles
(b) Because gravitational pull is more at the poles
(c) Because gravitational pull is less at the poles
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (b).
• This is similar to Q3 from 1979. A person's
weight depends on the force of gravity.
• The Earth is an oblate spheroid, meaning the
polar radius is shorter than the equatorial
radius. This places the surface at the poles
closer to the Earth's center of mass.
• Due to the inverse square law of gravitation,
the gravitational pull is stronger (more) at the
poles.

•

•
•

Additionally, the centrifugal force caused by
the Earth's rotation is maximum at the
equator and zero at the poles. This outward
force slightly counteracts gravity at the
equator, making the effective gravity weaker
there.
Both effects combine to make a person weigh
more at the poles.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q12. Mark the correct answer
(a) Planets have an apparent motion and twinkle
(b) Planets have no apparent motion but twinkle
(c) Planets have an apparent motion but do not
twinkle
(d) Planets have no apparent motion and do not
twinkle
The correct option is (c).
• Apparent Motion: Planets, being part of our
solar system, orbit the Sun just as Earth does.
From our perspective, they appear to move
against the background of distant, "fixed"
stars. This wandering motion is their
apparent motion.
• Twinkling: Stars are so far away that they
appear as single points of light. As this point
of light passes through Earth's turbulent
atmosphere, it is refracted multiple times,
causing it to twinkle.
• Planets, being much closer, appear as tiny
disks of light, not single points. The light from
this disk averages out as it passes through the
atmosphere, so they do not twinkle and shine
with a steady light.
• Therefore, planets have an apparent motion
but do not twinkle.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q13. Why is it easier to cook in a pressure cooker?
(a) High pressure creates turbulence
(b) More steam is formed inside the cooker
(c) Boiling point of water is raised
(d) Boiling point of water is lowered
The correct option is (c).

•
•

•
•

•

•

A pressure cooker is a sealed pot that does
not allow steam to escape.
As the water inside heats up and turns to
steam, the pressure inside the cooker builds
up to a level higher than the normal
atmospheric pressure.
The boiling point of a liquid increases as the
pressure above it increases.
Inside the pressure cooker, water boils at a
temperature significantly higher than its
normal 100°C (e.g., around 121°C).
Cooking food at this higher temperature
significantly speeds up the chemical reactions
involved in cooking, making it much faster
and easier.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q14. What happens when the temperature of water
falls from 30°C to 0°C?
(a) The volume of ice formed will be less than the
volume of water
(b) The volume of ice formed will be more than the
volume of water
(c) Water will not freeze at 0°C
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (b).
• This question describes the process of water
cooling and freezing.
• Water exhibits an anomalous expansion. As it
cools, it contracts and becomes denser until it
reaches its maximum density at 4°C.
• As it cools further from 4°C to 0°C, it begins to
expand and become less dense.
• Upon freezing at 0°C, it undergoes a
significant expansion. The crystalline
structure of ice takes up more space than the
molecules in liquid water.
• Therefore, a given mass of water will have a
larger volume as ice. The volume of ice
formed will be more than the volume of the
water it came from.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1982
Q1. To an astronaut in the spacecraft, the sky
appears to be
(a) Blue
(b) White
(c) Dark
(d) Red
The correct option is (c).
• The sky on Earth appears blue because of a
phenomenon called Rayleigh scattering,
where the molecules in the atmosphere
scatter short-wavelength light (like blue and
violet) more effectively than long-wavelength
light.
• In space, there is no atmosphere to scatter
the sunlight.
• Without this scattering effect, an astronaut in
a spacecraft sees the Sun as a bright star
against the vacuum of space, which appears
completely dark or black.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. A fan makes a hot noon feel cool by
(a) Blowing out hot air
(b) Increasing the rate of evaporation
(c) Letting in cool air
(d) Regulating the air flow
The correct option is (b).
• A fan does not actually cool the air in a room;
in fact, its motor adds a small amount of heat.
• The cooling sensation we feel is caused by the
moving air from the fan increasing the rate of
evaporation of sweat from our skin.
• Evaporation is a cooling process because it
requires energy (heat) to change liquid sweat
into water vapor. By accelerating this process,
the fan helps our body lose heat more
efficiently, making us feel cooler.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. When a ship enters a sea from a river, its portion
under water will
(a) Increase
(b) Decrease

(c) Show no change
(d) Increase and decrease alternately
The correct option is (b).
• This phenomenon is explained by
Archimedes' principle and the concept of
density.
• Sea water is denser than fresh river water
because of its dissolved salt content.
• According to Archimedes' principle, a floating
object (the ship) displaces a weight of fluid
equal to its own weight.
• Since the ship's weight is constant, it needs to
displace a smaller volume of the denser sea
water to support its weight compared to the
larger volume of less dense river water.
• Therefore, when a ship moves from a river to
the sea, it will float higher, and the portion of
its hull under water will decrease.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Hygrometer is used to measure
(a) Density of a liquid
(b) Relative humidity of atmosphere
(c) Absorption power of plants
(d) Temperature of air
The correct option is (b).
• A hygrometer is an instrument used for
measuring the amount of water vapor in the
air, in soil, or in confined spaces.
• It specifically measures the relative humidity
of the atmosphere, which is the ratio of the
actual amount of water vapor in the air to the
maximum amount it could hold at that
temperature.
• An instrument used to measure the density of
a liquid is called a hydrometer. (Note: The
provided answer key says (a), which is
incorrect. A hygrometer measures humidity).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Kilowatt is the unit of
(a) Voltage
(b) Power
(c) Resistance
(d) Current

The correct option is (b).
• The fundamental unit of power in the
International System of Units (SI) is the Watt
(W). Power is the rate at which energy is used
or work is done.
• A kilowatt (kW) is a multiple of the watt.
"Kilo" is the prefix for one thousand.
• Therefore, one kilowatt is equal to 1,000
watts. It is a unit of power, commonly used to
express the output of engines or the power
consumption of electrical devices. (Note: The
provided explanation incorrectly describes a
kilowatt-hour, which is a unit of energy, not
power).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. The sun derives its energy from
(a) Nuclear fission reactions
(b) Nuclear fusion reactions
(c) Oxidation of helium
(d) Fission of noble metals
The correct option is (b).
• The immense energy produced by the Sun is
the result of nuclear fusion reactions
occurring in its extremely hot and dense core.
• In this process, hydrogen nuclei (protons) are
fused together under immense pressure and
temperature to form helium nuclei.
• During this fusion, a small amount of mass is
converted into a tremendous amount of
energy, as described by E=mc². This energy
radiates outwards, providing light and heat to
the solar system.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q7. A man starts from a certain point O and follows
the route as shown in the graph. Which of the
following statements is correct?

•

•

(a) He travels with a uniform velocity throughout
(b) He travels with a greater velocity from O to A than
from A to B
(c) He travels with a greater velocity from A to B than
from O to A
(d) He returns to the original point with a non-uniform
velocity
The correct option is (c).
• The graph shows Distance on the y-axis and
Time on the x-axis. Velocity is represented by
the slope (steepness) of the line on a
distance-time graph.
• From O to A, the man travels a certain
distance in a given time, represented by the
slope of the line segment OA.
• From A to B, the man travels a larger distance
in a shorter amount of time. The line
segment AB is steeper than the line segment
OA.
• A steeper slope indicates a greater velocity.
Therefore, the man travels with a greater
velocity from A to B than from O to A.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. Electrodes are used in Voltaic cells. These are of:
(a) Zinc and lead
(b) Carbon and zinc
(c) Zinc and copper
(d) Carbon and nickel
The correct option is (c).
• A classic example of a voltaic cell (or galvanic
cell) is the Daniell cell.
• This cell uses an electrode made of zinc (the
anode, where oxidation occurs) placed in a
zinc sulfate solution, and an electrode made

of copper (the cathode, where reduction
occurs) placed in a copper sulfate solution.
The difference in the electrochemical
potential between the zinc and copper
electrodes drives the flow of electrons,
generating an electric current. This
combination is a fundamental demonstration
of how a voltaic cell works.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1983

Q1. What is the difference between an explosion and
combustion?
(a) In the former nuclear reaction takes place while in
the latter case burning takes place
(b) In the former chemical energy is transformed into
nuclear energy while in the latter case the chemical
energy is transformed into heat energy
(c) In the former it is a violent and rapid reaction with
the release of much heat energy while in the latter
case it is generally a slow reaction with the release of
less heat energy
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (c).
• Combustion is a chemical process that
releases energy in the form of heat and light.
It is essentially burning.
• While it can be rapid (like in a fire), it is
generally a controlled or slower process
compared to an explosion.
• An explosion is a type of combustion that
occurs extremely rapidly, producing a large
volume of hot gas in a very short time.
• This rapid expansion of gas creates a
shockwave and is characterized by its violent
and rapid nature, releasing a large amount of
energy almost instantaneously.
• Therefore, the key difference is the speed and
violence of the reaction — an explosion is a
very fast, violent form of combustion.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. Assertion (A): The same side of the moon is
always seen from the earth.

Reason (R): The moon rotates on its axis once in each
orbital revolution.
(a) If both A and R are true but R is not the reason for
A
(b) If both A and R are true and R is the reason for A
(c) If neither A nor R is correct
(d) If A is correct but R is wrong
The correct option is (b).
• Assertion (A) is true — from our perspective
on Earth, we always see the same face of the
Moon, a phenomenon known as synchronous
rotation or tidal locking.
• Reason (R) is also true — the Moon
completes one rotation on its axis in
approximately the same amount of time it
takes to complete one orbit around the Earth
(about 27.3 days).
• R is the correct explanation of A, because the
Moon’s rotation period perfectly matches its
orbital period, so the same hemisphere
constantly faces Earth.
• This synchronous rotation is the direct cause
of the phenomenon described in the
assertion.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. "Mach Number" is a term associated with the
speed of
(a) Ships
(b) Cars
(c) Aeroplanes
(d) Light
The correct option is (c).
• Mach number is a dimensionless quantity in
fluid dynamics, representing the ratio of the
speed of an object moving through a fluid to
the local speed of sound in that fluid.
• It is most commonly used to describe the
speed of high-speed aircraft, such as jets and
rockets.
• For example, Mach 1 is the speed of sound,
and an aircraft flying at Mach 2 is traveling at
twice the speed of sound.

•

•

This term is crucial in aerodynamics and is
almost exclusively associated with the speed
of aeroplanes and aerospace vehicles.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q1. Why do we feel chill after having a dip in sea
water?
(a) The difference in temperature between sea water
and the atmosphere
(b) Evaporation of water from the body
(c) Deposition of salt on the body
(d) None of these
The correct option is (b).
• The primary reason for feeling a chill after
getting out of water is the process of
evaporation.
• For the water on your skin to evaporate (turn
from a liquid to a gas), it needs energy in the
form of heat.
• This heat energy is drawn from your body,
causing your skin temperature to drop and
making you feel cold.
• This process is known as evaporative cooling
or the wind-chill effect.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. The surface of the water in a lake is frozen and
its temperature is -15°C. The maximum possible
temperature of the water inside the lake in contact
with the ice layer will be
(a) 0°C
(b) 4°C
(c) -15°C
(d) Insufficient data to predict
The correct option is (a).
• The layer of water directly in contact with the
underside of the ice must be at the
freezing/melting point of water.
• The freezing point of fresh water is 0°C, so
the water just below the ice will be at 0°C.
• Deeper water below this layer might be
warmer (up to 4°C), where water reaches its

maximum density, but the layer immediately
touching the ice is always 0°C.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
(Note: The provided key says (b), but that
refers to water at the lake bottom, not in
contact with the ice layer.)

•

Q3. A stone is tied to a rope and attached to a
wooden bar which rotates at a constant angular
velocity. Suddenly the bar is stopped. The stone gets
encircled around the bar. What happens to the
angular velocity of the stone?
(a) Increases
(b) Decreases
(c) Remains the same
(d) First increases and then decreases
The correct option is (a).
• This is an example of the conservation of
angular momentum.
• Angular momentum (L) = I × ω, where I is the
moment of inertia and ω is the angular
velocity.
• When the bar stops, the rope begins to wrap
around it, causing the radius (r) of the stone’s
circular path to decrease.
• Since I = m × r², a smaller radius means a
smaller moment of inertia (I).
• With no external torque, L remains constant,
so if I decreases, ω must increase.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

•

•
•

Q4. The cover of a solar cooker is made of glass.
Why?
(a) It absorbs sun rays
(b) It reflects sun rays
(c) Sun heat is transmitted through glass without
much absorption
(d) None of these
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•
•

A solar cooker works by trapping heat from
the sun — this is the greenhouse effect.
The glass cover is transparent to shortwavelength visible light, allowing sunlight to
enter the cooker.
This light is absorbed by the dark interior and
cooking pot, which heats up.
The heated interior re-radiates longwavelength infrared radiation, which the
glass does not let escape easily.
Thus, the glass transmits sunlight but traps
heat inside.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. When a brick is dropped in water, bubbles are
seen to rise. This is due to
(a) Air in the pores of the brick
(b) Air in the water
(c) Water displaced by the brick
(d) None of these
The correct option is (a).
• Bricks are porous materials containing many
tiny air-filled spaces.
• When a dry brick is dropped into water, the
water seeps into these pores, displacing the
trapped air.
• This air escapes to the surface in the form of
bubbles.
• The displacement of water (option c) does
occur but does not cause the rising air
bubbles.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

(d) None of these

It is the only short-period comet regularly
visible to the naked eye and can appear twice
in a lifetime.
Its appearances have been recorded since 240
BC.
It last appeared in 1986 and will return

Hence, option (a) is correct.

•

The jerk is due to the Law of Inertia
(Newton’s First Law of Motion).
• When the bus is moving, the passenger’s body
is also in motion.
• When the bus suddenly stops, the lower part
of the body stops, but the upper part
continues moving due to inertia.
• This causes the person to jerk forward.
Hence option a is correct.

Q7.Assertion (A): Eclipses do not occur on all full
moon and new moon days.
Reason (R): The moon revolves round the earth in an
elliptical orbit.
Options:
(a) A and R both are incorrect
(b) A is correct but R is incorrect
(c) A and R are correct and R is the correct explanation
of A
(d) A and R are correct but R is not the correct
explanation of A
Correct Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Eclipses do not occur on every full moon or
new moon day.
• This is because the Moon’s orbital plane is
inclined by about 5° to the Earth’s orbital
plane (the ecliptic).
• Hence, the Sun, Earth, and Moon do not
always align perfectly.
• The Moon’s orbit is indeed elliptical, but that
is not the reason for the absence of monthly
eclipses.
• Therefore, both A and R are correct, but R
does not explain A.

Q1. For pasteurization by the Flash Method, milk is
heated to a temperature of
(a) 0°C
(b) 100°C
(c) -4°C
(d) 72°C
The correct option is (d).
• Pasteurization is a process of heating a liquid
to a specific temperature for a predefined
time and then rapidly cooling it to slow
microbial growth.
• The Flash Method, also known as HighTemperature Short-Time (HTST)
pasteurization, is the most common modern
method.
• In this method, milk is heated to 72°C
(161.5°F) for 15 seconds, followed by rapid
cooling.
• This method kills harmful bacteria while
maintaining the taste and nutrition of milk.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Halley’s comet has to appear in the solar family
in the year
(a) 1986
(b) 1988
(c) 1989
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (a).
• Halley’s Comet is a periodic comet, returning
approximately every 75–76 years.
• It has been observed for centuries and is one
of the most famous celestial phenomena.

•
•

Its most recent appearance was in 1986, and
the next one is expected in 2061.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. Mesons are found in
(a) X-rays
(b) Cosmic rays
(c) Gamma rays
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (b).
• Mesons are unstable subatomic particles
composed of one quark and one antiquark.
• They are not found naturally on Earth but are
produced during high-energy particle
interactions.
• Cosmic rays, which are high-energy particles
from outer space, collide with atmospheric
particles and produce secondary particles,
including mesons (like pions and kaons).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Study the frequency of the following
electromagnetic radiations and select the correct
relationship ('>' implies greater than):
I. Infra-red
II. Microwave
III. Ultra-violet
Codes:
(a) I > II > III
(b) III > I > II
(c) III > II > I
(d) II > III > I
The correct option is (b).
• The electromagnetic spectrum is arranged (by
increasing frequency) as:
Radio waves → Microwaves → Infra-red →
Visible → Ultra-violet → X-rays → Gamma
rays.
• Among the given:
o Microwave (II) = lowest frequency
o Infra-red (I) = higher frequency than
microwaves
o Ultra-violet (III) = highest frequency
of all three

•
•

Therefore, III > I > II (Ultra-violet > Infra-red >
Microwave).
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. Given:
Humidity – H
Wind Velocity – W
Temperature – T
High – A
Low – B
The desert cooler is an appropriate cooling device for
regions where
(a) H—A, W—A, T—B
(b) H—B, W—B, T—A
(c) H—A, W—B, T—B
(d) H—B, W—A, T—A
The correct option is (d).
• A desert cooler works on the principle of
evaporative cooling.
• It is most efficient when:
o Humidity (H) is Low (B) — dry air
allows faster evaporation.
o Temperature (T) is High (A) — more
heat available to evaporate water.
o Wind Velocity (W) is High (A) —
ensures air circulation and cool air
distribution.
• These conditions — low humidity, high wind,
high temperature — are typical of arid
regions.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. Black hole is
(a) A magnetic dark cavity
(b) Name of a star
(c) A hole in the moon
(d) A dying star
The correct option is (d).
• A black hole is a region of spacetime where
gravity is so intense that nothing can escape,
not even light.
• It is formed when a massive star exhausts its
fuel and its core collapses under its own
gravity.

•

•
•

This collapse leads to an object of infinite
density and zero volume, creating an event
horizon from which no light escapes.
Thus, a black hole is the final stage of a dying
massive star.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q7. Ball-bearings are used to reduce the friction by
(a) Reducing the area of contact with the use of
metallic balls
(b) Increasing the area of contact with the use of
metallic balls
(c) Applying lubricants to the balls used
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (a).
• Ball bearings are used to reduce friction
between moving parts.
• They do so by replacing sliding friction with
rolling friction, which is much smaller.
• The metallic balls create a very small contact
area, thus reducing frictional force.
• Though lubricants may be used, the main
principle is rolling over reduced contact area.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8. A microphone is a device in which the
(a) Sound waves are converted into electrical energy
and then reconverted into sound after transmission
(b) Sound waves are directly transmitted
(c) Electrical energy is converted into sound waves
directly
(d) Amplification is not required at all
The correct option is (a).
• A microphone acts as a transducer that
converts sound waves into electrical energy.
• The electrical signal is then transmitted or
amplified.
• Later, a speaker performs the reverse
process, converting the electrical signal back
into sound.
• Therefore, the microphone’s function is part
of the sound-to-electric-to-sound
transmission process.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q9. Ice cubes are floating in a glass of water. When
the ice cubes melt, the water level in the glass
(a) Will first increase and then start decreasing
(b) Will first decrease and then start increasing
(c) Will remain unchanged
(d) Will increase
The correct option is (c).
• This is explained by Archimedes’ principle.
• A floating object displaces a volume of water
equal in weight to itself.
• The ice cube displaces a volume of water
whose weight equals the weight of the ice.
• When the ice melts, it turns into water of the
same weight, occupying the same displaced
volume.
• Thus, the water level remains constant.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

(d) Lactometer – Specific gravity of liquids
The correct option is (d).
• (a) Seismograph – measures earthquakes →

• (b) Anemometer – measures wind speed →

Correct.
• (d) Lactometer – measures purity/density of
milk, not general liquid density → Incorrect.
• The instrument for specific gravity of liquids
in general is a hydrometer.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
