Q12.If the distance that a radio wave can travel in the
time of one complete cycle is 10 metres, then the
frequency of the radio wave in cycles per second is —
Options:
(a) 3 × 10⁸ cycle/sec
(b) 3 × 10⁶ cycle/sec
(c) 3 × 10⁹ cycle/sec
(d) 3 × 10⁷ cycle/sec
Correct Option: (d)
Explanation:
• The speed of radio waves (electromagnetic
waves) in air or vacuum = 3 × 10⁸ m/s.
• The distance travelled in one cycle =
wavelength (λ) = 10 m.
• The relationship between velocity, frequency,
and wavelength is:
𝑣 =𝑓×𝜆
•

Substituting values:
3 × 108 = 𝑓 × 10
𝑓 = 3 × 107 cycles/second (or Hz)

•

Therefore, the frequency of the radio wave =
3 × 10⁷ Hz.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q13.The centre of gravity of the floating ship —
Options:
(a) Lies above the metacentre
(b) Lies below the metacentre
(c) Coincides with the metacentre
(d) None of the above
Correct Option: (a)
Explanation:
• A floating body (like a ship) remains stable
due to the concept of metacentric height.
• When the ship tilts, the centre of buoyancy
shifts sideways.

•

The point where the line of action of buoyant
force cuts the original vertical line is called the
metacentre (M).
• The centre of gravity (G) of the ship lies
slightly below the upper deck, above the
metacentre.
• The distance between metacentre and centre
of gravity is called metacentric height (GM).
• A positive metacentric height provides
restoring couple, helping the ship return to its
upright position — ensuring stability even in
turbulent seas.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1986
Q1. Atmospheric temperature increases at the higher
altitudes due to
(a) Convection
(b) Radiation
(c) Conduction
(d) Inversion
The correct option is (d).
• Normally, in the troposphere, temperature
decreases as altitude increases (the normal
lapse rate).
• Temperature inversion is a phenomenon
where this normal pattern is reversed, and a
layer of warmer air sits on top of a layer of
cooler air.
• Inversions can trap pollutants and are
common on clear, calm nights.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. A small wooden block is floating in a tub of
water. The water is gradually heated. The volume of
the wooden block visible above the water level
(a) Increases
(b) Decreases
(c) Fluctuates
(d) Remains the same
The correct option is (b).
• By Archimedes' principle, a floating object
displaces a weight of fluid equal to its own
weight; the block’s weight is constant.

•
•

•
•

When water is heated, it expands and its
density decreases.
To support the same weight in less dense
water, a larger submerged volume is
required, so the block sinks deeper.
Therefore, the visible part of the block above
the water decreases.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. If a toy boat in a tank sinks, the level of water
will
(a) Increase
(b) Decrease
(c) Fluctuate
(d) Remain the same
The correct option is (b).
• While floating, the toy boat displaces a
volume of water whose weight equals the
boat’s weight.
• When it sinks, it is fully submerged and
displaces a volume equal to the solid material
of the boat (not the larger volume it displaced
while floating).
• Since the floating displacement was larger
(boat is hollow), when it sinks it displaces less
water, so the water level decreases.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Black hole
(a) Does not emit any radiations
(b) Converts UV/radiations to infra-red radiations
(c) Absorbs all the radiations that fall on it
(d) Is an imaginary concept in Physics
The correct option is (c).
• A black hole has a gravitational field so
intense that nothing, not even light, can
escape from it.
• Because nothing can escape, a black hole
effectively absorbs all radiation that falls on
it, appearing perfectly black.
• (Note: Hawking radiation is a theoretical
exception over extremely long timescales, but
the defining property is absorption.)
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. Which of the following is the correct sequence in
order of increasing power consumption?
(a) Television, Fan, Electric Kettle, Electric Iron
(b) Television, Fan, Electric Iron, Electric Kettle
(c) Fan, Television, Electric Kettle, Electric Iron
(d) Fan, Television, Electric Iron, Electric Kettle
The correct option is (d).
• Typical power ranges (approximate): Fan
(~50–100 W) < Television (~100–200 W) <
Electric Iron (~1000–1500 W) < Electric Kettle
(~1500–3000 W).
• Therefore, the increasing sequence is Fan <
Television < Electric Iron < Electric Kettle.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. To increase the voltage of an alternating current,
we can use
(a) Capacitor
(b) Transformer
(c) Inverter
(d) Rectifier
The correct option is (b).
• A transformer transfers electrical energy
between circuits by electromagnetic
induction and can increase (step-up) or
decrease (step-down) AC voltages.
• A capacitor stores energy, an inverter
converts DC → AC, and a rectifier converts AC
→ DC.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Assertion (A): Solar eclipse occurs on a new
moon day.
Reason (R): Moon is in conjunction with the sun.
(a) if A and R both are correct and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) if A and R both are correct but R is not the correct
explanation of A
(c) if A is correct and R is incorrect
(d) if A is incorrect and R is correct
The correct option is (a).
• A is true: a solar eclipse happens when the
Moon passes between the Sun and Earth,
which occurs at New Moon.

•

•

•

R is true: the Moon being in conjunction with
the Sun means it is aligned between Earth
and Sun.
R correctly explains A, since this alignment
(conjunction) is the condition for a solar
eclipse.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q8. Assertion (A): Comets have gaseous tails.
Reason (R): They are very hot bodies.
(a) if A and R both are correct and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) if A and R both are correct but R is not the correct
explanation of A
(c) if A is correct and R is incorrect
(d) if A is incorrect and R is correct
The correct option is (c).
• A is true: comets develop gaseous tails of gas
and dust when near the Sun.
• R is false: comets are not inherently hot
bodies; they are composed of ice and dust.
• The tail forms because solar radiation causes
sublimation of ices and the solar wind pushes
the gas/dust away, not because the comet
itself is hot.
• Therefore, A is correct and R is incorrect.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1987
Q1. Which of the following groups of instruments is
required to have a communication line between two
television relay centres?

(a) Microwave link, telephone, booster relay, satellite
(b) Radio telescope, microwave link, booster relay,
telephone
(c) Microwave link, booster relay, satellite
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (c).
• To transmit a television signal over a long
distance between two relay centers, a
combination of technologies is used.
• Microwave link: This is used for line-of-sight
terrestrial communication over shorter to
medium distances. The signal is transmitted
from one tower to another.
• Booster relay: These are repeaters or
amplifiers placed along the communication
path to boost the signal strength and
compensate for attenuation over distance.
• Satellite: For very long distances or to
overcome geographical obstacles, the signal is
sent up to a communication satellite, which
then re-transmits it down to the receiving
relay center.
• A telephone is a terminal device for voice
communication and a radio telescope is for
astronomy; neither is a core part of the
transmission infrastructure between relay
centers.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. The ozone layer of the atmosphere absorbs
(a) Ultraviolet rays
(b) Infrared rays
(c) All radiations of light
(d) Cosmic rays
The correct option is (a).
• The ozone layer, located in the stratosphere,
plays a crucial role in protecting life on Earth.
• Its primary function is to absorb the majority
of the Sun's harmful ultraviolet (UV)
radiation, particularly the more dangerous
UVB and UVC wavelengths.
• By absorbing this radiation, the ozone layer
prevents it from reaching the Earth's surface,
where it can cause skin cancer, cataracts, and
damage to plant and animal life.

•

•

Infrared rays are associated with heat, and
cosmic rays are high-energy particles from
space.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

•

•

Q3. The fuel used in the Fast Breeder Test Reactor at
Kalpakkam is
(a) Enriched Uranium
(b) Thorium
(c) Plutonium
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (c).
• The Fast Breeder Test Reactor (FBTR) at the
Indira Gandhi Centre for Atomic Research
(IGCAR) in Kalpakkam is a key part of India's
three-stage nuclear power program.
• Unlike conventional reactors that use
enriched uranium, a fast breeder reactor is
designed to "breed" more fissile material than
it consumes.
• The FBTR was designed to use a mixed
carbide fuel of plutonium and uranium. The
initial core was loaded with weapons-grade
plutonium, making it the fuel for this type of
reactor.
• Thorium is used in the third stage of India's
nuclear program.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. Which of the following is a conventional source
of energy?
(a) Geothermal
(b) Hydropower
(c) Solar
(d) Wind
The correct option is (b).
• Conventional sources of energy are those
that have been in common use for a long time
and are typically non-renewable (like fossil
fuels) or are large-scale and well-established
renewable sources.
• Hydropower, especially from large dams, has
been used for electricity generation on a
massive scale for over a century and is
considered a conventional source of energy.

Geothermal, solar, and wind energy, while
renewable, are generally classified as nonconventional or alternative energy sources
because their large-scale use is more recent.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. Acceleration due to gravity on the moon is 0.166
times that on the earth. A man weighing 60 kg on
earth would weigh ------kg on the moon.
(a) 16.6 kg
(b) 30 kg
(c) 60 kg
(d) 10 kg
The correct option is (d).
• This question contains a common confusion
between mass and weight.
• The man's mass is 60 kg. Mass is the amount
of matter in an object and it remains constant
regardless of location.
• Weight is the force of gravity acting on that
mass (Weight = mass × g). The unit of weight
is the Newton (N). The question incorrectly
asks for weight in kg, but it clearly intends to
find the apparent weight or the mass that
would produce the same force on Earth.
• Weight on Earth = 60 kg × g_earth.
• Weight on Moon = 60 kg × g_moon = 60 kg ×
(0.166 × g_earth) ≈ 9.96 × g_earth.
• This means on the Moon, the man would feel
as if he weighed approximately 10 kg on
Earth.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. Resistance of a 100-watt bulb is R₁ and that of a
60-watt bulb is R₂. Which of the following is correct?
(a) R₁/R₂ > 1
(b) R₁/R₂ < 1
(c) R₁/R₂ = 1
(d) Data is inadequate
The correct option is (b).
• The relationship between power (P), voltage
(V), and resistance (R) is given by the formula
P = V²/R.
• This can be rearranged to R = V²/P.

•

•

•
•

Assuming both bulbs operate at the same
standard voltage (V), their resistance is
inversely proportional to their power rating
(R ∝ 1/P).
This means the bulb with the higher power
rating (100 W) will have the lower resistance,
and the bulb with the lower power rating (60
W) will have the higher resistance.
Therefore, R₁ (100W) is less than R₂ (60W), so
R₁/R₂ < 1.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q7. A man coming down in a lift leaves a ball from
his hand. The ball will
(a) Go up
(b) Fall down
(c) Remain stationary with respect to the man
(d) Oscillate up and down
The correct option is (c).
• This question describes the principle of
relativity of motion.
• When the man releases the ball, both he and
the ball are already moving downwards with
the same velocity as the lift.
• Assuming the lift is moving at a constant
velocity (not accelerating or decelerating),
both the man and the ball will continue to be
affected by gravity in the same way.
• Since they both start with the same
downward velocity and are subject to the
same downward acceleration (g), their
relative positions will not change.
• Therefore, from the man's perspective, the
ball will appear to remain stationary with
respect to him.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. The stars receive their energy from which of the
following?
(a) Chemical reaction
(b) Nuclear Fusion
(c) Nuclear Fission
(d) Gravitational pull
The correct option is (b).

•
•

•

•

•

Stars, including our Sun, are powered by
nuclear fusion occurring in their cores.
Under the immense pressure and extreme
temperatures found in the core of a star,
lighter atomic nuclei (primarily hydrogen) are
forced together to form heavier nuclei (like
helium).
This fusion process releases a vast amount of
energy in the form of light and heat, which is
what makes stars shine.
Nuclear fission is the splitting of heavy atoms,
and chemical reactions release far too little
energy to power a star.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q9. Reading of the barometer going down is an
indication of
(a) Storm
(b) Rainfall
(c) Snow
(d) Intense heat
The correct option is (b).
• A barometer measures atmospheric pressure.
• A falling or "going down" barometer indicates
that the atmospheric pressure is decreasing.
• Low pressure systems are associated with
rising air, which cools and condenses to form
clouds and precipitation.
• Therefore, a falling barometer is a classic
indication of approaching bad weather, such
as rainfall or a storm. A rapid or sudden drop
often indicates a storm (a), but rainfall (b) is
the more general and certain outcome of
falling pressure.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q10. Which of the following is used in a diesel
engine?
1. Cylinder
2. Spark Plug
3. Piston
Codes: (a) 1, 2 and 3 (b) 1 and 2 (c) 2 and 3 (d)
1 and 3
The correct option is (d).

•

•

•

•
•

1. Cylinder: This is a fundamental component
of any internal combustion engine, including
diesel engines. It is the chamber where
combustion takes place.
2. Spark Plug: This is not used in a diesel
engine. Diesel engines use compression
ignition, where the air in the cylinder is
compressed to such a high temperature that
the injected diesel fuel ignites spontaneously
without the need for a spark. Gasoline
engines use spark plugs.
3. Piston: This is another essential
component. The piston moves up and down
inside the cylinder, compressing the air and
being pushed down by the force of
combustion.
Therefore, a diesel engine uses a cylinder and
a piston, but not a spark plug.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q11. Distribution of electric power from one place to
another is done at high A.C. voltage because
(a) Wastage of electricity is minimised
(b) Stealing of electric wires is prevented
(c) Electricity spreads in a very short time
(d) It adds brightness
The correct option is (a).
• When electricity is transmitted over long
distances through power lines, some energy is
lost as heat due to the electrical resistance of
the wires. This power loss is given by P_loss =
I²R, where I is the current and R is the
resistance.
• To transmit a certain amount of power (P = V
× I), if you increase the voltage (V), you can
decrease the current (I) for the same amount
of power.
• By transmitting at a very high voltage, the
current is kept very low. Since power loss is
proportional to the square of the current (I²),
this dramatically reduces the amount of
energy lost as heat.
• Therefore, transmitting at high AC voltage
minimises the wastage of electricity.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q12. A ball is thrown up vertically in a vacuum.
Which of the following time vs velocity graphs
depicts the motion?

(a)
(b)
(c)
(d)
The correct option is (d).
• The question describes the motion of an
object under constant acceleration (gravity)
in a vacuum, meaning there is no air
resistance.
• Velocity–Time Graph: When the ball is
thrown up, it has an initial positive (upward)
velocity.
• Gravity acts downwards, causing a constant
negative acceleration (-g). This means the
velocity will decrease linearly over time.
• The velocity will decrease from its initial
positive value, pass through zero at the peak
of its trajectory, and then become increasingly
negative as it falls back down.
• Graph (d) correctly shows a straight line with
a constant negative slope, starting with a
positive velocity, crossing the time axis (zero
velocity), and continuing into negative
velocities.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q13. An astronaut weighing 100 kg fires a bullet
weighing 1 kg with a gun of 10 kg at the speed of 50
metres/second. What will be the recoil velocity of
the gun?
(a) 5 cm/s
(b) 50 cm/s
(c) 0.5 cm/s
(d) 0.05 cm/s
The correct option is (b).
• This problem is an application of the law of
conservation of linear momentum. The total
momentum of the system (astronaut + gun +

•

•
•
•
•
•

•

•
•
•

bullet) before firing is zero. Therefore, the
total momentum after firing must also be
zero.
The astronaut's mass is irrelevant if we are
only considering the recoil of the gun relative
to the astronaut. Let's assume the question
means the recoil of the gun held by the
astronaut. The total mass recoiling is the mass
of the astronaut plus the gun.
Let M = mass of astronaut + gun = 100 kg + 10
kg = 110 kg.
Let m = mass of bullet = 1 kg.
Let V = recoil velocity of the astronaut and
gun.
Let v = velocity of the bullet = 50 m/s.
Conservation of momentum: (Momentum of
astronaut+gun) + (Momentum of bullet) = 0
→ M × V + m × v = 0.
110 × V + 1 × 50 = 0 → 110 × V = -50 → V = -50
/ 110 ≈ -0.4545 m/s. The negative sign
indicates direction opposite to the bullet.
Converting to cm/s: -0.4545 m/s × 100 cm/m
= -45.45 cm/s. This is approximately 50 cm/s.
Therefore, the recoil velocity (magnitude) is
closest to 50 cm/s.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q14. To a person on the moon, the sky appears
(a) White
(b) Black
(c) Blue
(d) Orange
The correct option is (b).
• This is similar to previous questions about sky
colour. The sky appears blue on Earth due to
the scattering of sunlight by the molecules in
our atmosphere (Rayleigh scattering).
• The Moon has no significant atmosphere.
• Without an atmosphere to scatter sunlight, an
observer on the Moon would see the Sun as a
very bright star in a completely black sky,
even during the lunar day.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1988
Q1. Neutron was discovered by
(a) Otto Hahn
(b) Enrico Fermi
(c) Lise Meitner
(d) James Chadwick
The correct option is (d).
• In 1932, the English physicist James Chadwick
conducted experiments proving the existence
of a neutral particle within the atomic
nucleus.
• He identified this particle — similar in mass to
a proton but with no electrical charge — and
named it the neutron.
• This discovery was a landmark in nuclear
physics and completed the basic atomic
model (protons, neutrons, electrons).
• Chadwick was awarded the Nobel Prize in
Physics (1935) for this discovery.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Let E₁, E₂, E₃ denote the efficiency of a diesel, a
petrol, and a steam engine respectively. Which of the
following is correct?
(a) E₂ > E₁ > E₃
(b) E₃ < E₂ < E₁
(c) E₁ > E₂ > E₃
(d) E₃ < E₁ < E₂
The correct option is (c).
• Steam engines (E₃) are external combustion
engines with the lowest efficiency (≈15–20%)
due to large heat losses.
• Petrol (gasoline) engines (E₂) are internal
combustion engines with moderate efficiency
(≈25–30%).
• Diesel engines (E₁) operate at higher
compression ratios and therefore have higher
thermal efficiency (≈40–45%).
• Thus the order is Diesel > Petrol > Steam, i.e.,
E₁ > E₂ > E₃.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. The resolving power of an electron microscope is
much higher than that of an ordinary light

microscope because resolving power increases
(a) If the wavelength is short and the wavelength of
the electron beam can be made quite small by
accelerating the electrons.
(b) If the wavelength is short and light given by
electrons has very short wavelength.
(c) If magnification is high and electronic lens provides
much higher magnification
(d) If magnification is high and the electron
microscope has a battery of lenses to provide high
magnification.
The correct option is (a).
• Resolving power depends fundamentally on
the wavelength of the illuminating radiation
— shorter wavelength → higher resolution.
• A light microscope uses visible light
(wavelengths ~400–700 nm).
• An electron microscope uses accelerated
electrons, whose de Broglie wavelength can
be made extremely short by high acceleration
(much shorter than visible light).
• That very short wavelength is the primary
reason for the vastly superior resolving
power of electron microscopes.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. The Sri Lanka Broadcasting Corporation
broadcasts its programmes on the 25-metre band.
Which one of the following is the frequency of this
transmission?
(a) 12 Kilo Hertz
(b) 1.2 Kilo Hertz
(c) 12 Mega Hertz
(d) 120 Mega Hertz
The correct option is (c).
• Wave speed relation: c = f × λ, where c ≈ 3 ×
10⁸ m/s and λ = 25 m.
• Frequency: f = c / λ = (3 × 10⁸) / 25 = 1.2 × 10⁷
Hz = 12 × 10⁶ Hz = 12 MHz.
• Therefore the 25-metre band corresponds to
~12 Mega Hertz (MHz).
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. If camera A has an f/4.5 lens and camera B has
an f/2.8 lens, and the diameters of both the lenses

are equal, then
(a) Pictures taken by A will always be sharper.
(b) Pictures taken by B will always be sharper.
(c) A is better for photographing fast-moving objects.
(d) B is better for photographing fast-moving objects.
The correct option is (d).
• The f-number (e.g., f/4.5, f/2.8) is the ratio of
focal length to aperture diameter; a lower fnumber = wider aperture.
• A wider aperture (lower f-number) allows
more light, enabling faster shutter speeds for
the same exposure.
• Faster shutter speeds help freeze motion, so
a camera with f/2.8 (B) is better for
photographing fast-moving objects.
• Sharpness depends on many factors (lens
quality, depth of field), not just f-number.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. Seawater is saltier than rainwater because
(a) Sea beds have salt-producing mines.
(b) Rivers wash away salts from the earth and pour
them into the sea.
(c) The air around the sea is saltish.
(d) Sea animals are salt-producing.
The correct option is (b).
• Rain erodes rocks on land, dissolving minerals
and salts.
• Rivers and streams carry these dissolved salts
into the oceans.
• When water evaporates from the ocean, the
salts remain behind, causing gradual
accumulation of salt over geological time.
• This continuous cycle makes the seawater
salty, whereas rainwater is relatively pure.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. A free-floating astronaut 'A' pushes another freefloating astronaut 'B' in space. The mass of 'A' is
greater than that of 'B'. The force exerted by the
astronaut 'A' on the astronaut 'B' will be
(a) Equal to zero.
(b) Equal to the force exerted by 'B' on 'A'.
(c) Greater than the force exerted by 'B' on 'A'.
(d) Less than the force exerted by 'B' on 'A'.

The correct option is (b).
• By Newton's Third Law, every action has an
equal and opposite reaction.
• When A pushes B, B pushes A with an equal
magnitude force in the opposite direction.
• The masses affect the resulting accelerations
(F = m·a) — the less massive astronaut will
experience greater acceleration — but the
forces are equal and opposite.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q8. On the Moon, an astronaut inflates a rubber
balloon with hydrogen gas and releases it at a height
of 2 metres. The balloon will
(a) Fall down
(b) Go upwards
(c) Remain at the height of 2 metres
(d) First go up and finally come down
The correct option is (a).
• Buoyancy requires a surrounding fluid
(atmosphere); an object rises if it is less dense
than the fluid it displaces.
• The Moon has no significant atmosphere
(essentially a vacuum), so no buoyant force
will act on the balloon.
• With no air to displace, the only significant
force is the Moon’s gravity, so the balloon will
fall down.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9. The phenomenon of mirage is due to
(a) Increased absorption of light by air at higher
temperature
(b) Change in the refractive index of air with the
change in temperature
(c) Total internal reflection
(d) Decreased absorption of light by air at higher
temperature
The correct option is (b).
• A mirage occurs because layers of air at
different temperatures have different
densities, thus different refractive indices.
• Light rays bend (refract) when passing
through these layers; on hot surfaces the
refractive index gradient can bend rays

•

•

upwards, making distant sky or objects
appear as reflections on the ground.
While total internal reflection can sometimes
be involved, the fundamental cause is the
change in refractive index with temperature.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q10. The limit beyond which stars suffer internal
collapse is called
(a) Chandrasekhar limit
(b) Eddington limit
(c) Hoyle limit
(d) Fowler limit
The correct option is (a).
• The Chandrasekhar limit (~1.4 solar masses)
is the maximum mass a white dwarf can have
while being supported by electron
degeneracy pressure.
• If a white dwarf exceeds this limit, electron
degeneracy pressure fails and the star can
collapse, possibly leading to a supernova or
formation of a neutron star.
• The Eddington limit concerns maximum
luminosity before radiation pressure
overcomes gravity.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1989
Q1. A boat filled with some stones is floating in
water. If the stones are dropped into the water, the
level of the water will
(a) Rise
(b) Decrease
(c) Remain the same
(d) Insufficient data to predict
The correct option is (b).
• This is a classic physics problem involving
Archimedes' Principle.
• Case 1 (stones in the boat): the floating boat
displaces a volume of water whose weight
equals the combined weight of the boat and
stones.
• Case 2 (stones in the water): the stones
displace water equal to their own volume,
and the boat now displaces less water.
• Because stones are much denser than water,
the submerged volume they occupy is much
less than the volume they caused the boat to
displace while onboard.
• Therefore the total displaced volume (and
thus the water level) decreases when the
stones are dropped overboard.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. Total internal reflection occurs when light travels
from
(a) A rarer medium to a denser medium
(b) A denser medium to a rarer medium
(c) A rarer medium to a denser medium and the angle
of incidence is less than the critical angle.
(d) A rarer medium to a denser medium and the angle
of incidence is greater than the critical angle.
The correct option is (b).
• Total Internal Reflection (TIR) occurs when
light tries to pass from a denser medium to a
rarer medium (e.g., glass → air).
• A second necessary condition is that the angle
of incidence exceeds the critical angle for that
interface.
• Option (b) correctly identifies the required
direction of travel for TIR to be possible.

•

Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. A chair is tilted about two of its legs and then
left. It would return to its original position if
(a) Its centre of gravity falls outside the base.
(b) Its centre of gravity falls within the base.
(c) It is tilted through an angle of 60°.
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (b).
• Stability depends on the position of the
centre of gravity (CG) relative to the base of
support.
• If the vertical line from the CG falls within the
base, the object is stable and will return to its
original position.
• If the CG moves outside the base, the object
becomes unstable and will topple.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Very small-time intervals are accurately
measured by the
(a) Pulsars
(b) White dwarfs
(c) Atomic clocks
(d) Quartz clocks
The correct option is (c).
• Atomic clocks use the resonant frequency of
atoms (e.g., caesium-133) as their
timekeeping standard.
• The atomic oscillations are extremely regular,
allowing measurement of very small time
intervals with outstanding precision.
• Quartz clocks are accurate but less precise
than atomic clocks; pulsars are astronomical
timekeepers but not standard instruments for
general use.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. Insolation refers to
(a) Restraining the flow of electric current
(b) Restraining the flow of water
(c) Exposure to the rays of the sun
(d) Exposure to the light
The correct option is (c).

•

•
•

Insolation = incoming solar radiation; it
denotes the amount of solar energy reaching
a surface.
It is a key meteorological/climatological term
that quantifies exposure to the Sun's rays.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. Which of the following is true?
(a) Sound waves exhibit interference.
(b) Light waves exhibit interference.
(c) Both the light and sound waves exhibit
interference.
(d) Neither sound waves nor light waves exhibit
interference.
The correct option is (c).
• Interference is a fundamental property of
waves—it occurs when two or more waves
superpose.
• Both sound and light are wave phenomena
and therefore exhibit interference, giving rise
to constructive and destructive patterns.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. Heat from the sun is received by the earth
through
(a) Conduction
(b) Convection
(c) Radiation
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (c).
• The space between the Sun and Earth is
essentially a vacuum, so conduction and
convection cannot transfer heat across it.
• Energy travels as electromagnetic radiation
(visible light, infrared, UV), which does not
require a medium.
• Thus the Earth receives the Sun’s heat by
radiation.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. 'Black holes' refers to
(a) Holes occurring in heavenly bodies
(b) Bright spots on the sun
(c) Collapsing object of high density
(d) Collapsing object of low density

The correct option is (c).
• A black hole is the result of a massive star's
gravitational collapse, producing an object of
extremely high density.
• Its gravity is so strong that nothing, not even
light, can escape from within its event
horizon.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9. Atmospheric pressure exerted on earth is due to
the
(a) Rotation of earth
(b) Revolution of earth
(c) Gravitational pull
(d) Uneven heating of earth
The correct option is (c).
• Atmospheric pressure is the force per unit
area due to the weight of the air column
above a surface.
• The Earth’s gravity holds the atmosphere and
causes the air mass above to exert pressure
on the surface.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q10. A small weight put on the pressure cooker
increases the pressure of steam because
(a) It is air tight
(b) Area of contact is very small
(c) Density of metal is very high
(d) The weight acts as a valve, trapping steam until a
specific pressure is reached.
The correct option is (d).
• The small weight sits on the cooker’s vent and
functions as a pressure-regulating valve.
• As steam builds, the weight traps it, allowing
pressure to increase until the steam force lifts
the weight and a pulse of steam escapes.
• This mechanism maintains a constant
elevated pressure and temperature for
efficient cooking.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q11. Birds get thrust (forward motion) and lift
(upward motion) from the
(a) Flapping of wings

(b) Twisting of feathers
(c) Shape of wings which is similar to aeroplane blades
(d) Air sacs
The correct option is (a).
• Lift is largely generated by the airfoil shape of
the wings (pressure difference top vs bottom),
while thrust is produced mainly by the
flapping motion.
• The down-and-back stroke of flapping pushes
air backward, producing forward thrust
(Newton’s Third Law), and also contributes to
additional lift.
• While wing shape matters for lift, the flapping
provides both primary thrust and dynamic lift
needed in active flight.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1990
Q1. The conversion of electrical energy into chemical
energy is observed in
(a) Fan
(b) Heater
(c) Incandescent bulb
(d) Storage battery
The correct option is (d).
• A fan converts electrical energy into
mechanical energy.
• A heater converts electrical energy into
thermal energy.
• An incandescent bulb converts electrical
energy into heat and light.
• A storage battery, on the other hand,
converts electrical energy into chemical
energy through oxidation at the anode and
reduction at the cathode.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. The evidence for the rotation of stars comes
mainly from the
(a) Doppler effect
(b) Photoelectric effect
(c) Stark effect
(d) Zeeman effect
The correct option is (a).

•

•

•

•

The Doppler Effect refers to the change in
frequency or wavelength of waves (sound or
light) due to the relative motion between the
source and observer.
In astronomy, the red shift (movement away)
and blue shift (movement toward) of light
from stars provide evidence of their rotation
and motion.
The expanding universe theory and details
about galactic rotation are derived from
Doppler shift observations.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. Assertion (A): A tennis ball will bounce higher in
Calcutta than it would in Shimla.
Reason (R): The downward pull due to gravity is less
and air is more rarefied in Shimla than in Calcutta.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is not a correct
explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
The correct option is (d).
• The force of gravity is slightly less at higher
altitudes because they are farther from the
Earth’s center.
• Shimla, being at a higher altitude than
Calcutta, has weaker gravity.
• Therefore, a tennis ball would bounce higher
in Shimla, not in Calcutta.
• The assertion is false, but the reason (less
gravity and rarer air at higher altitude) is
true.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. Assertion (A): When a ship moves from the sea
water into fresh water of a river, the ship will
relatively sink.
Reason (R): The density of sea water is higher than
that of the fresh water.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is not a correct
explanation of A.

(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
The correct option is (a).
• Seawater has a higher density due to the
presence of dissolved salts.
• Fresh water is less dense, so to displace the
same weight of water, the ship must sink
deeper when it moves into fresh water.
• Therefore, both the assertion and reason are
true, and the reason correctly explains the
assertion.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. Assertion (A): When a wheel is in motion, a point
(on the rim) which is farthest away from the ground
will have the highest speed while the point (on the
rim) closest to the ground will have the lowest
speed.
Reason (R): The speed of the point on the rim in the
direction of motion and along the circumference of
the wheel are additive.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is not a correct
explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
The correct option is (a).
• The topmost point on a rotating wheel moves
forward with both the translational speed of
the wheel and the rotational speed of that
point — hence, speeds are additive.
• The bottom point is momentarily at rest
relative to the ground because its rotational
motion cancels the translational motion.
• Thus, the top point moves fastest, and the
bottom point slowest, exactly as explained by
the reason.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. Given below are colours of the visible spectrum.
Identify the correct sequence in order of increasing
wavelengths:
(a) Red, Yellow, Blue, Violet
(b) Violet, Yellow, Blue, Red

(c) Blue, Yellow, Red, Violet
(d) Violet, Blue, Yellow, Red
The correct option is (d).
• In the visible spectrum, colours are arranged
in order of increasing wavelength as follows:
Violet → Blue → Green → Yellow → Orange
→ Red.
• Violet has the shortest wavelength (≈390–
455 nm) and Red the longest (≈622–780 nm).
• Therefore, in the given options, the correct
increasing wavelength order is Violet, Blue,
Yellow, Red.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1991
Q1. Photons have properties similar to that of
(a) Waves
(b) Particles
(c) Both particles and waves
(d) Neither particles nor waves
The correct option is (c).
• In modern physics, the concept of waveparticle duality is fundamental. It states that
all quantum entities, including photons,
exhibit the properties of both particles and
waves.
• Particle Properties: Photons can be detected
as discrete packets of energy, they have
momentum, and they can collide with other
particles (like in the photoelectric effect).
• Wave Properties: Photons also exhibit
behaviors characteristic of waves, such as
diffraction (bending around obstacles) and
interference (creating patterns of
reinforcement and cancellation when they
overlap).
• Therefore, a photon is not strictly a particle or
a wave, but has properties of both particles
and waves.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. Assume that –
A – denotes narrow wheel with wider wheel.
B – denotes small free wheel with large wheel.

C – denotes gear wheel with large wider wheel.
In a rural area where there are sandy tracts, it will be
advisable to use a bicycle with
(a) A
(b) B
(c) C
(d) All of these
The correct option is (a).
• This question is about choosing the best
bicycle setup for riding on sandy terrain.
• Sandy tracts are soft surfaces. To avoid
sinking into the sand, the pressure exerted
on the ground needs to be minimized.
• Pressure = Force / Area.
• A wider wheel (or tire) has a larger contact
area with the ground. For the same force (the
weight of the rider and bicycle), a larger area
results in lower pressure.
• This prevents the wheel from digging into the
sand.
• Therefore, using a bicycle with wider wheels
is most advisable for sandy tracts. Option (a)
describes this choice.
• Gears (C) help with pedaling effort but don't
address the sinking issue.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. A passing motorcycle causes distortion or
disturbance with the reception of a signal in radio
and T.V. The cause of this is
(a) The intense sound waves affect the small
electronic devices of the receptor
(b) The vehicle's metal parts block/distort the radio
waves
(c) The spark plug fitted in the engine produces
electro-magnetic signals due to sparking
(d) Modern motorcycles are fitted with an electric
ignition system which produces radio waves
The correct option is (c).
• The interference experienced by radio and TV
signals from a passing motorcycle (with a
gasoline engine) is a form of electromagnetic
interference (EMI).

•

•

•

•

The spark plug in a gasoline engine creates a
high-voltage electrical spark to ignite the
fuel-air mixture.
This rapid, high-energy electrical discharge
generates a burst of broad-spectrum
electromagnetic radiation (radio waves).
These unwanted radio waves are picked up by
the radio or TV antenna and are interpreted
as static or noise, causing distortion in the
reception.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. The path of Halley's comet in its orbit around the
sun is
(a) Parabolic
(b) Hyperbolic
(c) Elliptical
(d) Circular
The correct option is (c).
• Comets that are gravitationally bound to the
Sun and return periodically travel in elliptical
orbits.
• Halley's Comet is a famous periodic comet
with a highly elliptical orbit.
• Its orbit takes it very close to the Sun
(perihelion) and then far out into the outer
solar system (aphelion).
• Circular orbits are a special case of an ellipse.
• Parabolic and hyperbolic paths are associated
with objects that make a single pass by the
Sun and then escape its gravity, never to
return.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. An instrument measures the voltage of a 50 Hz
sinusoidal current as 220 V. The mean average
voltage of the sinusoidal current is
(a) Zero
(b) 4.4 V
(c) 220 V
(d) 220 / √2 V
The correct option is (a).
• A sinusoidal current (or voltage) is an
alternating current (AC) that varies
sinusoidally over time.

•
•

•

•
•

•

It has a positive half-cycle and a negative
half-cycle that are perfectly symmetrical.
The mean average voltage over one complete
cycle is calculated by integrating the voltage
over the cycle and dividing by the period.
Because the positive and negative half-cycles
are identical in shape but opposite in sign,
they cancel each other out perfectly over a
full cycle.
Therefore, the mean average voltage of any
pure sinusoidal AC waveform is always zero.
The 220V measurement is the RMS (Root
Mean Square) voltage, not the average
voltage.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q6. Superconductors are so called because they
(a) Efficiently conduct heat from one hot body to
another
(b) Offer no resistance to the conduction of heat at
high temperatures
(c) Offer no resistance to the conduction of electricity
at low temperatures
(d) Produce high temperatures while conducting
electricity
The correct option is (c).
• Superconductivity is a phenomenon observed
in certain materials where their electrical
resistance drops to exactly zero when they
are cooled below a specific critical
temperature.
• This means they can conduct electricity with
perfect efficiency, without any energy loss as
heat.
• This property of offering no resistance to the
conduction of electricity is what defines a
superconductor.
• This phenomenon occurs only at very low
temperatures (though research into hightemperature superconductors is ongoing).
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. In solar heaters, in which adequate sunlight can
be used to provide safe drinking water in far-off
areas, the water to be purified is contained in a black

tray. The lid of the tray is made of glass. Water gets
converted into steam/vapours, which condense on
the glass, slides to the sides/edges of the glass plates
and drops into another container and is collected.
The most appropriate shape of the glass lid could be

(a)
(b)
(c)
(d)
The correct option is (c).
• The device described is a solar still, which
uses solar energy to purify water through
distillation.
• Sunlight heats the water in the black tray,
causing it to evaporate.
• The water vapor rises and comes into contact
with the cooler inner surface of the glass lid.
• The vapor condenses back into liquid water
droplets on the glass.
• For the collected pure water to be channeled
into a separate container, the glass lid must
be sloped.
• The shape in (c), a tilted flat plane or a simple
A-frame, is the most effective design.
• The condensed droplets will run down the
sloped surface due to gravity and can be
collected in a channel at the lower edge.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q8. Weights of two pieces, one of gold and one of
silver each weighing 100 gm were recorded on a
spring balance. Then they were fully immersed in a
liquid bath, by letting them hang through a light
thread tied to the balance. Compare the recorded
weights of the two pieces under that condition. Then
(a) The gold piece will weigh more than the silver
piece
(b) The silver piece will weigh more than the gold
piece
(c) The weights of the two pieces will be equal
(d) Nothing can be said without knowing the density
of the liquid
The correct option is (a).
• When an object is immersed in a liquid, it
experiences an upward buoyant force equal
to the weight of the liquid displaced
(Archimedes' principle).
• The apparent weight recorded by the spring
balance will be: Apparent Weight = True
Weight - Buoyant Force.
• Both pieces have the same true weight (100
gm).
• The density of gold (≈ 19.3 g/cm³) is much
higher than the density of silver (≈ 10.5
g/cm³).
• For the same mass (100 gm), the silver piece
will have a larger volume than the gold piece.
• Because the silver piece has a larger volume,
it will displace more liquid and therefore
experience a greater buoyant force.
• Since the silver piece has a larger buoyant
force acting on it, its apparent weight will be
reduced more than the gold piece's apparent
weight.
• Therefore, when immersed, the gold piece
will weigh more than the silver piece.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9. A cubic metre of water at 0° C is solidified into
ice. Density of ice is 0.96 of water at 0° C. Which of
the following deductions are true?
1. Water expands when solidified.
2. Ice will float in water at 0° C with half its
volume above the surface.

3. Density of all liquids are higher than its
density when solidified.
Select the correct answer from the codes given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1 and 2
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is true. The density of ice (0.96
g/cm³) is less than the density of water (≈ 1.0
g/cm³).
• Since Density = Mass/Volume, for a constant
mass, a lower density implies a larger
volume.
• Therefore, water expands when it solidifies
into ice.
• Statement 2 is false. The fraction submerged
is equal to density ratio (ρ₁/ρ₂ = 0.96/1.0 =
0.96), meaning 96% submerged, 4% above,
not half.
• Statement 3 is false. This is a generalization
that is not true. Water is an exception; most
substances are denser when solid.
• Therefore, only Statement 1 is correct.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q10. The rainbow and the rainbow-like colours seen
on a thin film of oil on a water surface are two
different phenomena. They are
(a) Similar in nature and both are due to refraction of
light
(b) Different in nature and rainbow is due to
interference of light, while colour of thin film is due to
diffraction
(c) Similar in nature and both are due to interference
of light
(d) Different in nature and rainbow is due to
refraction, while colour of thin film is due to
interference of light
The correct option is (d).
• The two phenomena are indeed different and
caused by different optical principles.
• A rainbow is formed by the dispersion and
total internal reflection of sunlight by water
droplets in the atmosphere.

•
•
•

•

As light enters a droplet, it refracts, reflects,
and disperses into its constituent colors.
The iridescent colours seen on an oil film are
caused by thin-film interference.
Light waves reflecting off the top and bottom
surfaces of the film interfere constructively or
destructively depending on film thickness and
angle of viewing.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1992

Q1. A wooden block is floating in water. If 4/5 of its
volume is immersed in water and the volume of the
water displaced is 800 ml, the volume of the block is
(a) 1140 ml
(b) 200 ml
(c) 800 ml
(d) 1000 ml
The correct option is (d).
• According to Archimedes' principle, the
volume of a floating object that is submerged
is equal to the volume of the fluid it
displaces.
• The problem states that the volume of water
displaced is 800 ml.
• This means the volume of the block
immersed in water is also 800 ml.
• Given that this immersed volume (800 ml) is
4/5 of the total volume, let total volume = V.
• Then, (4/5) × V = 800 ml → V = 800 × (5/4) =
1000 ml.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. What is the increasing order of the wavelengths
of the following colours?
1. Orange
2. Indigo
3. Yellow
4. Violet
Select the correct answer using the codes
given below:
(a) 1, 2, 3 and 4
(b) 3, 1, 4 and 2

(c) 1, 3, 2 and 4
(d) 4, 2, 3 and 1
The correct option is (d).
• The colors of the visible spectrum follow the
order VIBGYOR (Violet → Indigo → Blue →
Green → Yellow → Orange → Red).
• This is also the order of increasing
wavelength.
• Hence, starting with the shortest to longest
wavelength:
o Violet (4)
o Indigo (2)
o Yellow (3)
o Orange (1)
• Therefore, the correct sequence is 4, 2, 3, 1.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q4. Which one of the following figures represents
the correct path of a ray of light through a glass
prism?

•
•

•
•
•

•
•
•

The correct option is (c).
• When light passes through a prism, it
undergoes refraction twice.
• First refraction: As light enters the denser
medium (glass) from air, it bends towards the
normal.
• Second refraction: As light exits from glass to
air, it bends away from the normal.
• The net deviation is towards the base of the
prism.
• Figure (c) correctly shows the ray bending
towards the normal on entry and away from
the normal on exit.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. Which one of the following will take place when
a watch based on an oscillating spring is taken to a
deep mine?
(a) It will become slow
(b) It will become fast
(c) It will indicate the same time as on earth's surface
(d) It will stop working
The correct option is (c).

A spring-based watch operates using an
oscillating balance spring system.
The time period depends on the spring
constant (k) and moment of inertia (I) of the
balance wheel.
𝑇 = 2𝜋√𝐼/𝑘
This motion does not depend on gravity (g).
Even though gravity is slightly less in a deep
mine, it has no effect on the working of a
spring-based watch.
A pendulum clock, however, would become
faster or slower, since it depends on gravity.
Therefore, the spring-based watch will
indicate the same time.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. A body is attached to a spring balance suspended
from a stand. The reading on the balance is 0.5 kg.
The two together are detached from the stand and
allowed to fall through a height. While falling, the
reading in the balance will be
(a) Zero
(b) Less than 0.5 kg but not zero
(c) More than 0.5 kg depending on the height
(d) 0.5 kg
The correct option is (a).
• A spring balance measures weight through
the tension in the spring.
• The reading of 0.5 kg corresponds to the
weight of the body when stationary.
• When the system is in free fall, both the body
and the balance accelerate downward at the
same rate (g).
• The body exerts no relative force on the
spring, creating a condition of weightlessness.
• Therefore, the spring is unstretched, and the
balance reads zero.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1993
Q1. When there is depletion of ozone in the
stratosphere, the wavelength of radiation striking
the earth's surface will be
(a) 10⁻⁷ m
(b) 10⁻¹⁰ m
(c) 10⁻² m
(d) 10² m
The correct option is (a).
• The ozone layer primarily absorbs ultraviolet
(UV) radiation from the Sun.
• UV radiation occupies the portion of the
electromagnetic spectrum with wavelengths
between 10 nm and 400 nm (10⁻⁸ m to 4 ×
10⁻⁷ m).
• When the ozone layer is depleted, more
harmful UVB and UVC radiation reach the
Earth's surface.
• Among the given options, 10⁻⁷ m (100 nm) lies
in the UV range.
• The other options correspond to X-rays (10⁻¹⁰
m), microwaves/infrared (10⁻² m), and radio
waves (10² m) — none of which are relevant
to ozone depletion.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. The working of a quartz crystal in the watch is
based on the
(a) Photoelectric Effect
(b) Johnson Effect
(c) Piezo-electric Effect
(d) Edison Effect
The correct option is (c).
• The timekeeping mechanism in a quartz
watch relies on the piezoelectric effect.
• The piezoelectric effect is the ability of certain
crystals (like quartz) to generate a small
electric voltage when subjected to
mechanical stress.
• Conversely, when electric voltage is applied,
they vibrate mechanically.
• In a quartz watch, a small battery sends
current to a quartz crystal, causing it to
vibrate precisely 32,768 times per second.

•

•

An electronic circuit counts these vibrations
and converts them into one-second pulses to
drive the watch hands or display.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q3. Consider the figures of a metal ball and a metal
ring given below:

The metal ball can just pass through the hole of a
metal ring formed out of a strip. When the ball is
heated it gets stuck. But when the metal ring is
heated,
(a) the ball can still pass through it because the ring
diameter expands on heating
(b) the ball gets stuck because the diameter of the
hole decreases on expansion
(c) the ball will still pass through because the hole
diameter does not change
(d) the ball will pass through because there is no
change in the ring
The correct option is (a).
• This demonstrates the principle of thermal
expansion — most materials expand when
heated.
• When the metal ring is heated, it expands in
all directions — including the circumference
of the hole.
• A common misconception is that the hole
shrinks, but in reality, the entire ring
expands, and the hole gets larger.
• Think of it as a photographic enlargement —
every part, including the hole, increases in
size.
• Thus, when the ring is heated, the hole
enlarges, allowing the ball to pass through
easily.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q4. Which one of the following is NOT an
astronomical object?
(a) Pulsar
(b) Brittle Star
(c) Black hole
(d) Quasar
The correct option is (b).
• Pulsar: A highly magnetized, rotating neutron
star emitting electromagnetic radiation — an
astronomical object.
• Brittle Star: A marine animal, belonging to
the echinoderm family (related to starfish),
found on the sea floor — not an astronomical
object.
• Black Hole: A region of spacetime with
gravitational pull so strong that nothing, not
even light, can escape — astronomical object.
• Quasar: An extremely luminous and distant
active galactic nucleus, powered by a
supermassive black hole — astronomical
object.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. The formation of brilliant colours in a soap film is
a consequence of the phenomenon of
(a) multiple reflection and interference
(b) multiple refraction and dispersion
(c) diffraction and dispersion
(d) polarisation and interference
The correct option is (a).
• The brilliant colours seen in a soap film or oil
film result from thin-film interference.
• Light waves reflect from both the top and
bottom surfaces of the film.
• These two reflected waves interfere with
each other —
o Constructive interference enhances
certain colours.
o Destructive interference cancels
others.
• This process involves multiple reflections
inside the thin film and the resulting
interference pattern.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q6. At what temperature are the readings of a
Centigrade and Fahrenheit thermometer the same?
(a) -40°
(b) 212°
(c) 40°
(d) 100°
The correct option is (a).
• The conversion formula between Celsius (C)
and Fahrenheit (F) is:
F = (9/5)C + 32.
• For equal readings, set F = C = T.
𝑇 = (9/5)𝑇 + 32
𝑇 − (9/5)𝑇 = 32
(−4/5)𝑇 = 32
𝑇 = −40.
• Therefore, at -40°, both thermometers show
the same reading.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1994
Q1. The pitch of the voice of women is in general
(a) marginally lower than that of men
(b) higher than that of men
(c) much lower than that of men
(d) the same as that of men
The correct option is (b).
• Pitch in sound is determined by the frequency
of the sound waves. A higher frequency
corresponds to a higher pitch.
• In humans, the frequency of the voice is
determined by the vibration of the vocal
cords.

•

•
•

Women generally have shorter and thinner
vocal cords than men. This anatomical
difference causes their vocal cords to vibrate
at a higher frequency.
As a result, the pitch of a woman's voice is
typically higher than that of a man.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. Consider the figure of a fountain with four holes
given below:

Which one of the holes in the fountain will throw the
water farthest?
(a) 4
(b) 3
(c) 2
(d) 1
The correct option is (c).
• This problem relates to Torricelli's law, which
describes the speed of fluid flowing out of an
orifice. The speed of the water exiting a hole
is given by v = √(2gh), where g is the
acceleration due to gravity and h is the depth
of the hole below the water's surface.
• The horizontal distance (range) the water
travels is given by Range = v × t, where t is the
time it takes to fall to the ground. The time of
fall depends on the height of the hole above
the ground.
• The maximum range is achieved from a hole
located at the midpoint of the total water
height.
• In the given diagram, hole 2 is located closest
to the vertical midpoint of the water column.
Water from this hole will have an optimal
combination of horizontal exit speed and
time in the air, allowing it to travel the
farthest horizontal distance.

•

Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q3. In an electronic watch, the component
corresponding to the pendulum of a pendulum clock
is a
(a) transistor
(b) crystal oscillator
(c) diode
(d) balance wheel
The correct option is (b).
• The function of a pendulum in a pendulum
clock is to act as a timekeeping element by
oscillating at a very regular and predictable
frequency.
• In a modern electronic watch (specifically a
quartz watch), this timekeeping function is
performed by a crystal oscillator.
• A tiny, tuning-fork-shaped quartz crystal is
made to vibrate at an extremely precise
frequency (usually 32,768 Hz) by an electric
current from the battery. An integrated circuit
counts these vibrations to keep accurate time.
• A balance wheel (d) is the timekeeping
element in a mechanical watch, not an
electronic one.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. A man jumped at a speed of 5 metres per second
from a stationary boat and the boat moved off with
the speed of 0.5 metre per second. How many times
is the mass of the boat greater than that of the man?
(a) 5.5 times
(b) 4.5 times
(c) 2.5 times
(d) 10 times
The correct option is (d).
• This problem is an application of the law of
conservation of linear momentum.
• The total momentum of the system (man +
boat) before the jump is zero, as they are
stationary. Therefore, the total momentum
after the jump must also be zero.
• Let m₁ be the mass of the man and v₁ be his
velocity (5 m/s).

•

•
•
•
•
•

Let m₂ be the mass of the boat and v₂ be its
velocity (-0.5 m/s, the negative sign indicates
it moves in the opposite direction).
According to conservation of momentum:
m₁v₁ + m₂v₂ = 0
Substituting the values: (m₁ * 5) + (m₂ * -0.5) =
0 → 5m₁ = 0.5m₂
Therefore m₂ / m₁ = 5 / 0.5 = 10.
The mass of the boat is 10 times greater than
the mass of the man.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q5. One mole of hydrogen gas burns in excess of
oxygen to give 290 kJ of heat. What is the amount of
heat produced when 4g of hydrogen gas is burnt
under the same conditions?
(a) 145 kJ
(b) 290 kJ
(c) 580 kJ
(d) 1160 kJ
The correct option is (c).
• First, relate the mass of hydrogen to moles.
The molar mass of hydrogen gas (H₂) is
approximately 2 g/mol.
• Burning one mole of H₂ produces 290 kJ of
heat. Since one mole of H₂ is 2 g, burning 2 g
of H₂ produces 290 kJ.
• 4 g of hydrogen gas is 2 moles (4 g / 2 g/mol =
2 moles).
• Total heat produced = 2 moles × 290 kJ/mole
= 580 kJ.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. If a gas is compressed to half of its original
volume at 27°C, to what temperature should it be
heated to make it occupy its original volume?
(a) 327°C
(b) 600°C
(c) 54°C
(d) 300°C
The correct option is (a).
• This problem uses Charles's Law: V₁/T₁ =
V₂/T₂, with temperatures in Kelvin.
• Initial temperature T₁ = 27°C = 300 K. If the
gas is compressed to V₁ = V/2 (at 300 K) and

•
•

we want V₂ = V, then:
(𝑉/2)/300 = 𝑉/𝑇2 → 𝑇2 = 300 × 2 = 600𝐾.
Convert back to Celsius: T₂ (°C) = 600 K − 273
= 327°C.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q7. Which one of the following combinations of
aperture and shutter speed of a camera will allow
the maximum exposure?
(a) f/22, 1/60 s
(b) f/16, 1/125 s
(c) f/8, 1/250 s
(d) f/5.6, 1/1000 s
The correct option is (a).
• Exposure = amount of light reaching the
sensor, determined by aperture and shutter
speed.
• Aperture: larger f-number = smaller opening
(less light).
• Shutter speed: longer duration = more light.
• The given combinations are reciprocal
equivalents (they balance aperture and
shutter speed), but the longest duration
among options is 1/60 s (a).
• Therefore, option (a) has the longest
exposure time and thus allows maximum
exposure under the interpretation of longest
duration.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8. A corked bottle full of water when frozen will
break because
(a) the bottle contracts on freezing
(b) the volume of water decreases on freezing
(c) the volume of water increases on freezing
(d) glass is a bad conductor of heat
The correct option is (c).
• This involves the anomalous expansion of
water.
• Unlike most substances, water expands when
it freezes because ice has a crystalline lattice
that occupies more volume.
• The increase in volume exerts pressure on the
sealed bottle, which can break the glass.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q9. The half-life of a radioactive element is 5 years.
The fraction of the radioactive substance that
remains after 20 years is
(a) 1/2
(b) 1/4
(c) 1/8
(d) 1/16
The correct option is (d).
• Half-life is the time for half the radioactive
atoms to decay.
• Number of half-lives in 20 years = 20 / 5 = 4.
• Remaining fraction = (1/2)4 = 1/16.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q10. The speed of light will be minimum while
passing through
(a) glass
(b) vacuum
(c) water
(d) air
The correct option is (a).
• Speed of light in a medium is 𝑐/𝑛, where n is
the refractive index. Higher n → lower speed.
• Approximate refractive indices: vacuum = 1.0,
air ≈ 1.0003, water ≈ 1.33, glass ≈ 1.5.
• Glass has the highest refractive index among
the options, so light travels slowest in glass.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q11. The acceleration due to gravity of a
catastrophic earthquake will be
(a) > 550 cm/sec²
(b) > 750 cm/sec²
(c) > 950 cm/sec²
(d) > 980 cm/sec²
The correct option is (d).
• Standard gravity g ≈ 980 cm/s².
• In a very powerful earthquake, ground
acceleration can momentarily exceed g,
throwing objects into the air.
• Therefore, acceleration > 980 cm/s² is
possible in catastrophic events.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q12. The colour of an opaque object is due to the
colour it
(a) absorbs
(b) refracts
(c) reflects
(d) scatters
The correct option is (c).
• White light contains all visible wavelengths.
• An opaque object absorbs some wavelengths
and reflects others.
• The perceived colour is the wavelength(s)
reflected into our eyes.
• Thus a red apple appears red because it
reflects red and absorbs others.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q13. The colour of a star is an indication of its
(a) distance from the sun
(b) luminosity
(c) distance from the earth
(d) temperature
The correct option is (d).
• A star’s colour corresponds to its surface
temperature (black-body radiation).
• Hotter stars emit more at shorter
wavelengths → appear blue/white.
• Cooler stars emit more at longer wavelengths
→ appear red/orange.

•
•

Hence, the colour is an indicator of
temperature.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1995
