Q1. A meteor is
(a) a rapidly moving star
(b) a piece of matter which has entered the earth's
atmosphere from outer space
(c) part of a constellation
(d) a comet without a tail
The correct option is ( b ).
• A meteoroid is a small rocky or metallic body
traveling through outer space.
• When a meteoroid enters the Earth's
atmosphere at high speed, friction with the
air causes it to heat up and incandesce,
creating a streak of light in the sky. This visible
phenomenon is called a meteor, or a
"shooting star".
• Therefore, a meteor is the visible path of a
piece of matter which has entered the earth's
atmosphere from outer space. If any part of it
survives the journey and hits the ground, it is
called a meteorite
• Hence, option ( b ) is correct.
Q2. A parachutist jumps from a height of 5000
metres. The relationship between his falling speed 'v'
and the distance fallen through 'd' is best
represented as

The correct option is ( a ).
• This question describes the motion of a falling
object subject to air resistance.
• Initially (small d): As the parachutist starts
falling, their speed (v) increases due to
gravity. At low speeds, air resistance is small.
The speed increases rapidly at first.

•

•

As speed increases: Air resistance increases
(proportional to the square of the speed). This
opposing force counteracts gravity, causing
the rate of acceleration to decrease. The
speed continues to increase, but more slowly.
Terminal Velocity: Eventually, the upward
force of air resistance becomes equal in
magnitude to the downward force of gravity.
At this point, the net force is zero, and the
parachutist stops accelerating. They continue
to fall at a constant maximum speed called
terminal velocity.
Graph (a) correctly represents this
relationship: the speed (v) increases with
distance (d) at first, but the rate of increase
slows down until the speed becomes constant
(the flat part of the curve), representing
terminal velocity.
Hence, option ( a ) is correct.

Q3. The technique used to transmit audio signals in
television broadcasts is
(a) Amplitude Modulation
(b) Frequency Modulation
(c) Pulse Code Modulation
(d) Time Division Multiplexing
The correct option is ( b ).
• Analog television broadcasting uses different
modulation techniques for its video and audio
components to avoid interference.
• The video signal (picture) is typically
transmitted using Amplitude Modulation
(AM).
• The audio signal (sound) is transmitted using
Frequency Modulation (FM).
• Using FM for the audio provides better sound
quality and is less susceptible to noise and
interference compared to AM. Pulse Code
Modulation (PCM) is a digital technique, and
Time Division Multiplexing (TDM) is a
method for combining multiple signals, not a
modulation technique itself. (Note: The
provided answer key says (d), which is
incorrect).
• Hence, option ( b ) is correct.

Q4. Which one of the following can be used to
confirm whether drinking water contains a gammaemitting isotope or not?
(a) Microscope
(b) Lead plate
(c) Scintillation counter
(d) Spectrophotometer
The correct option is ( c ).
• Gamma rays are a form of high-energy
electromagnetic radiation emitted by
radioactive isotopes. They cannot be seen
with a microscope.
• A scintillation counter is a specific type of
radiation detector. It works by using a
material (a scintillator) that emits a flash of
light (a scintillation) when it is struck by
ionizing radiation, such as a gamma ray.
• A photosensor then detects this flash of light
and converts it into an electrical pulse, which
can be counted. This allows for the detection
and measurement of gamma radiation.
• A lead plate is used for shielding against
radiation, not detecting it. A
spectrophotometer measures the intensity of
light, but not ionizing radiation.
• Hence, option ( c ) is correct.
Q5. When the same note is played on a sitar and a
flute, the sound produced can be distinguished from
each other because of the difference in
(a) pitch, loudness and quality
(b) pitch and loudness
(c) quality only
(d) loudness only
The correct option is ( c ).
• Sound has three main perceptual attributes:
• Pitch: Determined by the fundamental
frequency of the sound wave. Playing the
"same note" means the fundamental
frequency, and therefore the pitch, is the
same for both instruments.
• Loudness: Determined by the amplitude
(intensity) of the sound wave. The
instruments could be played at the same
loudness.

•

•

•

Quality (or Timbre): This is what allows us to
distinguish between two different
instruments playing the same note at the
same loudness. It is determined by the
presence and relative intensity of overtones
or harmonics (multiples of the fundamental
frequency) produced by the instrument.
Since the note (pitch) is the same, and we can
assume they could be played at the same
loudness, the distinguishing characteristic is
the quality or timbre.
Hence, option ( c ) is correct.

Q6. Assertion (A): A diamond sparkles more than a
glass imitation cut to the same shape.
Reason (R): The refractive index of diamond is less
than that of glass.
In the context of the above two statements, which
one of the following is correct?
(a) Both A and R are true, and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true, but R is not a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true, but R is false
(d) A is false, but R is true
The correct option is ( c ).
• Assertion (A) is true. A well-cut diamond
famously sparkles with brilliance and "fire"
(dispersion of light into colors) far more than
a piece of glass cut to the same shape.
• Reason (R) is false. The sparkle of a diamond
is due to its very high refractive index (about
2.42) and high dispersion. The high refractive
index leads to a small critical angle, causing a
large amount of total internal reflection,
which traps light inside the gem and makes it
"sparkle". Glass has a much lower refractive
index (about 1.5). The statement that the
refractive index of diamond is less than glass
is incorrect.
• Since the assertion is true but the reason is
false, the correct option is (c).
• Hence, option ( c ) is correct.

Q7. Who among the following anticipated Newton
by declaring that all things gravitate to the earth?
(a) Aryabhata
(b) Varahamihira
(c) Buddhagupta
(d) Brahmagupta
The correct option is ( d ).
• Long before Isaac Newton formulated his
universal law of gravitation, several ancient
and medieval scholars had ideas about
gravity.
• The 7th-century Indian mathematician and
astronomer Brahmagupta wrote in his text,
the Brahmasphutasiddhanta, that "all things
fall to the earth by a law of nature, for it is the
nature of the earth to attract and keep
things."
• This statement is a remarkable early
description of gravity as an attractive
property of the Earth, anticipating Newton's
concept by over a thousand years, although it
lacked the mathematical formulation and
universality of Newton's law.
• Hence, option ( d ) is correct.
Q8. Optical fibre works on the principle of
(a) total internal reflection
(b) refraction
(c) scattering
(d) interference
The correct option is ( a ).
• An optical fiber is a thin, flexible strand of
glass or plastic that acts as a waveguide to
transmit light between its two ends.
• It works on the principle of total internal
reflection (TIR).
• The fiber consists of a central core with a high
refractive index, surrounded by a cladding
with a slightly lower refractive index.
• Light is sent into the core at a shallow angle.
When the light hits the boundary between the
core and the cladding, it strikes at an angle
greater than the critical angle, causing it to be
completely reflected back into the core.

•

•

This process repeats itself, with the light
bouncing off the internal walls of the fiber,
allowing it to travel long distances with very
little loss of signal.
Hence, option ( a ) is correct.

Q9. Suppose a rocketship is receding from the Earth
at speed = 2/10 c. A light in the rocket appears blue
to passengers. What colour does it appear to an
observer on Earth?
(a) Blue
(b) Orange
(c) Yellow
(d) Yellow-orange
Correct Option: (d)
• Source is receding, so observed wavelength
on Earth is red-shifted (wavelength
increases).
• Using the given (approx) relation 𝜆obs =
𝜆source /(1 − 𝑣/𝑐)with 𝑣/𝑐 = 0.2: factor =
1/(1 − 0.2) = 1.25.
• Blue light range ≈ 450–475 nm. Multiply by
1.25 → 562.5–593.75 nm.
• This range falls in the yellow–orange /
yellow-orange region of visible spectrum.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q10. The variations in temperatures from 0°C to
100°C with respect to time of two liquids P, Q are

shown in the graph given below. Which one of the
following statements is correct?

(a) During heating, liquid P remained hotter than
liquid Q throughout
(b)At no point of time during heating did the two
liquids have the same temperature
(c) P attained the temperature of 100°C faster than Q
(d)Q attained the temperature of 100°C faster than P
Correct Option: (d)
• Graph shows P is hotter than Q initially until
an intersection point.
• At the intersection both liquids have the same
temperature (so (b) is wrong).
• After intersection, Q becomes hotter and
reaches 100°C earlier than P (slope/time to
100°C smaller for Q).
• Therefore Q attains 100°C faster.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q11.Difference between a nuclear reactor and an
atomic bomb
(a) No chain reaction in reactor but in bomb
(b) Chain reaction in reactor is controlled
(c) Chain reaction in reactor is not controlled
(d) No chain reaction in bomb while reactor has it
Correct Option: (b)
• Both devices rely on nuclear fission chain
reactions.
• In a nuclear reactor the chain reaction is
controlled using control rods, moderators and
design for steady power.

•

In an atomic bomb the chain reaction is
uncontrolled, extremely rapid, producing
massive energy.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q13. An air bubble in water will act like a —
(a) Convex mirror
(b) Convex lens
(c) Concave mirror
(d) Concave lens
Correct Option: (d)
• The air bubble is surrounded by water, a
denser medium.
• The refractive index of air (≈1) is less than
that of water (≈1.33).
• When light passes from a denser to a rarer
medium (water → air), rays bend away from
the normal.
• As a result, the air bubble diverges light rays
passing through it.
• A lens that diverges light rays is known as a
concave lens.
• That’s why the air bubble acts like a concave
lens, making it appear bright due to
scattering.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1996
Q1. Distance-time graph in respect of a race among
four persons is shown in the given figure. Consider
the following statements in this regard:

1. 'A' stood first in the race.
2. 'C' led all the way.
3. 'D' ran faster than others in the later part of
the race.
Of these statements:
(a) 1 and 3 are false and 2 is true
(b) 1 and 2 are false and 3 is true

(c) 1 and 3 are true and 2 is false
(d) 1 is true and 2 and 3 are false
The correct option is (b).
• In a distance-time graph, the person who
covers the total distance in the shortest time
wins the race.
• Statement 1 is false. The graph shows that
person 'A' takes the longest time to finish the
race, while 'D' finishes in the shortest time.
• Statement 2 is false. To lead all the way
means to be ahead at all times. The graph
shows that 'C' was behind 'A' and 'B' initially
and only led later.
• Statement 3 is true. The slope of a distancetime graph represents speed. In the later part
of the race, 'D' has the steepest slope,
showing the highest speed.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. When an air bubble at the bottom of a lake rises
to the top, it will
(a) increase in size
(b) decrease in size
(c) maintain its size
(d) flatten into a disk-like shape
The correct option is (a).
• This phenomenon is governed by Boyle’s Law,
which states that pressure and volume are
inversely proportional for a fixed amount of
gas at constant temperature (P₁V₁ = P₂V₂).
• At the bottom of the lake, pressure is high
due to the water column above.
• As the bubble rises, pressure decreases,
causing its volume to increase.
• The bubble therefore expands as it rises.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. When a mirror is rotated by an angle of θ, the
reflected ray will rotate by
(a) 0°
(b) θ/2
(c) θ
(d) 2θ
The correct option is (d).

•
•
•
•
•

By the law of reflection, angle of incidence =
angle of reflection.
If the mirror is rotated by an angle θ, the
normal also rotates by θ.
The new reflected ray makes an angle of (i +
θ) with the normal.
The change in the direction of the reflected
ray = 2θ.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q4. A truck, a car, and a motorcycle have equal
kinetic energies. If equal retarding forces are applied,
their stopping distances will be in the ratio:
(Let x, y, z be the stopping distances for truck, car,
motorcycle respectively)
(a) x > y > z
(b) x < y < z
(c) x = y = z
(d) x = 4y = 8z
The correct option is (c).
• The Work-Energy Theorem states that the
work done by a force equals the change in
kinetic energy.
• 𝑊 = 𝐹 × 𝑑 = 𝐾𝐸.
• Since all vehicles have equal kinetic energies
and are stopped by equal retarding forces,
the stopping distances (d) must be equal.
• Therefore, x = y = z.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. A liquid is flowing in a streamlined manner
through a cylindrical pipe. Along a section containing
the axis of the pipe, the flow profile will be:

The correct option is (d).
• The flow is laminar (or streamlined) and
governed by viscosity.
• The no-slip condition makes the velocity zero
at the pipe wall.
• Velocity increases toward the center, forming
a parabolic profile.
• The maximum velocity occurs at the center,
and zero velocity at the walls.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. The variation of displacement (d) with time (t) in
the case of a particle falling freely under gravity from
rest is correctly shown in:

The correct option is (b).
• For free fall from rest, d = ½gt², showing
displacement ∝ time².
• The d–t graph is a parabola opening upward
from the origin.
• Graph (b) correctly represents this parabolic
relationship, unlike (c) which shows a linear
relation (constant velocity).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Total internal reflection can take place when
light travels from

(a) diamond to glass
(b) water to glass
(c) air to water
(d) air to glass
The correct option is (a).
• Total Internal Reflection (TIR) occurs when
light passes from a denser to a rarer medium
and the angle of incidence exceeds the
critical angle.
• Diamond (n ≈ 2.42) → Glass (n ≈ 1.5) satisfies
this condition.
• The other cases (b, c, d) are from rarer to
denser, where TIR is not possible.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8. Assertion (A): A transformer is useful for
stepping up or stepping down voltages.
Reason (R): A transformer is a device used in D.C.
circuits.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is not a correct
explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true: transformers change AC
voltage levels (step-up or step-down).
• Reason (R) is false: transformers work on
mutual electromagnetic induction, which
requires a changing magnetic field (AC).
• DC produces a constant field, so a
transformer does not work with DC.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9. Domestic electrical wiring is basically a
(a) series connection
(b) parallel connection
(c) combination of series and parallel connections
(d) series connection within each room and parallel
connection elsewhere
The correct option is (b).
• Domestic wiring connects all appliances in
parallel to the main supply.

•
•
•
•

Ensures constant voltage across all
appliances.
Allows independent operation (each can be
switched on/off separately).
A failure in one appliance does not affect
others.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q10. Consider the following statements:
At the present level of technology available in India,
solar energy can be conveniently used to
1. supply hot water to residential buildings.
2. supply water for minor irrigation projects.
3. provide street lighting.
4. electrify a cluster of villages and small towns.
Of these statements:
(a) 1, 2, 3 and 4 are correct
(b) 2 and 4 are correct
(c) 1 and 3 are correct
(d) 1, 2 and 3 are correct
The correct option is (a).
• All four applications are feasible and already
implemented in India.
• Hot water: via solar water heaters in
buildings.
• Irrigation: through solar-powered pumps.
• Street lighting: with integrated solar street
lights.
• Electrification: through solar mini-grids and
micro-grids in remote areas.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q11. A simple machine helps a person in doing
(a) less work.
(b) the same amount of work with lesser force.
(c) the same amount of work.
(d) the same amount of work much faster.
The correct option is (b).
• Work (W = F × d) remains constant in an ideal
machine (ignoring friction).
• A simple machine provides mechanical
advantage by allowing less force over a
greater distance.
• Thus, the same work is done using less force.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q12. 'Diamond Ring' is a phenomenon observed
(a) at the start of a total solar eclipse
(b) at the end of a total solar eclipse
(c) only along the peripheral regions of the totality
trail
(d) both at the start and at the end of a total solar
eclipse
The correct option is (d).
• The Diamond Ring Effect occurs just before
and just after totality in a solar eclipse.
• The last/first sliver of sunlight shines through
a lunar valley, resembling a diamond on the
faint solar corona ring.
• Thus, it is seen both at the beginning and end
of totality.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q13.Consider the following statements:
A person in a spaceship located halfway between the
Earth and the Sun will notice that —
1. The sky is jet black.
2. Stars do not twinkle.
3. Temperature outside the spaceship is much
higher than that on the surface of the Earth.
Of these statements:
(a) Only 3 is correct
(b) 1 and 2 are correct
(c) 1 and 3 are correct
(d) 1, 2 and 3 are correct
Correct Option: (d)
• (1) Sky is jet black:
o There is no atmosphere in space to
scatter sunlight.
o Hence, the sky appears completely
black to an observer in a spaceship.
• (2) Stars do not twinkle:
o On Earth, twinkling occurs due to
atmospheric refraction caused by
varying air densities.

o

In space, there is no atmosphere, so
stars appear steady and nontwinkling.
• (3) Temperature outside the spaceship is
much higher:
o In space, there is direct exposure to
intense solar radiation without
atmospheric filtering.
o Hence, the outer surface of the
spaceship can reach extremely high
temperatures, much higher than
Earth’s surface.
All three statements are correct.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1997
Q1. The tail of a comet is directed away from the sun
because
(a) as the comet rotates around the sun, the lighter
mass of the comet is pushed away due to the
centrifugal force alone
(b) as the comet rotates, the lighter mass of the
comet is attracted by some star situated in the
direction of its tail
(c) the radiation emitted by the sun exerts a radial
pressure on the comet throwing its tail away from the
sun
(d) the tail of the comet always exists in the same
orientation
The correct option is (c).
• A comet’s tail forms only when it gets close to
the Sun, as solar heat causes ices to
sublimate, releasing gas and dust (the coma).
• Two forces from the Sun create the tails:
o Solar Radiation Pressure: pushes dust
particles away, forming the curved
yellowish dust tail.
o Solar Wind: pushes ionized gases
away, forming the bluish straight ion
tail.
• Both effects push the tail away from the Sun.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. The tendency of a liquid drop to contract and
occupy the minimum area is due to
(a) viscosity
(b) surface tension
(c) density
(d) vapour pressure
The correct option is (b).
• Surface tension arises due to cohesive forces
among liquid molecules.
• Molecules at the surface experience a net
inward pull, unlike those inside the liquid.
• This inward pull creates a tension on the
surface, making it behave like a stretched
elastic skin.
• The surface thus tends to minimize area,
pulling the liquid into a sphere — the shape
with minimum surface area for a given
volume.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following is a vector quantity?
(a) Momentum
(b) Pressure
(c) Energy
(d) Work
The correct option is (a).
• A scalar quantity has only magnitude, e.g.,
energy, work, pressure, mass, temperature.
• A vector quantity has both magnitude and
direction.
• Momentum (p = mv) depends on velocity,
which has direction, so momentum is a
vector.
• Pressure, energy, and work are scalars.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. The planet Mercury is revolving in an elliptical
orbit around the sun as shown in the given figure.
The kinetic energy of mercury is greatest at the point
labelled

(a) A
(b) B
(c) C
(d) D
The correct option is (a).
• According to Kepler’s Second Law, a planet
moves fastest when closest to the Sun
(perihelion) and slowest when farthest
(aphelion).
• Kinetic energy (KE = ½mv²) depends on
velocity (v); thus, it is maximum when speed
is highest.
• At point A (perihelion), Mercury’s speed and
kinetic energy are greatest.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. A boy standing at the point 'O' in the given
diagram, throws a ball three times with the same
force, but projecting it along different inclinations
from the ground. The results of the throws have
been plotted in the diagram. Which one of the
following is a valid conclusion?

(a) The larger the initial inclination, the longer the
throw
(b) The larger the height reached, the longer the
throw
(c) The larger the height reached, the shorter the
throw
(d) The larger the initial inclination, the greater the
height reached
The correct option is (d).
• The question involves projectile motion.
• The maximum height depends on the vertical
component of velocity — larger angle gives
greater height.
• The range depends on both components and
is maximum at 45°.

•

•

Therefore:
o (a) false — highest angle ≠ longest
range.
o (b) false — higher throws don’t
always go farther.
o (c) false — not always true.
o (d) true — as inclination increases,
height increases.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. The working principle of a washing machine is
(a) centrifugation
(b) dialysis
(c) reverse osmosis
(d) diffusion
The correct option is (a).
• Washing machines work on centrifugation
during the spin cycle.
• The drum rotates rapidly, creating centrifugal
force that pushes water outward through
small holes.
• This removes excess water, leaving clothes
partially dry.
• Dialysis, reverse osmosis, and diffusion
involve movement through membranes, not
used here.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. A girl is swinging on a swing in a sitting position.
When the same girl stands up, the period of the
swing will
(a) be shorter
(b) be longer
(c) depend on the height of the girl
(d) not change
The correct option is (a).
• A swing acts like a simple pendulum, with
period T = 2π√(L/g).
• L = distance from the pivot to the center of
mass.
• When the girl stands, her center of mass
rises, effectively reducing L.
• Since T ∝ √L, a smaller L gives a shorter
period.
• The swing therefore oscillates faster.

•

Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2000

•

Q1. Which one of the following does a TV remote
control unit use to operate a TV set?
(a) Light waves
(b) Sound waves
(c) Microwaves
(d) Radio waves
The correct option is (a).
• Most standard television remote controls
operate using infrared (IR) radiation.
• Infrared radiation is a type of
electromagnetic wave, just like visible light,
but with a wavelength that is too long for the
human eye to see.
• It is essentially a form of "invisible light".
• The remote sends a coded pulse of infrared
light to a receiver on the TV set.
• This is why you generally need a clear line of
sight between the remote and the TV for it to
work.
• While some modern remotes may use radio
waves (RF) or Bluetooth, the vast majority,
especially at the time this question was
relevant, use infrared light waves.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

•

Q2. A noise level of 100 decibels would correspond
to
(a) just audible sound
(b) ordinary conversation
(c) sound from a noisy street
(d) noise from a machine-shop
The correct option is (d).
• The decibel (dB) scale is logarithmic and
measures sound intensity.
• 0 dB: Threshold of human hearing.
• 60 dB: The level of an ordinary conversation.
• 80–90 dB: The level of a noisy street with
heavy traffic.
• 100 dB: This is a very loud noise level,
equivalent to what one would experience in

a machine shop, near a jackhammer, or at a
rock concert.
Prolonged exposure at this level can cause
hearing damage.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q3. A 'black hole' is a body in space which does not
allow any radiation to come out. This property is due
to its
(a) very small size
(b) very large size
(c) very high density
(d) very low density
The correct option is (c).
• A black hole is an object with an incredibly
strong gravitational field.
• This immense gravity is not due to its size,
but due to its very high density—an
enormous amount of mass packed into an
extremely small volume.
• The gravitational pull is so strong that the
escape velocity from its surface exceeds the
speed of light.
• Since nothing, not even light (which is a form
of radiation), can travel faster than the speed
of light, no radiation can escape from a black
hole once it crosses the event horizon.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. Fluorescent tubes are fitted with a choke. The
choke coil
(a) steps up the line voltage
(b) steps down the line voltage
(c) reduces current in the circuit
(d) chokes low frequency currents
The correct option is (a).
• The correct option is (a) and (c). The primary
initial function is (a), and the continuous
function is (c). Given the options, (a) is the
better answer for starting the tube.
• A choke coil (an inductor) in a fluorescent
tube circuit serves two main purposes:
• To provide a high voltage kick to start the
lamp: When the starter switch opens, the
magnetic field in the choke coil collapses

•

•

•

rapidly. This induces a very high voltage (a
"kick") across the ends of the tube, which is
necessary to ionize the gas inside and initiate
the electrical discharge. So, it effectively
steps up the voltage momentarily.
To limit the current during operation: Once
the gas is ionized and the tube is lit, it
exhibits negative resistance, meaning its
resistance decreases as current increases.
Without a current-limiting device, the
current would rise uncontrollably and
destroy the tube. The choke coil's inductive
reactance acts to reduce and stabilize the
current flowing through the tube during
normal operation.
Both (a) and (c) are correct functions, but the
initial high voltage step-up is the crucial
"choke" action that starts the lamp.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. When a CD (Compact Disc used in audio and
video systems) is seen in sunlight, rainbow-like
colours are seen. This can be explained on the basis
of the phenomenon of
(a) reflection and diffraction
(b) reflection and transmission
(c) diffraction and transmission
(d) refraction, diffraction and transmission
The correct option is (a).
• The rainbow-like colors seen on the surface
of a CD are a result of diffraction and
reflection.
• A CD has a surface with billions of tiny,
closely spaced pits arranged in a spiral track.
This track acts as a diffraction grating.
• When white light (like sunlight) shines on the
CD, the light reflects off the surface. As the
reflected light waves pass over the
diffraction grating (the tracks), they are bent
or spread out (diffracted).
• The different wavelengths (colors) of light
are diffracted at slightly different angles. This
separation of colors, combined with the
interference of the diffracted waves,

•

produces the iridescent, rainbow-like
patterns we see
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q6. Assertion (A): A man standing on a completely
frictionless surface can propel himself by whistling.
Reason (R): If no external force acts on a system, its
momentum cannot change.
(a) Both A and R are true, and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true, but R is not a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true, but R is false
(d) A is false, but R is true
The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true. When a person
whistles, they forcefully expel a mass of air
from their mouth in one direction. According
to Newton's Third Law of Motion (action–
reaction), the air pushes back on the person
with an equal and opposite force. On a
frictionless surface, this tiny reaction force
will cause the person to accelerate and move
in the opposite direction to the whistled air.
• Reason (R) is true. This statement is the Law
of Conservation of Linear Momentum. The
system here is the man plus the air he expels.
Initially, the total momentum is zero. When
he whistles, he gives momentum to the air in
one direction. To keep the total momentum
of the system at zero (since there is no
external force), he must gain an equal and
opposite momentum, causing him to move.
• R is the correct explanation of A. The
principle of conservation of momentum is
the fundamental reason why the action–
reaction pair described in the assertion
results in motion.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. Assertion (A): In a motion picture, usually 24
frames are projected every second over the whole
length of the film.
Reason (R): An image formed on the retina of the eye
persists for about 0.1 s after the removal of the

stimulus.
(a) Both A and R are true, and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true, but R is not a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true, but R is false
(d) A is false, but R is true
The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true. The standard frame rate
for motion pictures is indeed 24 frames per
second (fps). This means 24 still images are
projected onto the screen every second.
• Reason (R) is true. This phenomenon is called
persistence of vision. The human eye and
brain retain an image for a brief period
(about 1/16th to 1/10th of a second) after
the image is gone.
• R is the correct explanation of A. Because of
persistence of vision, when a series of still
images are shown in rapid succession (faster
than the persistence time, like 24 fps), our
brain blends them together, creating the
illusion of smooth, continuous motion. This is
the fundamental principle behind all motion
pictures and animation.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8. Assertion (A): Small glass beads fixed on traffic
signals glow brightly when light falls upon them.
Reason (R): Light is totally reflected when the angle
of incidence exceeds a certain critical value and light
travelling in a denser medium is reflected back in the
same medium from a rarer medium.
(a) Both A and R are true, and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true, but R is not a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true, but R is false
(d) A is false, but R is true
The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true. The reflective materials
used on traffic signs, road markings, and
safety clothing often use tiny glass beads.
These beads glow brightly when illuminated
by headlights at night because they reflect

•

•

•

the light directly back towards the source
(the driver's eyes). This is known as
retroreflection.
Reason (R) is a correct statement of the
principle of Total Internal Reflection (TIR). It
accurately describes the two conditions
required for TIR to occur.
R is the correct explanation of A. The
retroreflective property of the glass beads is
achieved through total internal reflection.
Light from a car's headlights enters the
spherical bead, refracts, reflects off the back
surface of the bead via TIR, and then refracts
again as it exits, traveling back along a path
parallel to the incoming light.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2001

Q1. Which of the following weather conditions is
indicated by a sudden fall in the barometer reading?
(a) Stormy weather
(b) Calm weather
(c) Cold and dry weather
(d) Hot and sunny weather
The correct option is (a).
• A barometer is an instrument that measures
atmospheric pressure.
• Atmospheric pressure is a key indicator of
weather patterns.
• High pressure is generally associated with
stable, calm, and clear weather because it
involves sinking air that inhibits cloud
formation.
• Low pressure is associated with rising air,
which cools, condenses, and forms clouds,
leading to unsettled weather like wind and
rain.
• A sudden and rapid fall in the barometer
reading indicates that a low-pressure system
is approaching quickly.
• This rapid change in pressure is a classic sign
of an impending storm.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2. Cloudy nights are warmer compared to clear
cloudless nights, because clouds
(a) prevent cold waves from the sky from descending
on Earth
(b) reflect back the heat given off by Earth
(c) produce heat and radiate it towards Earth
(d) absorb heat from the atmosphere and send it
towards Earth
The correct option is (b).
• During the day, the Earth's surface absorbs
heat from the sun.
• At night, it radiates this heat back into space
in the form of infrared radiation, causing the
surface to cool down.
• On a clear night, this heat escapes freely into
space, leading to significant cooling.
• On a cloudy night, the clouds (which are
composed of water droplets or ice crystals)
act like a blanket.
• They are effective at absorbing the outgoing
infrared radiation from the Earth and then
reflecting or re-radiating a significant portion
of that heat back down to the surface.
• This process traps the heat, preventing it
from escaping into space and thus keeping
the night warmer than it would be under a
clear sky.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. A radioactive substance has a half-life of four
months. Three-fourths of the substance would decay
in
(a) 3 months
(b) 4 months
(c) 8 months
(d) 12 months
The correct option is (c).
• Half-life is the time it takes for half of a
radioactive substance to decay.
• The question asks for the time it takes for
three-fourths (3/4) of the substance to decay.
This means that one-fourth (1/4) of the
substance remains.
• After the first half-life (4 months), 1/2 of the
substance remains.

•

•

•
•

After the second half-life (another 4 months),
half of the remaining substance decays. So,
(1/2) × (1/2) = 1/4 of the original substance
remains.
The time taken for 1/4 of the substance to
remain (and thus 3/4 to decay) is two halflives.
Total time = 2 × (half-life) = 2 × 4 months = 8
months.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. Assertion (A): A stick is dipped in water in a
slanting position. If observed sideways, the stick
appears short and bent at the surface of the water.
Reason (R): The light coming from the stick
undergoes scattering from water molecules giving
the stick a short and bent appearance.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is NOT a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true. This is a commonly
observed optical illusion. When a straight
object like a stick is partially submerged in
water, it appears to be bent at the water's
surface and the submerged part looks shorter.
• Reason (R) is false. The phenomenon is
caused by the refraction of light, not
scattering.
• Light rays traveling from the submerged part
of the stick (a denser medium, water) to the
observer's eye (through a rarer medium, air)
bend away from the normal at the water–air
interface.
• Our brain interprets these bent rays as having
come in a straight line from an apparent,
shallower position, making the stick look bent
and short.
• Scattering is what makes the sky blue; it is
not the cause here.
• Since the assertion is true but the reason is
false, the correct option is (c).

•

Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. Consider the following statements: A simple
pendulum is set into oscillation. Then
1. The acceleration is zero when the bob passes
through the mean position.
2. In each cycle the bob attains a given velocity
twice.
3. Both acceleration and velocity of the bob are
zero when it reaches its extreme position
during its oscillation.
4. The amplitude of oscillation of the simple
pendulum decreases with time.
Which of these statements are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 3 and 4
(c) 1, 2 and 4
(d) 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is true. At the mean
(equilibrium) position, the net force on the
pendulum bob is zero (gravity is balanced by
tension). Since Force = mass × acceleration,
the acceleration is also zero at this point.
• Statement 2 is true. For any given speed
between zero and the maximum speed, the
bob will have that speed twice in one full
cycle: once while moving in one direction and
once while moving in the opposite direction.
• Statement 3 is false. At the extreme position,
the bob momentarily stops, so its velocity is
zero. However, at this point, the restoring
force (and thus the acceleration) is at its
maximum, pulling the bob back towards the
center.
• Statement 4 is true. In any real-world
pendulum, there are resistive forces like air
drag. These forces cause the pendulum to
lose energy with each swing, so the
amplitude of oscillation gradually decreases
over time (damping).
• Therefore, statements 1, 2, and 4 are correct.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. Assertion (A): A piece of copper and a piece of
glass are heated to the same temperature. When
touched, thereafter, the copper piece appears hotter
than the glass piece.
Reason (R): The density of copper is more than that
of glass.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is NOT a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (b).
• Assertion (A) is true. If you touch two objects
at the same high temperature, the one that
feels hotter is the one that transfers heat to
your hand more quickly.
• Copper is an excellent thermal conductor,
while glass is a poor thermal conductor (an
insulator). Therefore, the copper piece will
transfer heat to your hand much faster,
making it feel hotter.
• Reason (R) is true. The density of copper (≈
8.96 g/cm³) is indeed much more than that of
typical glass (≈ 2.5 g/cm³).
• R is NOT the correct explanation of A. The
reason copper feels hotter is its high thermal
conductivity, not its density.
• While both statements are individually true,
density does not explain the difference in
perceived temperature.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Assertion (A): The boiling point of water
decreases as the altitude increases.
Reason (R): The atmospheric pressure increases with
altitude.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is NOT a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).

•

•
•

•
•
•

Assertion (A) is true. Boiling occurs when the
vapor pressure of a liquid equals the
surrounding atmospheric pressure. As
altitude increases, the atmospheric pressure
decreases. Therefore, water can reach its
boiling point at a lower temperature.
For example, at sea level water boils at 100°C,
but on Mount Everest, it boils at around 71°C.
Reason (R) is false. The atmospheric pressure
decreases with increasing altitude, not
increases.
There is less air above you at higher altitudes,
so the weight of the air column is less.
Since the assertion is true but the reason is
false, the correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q8. When light waves pass from air to glass, the
variables affected are
(a) Wavelength, frequency and velocity
(b) Velocity and frequency
(c) Wavelength and frequency
(d) Wavelength and velocity
The correct option is (d).
• When a wave (including light) passes from
one medium to another, its frequency
remains constant. The frequency is
determined by the source of the wave and
does not change as the medium changes.
• The velocity of light changes when it enters a
new medium. It slows down when moving
from a rarer medium (air) to a denser
medium (glass).
• The relationship between velocity (v),
frequency (f), and wavelength (λ) is v = fλ.
• Since v changes and f remains constant, the
wavelength (λ) must also change to maintain
the equation.
• Therefore, the variables affected are
wavelength and velocity.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. When water is heated from 0°C to 10°C, its
volume
(a) Increases

(b) Decreases
(c) does not change
(d) first decreases and then increases
The correct option is (d).
• This question refers to the anomalous
expansion of water.
• Unlike most substances, which expand
continuously when heated, water exhibits
unusual behavior near its freezing point.
• As water is heated from 0°C, it actually
contracts and its density increases.
• Water reaches its maximum density at
approximately 4°C.
• As the water is heated further from 4°C to
10°C, it begins to expand normally, and its
volume increases.
• Therefore, the overall process from 0°C to
10°C involves the volume first decreasing
(0→4°C) and then increasing (4→10°C).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q10. Consider the following statements: In a nuclear
reactor, a self-sustained chain reaction is possible,
because
(a) more neutrons are released in each of the fission
reactions.
(b) the neutrons immediately take part in the fission
process.
(c) the fast neutrons are slowed down by Graphite.
(d) every neutron released in the fission reaction
initiates further fission.
Which of these statements are correct?
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 1 and 3
(c) 2 and 4
(d) 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (b).
• This question asks for the correct statements
that explain why a self-sustained chain
reaction is possible in a nuclear reactor.
• Statement (a) / 1 is correct. For a chain
reaction to be sustained, each fission event
must produce, on average, at least one
neutron that goes on to cause another fission.
The fission of Uranium-235 releases more

•

•

•

•
•

than one neutron (typically 2 to 3), making a
chain reaction possible.
Statement (b) / 2 is incorrect. The neutrons
released from fission are "fast" neutrons. U235 is much more likely to capture a "slow"
(thermal) neutron. Therefore, the neutrons
do not immediately take part in the next
fission; they must be slowed down first.
Statement (c) / 3 is correct. A moderator is
used in a reactor to slow down the fast
neutrons produced by fission. Graphite is a
common material used as a moderator for
this purpose. By slowing the neutrons, it
increases the probability that they will be
captured by another U-235 nucleus and cause
another fission.
Statement (d) / 4 is incorrect. Not every
neutron released causes further fission. Some
neutrons are absorbed by non-fissile
materials (like U-238 or control rods), and
some escape the reactor core entirely. A
reaction is self-sustaining if, on average,
exactly one neutron from each fission causes
another fission.
Therefore, statements 1 and 3 are correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q11. Which of the following distance-time graphs (xt) represents one-dimensional uniform motion?

The correct option is (a).
• Uniform motion means that an object is
moving at a constant velocity. This implies
that the object covers equal distances in
equal intervals of time.
• In a distance–time graph (position–time
graph), the slope represents the velocity.
• For the velocity to be constant, the slope of
the graph must be constant.

•
•

•

•

A graph with a constant, non-zero slope is a
straight line that is not horizontal.
Graph (a) shows a straight line with a positive
slope, representing an object moving away
from the origin at a constant positive
velocity. This is the correct representation of
one-dimensional uniform motion.
Graph (b) shows changing slope
(acceleration). Graph (c) shows the object is
stationary. Graph (d) is not a function of time
(it shows multiple positions at the same
time), which is physically impossible.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2002

Q1. Consider the following natural phenomena:
1. Terrestrial heating
2. Reflection of light
3. Refraction of light
4. Diffraction of light
Due to which of these phenomena is a mirage
formed?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 4 only
The correct option is (c).
• A mirage is an optical phenomenon that
creates a distorted or displaced image of
distant objects.
• Terrestrial heating is essential. A mirage
occurs when there is a significant
temperature gradient in the air near the
ground. The ground becomes very hot (due to
solar radiation), which in turn heats the layer
of air directly above it, creating layers of air
with different temperatures, densities, and
refractive indices.
• Refraction of light is the primary cause. As
light rays from a distant object (like the sky)
travel downwards towards the hot ground,
they pass through air layers of decreasing
density. This causes the light to continuously
bend or refract upwards.

•

•
•

When these upward-bending rays reach an
observer's eye, the brain interprets them as
having traveled in a straight line from the
ground, creating an inverted image of the sky
on the surface, which looks like a pool of
water.
Reflection and diffraction are not the primary
mechanisms behind this type of mirage.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. The Sun's halo is produced by the refraction of
light in
(a) Water vapour in Stratus clouds
(b) Ice crystals in Cirro-Cumulus clouds
(c) Ice crystals in Cirrus clouds
(d) Dust particles in Stratus clouds
The correct option is (c).
• A solar halo is a ring of light that appears to
encircle the Sun (or sometimes the Moon).
• This optical phenomenon is created by the
refraction and reflection of sunlight by
millions of tiny, hexagonal ice crystals
suspended in the atmosphere.
• These ice crystals are found in high-altitude,
thin, wispy clouds. The type of cloud most
commonly associated with halos is the Cirrus
cloud or cirrostratus cloud, which are
composed almost entirely of ice crystals.
• The specific hexagonal shape of the ice
crystals bends the sunlight at a precise angle
of 22 degrees, creating the characteristic
circular halo.
• Water droplets in lower-altitude clouds like
stratus clouds cause rainbows, not halos.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following statements:
1. Light of longer wavelength is scattered much
more than the light of shorter wavelength.
2. The speed of visible light in water is 0.95
times the speed in a vacuum.
3. Radio waves are produced by rapidly
oscillating electrical currents.

4. To detect over-speeding vehicles, police use
the Doppler effect of reflected short radio
waves.
Which of these statements are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 1 and 3
(c) 2 and 4
(d) 3 and 4
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. According to
Rayleigh scattering, scattering is inversely
proportional to the fourth power of the
wavelength (Scattering ∝ 1/λ⁴). This means
light of shorter wavelength (like blue and
violet) is scattered much more than light of
longer wavelength (like red). This is why the
sky appears blue.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The refractive index
of water is approximately 1.33. The speed of
light in a medium is the speed in a vacuum
divided by the refractive index (v = c/n).
Therefore, the speed of light in water is
approximately c/1.33 ≈ 0.75 times the speed
in a vacuum, not 0.95 times.
• Statement 3 is correct. Radio waves are a
form of electromagnetic radiation. They are
generated when charged particles (like
electrons) are accelerated. In a transmitting
antenna, a rapidly oscillating electrical
current causes electrons to accelerate back
and forth, which in turn radiates energy away
as radio waves.
• Statement 4 is correct. Police radar guns work
on the principle of the Doppler effect. They
transmit a beam of radio waves (or
microwaves) towards a moving vehicle. The
waves reflect off the vehicle and return to the
gun. The frequency shift of the reflected
waves depends on the vehicle's speed relative
to the gun. By measuring this shift, the radar
gun calculates the vehicle's speed.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. Assertion (A): With the increase of temperature,
the viscosity of glycerin increases.

Reason (R): Rise of temperature increases the kinetic
energy of molecules.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is false. Viscosity in liquids is
caused by cohesive forces (intermolecular
attraction) between molecules. As the
temperature of a liquid like glycerin increases,
the molecules gain kinetic energy and move
more vigorously, which weakens these
cohesive forces. This allows the molecules to
slide past each other more easily, causing the
viscosity to decrease, not increase.
• Reason (R) is true. According to the kinetic
theory of matter, the temperature of a
substance is a measure of the average kinetic
energy of its molecules. A rise in temperature
directly corresponds to an increase in the
kinetic energy of the molecules.
• Since the assertion is false but the reason is
true, the correct option is (d).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q5. Assertion (A): An iron ball floats on mercury but
gets immersed in water.
Reason (R): The specific gravity of iron is more than
that of mercury.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true. An object floats in a fluid
if its density (or specific gravity) is less than
the density of the fluid. Iron (specific gravity ≈
7.8) is less dense than mercury (specific
gravity ≈ 13.6), so it floats on mercury.

•

•
•

However, iron is much denser than water
(specific gravity ≈ 1.0), so it sinks in water.
Reason (R) is false. The statement says the
specific gravity of iron is more than that of
mercury, which is incorrect. The specific
gravity of iron is less than that of mercury,
which is precisely why it floats.
Since the assertion is true but the reason is
false, the correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2003

Q1. Diffusion of light in the atmosphere takes place
due to:
(a) Carbon dioxide
(b) Dust particles
(c) Helium
(d) Water vapours
The correct option is (b).
• Diffusion of light, also known as scattering, is
the phenomenon where light rays are
deflected from their straight path by
obstacles in the medium.
• In the Earth's atmosphere, the primary
obstacles that cause light to scatter are gas
molecules (like nitrogen and oxygen), water
vapor, and suspended particulate matter like
dust particles, pollen, and pollutants.
• While gas molecules cause Rayleigh
scattering (which makes the sky blue), larger
particles like dust are responsible for Mie
scattering, which scatters all wavelengths of
light more evenly and contributes to diffuse
skylight, hazy skies, and red sunsets.
• Among the given options, dust particles are
the most significant agent for the general
diffusion or scattering of light.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. An oil tanker is partially filled with oil and moves
forward on a level road with uniform acceleration.
The free surface of the oil then:
(a) remains horizontal
(b) is inclined to the horizontal with smaller depth at

the rear end.
(c) is inclined to the horizontal with larger depth at
the rear end
(d) assumes a parabolic curve
The correct option is (c).
• When the tanker accelerates forward, the oil
inside, due to its inertia, tends to resist this
change in motion.
• This inertia causes the oil to pile up towards
the rear of the tanker.
• As a result, the free surface of the oil will no
longer be horizontal. It will be tilted, with the
depth being larger at the rear end and
smaller at the front end.
• The surface becomes an inclined plane, with
the angle of inclination depending on the
magnitude of acceleration.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. If the radius of the earth were to shrink by one
percent, its mass remaining the same, the value of 'g'
on the earth's surface would:
(a) increase by 0.5%
(b) increase by 2%
(c) decrease by 0.5%
(d) decrease by 2%
The correct option is (b).
• The formula for acceleration due to gravity
(g) is g = GM/R², where G is the gravitational
constant, M is the mass, and R is the radius.
• Since M remains the same, g ∝ 1/R².
• If the radius shrinks by 1%, the new radius R' =
0.99R.
• The new gravity, g' = GM / (0.99R)² = GM /
(0.9801R²) = (1 / 0.9801) × g ≈ 1.0203g.
• Therefore, g increases by approximately 2%.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. A hydrogen-inflated polythene balloon is
released from the surface of the earth. As the
balloon rises to an altitude up in the atmosphere, it
will:
(a) decrease in size
(b) flatten into a disc-like shape

(c) increase in size
(d) maintain the same size and shape
The correct option is (c).
• This is an application of Boyle’s Law, which
states that pressure × volume = constant (for
a fixed mass of gas at constant temperature).
• As the balloon rises, the external atmospheric
pressure decreases.
• The pressure of the hydrogen gas inside the
balloon is now greater than the pressure
outside.
• To equalize pressure, the gas inside the
balloon pushes outward, causing the
polythene material to stretch, and the
balloon to expand in size.
• This expansion continues until the balloon
either bursts or reaches an altitude where its
density equals the surrounding air.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. Consider the following statements:
1. Steam at 100°C and boiling water at 100°C
contain the same amount of heat.
2. Latent heat of fusion of ice is equal to the
latent heat of vaporization of water.
3. In an air-conditioner, heat is extracted from
the room-air at the evaporator coils and is
rejected out at the condenser coils.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) Only 2
(d) Only 3
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Steam at 100°C
contains more heat energy than boiling water
at 100°C because of the latent heat of
vaporization required to convert boiling
water into steam.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The latent heat of
fusion of ice is about 334 J/g, while the latent
heat of vaporization of water is about 2260
J/g. They are not equal.
• Statement 3 is correct. In an air conditioner,
heat is absorbed from indoor air by the

•
•

refrigerant at the evaporator coils, and this
absorbed heat is released outside at the
condenser coils.
Therefore, only statement 3 is correct.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. Consider the following statements:
A 4-wheel vehicle moving in a sharp circular path at
high speed will:
1. overturn about its outer wheels.
2. overturn about its inner wheels.
3. skid outwards.
4. skid inwards.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 3
(b) 2 and 4
(c) 2 and 3
(d) 1 and 4
The correct option is (a).
• When a vehicle moves in a circular path, it
experiences a centripetal force directed
towards the center of the circle. This force is
provided by friction between the tires and the
road.
• Skidding: If the required centripetal force is
greater than the maximum static friction, the
vehicle will lose grip and skid outwards, away
from the center of the turn. Hence, statement
3 is correct.
• Overturning: Due to inertia, the vehicle's
center of gravity tends to continue in a
straight line, creating a torque that tends to
lift the inner wheels and rotate the vehicle
around its outer wheels. Hence, statement 1
is correct.
• Therefore, the vehicle is at risk of overturning
about its outer wheels and skidding
outwards.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. Which one of the following statements is NOT
correct?
(a) The velocity of sound in air increases with the
increase of temperature.
(b) The velocity of sound in air is independent of

pressure.
(c) The velocity of sound in air decreases as the
humidity increases.
(d) The velocity of sound in air is not affected by the
change in amplitude and frequency.
The correct option is (c).
• (a) is correct — The speed of sound in air is
proportional to the square root of
temperature (√T). As temperature increases,
air molecules move faster, allowing sound to
travel faster.
• (b) is correct — For an ideal gas, the speed of
sound is independent of pressure because
pressure and density change proportionally.
• (c) is NOT correct — Humid air is less dense
than dry air since water vapor (H₂O) is lighter
than nitrogen (N₂) and oxygen (O₂).
Therefore, the velocity of sound increases as
humidity increases, not decreases.
• (d) is correct — The velocity of sound
depends on the properties of the medium
(temperature, density, etc.) and is not
affected by amplitude or frequency of the
sound wave.
• Hence, option (c) is correct (as the incorrect
statement).
UPSC PYQs – 2004
Directions: The following question consists of two
statements; one labeled as the ‘Assertion (A)' and
the other as 'Reason (R)'. You are to examine these
two statements carefully and select the answers to
these items using the codes given below:
Codes:
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
Q1. Assertion (A): In our houses, the current in AC
electricity line changes direction 60 times per
second.
Reason (R): The frequency of alternating voltage

supplied is 60 hertz.
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is false.
• An alternating current (AC) with a frequency
of 60 Hz completes 60 full cycles per second.
• In each cycle, the current flows in one
direction and then reverses to flow in the
opposite direction.
• This means the current changes its direction
twice per cycle.
• Therefore, for a 60 Hz supply, the direction
changes 60 × 2 = 120 times per second, not
60.
• Reason (R) is true.
• The standard frequency for AC power supply
in some parts of the world, particularly North
America, is indeed 60 Hertz (Hz). (In other
regions, like Europe and India, the standard
is 50 Hz).
• Since the assertion is false but the reason is a
true statement, the correct option is (d).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. A car is running on a road at a uniform speed of
60 km/hr. The net resultant force on the car is:
(a) driving force in the direction of car's motion
(b) resistance force opposite to the direction of car's
motion
(c) an inclined force
(d) equal to zero
The correct option is (d).
• This question is a direct application of
Newton's First Law of Motion (the law of
inertia).
• The law states that an object will remain at
rest or continue to move at a constant
velocity (i.e., constant speed in a straight line)
unless acted upon by a net external force.
• The car is moving at a uniform speed, which
means its velocity is constant and its
acceleration is zero.
• According to Newton's Second Law (F = ma),
if the acceleration (a) is zero, the net
resultant force (F) acting on the object must
also be zero.

•

•

While there are individual forces acting on
the car (the driving force from the engine, air
resistance, road friction), they are all
balanced, resulting in a net force of zero.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q3. A weightless rubber balloon is filled with 200 cc
of water. Its weight in water is equal to:
(a) 9.8/5 N
(b) 9.8/10 N
(c) 9.8/2 N
(d) zero
The correct option is (d).
• This problem deals with the concept of
buoyancy and Archimedes' principle.
• The balloon is filled with water and is
submerged in water. The density of the water
inside the balloon is the same as the density
of the water outside the balloon.
• The downward force on the balloon is its
weight (mass of the water inside × g).
• The upward buoyant force is equal to the
weight of the water displaced by the balloon.
Since the balloon is filled with water, the
volume of water it displaces is equal to its
own volume.
• Therefore, the buoyant force is equal to the
weight of the water inside the balloon.
• Because the upward buoyant force is exactly
equal to the downward gravitational force
(weight), the net force on the balloon is zero.
• An object with zero net force has an apparent
weight of zero. It is neutrally buoyant and will
neither sink nor rise.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. A person stands at the middle point of a wooden
ladder which starts slipping between a vertical wall
and the floor of a room, while continuing to remain
in a vertical plane. The path traced by the person
standing at the middle point of the slipping ladder is:
(a) a straight line
(b) an elliptical path
(c) a circular path

(d) a parabolic path
The correct option is (c).
• Let the length of the ladder be 2L. The person
is at the midpoint, L.
• Let the base of the ladder be at a distance 'x'
from the wall, and the top of the ladder be at
a height 'y' up the wall. By the Pythagorean
theorem, x² + y² = (2L)² = 4L².
• The coordinates of the person at the midpoint
of the ladder will be (x/2, y/2). Let's call these
coordinates (X, Y).
• So, X = x/2 and Y = y/2. This means x = 2X and
y = 2Y.
• Substitute these back into the Pythagorean
equation: (2X)² + (2Y)² = 4L² ⇒ 4X² + 4Y² = 4L²
⇒ X² + Y² = L².
• This is the standard equation of a circle with
its center at the origin (0,0) and a radius of L.
Therefore, the path traced by the person at
the midpoint is a circular arc.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2005
Q1. What is the order of magnitude of the electric
resistance of the human body (dry)?
(a) 10² ohm
(b) 10⁴ ohm
(c) 10⁶ ohm
(d) 10⁸ ohm
The correct option is (b).
• The electrical resistance of the human body
is highly variable and depends on many
factors, including the path of the current, the
contact area, and the condition of the skin.
• The skin is the primary contributor to the
body's resistance. Dry skin has a very high
resistance, typically ranging from 100,000
ohms (10⁵ ohm) to 1,000,000 ohms (10⁶ ohm).
• However, the internal resistance of the body
(once the skin is bypassed) is much lower,
around 300-500 ohms.
• For a hand-to-hand measurement on dry skin,
the total resistance is often cited in the range
of tens of thousands of ohms.

•

•

Given the options, 10⁴ ohm (10,000 ohm) is
the most representative order of magnitude
for the resistance of a dry human body under
typical contact conditions. 10⁶ ohm is possible
but represents a very high-end estimate,
while 10² ohm would only be possible with
broken or very wet skin. (Note: The provided
answer key says (c), but 10⁴ ohm is a more
commonly accepted average value in safety
and electrical engineering contexts).
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. Consider the following statements:
1. During the process of osmosis, the solvent
travels from the concentrated solution to the
dilute solution.
2. In reverse osmosis, external pressure is
applied to the dilute solution.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2**
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Osmosis is the net
movement of solvent molecules (like water)
across a semi-permeable membrane from a
region of higher solvent concentration (a
dilute solution) to a region of lower solvent
concentration (a concentrated solution). The
statement describes the opposite direction of
flow.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. Reverse osmosis is a
process used to purify water by forcing it
through a semi-permeable membrane. To
overcome the natural osmotic pressure,
external pressure must be applied to the side
with the concentrated solution (e.g., salt
water), not the dilute solution. This forces
the solvent (pure water) to move from the
concentrated side to the dilute side, against
its natural osmotic gradient.
• Since both statements are incorrect, the
correct option is (d).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs – 2006
Q1. What does the term Dolby B or Dolby C printed
on tape recorders and other sound systems refer to?
(a) Frequency modulated system
(b) Amplitude Modulated system
(c) Noise reduction circuit
(d) Both DC and AC power can be used
The correct option is (c).
• Dolby B and Dolby C are analog noise
reduction systems developed by Dolby
Laboratories.
• Analog tape recording is inherently
susceptible to low-level, high-frequency hiss.
• The Dolby system works by boosting (preemphasizing) the high-frequency parts of the
audio signal before recording onto tape.
• During playback, a complementary circuit
reduces the high frequencies back to their
original level.

In doing so, it also reduces the volume of the
inherent tape hiss, making the sound cleaner
and clearer.
Therefore, Dolby B and Dolby C refer to a
noise reduction circuit.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. In which one among the following is the speed of
sound maximum?
(a) Air at 0°C
(b) Air at 100°C
(c) Water
(d) Wood
The correct option is (d).
• The speed of sound depends on the
medium’s elasticity and density.
• Sound travels fastest in solids, slower in
liquids, and slowest in gases.
• Comparison:
o Air at 0°C (gas): ~331 m/s
o Air at 100°C (gas): ~386 m/s
o Water (liquid): ~1480 m/s
o Wood (solid): ~3300–5000 m/s
(depending on the type of wood)
• As shown, sound travels fastest in wood, a
solid medium, compared to water or air.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following is printed on a
commonly used fluorescent tube light?
(a) 220 K
(b) 273 K
(c) 6500 K
(d) 9000 K
The correct option is (c).
• The number followed by “K” printed on a light
bulb or tube refers to its Correlated Color
Temperature (CCT) measured in Kelvin (K).
• Color temperature describes the color
appearance of light emitted by the source:
o 2700K–3000K: Warm or yellowishwhite (like incandescent bulbs)
o 4000K–5000K: Cool or neutral white
o 6000K and above: Daylight or bluishwhite

•
•
•
•

A common CCT for standard fluorescent
tubes, especially labeled “Daylight,” is 6500 K.
This mimics the natural daylight color on an
overcast day.
Other values listed are not standard CCT
ratings for common fluorescent lighting.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Hence, sound in rocks is not limited
to longitudinal waves; it includes
both longitudinal and transverse
waves.
Since both statements are incorrect, the
