Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs – 2007
Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. A flute of smaller length produces waves of
lower frequency.
2. Sound travels in rocks in the form of
longitudinal elastic waves only.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect.
o In wind instruments like a flute, the
length of the vibrating air column
determines the fundamental
frequency (pitch).
o A shorter air column vibrates faster,
producing waves of higher frequency
(a higher-pitched sound).
o Therefore, a flute of smaller length
produces waves of higher, not lower,
frequency.
• Statement 2 is incorrect.
o Sound can travel through solids like
rocks in two forms:
▪ Longitudinal waves (P-waves
or compressional waves):
Particles vibrate parallel to
the direction of wave
propagation.
▪ Transverse waves (S-waves
or shear waves): Particles
vibrate perpendicular to the
direction of wave
propagation.

Q2. What is the average distance (approximate)
between the Sun and the Earth?
(a) 70 × 10⁵ km
(b) 100 × 10⁵ km
(c) 110 × 10⁶ km
(d) 150 × 10⁶ km
The correct option is (d).
• The average distance from the Earth to the
Sun is a fundamental astronomical
measurement known as one Astronomical
Unit (AU).
• The value of 1 AU ≈ 149.6 million kilometers.
• Comparing the options:
o (a) 70 × 10⁵ km = 7 million km
o (b) 100 × 10⁵ km = 10 million km
o (c) 110 × 10⁶ km = 110 million km
o (d) 150 × 10⁶ km = 150 million km
• The value 150 × 10⁶ km (150 million
kilometers) matches the standard average
Earth–Sun distance.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2008
Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. The albedo of an object determines its visual
brightness when viewed with reflected light.
2. The albedo of Mercury is much greater than
the albedo of the Earth.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only

(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Albedo measures how much light that hits a
surface is reflected without being absorbed.
• A surface with high albedo (like fresh snow)
appears bright, while a low albedo surface
(like asphalt) appears dark.
• Therefore, an object’s albedo determines its
visual brightness when seen by reflected
light.
• Mercury’s albedo is very low (≈0.1), while
Earth’s albedo is higher (≈0.3) due to clouds,
oceans, and ice caps.
• Hence, only statement 1 is correct.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. A person is sitting in a car which is at rest. The
reaction from the road at each of the four wheels of
the car is R. When the car runs on a straight level
road, how will the reaction at either of the front
wheels vary?
(a) It will be greater than R
(b) It will be less than R
(c) It will be equal to R
(d) It shall depend on the material of the road
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• When the car is at rest, its weight is equally
distributed among the four wheels.
• The reaction force (R) at each wheel equals
one-fourth of the total weight.
• When the car moves at a uniform speed on a
straight, level road, it remains in equilibrium
— there is no vertical acceleration.
• The forces (driving force, air resistance, etc.)
are balanced, so the weight distribution
remains the same.
• Hence, the reaction at each wheel remains
equal to R.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Assertion (A): Radio waves bend in a magnetic
field.

Reason (R): Radio waves are electromagnetic in
nature.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• Radio waves, like all electromagnetic waves,
consist of photons.
• Photons are electrically neutral, so magnetic
fields do not act on them.
• Therefore, radio waves do not bend in a
magnetic field.
• The reason is true — radio waves are
electromagnetic in nature.
• Assertion is false, Reason is true.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. Consider the following statements:
1. A widely used musical scale called the diatonic
scale has seven frequencies.
2. The frequency of the note Sa is 256 Hz and
that of Ni is 512 Hz.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• The diatonic scale is the foundation of
Western music, consisting of seven distinct
notes (C, D, E, F, G, A, B).
• Statement 1 is correct.
• In Indian classical music, if Sa = 256 Hz, the
next Sa = 512 Hz, but Ni ≈ 480 Hz, not 512 Hz.
• Thus, statement 2 is incorrect.
• Therefore, only statement 1 is correct.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. Which one of the following laser types is used in
a laser printer?
(a) Dye laser

(b) Gas laser
(c) Semiconductor laser
(d) Excimer laser
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• Laser printers use a semiconductor (diode)
laser to form an electrostatic image on a
photosensitive drum.
• Toner particles attach to this image and are
then transferred to paper.
• Semiconductor lasers are compact, efficient,
reliable, and fast-modulating, ideal for
printing.
• Other lasers (dye, gas, excimer) are used in
scientific, industrial, or medical applications.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. Consider the following statements in respect of a
jet engine and a rocket:
1. A jet engine uses the surrounding air for its
oxygen supply and so is unsuitable for motion
in space.
2. A rocket carries its own supply of oxygen in
the gas form, and fuel.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• A jet engine is an air-breathing engine, using
atmospheric oxygen to burn fuel — so it
cannot work in space.
• A rocket carries its own fuel and oxidizer, but
the oxidizer (e.g., liquid oxygen) is stored in
liquid form, not gas form.
• Thus, statement 1 is correct, statement 2 is
incorrect.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. Assertion (A): In the visible spectrum of light, red
light is more energetic than green light.
Reason (R): The wavelength of red light is more than
that of green light.

(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• Energy of a photon (E = hf) is directly
proportional to frequency (f).
• Green light has a higher frequency than red
light, hence more energetic.
• Red light has a longer wavelength than green
light.
• Thus, A is false, R is true.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs – 2009
Q1. Which one among the following has the highest
energy?
(a) Blue light
(b) Green light
(c) Red light
(d) Yellow light
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:

•

•
•

•
•
•

The energy of a photon is directly
proportional to frequency and inversely
proportional to wavelength (E = hf = hc/λ).
Therefore, shorter wavelength → higher
frequency → higher energy.
In the visible spectrum, the order from lowest
to highest energy is: Red → Orange → Yellow
→ Green → Blue → Indigo → Violet
(ROYGBIV).
Red light has the longest wavelength and
lowest energy.
Blue light has the shortest wavelength among
the options and hence the highest energy.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2. Which one of the following planets has the
largest number of natural satellites or moons?
(a) Jupiter
(b) Mars
(c) Saturn
(d) Venus
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• The question asks which planet has the most
moons.
• The number of known moons changes as new
ones are discovered.
• As of the early 2020s, the confirmed moon
counts are approximately:
o Venus: 0
o Mars: 2
o Jupiter: 95
o Saturn: 146
• Saturn has overtaken Jupiter in recent
discoveries and now has the largest number
of natural satellites.
• (Note: In older data, Jupiter was listed as the
planet with the most moons.)
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following types of waves are
used in a night vision apparatus?
(a) Radio waves
(b) Microwaves

(c) Infra-red waves
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• Night vision technology detects and amplifies
electromagnetic radiation that is invisible to
the human eye.
• There are two main types of night vision:
o Image Intensification: Amplifies
ambient light (like starlight or
moonlight) including visible and nearinfrared light.
o Thermal Imaging: Detects infrared
radiation (heat) emitted by all
objects. Warm objects (like humans
or animals) emit more infrared
radiation and thus appear brighter.
• Both technologies rely on infrared waves.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2010
Q1. Which one of the following reflects back more
sunlight as compared to the other three?
(a) Sand desert
(b) Paddy crop land
(c) Land covered with fresh snow
(d) Prairie land
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• The measure of how much light a surface
reflects is called its albedo.
• A higher albedo means more sunlight is
reflected.
• Fresh snow has a very high albedo, reflecting
about 80–95% of the sunlight.
• Sand deserts have a moderate albedo (20–
40%).
• Paddy crop land and prairie land have low
albedos (10–25%) because vegetation
absorbs sunlight for photosynthesis.
• Therefore, land covered with fresh snow
reflects the most sunlight.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q2. What is the principle by which a cooling system
(radiator) in a motor car works?
(a) Conduction only
(b) Convection
(c) Radiation only
(d) Both conduction and radiation
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• A car’s cooling system transfers excess heat
from the engine to the surrounding air.
• Conduction: Heat moves from hot engine
parts to the coolant fluid.
• Convection: The coolant circulates, carrying
heat to the radiator, where airflow removes
the heat — this is forced convection.
• Radiation: Some heat is lost via infrared
radiation, but it is secondary.
• The primary mechanism is convection, due to
the movement of coolant and air.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. If a potato is placed on a pure paper plate which
is white and unprinted and put in a microwave oven,
the potato heats up but the paper plate does not.
This is because
(a) Potato is mainly made up of starch whereas paper
is mainly made up of cellulose
(b) Potato transmits microwaves whereas paper
reflects microwaves
(c) Potato contains water whereas paper does not
contain water
(d) Potato is a fresh organic material whereas paper is
a dead organic material
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• Microwave ovens heat food using microwave
radiation, which is absorbed by water
molecules.
• When water molecules absorb microwaves,
they vibrate rapidly, generating heat through
friction.
• Potato contains a large amount of water, so it
absorbs microwave energy efficiently and
heats up.

•

•

•
•

Paper plates contain very little or no water,
so they do not absorb microwaves and
remain cool.
Any warmth in the plate comes only from
heat conducted by the hot potato, not direct
microwave absorption.
(Note: Paper is transparent to microwaves; it
does not reflect them.)
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. Indiscriminate disposal of used fluorescent
electric lamps causes mercury pollution in the
environment. Mercury is used in the lamps because:
(a) A mercury coating on the inside of the lamp makes
the light bright white
(b) When the lamp is switched on, the mercury in the
lamp causes the emission of ultra-violet radiations
(c) When the lamp is switched on, it is the mercury
which converts the ultra-violet energy into visible light
(d) None of the statements given above is correct
about the use of mercury in the manufacture of
fluorescent lamps
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• A fluorescent lamp contains mercury vapor
and an inert gas (like argon).
• When switched on, electric current excites
mercury atoms, causing them to emit ultraviolet (UV) radiation.
• The phosphor coating on the inside of the
tube then absorbs UV radiation and re-emits
it as visible light.
• Mercury’s role is to produce UV radiation,
not to make light white or convert UV into
visible light.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2011
Q1. Satellites used for telecommunication relay are
kept in a geostationary orbit. A satellite is said to be
in such an orbit when:
1. The orbit is geosynchronous.
2. The orbit is circular.

3. The orbit lies in the plane of the earth's
equator.
4. The orbit is at an altitude of 22,236 km.
Which of the correct answer using the codes
given below:
(a) 1, 2, and 3 only
(b) 1, 3 and 4 only
(c) 2 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3, and 4
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• A geostationary orbit is a specific type of
geosynchronous orbit where a satellite
appears stationary to an observer on Earth.
• Statement 1 – Correct: The orbit is
geosynchronous, meaning its orbital period
equals Earth’s rotational period (≈ 23 hrs 56
mins).
• Statement 2 – Correct: The orbit must be
perfectly circular (eccentricity = 0) to
maintain constant distance and position.
• Statement 3 – Correct: The orbit must lie in
the equatorial plane (inclination = 0°).
• Statement 4 – Incorrect: The correct altitude
is 35,786 km above Earth’s surface, not
22,236 km (which is in miles).
• Therefore, statements 1, 2, and 3 are correct.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2. What is the difference between a CFL and an LED
lamp?
1. To produce light, a CFL uses mercury vapour
and phosphor while an LED lamp uses semiconductor material.
2. The average life span of a CFL is much longer
than that of an LED lamp.

3. A CFL is less energy-efficient as compared to
an LED lamp.
Which of the statements given above is/are
correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, and 3
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• Statement 1 – Correct:
o A CFL (Compact Fluorescent Lamp)
works by passing electric current
through mercury vapour, producing
UV light that excites a phosphor
coating to emit visible light.
o An LED lamp uses semiconductor
material where electrons emit
photons directly when passing
through it.
• Statement 2 – Incorrect:
o LEDs last much longer (≈ 25,000–
50,000 hours) than CFLs (≈ 8,000–
10,000 hours).
• Statement 3 – Correct:
o LEDs are more energy-efficient and
produce more lumens per watt
compared to CFLs.
• Therefore, statements 1 and 3 are correct.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. An artificial satellite orbiting around the earth
does not fall down. This is so because the attraction
of Earth:
(a) Does not exist at such distance
(b) Is neutralized by the attraction of the moon
(c) Provides the necessary speed for its steady motion
(d) Provides the necessary acceleration for its motion
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• An artificial satellite is in continuous free fall
toward Earth but moves forward fast enough
to keep missing it.
• Earth’s gravitational pull provides the
centripetal force required for orbital motion.

•

•
•
•

This gravitational attraction causes a
continuous change in direction of the
satellite’s motion, i.e., centripetal
acceleration.
The forward speed from launch prevents it
from falling straight down.
The balance between gravity and inertia
keeps the satellite in orbit.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q4. What is the difference between asteroids and
comets?
1. Asteroids are small rocky planetoids, while
comets are formed of frozen gases held
together by rocky and metallic material.
2. Asteroids are found mostly between the
orbits of Jupiter and Mars, while comets are
found mostly between Venus and Mercury.
3. Comets show a perceptible glowing tail, while
asteroids do not.
Which of the statements given above is/are
correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 1 and 3 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2, and 3
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• Statement 1 – Correct:
o Asteroids are rocky and metallic.
o Comets are icy bodies made of frozen
gases, dust, and rock — often called
“dirty snowballs.”
• Statement 2 – Incorrect:
o The asteroid belt lies between Mars
and Jupiter, not Venus and Mercury.
o Comets originate in the Kuiper Belt
and Oort Cloud, far beyond Neptune.
• Statement 3 – Correct:
o When close to the Sun, comets form a
glowing coma and tail as ice
vaporizes, while asteroids do not
form tails.
• Therefore, statements 1 and 3 are correct.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. A layer in the Earth's atmosphere called the
ionosphere facilitates radio communication. Why?
1. The presence of ozone causes the reflection
of radio waves to the earth.
2. Radio waves have a very long wavelength.
Which of the statements given above is/are
correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• The ionosphere is a region filled with charged
particles (ions and electrons) created by solar
radiation.
• Statement 1 – Incorrect:
o Ozone is found mainly in the
stratosphere, where it absorbs UV
radiation.
o It does not reflect radio waves;
ionized particles do.
• Statement 2 – Incorrect:
o Radio waves have a wide range of
wavelengths, and not all are long.
o Reflection depends on frequency–
ionization interaction, not
wavelength alone.
• Since both statements are incorrect,
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. The jet aircrafts fly very easily and smoothly in
the lower stratosphere. What could be the
appropriate explanation?
1. There are no clouds or water vapour in the
lower stratosphere.
2. There are no vertical winds in the lower
stratosphere.
Which of the statements given above is/are
correct in this context?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• Commercial jets fly at 30,000–40,000 ft,
within the lower stratosphere.
• Statement 1 – Correct:
o The stratosphere has very little water
vapour, and clouds are absent —
avoiding turbulence and icing.
• Statement 2 – Correct:
o Due to a temperature inversion
(temperature increases with altitude),
air remains stable with no vertical
winds.
• Both factors make the lower stratosphere
ideal for smooth flight.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. The function of heavy water in a nuclear reactor
is to:
(a) Slow down the speed of neutrons
(b) Increase the speed of neutrons
(c) Cool down the reactor
(d) Stop the nuclear reaction
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• In reactors, neutrons from fission are fastmoving and must be slowed down to sustain
further fission efficiently.
• A moderator slows down these neutrons
through elastic collisions.

•

•
•

•

Heavy water (D₂O) is an excellent moderator,
slowing neutrons without absorbing many of
them.
This allows natural uranium to be used as
fuel.
Light water (H₂O) can also act as a moderator
but absorbs more neutrons, requiring
enriched uranium.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2012

Q1. Consider the following statements:
If there were no phenomenon of capillarity
1. it would be difficult to use a kerosene lamp
2. one would not be able to use a straw to
consume a soft drink
3. the blotting paper would fail to function
4. the big trees that we see around would not
have grown on the earth
Which of the following statements given
above are correct?
(a) 1, 2 and 3 only
(b) 1, 3 and 4 only
(c) 2 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• Capillary action is the movement of liquid in
narrow spaces due to adhesion and cohesion.
• Statement 1 — Correct: A kerosene lamp
relies on the wick (wicking action) to draw
fuel up from the reservoir — this is capillarity.
• Statement 2 — Incorrect: Drinking through a
straw works by creating a pressure difference
(lowering pressure in the mouth) so
atmospheric pressure pushes the liquid up,
not capillarity.
• Statement 3 — Correct: Blotting paper soaks
up ink via capillary action through its porous
fibers.
• Statement 4 — Correct: Water transport in
tall trees uses capillary action in narrow
xylem vessels (along with transpiration pull);

•
•

without it, upward water movement would be
severely limited.
Therefore, statements 1, 3, and 4 are correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. What is the role of ultraviolet (UV) radiation in
water purification systems?
1. It inactivates/kills the harmful
microorganisms in water.
2. It removes all the undesirable odours from
the water.
3. It quickens the sedimentation of solid
particles, removes turbidity and improves the
clarity of water.
Which of the statements given above is/are
correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, and 3
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• UV radiation (UV-C) is used primarily for
disinfection — it damages DNA/RNA of
bacteria, viruses and protozoa, preventing
reproduction and thereby inactivating
microorganisms.
• Statement 2 — Incorrect: Odours are caused
by dissolved chemicals; UV does not remove
these — activated carbon or other treatments
are needed.
• Statement 3 — Incorrect: UV does not cause
sedimentation nor remove turbidity; high
turbidity actually reduces UV effectiveness
because particles shield microbes. Prefiltration is often required before UV
treatment.
• Therefore, only statement 1 is correct.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Electrically charged particles from space
travelling at speeds of several hundred km/sec can
severely harm living beings if they reach the surface
of the earth. What prevents them from reaching the
surface of the earth?

(a) The Earth's magnetic field diverts them toward its
poles
(b) The Ozone layer around the Earth reflects them
back to outer space
(c) Moisture in the upper layers of atmosphere
prevents them from reaching the surface of the Earth
(d) None of the statements (a), (b), and (c) is correct.
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• The Earth’s magnetic field (magnetosphere)
deflects and funnels charged particles from
the solar wind toward the magnetic poles,
preventing most from reaching the surface.
• Where particles enter near the poles they
interact with atmospheric gases to produce
auroras (Northern/Southern Lights).
• Ozone protects against harmful ultraviolet
(UV) radiation, not charged particles, so
statement (b) is incorrect.
• Moisture in upper atmosphere does not stop
high-energy charged particles, so statement
(c) is incorrect.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs – 2013
Q1. Consider the following:
1. Electromagnetic radiation
2. Geothermal energy
3. Gravitational force
4. Plate movements
5. Rotation of the earth
6. Revolution of the earth
Which of the above are responsible for
bringing dynamic changes on the surface of
the earth?
(a) 1, 2, 3 and 4 only
(b) 1, 3, 5 and 6 only
(c) 2, 4, 5 and 6 only
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• Dynamic changes on the Earth's surface are
caused by both internal (endogenic) and
external (exogenic) forces.

•

•

•

•

•

•

•

•
•

Electromagnetic radiation: Solar radiation
drives the climate and weather systems,
causing erosion through wind, rain, and ice,
which constantly reshape the Earth's surface.
Geothermal energy: Heat from the Earth's
core drives mantle convection, which is the
engine for plate movements, leading to
earthquakes, volcanic eruptions, and
mountain building.
Gravitational force: Gravity causes mass
wasting (landslides), enables the flow of
rivers and glaciers (which cause erosion), and
drives tides (coastal erosion).
Plate movements: This is a direct and
powerful agent of change, creating
continents, ocean basins, mountain ranges,
and volcanoes.
Rotation of the earth: The Earth's rotation
generates the Coriolis effect, which dictates
patterns of global winds and ocean currents,
thereby influencing climate and erosion
patterns.
Revolution of the earth: The Earth's
revolution around the Sun, combined with its
axial tilt, causes the seasons; seasonal
changes in temperature and weather patterns
are major drivers of weathering and erosion.
The provided answer key states (c), which is
incorrect as it omits the fundamental roles of
gravitational force and electromagnetic
radiation.
Therefore, all six factors are responsible for
dynamic changes.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q2. During a thunderstorm, the thunder in the skies
is produced by the:
1. meeting of cumulonimbus clouds in the sky
2. lightning that separates the nimbus clouds
3. violent upward movement of air and water
particles
Select the correct answer using the codes
given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3

(c) 1 and 3
(d) None of the above produces the thunder
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• Thunder is the sound caused directly by a
lightning strike.
• The options provided do not accurately
describe this process.
• The correct mechanism: A bolt of lightning
rapidly heats the air in its path to extreme
temperatures (up to 30,000°C).
• This sudden heating causes the air to expand
explosively, creating a powerful shockwave.
• This shockwave is what we perceive as the
sharp crack or rumbling boom of thunder.
• Statement 1 is incorrect; clouds colliding does
not produce thunder.
• Statement 2 is incorrect; lightning is a result
of charge separation, it does not separate
clouds.
• Statement 3 describes the atmospheric
conditions that lead to the formation of
thunderclouds, but the air movement itself is
not the source of thunder.
• Since the direct cause—the explosive
expansion of air due to heating by lightning—
is not listed, "None of the above" is the
correct choice.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Fruits stored in a cold chamber exhibit longer
storage life because:
(a) exposure to sunlight is prevented
(b) increased concentration of carbon dioxide in
environment
(c) rate of respiration is decreased
(d) there is an increase in humidity
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• Even after being harvested, fruits are living
tissues that continue to undergo metabolic
processes, primarily respiration.
• Respiration is the process where the fruit
breaks down its stored sugars to produce

•
•

•

•

energy, which leads to ripening, aging, and
eventually, decay.
The rate of this chemical reaction is highly
dependent on temperature.
Storing fruits in a cold chamber lowers their
temperature, which significantly slows down
their rate of respiration.
By decreasing this metabolic rate, the
ripening and aging processes are delayed,
thus extending the storage life of the fruit and
keeping it fresh for longer.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. Ball bearings are used in bicycles, cars, etc.,
because:
(a) The actual contact between contact wheel and
axle increases.
(b) the effective contact between contact wheel and
axle reduces.
(c) The effective contact between contact wheel and
axle increases.
(d) None of the above statements is correct.
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• The fundamental purpose of ball bearings is
to reduce friction between a stationary part
and a rotating part.
• They achieve this by converting high-friction
sliding contact into low-friction rolling
contact.
• The key principle is that the spherical shape
of the balls ensures that they only touch the
inner and outer surfaces (races) at very small
points.
• This drastically reduces the effective area of
contact between the moving parts.
• By minimizing the contact area and utilizing
rolling motion, ball bearings allow wheels and
axles to rotate smoothly with significantly less
resistance, heat, and wear.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Consider the following phenomena:
1. Size of the sun at dusk
2. Colour of the sun at dawn

3. Moon being visible at dawn
4. Twinkle of stars in the sky
5. Polestar being visible in the sky
Which of the above are optical illusions?
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 3, 4 and 5
(c) 1, 2 and 4
(d) 2, 3 and 5
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• An optical illusion is a perception that is
different from physical reality.
• Size of the sun at dusk: This is a well-known
cognitive illusion (the "Moon Illusion"). The
sun or moon appears much larger near the
horizon than when overhead, even though its
angular size is the same. This is due to how
our brain interprets size relative to terrestrial
objects.
• Colour of the sun at dawn: This is an optical
effect caused by a physical phenomenon
(Rayleigh scattering). Light from the lowhanging sun travels through more
atmosphere, which scatters away blue light,
making the sun appear red or orange. It's an
illusion in the sense that the sun's actual color
isn't changing.
• Moon being visible at dawn: This is a real
astronomical event, not an illusion. The
moon's visibility depends on its phase and
position in its orbit relative to the sun and
Earth.
• Twinkle of stars in the sky: This is a classic
optical effect caused by atmospheric
scintillation. The light from a distant star is
bent and distorted by turbulent layers of air,
causing its apparent brightness and position
to fluctuate, which we perceive as twinkling.
• Polestar being visible in the sky: This is a real
astronomical fact. The North Star (Polestar) is
aligned with Earth's rotational axis, so it
appears stationary from the Northern
Hemisphere. This is not an illusion.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. Rainbow is produced when sunlight falls on
drops of rain. Which of the following physical
phenomena are responsible for this?
1. Dispersion
2. Refraction
3. Internal reflection
Select the correct answer using the codes
given below.
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• The formation of a rainbow involves a precise
sequence of three optical phenomena within
raindrops.
• Refraction: As sunlight enters a raindrop, it
bends because it is moving from air to a
denser medium (water).
• Dispersion: As the light refracts, it also splits
into its constituent colors (the spectrum).
This happens because each color's wavelength
bends at a slightly different angle.
• Internal Reflection: The separated colors of
light then travel to the back of the raindrop
and reflect off the inner surface, directing the
light back towards the observer.
• Finally, the light refracts again as it exits the
drop. All three steps—refraction, dispersion,
and internal reflection—are essential for a
rainbow to form.
• The provided answer key's choice (a) is
incorrect because it omits the crucial step of
internal reflection.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q7. The known forces of nature can be divided into
four classes, viz, gravity, electromagnetism, weak
nuclear force and strong nuclear force. With
reference to them, which one of the following
statements is not correct?
(a) Gravity is the strongest of the four.
(b) Electromagnetism acts only on particles with an
electric charge.

(c) Weak nuclear force causes radioactivity.
(d) Strong nuclear force holds protons and neutrons
inside the nucleus of an atom.
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• This question asks to identify the incorrect
statement about the four fundamental forces.
• (a) is not correct. Gravity is by far the
weakest of the four fundamental forces at the
subatomic scale. The strong nuclear force is
the strongest. While gravity dominates on
astronomical scales due to its infinite range
and cumulative effect, it is negligible at the
particle level.
• (b) is correct. The electromagnetic force acts
between any particles that have an electric
charge. It governs the structure of atoms and
molecules.
• (c) is correct. The weak nuclear force is
responsible for processes of particle decay,
including nuclear beta decay, which is a form
of radioactivity.
• (d) is correct. The strong nuclear force is the
most powerful force over very short ranges; it
binds quarks into protons and neutrons and
holds the nucleus together against
electrostatic repulsion.
• Hence, option (a) is the incorrect statement.
UPSC PYQs – 2015
Q1. Tides occur in oceans and seas due to which of
the following?
1. Gravitational Force of the Sun
2. Gravitational Force of the Moon
3. Centrifugal Force of the Earth (due to
rotation)
Select the correct answer using the code
given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, and 3
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:

•

•

•

•

•

•

Tides are the periodic rise and fall of sea
levels, caused by the combined effects of
gravitational and rotational forces acting on
Earth’s oceans.
Gravitational Force of the Sun:
o The Sun’s gravity exerts a tidal pull on
Earth’s oceans, producing solar tides.
o Though the Sun is very far away, its
large mass allows it to influence
ocean waters measurably.
Gravitational Force of the Moon:
o The Moon, being closer to Earth,
exerts a stronger gravitational pull
than the Sun.
o It creates lunar tides, which are more
than twice as strong as solar tides.
o The side of Earth facing the Moon
experiences a tidal bulge due to this
gravitational attraction.
Centrifugal Force of the Earth:
o The Earth–Moon system rotates
around a common center of mass
(the barycenter).
o This creates a centrifugal force that
pushes water outward on the side
opposite the Moon, forming a second
tidal bulge.
o This is why most coastal regions
experience two high tides and two
low tides per day.
The alignment of the Sun, Moon, and Earth
affects tide intensity:
o Spring Tides: During new and full
moons, the Sun and Moon align,
combining their gravitational forces to
produce higher high tides and lower
low tides.
o Neap Tides: During quarter moons,
the Sun and Moon act at right angles,
partially cancelling each other’s
effects, resulting in weaker tides.
Therefore, all three factors — solar gravity,
lunar gravity, and the centrifugal force due to
Earth’s rotation — contribute to the
occurrence of tides.

•

Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1979
Q1. Gobar gas contains mainly:
(a) Carbon dioxide
(b) Methane
(c) Ethylene
(d) Carbon monoxide
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• Gobar gas, also known as biogas, is produced
by the anaerobic (without oxygen) digestion
of organic matter, such as animal manure or
plant waste.
• The primary component of this gas mixture is
methane (CH₄), which is a highly flammable
gas and gives biogas its fuel value. Methane
typically makes up 50–75% of gobar gas.
• The other major component is carbon dioxide
(CO₂), which makes up most of the remainder
(25–50%).
• Small traces of other gases like hydrogen
sulfide (H₂S), nitrogen, and water vapor may
also be present.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. If a large number of people are enclosed in a
room, then:
(a) Oxygen decreases and carbon dioxide increases
(b) Oxygen increases and carbon dioxide decreases
(c) Both oxygen and carbon dioxide decrease
(d) Both oxygen and carbon dioxide increase
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• This question relates to the process of human
respiration.
• During respiration, humans inhale air, and
their lungs absorb oxygen (O₂), which is
essential for metabolic processes.
• As a byproduct of these processes, the body
produces carbon dioxide (CO₂), which is then
exhaled.
• In an enclosed room with many people,
everyone is simultaneously consuming
oxygen from the air and releasing carbon
dioxide into it.

•

•

Therefore, over time, the concentration of
oxygen in the room's air will decrease, while
the concentration of carbon dioxide will
increase.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. The density of sea water is highest as:
(a) Depth increases and salinity increases
(b) Depth decreases and salinity increases
(c) Depth increases and salinity decreases
(d) Depth decreases and salinity decreases
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• The density of seawater is primarily
determined by two factors: temperature and
salinity.
• Temperature: Water becomes denser as it
gets colder (down to about 4°C). In the ocean,
temperature generally decreases with
increasing depth. Colder water is denser and
sinks.
• Salinity: Salinity is the amount of dissolved
salts in the water. The more salt dissolved in
the water, the denser it becomes. Salinity also
tends to increase with depth in many parts of
the ocean.
• Therefore, the highest density is found where
the water is coldest and most saline —
typically where depth increases and salinity
increases.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. The temperature for pasteurisation of milk is
selected so as to:
(a) Kill the micro-organisms
(b) Kill all bacteria
(c) Store it for long time without coagulation
(d) Kill the micro organisms and other harmful
bacteria
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• Pasteurization is a process of heating a liquid
to a specific temperature for a set period to
kill harmful pathogenic (disease-causing)
microorganisms.

•

•

•

•

•

The primary goal is to make the milk safe for
consumption and to extend its storage life by
preventing spoilage and coagulation caused
by microbial growth.
While options (a) and (d) are partially correct
in that pasteurization does kill many
microorganisms, its main purpose from a
food preservation standpoint is to prevent
spoilage and allow for longer storage.
Option (b) is incorrect. Pasteurization does
not kill all bacteria; it is not sterilization. It
specifically targets the most heat-sensitive
pathogens and reduces the overall number of
spoilage organisms. Some harmless and heatresistant bacteria survive.
Therefore, the most complete answer
describing the outcome of the process is that
it allows milk to be stored for a long time
without coagulation (spoiling).
Hence, option (c) is the most appropriate
answer.

Q5. What is the most important factor for the
growth of pests in stored grains?
(a) The moisture of grains
(b) The temperature of grains
(c) The moisture and temperature of grains
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• The growth and proliferation of pests
(including insects and microorganisms like
molds) in stored grains are highly dependent
on environmental conditions.
• Moisture: Pests require water to live and
reproduce. Grains with high moisture content
provide an ideal environment for their
growth.
• Temperature: Pests have an optimal
temperature range for their metabolic
activities and reproduction. Warm
temperatures generally accelerate their life
cycles.
• While each factor is important individually, it
is the combination of both high moisture and

•

favorable temperature that creates the most
rapid and destructive pest infestations.
Controlling either one can limit pest growth,
but controlling both is the most effective
strategy for grain storage.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. Age of a tree can be determined:
(a) By counting the number of rings
(b) By thickness of the bark
(c) By bulk of the tree
(d) By number of leaves
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• The age of a tree in temperate climates can be
accurately determined by a method called
dendrochronology, which involves counting
its growth rings.
• Each year, a tree adds a new layer of wood to
its trunk. This layer consists of two parts: a
wider, lighter-colored ring of springwood
(formed during rapid growth in the spring)
and a narrower, darker ring of summerwood
or autumnwood (formed during slower
growth later in the season).
• One light ring plus one dark ring together
constitute one annual ring, representing one
year of growth.
• By counting the total number of these annual
rings from the center of the trunk outwards,
one can determine the tree's age. The other
factors like bark thickness, bulk, or number of
leaves are not reliable indicators of age.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. One litre of cold air weighs heavier than the dry
air (1 Litre) because of the:
(a) Increased number of collisions between the
molecules
(b) Increased number of molecules at low
temperature
(c) Greater energy of molecules at high temperature
(d) Lower energy of molecules at high temperature
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:

•

•

•

•

•

The weight of a gas in a given volume (its
density) depends on how closely its molecules
are packed together.
According to the ideal gas law, when air is
cooled, its molecules slow down and move
closer together. This means that in a given
volume, like one liter, you can fit more air
molecules when the air is cold than when it is
warm.
More molecules in the same volume result in
greater mass, and therefore, greater weight.
So, cold air is denser and heavier than warm
air.
The question has a slight ambiguity by
comparing "cold air" to "dry air". Assuming it
means comparing cold, dry air to warm, dry
air, the reason it's heavier is the increased
number of molecules. (Note: The provided
explanation also correctly mentions that
humid air is less dense than dry air, but the
core reason cold air is heavier than warm air is
the molecular density).
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q8. Which of the following metals is used in the
electromagnets?
(a) Soft iron
(b) Stainless steel
(c) Cobalt
(d) Copper
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• An electromagnet is a magnet that is created
by an electric current and loses its magnetism
when the current is turned off.
• The ideal material for the core of an
electromagnet is one that can be easily
magnetized but also easily demagnetized.
This property is known as low retentivity.
• Soft iron is a ferromagnetic material that has
high magnetic permeability (it strongly
enhances the magnetic field) but very low
retentivity. It becomes a strong magnet when
current flows through the coil around it but

•

•

loses almost all its magnetism instantly when
the current stops.
Materials like steel have high retentivity and
are used to make permanent magnets, not
electromagnets. Copper is used for the wire
coil but not the core, as it is not
ferromagnetic.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q9. The bats can fly in the dark because:
(a) They have a better vision in the dark
(b) The light startles them
(c) They produce ultrasonics
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• Bats navigate and hunt in complete darkness
using a biological sonar system called
echolocation.
• They emit high-frequency sound pulses,
known as ultrasonics, which are beyond the
range of human hearing.
• These sound waves travel outwards, bounce
off objects in the environment (like insects,
trees, or cave walls), and return to the bat's
large, sensitive ears as echoes.
• By interpreting the time delay and
characteristics of these returning echoes, the
bat can build a detailed mental "sound map"
of its surroundings, allowing it to determine
the location, size, shape, and even texture of
objects, and to fly and hunt with remarkable
precision in the dark.
• While bats are not blind, their vision is not
their primary sense for navigating in
darkness.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1980
Q1. The chief constituent of gobar gas is:
(a) Methane
(b) Carbon dioxide
(c) Acetylene
(d) Ethylene

The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Gobar gas, also known as biogas, is produced
from the anaerobic decomposition of organic
waste like cow dung.
• The primary and most significant component
of gobar gas is methane (CH₄), which typically
constitutes 50–75% of the gas by volume.
• Methane is the component that makes the
gas flammable and useful as a fuel.
• The second most abundant component is
carbon dioxide (CO₂), with other gases like
hydrogen sulfide (H₂S) present in small
amounts.
• Acetylene and ethylene are not primary
constituents of gobar gas.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. The heating element in an electric iron is made
of:
(a) Nichrome
(b) Tungsten
(c) Copper
(d) Iron
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• The heating element in devices like electric
irons, toasters, and heaters must have:
o High electrical resistance to generate
heat effectively (Joule heating).
o High resistance to oxidation and
melting at high temperatures.
• Nichrome, an alloy primarily of nickel (80%)
and chromium (20%), possesses these ideal
properties.
• It heats up significantly when electric current
passes through it.
• It forms a stable chromium oxide (Cr₂O₃)
layer, which prevents oxidation and increases
durability.
• Tungsten is used in bulb filaments, and
copper in wiring due to its low resistance.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. Dry ice is:
(a) Solid carbon dioxide
(b) Ice dust
(c) Liquified nitrogen
(d) Liquified hydrogen
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Dry ice is the solid form of carbon dioxide
(CO₂).
• At standard atmospheric pressure, carbon
dioxide does not become a liquid. Instead, it
sublimates—directly changes from a solid to
a gas.
• It is called “dry” ice because it does not melt
into a wet liquid like water ice.
• It is extremely cold, with a surface
temperature of –78.5°C (–109.3°F).
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. When iron rusts, the weight:
(a) Increases
(b) Decreases
(c) Remains the same
(d) First increases then decreases
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Rusting is a chemical oxidation reaction
where iron (Fe) reacts with oxygen (O₂) in the
presence of water.
• The chemical formula for rust is hydrated
iron(III) oxide (Fe₂O₃·nH₂O).
• During rusting, iron atoms combine with
oxygen and hydrogen atoms from the
environment.
• Because additional atoms (oxygen and
hydrogen) are added, the total mass of the
rusted object increases.
• Therefore, rusted iron weighs more than
before corrosion.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. What is mainly obtained from bauxite?
(a) Copper
(b) Aluminium

(c) Iron
(d) Gold
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• Bauxite is the primary ore of aluminium.
• It is a sedimentary rock rich in hydrated
aluminium oxides, mainly gibbsite (Al(OH)₃),
boehmite (γ-AlO(OH)), and diaspore (αAlO(OH)).
• The extraction of aluminium involves two key
processes:
o The Bayer Process – refining bauxite
to obtain alumina (Al₂O₃).
o The Hall–Héroult Process –
electrolytic reduction of alumina to
produce pure aluminium metal.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. Which substance is commonly used in
refrigerators?
(a) Freon
(b) Oxygen
(c) Ammonia
(d) Sulphur dioxide
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Refrigerators use a refrigerant that absorbs
and releases heat during compression and
expansion cycles.
• Freon is a trade name for a group of
chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs) and
hydrochlorofluorocarbons (HCFCs) used as
refrigerants.
• These compounds are non-flammable, noncorrosive, and efficient in heat transfer.
• However, Freon and other CFCs were later
found to cause ozone layer depletion and
have been phased out under the Montreal
Protocol, replaced by HFCs and other ecofriendly alternatives.
• Still, Freon remains the traditional answer for
this question due to its historical use.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q7. Municipal water in India is generally treated
with:
(a) Chlorine
(b) Potassium permanganate
(c) Sodium Chloride
(d) Sodium Carbonate
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• The final stage of municipal water treatment
involves disinfection to eliminate pathogenic
microorganisms.
• The most common and cost-effective method
is chlorination.
• Chlorine (added as gas, sodium hypochlorite,
or bleaching powder) acts as a strong
oxidizing agent, killing bacteria and viruses.
• When dissolved, chlorine forms hypochlorous
acid (HClO), the active disinfecting agent.
• A residual amount of chlorine is maintained
to ensure water safety during distribution.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1981
Q1. What is done to raise the dough while making
bread?
(a) Alcohol is added
(b) Yeast is added
(c) Carbon dioxide is passed
(d) Air is passed
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• The process of making dough rise is called
leavening, achieved through biological
fermentation.
• Yeast, a single-celled fungus, is added to the
dough and feeds on the sugars present in
flour.
• Through fermentation, yeast converts sugars
into ethanol (alcohol) and carbon dioxide
(CO₂).
• The bubbles of CO₂ get trapped in the dough’s
gluten structure, causing it to expand and
rise.

•
•

The alcohol evaporates during baking, leaving
a soft and porous texture.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. Which of the following statements is true?
(a) Both salinity and density of sea increase with
depth
(b) Both salinity and density of sea decrease with
depth
(c) Salinity increases but density of sea decreases with
depth
(d) Salinity decreases but density of sea increases with
depth
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Density: It is controlled mainly by
temperature and salinity. As depth increases,
temperature decreases, making the water
denser.
• Salinity: Surface layers can have lower or
higher salinity depending on evaporation and
freshwater inflow, but in the deep ocean,
salinity usually increases slightly due to
dense, saline water sinking from polar
regions.
• Therefore, both salinity and density generally
increase with depth in the ocean.
• The provided key (d) omits this nuance—
density consistently rises with depth, driven
by colder and saltier water.
• Hence, option (a) is the most accurate
statement.

Q3. Which of the following is commonly present in
the acids?
(a) Oxygen
(b) Hydrogen
(c) Sulphur
(d) Nitrogen
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• According to the Arrhenius definition, an acid
is a substance that ionizes in water to
produce hydrogen ions (H⁺).
• According to the Brønsted–Lowry definition,
an acid is a proton donor, and a proton is a
hydrogen ion (H⁺).
• Therefore, hydrogen is the common element
present in all acids and is responsible for their
acidic properties.
• Examples include: HCl, H₂SO₄, HNO₃, and
CH₃COOH — all of which release H⁺ ions in
solution.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. In what sequence are the following obtained
from a natural gas well?
1. Natural Gas
2. Water
3. Crude oil
Codes:
(a) 1, 3, 2
(b) 1, 2, 3
(c) 3, 2, 1
(d) 2, 1, 3
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• In underground reservoirs, natural gas, crude
oil, and water are layered according to their
densities.
• Natural gas being the lightest, collects at the
top (forming a gas cap).
• Crude oil occupies the middle layer.
• Water (brine), being the densest, settles at
the bottom.
• When a well is drilled, the substances are
released in sequence:
o Natural gas (topmost)

o

Crude oil (middle)
Water (bottom)
Hence, option (a) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1982

o

•

Q5. Why camphor disappears when kept on a plate?
(a) It sublimes
(b) It reacts with the metal of the container
(c) It reacts with the gases of the air
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Camphor disappears because it undergoes
sublimation.
• Sublimation is a phase change where a
substance goes directly from solid to gas,
without becoming a liquid.
• At room temperature, camphor has a high
vapor pressure, so its molecules escape
directly into the air.
• Over time, the entire solid turns into gas and
disperses into the surroundings.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. Gypsum is added to the soil:
(a) To increase the alkalinity
(b) To decrease the acidity
(c) To decrease the alkalinity
(d) As an insecticide
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• Gypsum (CaSO₄) is used as a soil amendment
for alkaline (sodic) soils.
• The calcium ions (Ca²⁺) from gypsum replace
sodium ions (Na⁺) in the soil.
• The displaced sodium is then leached away by
irrigation water.
• This process reduces alkalinity, improves soil
structure, and enhances water infiltration.
• While lime (CaCO₃) is used to reduce acidity,
gypsum is used to reduce alkalinity.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q1. The substance generally used in the tip of a
safety match stick is:
(a) Red phosphorus
(b) Sodium
(c) Yellow phosphorus
(d) Antimony trisulphide
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• This question is slightly tricky because it
differentiates between the match head and
the striking surface on the box.
• The striking surface on the side of a
matchbox contains red phosphorus, sand,
and a binder.
• The tip of the safety match stick contains
antimony trisulphide (Sb₂S₃), potassium
chlorate, sulfur, and fillers.
• When struck, friction converts red
phosphorus into white phosphorus, which
ignites and sets off the antimony trisulphide
and potassium chlorate, producing a flame.
• Thus, the fuel on the tip is antimony
trisulphide, not red phosphorus.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. The dress made out of which of the following is
safe to wear while cooking?
(a) Nylon
(b) Terylene
(c) Silk
(d) Cotton
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• Synthetic fabrics like Nylon and Terylene
(polyester) have a low melting point.
• When exposed to heat or flame, they melt
and stick to the skin, causing severe burns.
• Natural fibers such as Cotton and Silk behave
differently — they char and burn but do not
melt.
• This makes cotton the safest choice for
wearing while cooking or working near fire.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q3. Which of the following is useful in the chemistry
of photography?
(a) Silver bromide
(b) Aluminium hydroxide
(c) Sodium chloride
(d) Potassium nitrate
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Traditional black-and-white photography
relies on silver halides, which are lightsensitive compounds.
• Silver bromide (AgBr) is the most commonly
used silver halide in photographic films.
• When exposed to light, the AgBr crystals form
a latent image by reducing some silver ions to
metallic silver.
• During development, these exposed crystals
are converted to visible metallic silver,
forming the image, while the unexposed AgBr
is washed away during fixing.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. The process by which a metal surface is coated
with a thin layer of zinc is called:
(a) Vulcanising
(b) Galvanising
(c) Electroplating
(d) None of these
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• Galvanising is the process of coating iron or
steel with a thin layer of zinc to prevent
rusting.
• The most common method is hot-dip
galvanising, where the metal is dipped into
molten zinc.
• Vulcanising is used for rubber, not metal — it
strengthens rubber by heating it with sulfur.
• Electroplating involves depositing metal
using electricity, but galvanising specifically
refers to zinc coating.
• Therefore, galvanising is the most precise and
appropriate term.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. Gasohol is a mixture of gasoline and:
(a) Ethyl alcohol
(b) Methyl alcohol
(c) Kerosene
(d) Butyl alcohol
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Gasohol is a fuel blend consisting of gasoline
and ethyl alcohol (ethanol).
• The most common blend, E10, contains 10%
ethanol and 90% gasoline.
• Ethanol (C₂H₅OH) acts as an oxygenate,
promoting cleaner combustion and reducing
emissions.
• It also raises the octane number of the fuel,
improving engine performance.
• Methyl alcohol (methanol) is more toxic and
less commonly used.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. Ozone present in the ozone layer of the
atmosphere is important because:
(a) It gives oxygen on dissociation
(b) It shields other gases
(c) It helps in the growth of the plants by absorbing a
large proportion of the sun’s ultraviolet radiations
(d) None of these
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• The ozone layer, located in the stratosphere,
contains a high concentration of ozone (O₃).
• It absorbs the majority of the Sun’s
ultraviolet (UV) radiation, especially UV-B
and UV-C rays.
• This protects life on Earth, including plants,
animals, and humans, from DNA damage,
skin cancer, and cataracts.
• By filtering harmful UV radiation, it indirectly
supports plant growth by preventing
excessive UV exposure.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. After sowing seeds, the fertilizers used are:
(a) Phosphates
(b) Nitrates

(c) Green manures
(d) All of these
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• Fertilizers provide essential nutrients for
plant growth after sowing.
• Nitrates (source of nitrogen) promote leafy
growth and are applied post-sowing.
• Phosphates (source of phosphorus)
encourage root development and flowering.
• Green manures improve organic matter and
soil fertility, indirectly nourishing the sown
crop.
• All three types can play a role in post-sowing
nutrient management.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. The chief constituent of gobar gas is:
(a) Hydrogen
(b) Nitrogen
(c) Ethane
(d) Methane
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• Gobar gas (biogas) is produced by anaerobic
digestion of organic waste such as cow dung.
• Its chief constituent is methane (CH₄), making
up 50–75% of the gas.
• The remaining portion consists mainly of
carbon dioxide (CO₂) and small traces of
hydrogen sulfide and nitrogen.
• Methane is the component responsible for its
combustibility and energy value.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. Diamond and Emerald contain:
(a) Carbon and silicon
(b) Carbon and zinc
(c) Silicon, aluminium and beryllium
(d) Carbon and calcium
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• The question combines two distinct

UPSC PYQs - 1983

Q1. The internal antenna of a transistor set is made
of:
(a) Iron
(b) Ferrite rod
(c) Copper
(d) Alnico
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• The internal antenna in an AM transistor
radio is called a ferrite rod antenna or
loopstick antenna.
• It consists of a coil of fine copper wire wound
around a ferrite rod.
• Ferrite is a ceramic made by firing iron oxides
with manganese, zinc, or nickel oxides, giving
it high magnetic permeability.
• This property allows the ferrite to
concentrate magnetic fields, improving signal
strength and sensitivity.
• “Ferro-chrome” (mentioned in some texts) is
incorrect — it’s an alloy, not used for
antennas.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. The common ore of aluminium is:
(a) Bauxite
(b) Chromite
(c) Cryolite
(d) Monazite
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:

•
•

•

•

The primary ore of aluminium is bauxite, a
rock rich in hydrated aluminium oxides.
It is refined via the Bayer process to obtain
alumina (Al₂O₃), then reduced by the Hall–
Héroult process to pure aluminium.
Chromite is the ore of chromium, Cryolite is a
flux, and Monazite is an ore of thorium and
rare earths.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. Radiocarbon dating process is used to know the
age of:
(a) Fossils
(b) Plants
(c) Rocks
(d) All of these
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Radiocarbon dating (Carbon-14 dating)
measures the amount of radioactive carbon14 (¹⁴C) in organic remains.
• All living organisms absorb carbon; after
death, ¹⁴C decays at a known rate.
• Measuring remaining ¹⁴C reveals how long
ago the organism died.
• It applies to fossils containing organic matter
but not to rocks, which require other isotopic
dating (like K-Ar or U-Pb).
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. Aspirin is a:
(a) Pain reliever
(b) Sedative
(c) Cortisone
(d) Soporific
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• Aspirin (acetylsalicylic acid) is a non-steroidal
anti-inflammatory drug (NSAID).
• It acts as an analgesic (pain reliever),
antipyretic (fever reducer), and antiinflammatory agent.
• It also serves as an antiplatelet agent to
prevent blood clots.
• It is not a sedative, cortisone, or soporific.

•

Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. Arrange the following fertilizers according to the
decreasing order of their nitrogen content:
1. Ammonium sulphate
2. Ammonium nitrate
3. Potassium nitrate
4. Urea
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 4, 2, 3, 1
(b) 4, 2, 1, 3
(c) 4, 3, 2, 1
(d) 2, 4, 3, 1
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• Nitrogen contents:
o Urea: 46.6% (highest)
o Ammonium nitrate: 35%
o Ammonium sulphate: 21%
o Potassium nitrate: 13.8%
• Hence, the decreasing order is: Urea >
Ammonium nitrate > Ammonium sulphate >
Potassium nitrate (4, 2, 1, 3).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. Helium is used for respiration in deep water
instead of nitrogen because:
(a) It is heavier than nitrogen
(b) It is lighter than nitrogen
(c) It mixes less in blood than nitrogen
(d) It helps oxygen burn more quickly
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• At high pressures underwater, nitrogen
dissolves in blood, causing nitrogen narcosis
and decompression sickness (the bends).
• Helium is much less soluble in blood and
tissues, so it reduces these risks.
• Breathing mixtures like Heliox (helium +
oxygen) enable divers to go deeper safely.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. In a fluorescent tube which of the following
components are found?

(a) Mercury vapour
(b) Argon and carbon dioxide
(c) Helium and oxygen
(d) Argon and neon
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• A fluorescent tube contains a small amount of
mercury and an inert gas (like argon).
• When electricity passes through, mercury
vapour emits ultraviolet (UV) radiation.
• The phosphor coating inside the tube absorbs
the UV rays and re-emits visible light.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8. For artificial insemination, the semen of a bull
should be stored in:
(a) Cold water
(b) Ice
(c) Liquid nitrogen
(d) Liquid oxygen
The correct option is (c).
Explanation:
• Cryopreservation stores biological materials
like sperm at extremely low temperatures.
• Liquid nitrogen (–196°C) halts all biological
activity, preserving the semen for years.
• The semen is stored in sealed straws inside
liquid nitrogen tanks.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9. Which one is a high-yielding variety of rice?
(a) IR-8
(b) IR-21
(c) K-64
(d) M-986
The correct option is (a).
Explanation:
• IR-8 is a semi-dwarf, high-yielding rice
developed by the International Rice Research
Institute (IRRI) in the 1960s.
• It launched the Green Revolution by
dramatically increasing rice productivity in
Asia.
• It is short, sturdy, and fertilizer-responsive,
preventing lodging and producing high yields.

• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q10. In a remote Indian village with a scarcity of fuel,
the people draw water from a well. They should be
advised before drinking water to:
(a) Boil it
(b) Use ion exchange resins
(c) Use solar stills for distilling
(d) Use potassium permanganate for purifying the
water
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• When fuel is scarce, boiling is impractical.
• Ion exchange and solar distillation are costly
or low-output methods.
• Potassium permanganate (KMnO₄) is a
chemical disinfectant that kills bacteria and
pathogens by oxidation.
• It’s a low-cost, low-tech water purification
method ideal for rural conditions.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q11. Arrange the following in the decreasing order of
their carbon content:
1. Cast iron
2. Wrought iron
3. Steel
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1, 2, 3
(b) 2, 1, 3
(c) 3, 2, 1
(d) 1, 3, 2
The correct option is (d).
Explanation:
• Cast iron: 2–4% carbon → hard, brittle.
• Steel: 0.02–2.1% carbon → strong, ductile.
• Wrought iron: <0.08% carbon → soft,
malleable.
• Therefore, decreasing order of carbon
content: Cast iron > Steel > Wrought iron (1,
3, 2).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q12. Assertion (A): Galvanised iron pipes do not rust
easily.

Reason (R): Zinc has a higher rate of oxidation than
iron.
(a) If both A and R are true and R is not the reason for
A
(b) If both A and R are true and R is the reason for A
(c) If neither A nor R is correct
(d) If A is correct but R is wrong
The correct option is (b).
Explanation:
• Assertion (A) is true — galvanised iron is
coated with zinc, which prevents rusting.
• Reason (R) is true — zinc oxidizes more
readily than iron.
• Zinc offers sacrificial protection: even if the
coating is scratched, zinc corrodes first,
protecting the iron beneath.
• Thus, R correctly explains A.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1984
Q1. Which of the following is not an alloy?
(a) Bronze
(b) Brass
(c) Steel
(d) Zinc
The correct option is (d).
• An alloy is a substance made by melting two
or more elements together, at least one of
which is a metal.
• The resulting mixture has metallic properties
that are often different from its components.
• Bronze is an alloy, primarily of copper and tin.
• Brass is an alloy of copper and zinc.
• Steel is an alloy of iron and carbon.
• Zinc (Zn) is a pure chemical element with
atomic number 30. It is a metal but not an
alloy because it is not a mixture of elements.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Chlorine is:
(a) A rare earth element
(b) A halogen
(c) A noble gas
(d) Coinage metal

The correct option is (b).
• The periodic table organizes elements into
groups based on their chemical properties.
• Chlorine (Cl) is in Group 17 of the periodic
table.
• The elements in this group (Fluorine,
Chlorine, Bromine, Iodine, Astatine) are
known as the halogens.
• Halogens are highly reactive nonmetals that
readily form salts.
• Rare earth elements belong to the f-block
(lanthanides and actinides).
• Noble gases (Group 18) include Helium,
Neon, Argon, etc.
• Coinage metals (Group 11) are Copper, Silver,
and Gold.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3. Micro-elements needed to increase soil fertility
are:
(a) Nitrogen, hydrogen and calcium
(b) Nitrogen, phosphorus and potassium
(c) Iron, calcium and potassium
(d) Manganese, copper and zinc
The correct option is (d).
• Plant nutrients are divided into
macronutrients and micronutrients based on
the quantity required.
• Macronutrients are needed in large amounts
— Nitrogen (N), Phosphorus (P), and
Potassium (K).
• Secondary macronutrients include Calcium
(Ca), Magnesium (Mg), and Sulfur (S).
• Micronutrients (trace elements) are required
in very small quantities but are essential for
plant growth.
• These include iron (Fe), manganese (Mn),
copper (Cu), zinc (Zn), boron (B),
molybdenum (Mo), and chlorine (Cl).
• The group consisting entirely of microelements is manganese, copper, and zinc.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. Solution of washing soda in water is:
(a) Acidic

(b) Basic
(c) Neutral
(d) Sour
The correct option is (b).
• Washing soda is the common name for
sodium carbonate (Na₂CO₃).
• When dissolved in water, it undergoes
hydrolysis because it is a salt of a strong base
(NaOH) and a weak acid (H₂CO₃).
• The carbonate ions (CO₃²⁻) react with water
to form bicarbonate (HCO₃⁻) and hydroxide
ions (OH⁻).
• The excess hydroxide ions (OH⁻) make the
solution alkaline (basic) with pH > 7.
• The given answer key saying “acidic” is
incorrect — a washing soda solution is clearly
basic.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

(c) Ferromagnetic powder
(d) Zinc oxide
The correct option is (c).
• A magnetic tape is used to store sound or
data as magnetic patterns.
• The tape is a plastic strip (usually polyester)
coated with ferromagnetic materials like
ferric oxide (Fe₂O₃) or chromium dioxide
(CrO₂).
• These materials can be magnetized in
patterns corresponding to audio signals.
• The recording head magnetizes the tape, and
the playback head reads these magnetic
variations.
• Mica and zinc oxide are non-magnetic, while
copper sulphate is not used for magnetic
storage.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. Which of the following is an insecticide?
(a) TNT
(b) DDT
(c) Salicylic acid
(d) Ammonium phosphate
The correct option is (b).
• An insecticide is a substance used to kill
insects.
• DDT (Dichlorodiphenyltrichloroethane) is a
synthetic insecticide widely used in the mid20th century to control diseases like malaria
and typhus.
• Its use is now restricted in many countries
due to environmental persistence and
toxicity.
• TNT (Trinitrotoluene) is an explosive, not an
insecticide.
• Salicylic acid is used in skincare and as a
precursor to aspirin.
• Ammonium phosphate is a fertilizer, not a
pesticide.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1985
Q1. Which of the following is a weedkiller?
(a) Insecticide
(b) Methyl salicylate
(c) Herbicide
(d) None of these
The correct option is (c).
• A weedkiller is a substance specifically
designed to kill unwanted plants (weeds).
• The correct technical term for a weedkiller is
a herbicide. Herbicides can be selective
(targeting specific types of plants while
leaving others unharmed) or non-selective
(killing all vegetation they contact).
• An insecticide is a substance used to kill
insects.
• Methyl salicylate (oil of wintergreen) is an
organic compound used for its fragrance and
in topical pain-relief ointments.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1986
Q1. Which of the following statements about the
ozone layer is correct?
(a) It converts UV radiations into electrical energy
(b) It protects the earth's life forms by preventing the
damaging UV radiations from reaching earth's
surface
(c) It is of uniform thickness
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (b).
• The ozone layer is a region in the Earth's
stratosphere with a high concentration of
ozone (O₃).
• Its most critical function is to act as a natural
shield. It absorbs the majority of the Sun's
harmful, high-energy ultraviolet (UV)
radiation, particularly UV-B and UV-C.
• By preventing these damaging UV rays from
reaching the surface, the ozone layer protects
all life on Earth—including humans, animals,

and plants—from effects like skin cancer,
cataracts, and DNA damage.
• The layer does not convert UV to electrical
energy (a), and its thickness is not uniform; it
varies geographically and seasonally (c).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. A new biofertiliser is:
(a) Compost
(b) Farmyard manure
(c) Blue green algae
(d) Nitrates
The correct option is (c).
• A biofertiliser contains living microorganisms
which, when applied to soil, colonize the
plant's root system and promote growth by
increasing the supply of primary nutrients.
• Compost and farmyard manure are organic
manures, not biofertilisers, as they provide
nutrients through decomposition rather than
the action of living microbes.
• Nitrates are chemical fertilisers.
• Blue-green algae (also known as
cyanobacteria), such as Nostoc and Anabaena,
are classic examples of biofertilisers. They are
capable of nitrogen fixation—converting
atmospheric nitrogen gas into ammonia and
other nitrogen compounds that plants can
absorb and use. This makes them a natural,
living source of nitrogen for crops, particularly
rice in paddy fields.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Which of the following elements exists in
maximum quantity in atmosphere?
(a) Oxygen
(b) Hydrogen
(c) Carbon
(d) Nitrogen
The correct option is (d).
• The Earth's atmosphere is a mixture of several
gases.
• The most abundant gas by a large margin
is Nitrogen (N₂), which makes up
approximately 78% of the air we breathe.

•

Oxygen (O₂) is the second most abundant, at
about 21%.
• Argon makes up nearly 1%, and all other
gases, including carbon dioxide, hydrogen,
neon, etc., are present in much smaller, trace
amounts.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. Assertion (A): Carbon dioxide in air is necessary
for sustaining life.
Reason (R): Carbon dioxide is assimilated in
carbohydrates in photosynthesis.
(a) if A and R both are correct and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) if A and R both are correct but R is not the correct
explanation of A
(c) if A is correct and R is incorrect
(d) if A is incorrect and R is correct
The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true. Carbon dioxide (CO₂) is
essential for life on Earth, particularly for
plants and other photosynthetic organisms.
• Reason (R) is true. Photosynthesis is the
process by which plants use sunlight, water,
and carbon dioxide to create their own food.
In this process, CO₂ is "fixed"
or assimilated into energy-rich organic
molecules, specifically carbohydrates like
glucose.
• R is the correct explanation of A. The reason
CO₂ is necessary for sustaining life is
precisely because it is the fundamental
building block that plants use in
photosynthesis to create the carbohydrates
that form the base of almost all food chains
on Earth.
• Since both statements are true and the
reason correctly explains the assertion, the
answer is (a).
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. Assertion (A): Alkalis are used for washing
utensils.
Reason (R): Alkalis are readily soluble in water.

(a) if A and R both are correct and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) if A and R both are correct but R is not the correct
explanation of A
(c) if A is correct and R is incorrect
(d) if A is incorrect and R is correct
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is incorrect. Strong alkalis like
sodium hydroxide (NaOH) and potassium
hydroxide (KOH) are highly corrosive and
caustic. They are dangerous to handle and
would damage both the utensils and the
user's skin. They are not used for generalpurpose utensil washing. Soaps and
detergents, which may be mildly alkaline but
are not strong alkalis, are used instead.
• Reason (R) is true. Alkalis, by definition, are
bases that are soluble in water. For example,
sodium hydroxide and potassium hydroxide
are both readily soluble in water.
• Since the assertion is false but the reason is a
true statement, the correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. Which of the following is the organic matter of
animal and vegetable origin in the soil?
(a) Humus
(b) Vermiculite
(c) Fulminic Acid
(d) None of the above
The correct option is (a).
• Humus is the dark, organic component of soil,
formed by the decomposition of plant and
animal matter by soil microorganisms.
• It consists of the stable, decomposed remains
of leaves, roots, dead animals, and microbes.
• Humus is vital for soil fertility as it improves
soil structure, increases water and nutrient
retention, and provides a source of nutrients
for plants.
• Vermiculite is a mineral used to improve soil
aeration and water retention. Fulminic acid is
an explosive chemical and is not related to
soil.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q7. In India, the problem of utilisation of agriculture
wastes is in:
(a) Conversion of starch into alcohol
(b) Conversion of sugar into carbohydrates
(c) Conversion of bran into molasses
(d) Conversion of cellulose into sugar
The correct option is (d).
• Agricultural waste, such as straw, stalks, and
husks, is primarily composed
of lignocellulose, a complex matrix of
cellulose, hemicellulose, and lignin.
• A major challenge and area of research in
utilizing this waste is breaking down the
tough cellulose polymer into
simple sugars (like glucose).
• This process, called saccharification, is difficult
and expensive because cellulose is highly
resistant to degradation. If it could be done
efficiently, these sugars could then be easily
fermented into biofuels like ethanol.
• Conversion of starch to alcohol (a) is a wellestablished and relatively easy process (e.g.,
from corn). (b) and (c) are not standard
biochemical conversion challenges related to
agricultural waste. The main bottleneck is
converting cellulose to sugar.
Hence, option (d) is the most accurate answer.
UPSC PYQs - 1987
Q1. Main constituents of Biogas are:
(a) Methane and Carbon dioxide
(b) Ethylene and Carbon dioxide
(c) Butane and Carbon monoxide
(d) Methane and Carbon monoxide
The correct option is (a).
• Biogas (also known as gobar gas) is produced
by the anaerobic (oxygen-free) decomposition
of organic matter.
• The composition of biogas can vary, but its
two main components are
consistently Methane (CH₄) and Carbon
dioxide (CO₂) .
• Typically, biogas consists of about 55-75%
methane, which is the flammable component

that provides its fuel value, and 25-45%
carbon dioxide.
• It may also contain small trace amounts of
other gases like hydrogen sulfide (H₂S) and
water vapor.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. Urea is prepared by the chemical reaction of:
(a) Ammonia and Carbon dioxide
(b) Ammonium sulphate and calcium chloride
(c) Acetamide and ethyl alcohol
(d) Chlorine and water
The correct option is (a).
• Urea is synthesized on a large industrial scale
for use as a nitrogen fertilizer.
• The commercial production method is known
as the Bosch-Meiser urea process.
• This process involves reacting liquid ammonia
(NH₃) with liquid carbon dioxide (CO₂) under
high pressure and temperature.
• The overall chemical reaction is: 2NH₃ + CO₂
→ CO(NH₂)₂ + H₂O. First, ammonium
carbamate is formed, which then dehydrates
to produce urea and water.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. A biogas plant works to its maximum capacity
when:
(a) Conditions are aerobic and temperature is 40°C.
(b) Conditions are anaerobic and temperature is
40°C.
(c) Conditions are aerobic and sewage is supplied.
(d) Conditions are anaerobic and sewage is supplied.
The correct option is (b).
• The production of biogas is a biological
process carried out by specific groups of
bacteria.
• The key bacteria, known as methanogens, are
responsible for producing methane. These
bacteria are strictly anaerobic, meaning they
function only in the complete absence of
oxygen. The presence of oxygen will inhibit or
kill them.
• These bacteria also have an optimal
temperature range for their metabolic

activity. The mesophilic bacteria used in most
common biogas plants function best at
temperatures between 30°C and 40°C.
• Therefore, a biogas plant operates at its
maximum capacity under anaerobic
conditions and at a warm temperature
(around 40°C).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Radioactive element which has been found to
have large reserves in India is:
(a) Thorium
(b) Uranium
(c) Radium
(d) Plutonium
The correct option is (a).
• India has one of the world's largest reserves
of Thorium (Th).
• These vast reserves are primarily found in the
form of the mineral monazite, which is
abundant in the beach sands of states like
Kerala, Tamil Nadu, and Andhra Pradesh.
• Thorium itself is not fissile (it cannot sustain a
nuclear chain reaction), but it is "fertile."
When it absorbs a neutron, it can be
converted into Uranium-233, which is a fissile
material.
• India's three-stage nuclear power programme
is strategically designed to utilize these large
thorium reserves to achieve long-term energy
security.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. Chemical composition of pearl is:
(a) Calcium carbonate
(b) Calcium carbonate and magnesium carbonate
(c) Calcium sulphate
(d) Calcium chloride
The correct option is (a).
• Pearls are natural gems produced within the
soft tissue of living mollusks (like oysters and
mussels).
• Their chemical composition is
overwhelmingly calcium carbonate (CaCO₃),
typically in the crystalline form of aragonite.

•

These microscopic crystals of calcium
carbonate are bound together by an organic
protein called conchiolin, which acts as a glue.
• A pearl is essentially made of concentric
layers of calcium carbonate and conchiolin,
giving it its characteristic luster. While other
trace elements may be present, calcium
carbonate is the defining chemical
component.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. The chemical name for urea is:
(a) Aneurin
(b) Chloroethene
(c) Carbamide
(d) None of these
The correct option is (c).
• Urea is an organic compound with the
chemical formula CO(NH₂)₂.
• In systematic chemical nomenclature, it is
known by several names, but the most
common one, other than urea itself,
is carbamide.
• This name reflects its structure as the diamide
of carbonic acid. It can be seen as a carbonyl
group (=C=O) attached to two amine groups (NH₂).
• Aneurin is an old name for Thiamine (Vitamin
B1). Chloroethene is the chemical name for
vinyl chloride.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
