Q3. The newly discovered high-temperature
superconductors are:
(a) Metal alloys
(b) Pure rare-earth metals

(c) Ceramic oxides
(d) Inorganic polymers
The correct option is (c).
• The discovery of high-temperature
superconductivity in 1986 by Bednorz and
Müller marked a new era.
• The material was a lanthanum-based cuprate
perovskite, a ceramic oxide.
• These cuprate oxides (like YBCO) became
superconductive above 77 K, higher than
liquid nitrogen’s boiling point.
• Traditional superconductors were metal
alloys, but these new ones were ceramic
oxides.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. The drugs – caffeine, tannin and nicotine are:
(a) Steroids
(b) Cortisones
(c) Alkaloids
(d) Mild alkalis
The correct option is (c).
• Alkaloids are naturally occurring nitrogencontaining organic compounds.
• Found mainly in plants, they have strong
physiological effects.
• Caffeine (coffee, tea), tannin (tea, wine), and
nicotine (tobacco) are classic alkaloids.
• Steroids and cortisones belong to other
organic compound classes.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. Which one of the following is a non-metallic
mineral?
(a) Manganese
(b) Magnesium
(c) Gypsum
(d) Bauxite
The correct option is (c).
• Metallic minerals yield metals on processing
— e.g., Manganese, Magnesium, Bauxite
(Aluminium ore).
• Non-metallic minerals are used for industrial
purposes, not for metal extraction.

•

•

Gypsum (CaSO₄·2H₂O) is a non-metallic
mineral, used in plaster, cement, and
fertilizers.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. Which of the following chemicals in automobileexhaust can cause cancer?
(a) Carbon monoxide
(b) Polycyclic hydrocarbons
(c) Lead
(d) Oxides of nitrogen
The correct option is (b).
• Automobile exhaust contains polycyclic
aromatic hydrocarbons (PAHs) formed by
incomplete fuel combustion.
• Many PAHs, like benzopyrene, are
carcinogenic (cancer-causing).
• Carbon monoxide is toxic but not
carcinogenic.
• Lead is a neurotoxin, not a carcinogen.
• Oxides of nitrogen contribute to smog, not
directly to cancer.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Assertion (A): A dry cell cannot be recharged.
Reason (R): The chemical reaction in a dry cell is
reversible.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true — A dry cell (like a zinccarbon or alkaline battery) is a primary cell
and not rechargeable.
• Reason (R) is false — The reactions are
irreversible; once reactants are consumed,
they cannot be regenerated by current.
• Rechargeable (secondary) cells have
reversible reactions (e.g., lead-acid, Li-ion).
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q8. Assertion (A): Detergents can easily remove oil
and dirt from soiled garments.
Reason (R): Detergents increase the surface tension
of water.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true — Detergents are
effective cleaning agents.
• Reason (R) is false — Detergents decrease
(not increase) surface tension of water.
• Lower surface tension allows water to spread
and penetrate fabrics.
• Detergents have hydrophilic heads (waterloving) and lipophilic tails (oil-attracting),
helping remove grease.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9. Assertion (A): Red phosphorous is preferred to
white phosphorous in the manufacture of matches.
Reason (R): Red phosphorous is non-poisonous in
nature.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true — Modern matches use
red phosphorous on the striking surface.
• Reason (R) is true — White phosphorous is
poisonous, volatile, and flammable, causing
“phossy jaw” in workers.
• Red phosphorous is stable, safe, and nonpoisonous.
• Its safety is the reason for its use in match
manufacturing.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q10. If there were no organic life on the Earth, the
amount of oxygen in the atmosphere will:
(a) Remain unchanged
(b) Be approximately hundred per cent
(c) Be almost nil
(d) Be approximately fifty per cent
The correct option is (c).
• Most atmospheric oxygen comes from
photosynthesis by plants, algae, and
cyanobacteria.
• Without life, no photosynthesis → no oxygen
production.
• Existing oxygen would be used up in oxidation
of minerals and other chemical reactions.
• Over time, the atmosphere would have
almost no free oxygen.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q11. Ice skating can be used to demonstrate that
when ice is under pressure, its:
(a) Melting point is lowered
(b) Melting point is raised
(c) Melting point remains unchanged
(d) Coefficient of friction with metal is reduced
The correct option is (a).
• Ice is less dense than water; applying
pressure encourages it to melt into denser
liquid form.
• Under pressure, melting point decreases, so
ice melts slightly below 0°C.
• In skating, pressure from blades melts a thin
ice layer, forming liquid water that lubricates
the surface.
• Though frictional heating also helps, the
principle tested is pressure melting.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q1. The chief ingredient of the mosquito repellent
cream is derived from:
(a) Tulsi
(b) Neem
(c) Lemon
(d) Rice bran

The correct option is (b).
• Natural mosquito repellents often use
essential oils derived from plants.
• Neem oil, extracted from the seeds of the
Neem tree (Azadirachta indica), is a powerful
natural insect repellent.
• The active compound responsible for this
property is azadirachtin.
• While Tulsi and Lemon oils also possess
repellent qualities, Neem remains the most
potent and commonly used ingredient,
especially in India.
• Hence, option (b) is the most appropriate
answer.
Q2. Sparkling red colour after the blast of fire
crackers is due to the presence of:
(a) Strontium
(b) Sodium
(c) Sulphur
(d) Magnesium
The correct option is (a).
• The colors in fireworks come from metal salts
that emit characteristic colors when heated.
• Strontium salts (such as strontium carbonate
or strontium nitrate) produce a brilliant red
color.
• Sodium compounds give yellow/orange,
while magnesium burns with a bright white
flame.
• Sulphur acts as a fuel and contributes to the
explosion, but not to the color.
• The red coloration specifically indicates
strontium compounds.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. The chemical name of 'aspirin' is:
(a) Acetyl cyanamide
(b) Acetyl Salicylic Acid
(c) Benzyl Salicylate
(d) Tartaric Acid
The correct option is (b).
• Aspirin is the common name for
acetylsalicylic acid, a well-known pain

•

•

•
•

reliever, antipyretic, and anti-inflammatory
drug.
It is synthesized by acetylating salicylic acid,
where an acetyl group is added to the
molecule.
The resulting compound retains the beneficial
effects of salicylic acid but is less irritating to
the stomach.
The other options refer to different chemical
compounds unrelated to aspirin.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q4. Sodium vapour lamps are preferred to
incandescent lamps because of:
(a) Higher efficiency
(b) Higher tolerance in voltage fluctuations
(c) Easy installation
(d) Higher intensity of illumination
The correct option is (a).
• Sodium vapour lamps are known for their
very high luminous efficiency.
• Efficiency means producing more visible light
(lumens) per unit of energy (watts)
consumed.
• These lamps convert a greater proportion of
electrical energy into light rather than heat.
• Incandescent lamps, by contrast, waste over
90% of energy as heat.
• Though sodium lamps emit bright light, their
primary advantage lies in energy efficiency,
not just intensity.
• Hence, option (a) is the most accurate and
fundamental reason.
Q5. Which of the following is not an alloy?
(a) Brass
(b) Bronze
(c) Steel
(d) Zinc
The correct option is (d).
• An alloy is formed by combining two or more
metals (or metals with nonmetals).
• Brass → Alloy of Copper and Zinc.
• Bronze → Alloy of Copper and Tin.
• Steel → Alloy of Iron and Carbon.

•

Zinc is a pure metal, not a mixture, so it
cannot be classified as an alloy.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. The highest grade of coal, whose surface is
shining and has the highest calorific value, is:
(a) Lignite
(b) Peat
(c) Bituminous
(d) Anthracite
The correct option is (d).
• Coal is classified based on carbon content and
calorific value (energy output).
• The order from lowest to highest grade is:
o Peat → Partially decayed plant
matter, lowest carbon content.
o Lignite → Soft, brown coal with low
calorific value.
o Bituminous → Common industrial
coal with moderate to high energy
output.
o Anthracite → Hard, shiny, black coal,
with maximum carbon content and
highest calorific value.
• Anthracite burns cleanly, producing intense
heat and little smoke.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q1. Paraffin is a by-product of:
(a) Petroleum refining
(b) Processing of agricultural wastes
(c) Cottage industries involving beekeeping
(d) Leather industry
The correct option is (a).
• Paraffin wax is a white or colorless soft solid
obtained from petroleum, coal, or oil shale.
• It is derived during the petroleum refining
process — when crude oil is distilled, paraffin
wax separates from the lubricating oil
fraction.
• Chemically, it is a mixture of hydrocarbon
molecules.

•

It is used in candles, wax paper, crayons, and
coatings.
Beeswax, not paraffin, comes from
beekeeping.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2. Assertion (A): Unlike photographic film, which
can be used only once, video tapes can be reused
several times.
Reason (R): Photography involves a chemical change
but video recording is a physical change.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true but R is not a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true: Photographic film
undergoes a permanent change after
exposure and development, while video tapes
can be erased and reused.
• Reason (R) is true:
o Photography → involves a chemical
reaction in silver halide crystals,
which is irreversible.
o Video recording → involves
rearranging magnetic particles, which
is a reversible physical change.
• The reusability of video tapes is due to this
physical, non-destructive mechanism.
• Hence, both A and R are true, and R correctly
explains A.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Assertion (A): Activated charcoal is used for
decolourising sugar solution.
Reason (R): Activated charcoal absorbs the
impurities on its surface.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true but R is not a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true

The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true: Activated charcoal is
used in the sugar refining process to remove
colored impurities.
• Reason (R) is true: Activated charcoal has a
highly porous structure, giving it a large
surface area for adsorption.
• The colored compounds in raw sugar solution
adhere to its surface and are removed,
resulting in clear, refined sugar.
• The effectiveness of decolourisation is directly
due to adsorption of impurities on the
charcoal’s surface.
• Hence, both A and R are true and R correctly
explains A.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

•
•
•
•

Hard water contains dissolved calcium (Ca²⁺)
and magnesium (Mg²⁺) salts.
These ions form insoluble compounds with
soap, producing scum and reducing cleaning
efficiency.
They also cause limescale deposits in pipes
and heaters.
Soft water lacks these salts and lathers easily
with soap.
Heavy water (D₂O) is chemically different,
containing deuterium instead of hydrogen.
Mineral water may contain various salts but is
not necessarily hard.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1991

Q4. The process of retting in jute is:
(a) Purely physical
(b) Purely chemical
(c) Microbiological
(d) Physicochemical
The correct option is (c).
• Retting is the process of separating bast
fibers (like jute, flax, or hemp) from the plant
stem.
• The stalks are soaked in water, allowing
bacteria and fungi to decompose pectin —
the substance that binds fibers to the stem.
• This is a microbiological process, as it relies
on microbial activity, not chemical or physical
means.
• The microbes must be controlled carefully to
avoid damage to the fibers.
• Though soaking is a physical step, the actual
fiber separation is microbial in nature.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q1. Elements having atomic number higher than '92'
in the periodic table are known as:
(a) Transitional elements
(b) Rare earth elements
(c) Transuranic elements
(d) Alkaline earth metals
The correct option is (c).
• The element with atomic number 92 is
Uranium (U).
• Elements with atomic numbers greater than
92 are called Transuranic (or Transuranium)
elements.
• These include Neptunium (93), Plutonium
(94), and others up to Oganesson (118).
• All transuranic elements are radioactive and
mostly synthetic, created in nuclear reactors
or particle accelerators.
• Only traces of Neptunium and Plutonium
occur naturally.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

and magnesium is known as:
(a) Heavy water
(b) Soft water
(c) Hard water
(d) Mineral water
The correct option is (c).

Q2. Which of the following pairs of substances can
be completely separated by sublimation?
(a) Sand and iodine
(b) Ammonium chloride and iodine
(c) Sand and sulfur
(d) Salt and sulfur
The correct option is (a).

•

•

•

•
•
•

Sublimation is the process where a solid
changes directly into vapor without becoming
liquid.
Iodine sublimes on gentle heating, turning
into purple vapors, which can be re-solidified
on cooling.
Sand (SiO₂) has an extremely high melting
point and does not sublime, allowing
complete separation.
In (b) both ammonium chloride and iodine
sublime, so separation is not possible.
Sulfur in (c) and (d) melts before boiling, not
suitable for sublimation.
Hence, option (a) is the best answer.

Q3. Some medicines are given in the form of
capsules. The capsules are made of:
(a) Paper
(b) Gelatinous material
(c) Embryonic tissue
(d) Starch
The correct option is (b).
• Capsules are made from gelatin, a protein
obtained from animal collagen (skin, bones,
tendons).
• Gelatin is non-toxic, easily digestible, and
dissolves in the stomach, releasing the
medicine.
• Vegetarian alternatives like HPMC
(Hydroxypropyl Methylcellulose) or Pullulan
are also used.
• This form allows the drug to be protected
from air, light, and taste until ingestion.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q4. The chemical name of 'Aspirin' is:
(a) Acetyl cyanamide
(b) Acetyl salicylic acid
(c) Benzyl salicylate
(d) Tartaric acid
The correct option is (b).
• Aspirin is the common name for
Acetylsalicylic Acid.
• It is prepared by acetylating salicylic acid with
acetic anhydride, introducing an acetyl group
(-COCH₃).
• It acts as a pain reliever, fever reducer, antiinflammatory, and blood thinner.
• Other given options refer to different
unrelated compounds.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Atoms of the same element having the same
atomic number but different atomic weight are
called:
(a) Isobars
(b) Isotopes
(c) Polymers
(d) Isomers
The correct option is (b).
• Isotopes have the same number of protons
(same atomic number) but different numbers
of neutrons, resulting in different atomic
masses.
• Example:
o Carbon-12, Carbon-13, and Carbon14 → same element, different mass
numbers.
• Isobars: same mass number, different atomic
number (e.g., Argon-40 and Calcium-40).
• Isomers: same chemical formula, different
structural arrangement.
• Polymers: large molecules made of repeating
smaller units.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 1992
Q1. The gas used for artificial ripening of green fruits
is:
(a) Carbon dioxide
(b) Ethane
(c) Hydrogen
(d) Acetylene (ethyne)
The correct option is (d).
• The natural ripening hormone in fruits is
ethylene (C₂H₄).
• For artificial ripening, calcium carbide (CaC₂)
is commonly used.
• On reacting with moisture, calcium carbide
produces acetylene gas (C₂H₂).
• Acetylene mimics the effect of natural
ethylene and accelerates ripening of fruits
like mangoes, bananas, and papayas.
• However, its use is banned in many countries
due to toxic impurities such as arsenic and
phosphorus.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Three important micronutrients essential for
humans are:
(a) Copper, zinc and iodine
(b) Zinc, copper and potassium
(c) Nitrogen, molybdenum and iodine
(d) Sulphur, copper and iron
The correct option is (a).
• Micronutrients are required in very small
amounts but are vital for metabolic and
physiological functions.
• Copper, Zinc, and Iodine are essential trace
elements for humans.
• Iodine → required for thyroid hormone
synthesis.
• Zinc → supports immunity, enzyme function,
and wound healing.
• Copper → essential for red blood cell
formation and nerve health.
• Elements like potassium, sulphur, and
nitrogen are macronutrients, needed in larger
quantities.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. Natural rubber is a plant product which is
chemically known as:
(a) Mucilage
(b) Gum
(c) Latex
(d) Resin
The correct option is (c).
• Natural rubber is derived from the latex of
the rubber tree (Hevea brasiliensis).
• Latex is a milky, colloidal suspension of
polyisoprene particles in water.
• The latex is collected, coagulated, and dried
to form rubber sheets.
• Gums, resins, and mucilage are other plant
exudates but have different chemical
compositions and non-elastic properties.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. Gypsum is added to cement clinker to:
(a) Increase the tensile strength of the cement
(b) Bind the particles of calcium silicate
(c) Decrease the rate of setting of cement
(d) Facilitate the formation of colloidal gel
The correct option is (c).
• When cement clinker reacts with water, it
hardens quickly (flash set).
• To control the setting time, a small quantity
of gypsum (CaSO₄·2H₂O) is added.
• Gypsum acts as a retarder by slowing down
the reaction between tricalcium aluminate
(C₃A) and water.
• This delay gives workability time to mix,
transport, and pour the cement.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. German silver is an alloy of:
(a) Copper, zinc and nickel
(b) Copper, iron and tin
(c) Iron, chromium and nickel
(d) Copper, aluminium and nickel
The correct option is (a).
• German silver, also known as nickel silver, is
an alloy of copper (≈60%), zinc (≈20%), and
nickel (≈20%).

•
•
•

It contains no silver, despite the name — the
name refers to its silvery-white appearance.
It is hard, corrosion-resistant, and used in
musical instruments, cutlery, and ornaments.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q6. The variation of temperature with heating of a
solid substance, heated at constant rate, is shown in
the following figure:

Which portion of the above graph represents melting
(change from solid to liquid)?
(a) AB
(b) BC
(c) CD
(d) DE
The correct option is (b).
• On a heating curve, temperature is plotted
against heat added.
• AB: Heating of the solid phase —
temperature rises.
• BC: Melting stage — temperature remains
constant while the solid converts to liquid.
• Heat supplied is used as latent heat of fusion
to break intermolecular bonds.
• CD: Heating of liquid phase — temperature
rises again.
• DE: Boiling stage — temperature again
remains constant.
• Thus, melting occurs along BC.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Galvanising of sheet iron is done by dipping the
sheet metal into molten:
(a) Mercury
(b) Cadmium
(c) Lead
(d) Zinc

The correct option is (d).
• Galvanising is the process of coating iron or
steel with zinc to prevent corrosion.
• The method used is hot-dip galvanising,
where the cleaned metal is immersed in
molten zinc.
• Zinc acts as a sacrificial anode, protecting iron
even if the coating is damaged.
• The resulting layer provides durable and longterm rust protection.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Which one of the following is an element?
(a) Ruby
(b) Sapphire
(c) Emerald
(d) Diamond
The correct option is (d).
• An element contains only one type of atom.
• Ruby and Sapphire are varieties of corundum
(Al₂O₃) — a compound of aluminium and
oxygen.
• Emerald is beryllium aluminium silicate
(Be₃Al₂(SiO₃)₆) — also a compound.
• Diamond is a pure elemental form (allotrope)
of carbon (C).
• It has a crystalline structure where each
carbon atom is tetrahedrally bonded.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. It has been observed that after-effects of low
temperature treatment are helpful in promoting the
flowering in certain plants. Through this, the
vegetative period of plant becomes short and early
flowering takes place. This phenomenon is known as:
(a) Photoperiodism
(b) Floreignation
(c) Vernalisation
(d) Heliotropism
The correct option is (c).
• Vernalisation → Exposure of plants/seeds to
prolonged cold to induce or accelerate
flowering.
• It helps winter varieties (like winter wheat)
flower in spring/summer, not autumn.

•
•
•
•

Photoperiodism relates to response to day
length.
Heliotropism is movement toward sunlight.
Floreignation is an outdated term for floral
initiation signals.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q10. The minimum possible temperature beyond
which matter cannot be cooled is:
(a) -98.10° C
(b) -100.00° C
(c) -273.15° C
(d) -459.40° C
The correct option is (c).
• The lowest temperature possible is called
absolute zero.
• At this temperature, particle motion
theoretically ceases, and entropy reaches its
minimum.
• Absolute zero = 0 Kelvin (K) = -273.15°C = 459.67°F.
• It is the zero point of the Kelvin scale, used in
thermodynamics.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1993
Q1. Given below is a diary of disasters involving
hazardous chemicals. Which one of the following is
correctly matched?
(a) 1959, Minamata/Nigiata, Japan: Leakage at
pesticide plant
(b) 1976, Seveso, Italy: Mercury discharged into
waterways
(c) 1984, Bhopal, India: Leakage of dioxin
(d) 1987, Kotka, Finland: Monochloro-benzene spilt in
harbor
The correct option is (d).
• Let's analyze each option to find the correct
match.
• (a) is incorrect. The Minamata disaster was
caused by the release of industrial
wastewater contaminated with highly toxic
methylmercury, not a pesticide plant leakage.

•

•

•

•

(b) is incorrect. The Seveso disaster was
caused by the accidental release of TCDD (a
type of dioxin) from a chemical
manufacturing plant, not mercury.
(c) is incorrect. The Bhopal disaster was
caused by the leakage of highly toxic methyl
isocyanate (MIC) gas from a pesticide plant,
not dioxin.
(d) is correct. In 1987, a major chemical spill
occurred in the port of Kotka, Finland, when a
drum of monochlorobenzene fell and
ruptured during unloading from a ship.
Hence, option (d) is correctly matched.

Q2. Which one of the following is used for dating
archaeological specimens in wood, bones and shells?
(a) Uranium-238
(b) Argon isotope
(c) Carbon-14
(d) Strontium-90
The correct option is (c).
• The method used for dating ancient organic
materials is called radiocarbon dating.
• This technique relies on the radioactive
isotope Carbon-14 (¹⁴C). Living organisms (like
trees for wood, animals for bones, and
mollusks for shells) constantly absorb carbon
from the environment, maintaining a steady
level of ¹⁴C.
• When the organism dies, it stops taking in
new carbon, and the ¹⁴C it contains begins to
decay at a known, constant rate (a half-life of
about 5,730 years).
• By measuring the amount of ¹⁴C remaining in
a specimen, scientists can calculate how long
ago the organism died.
• Uranium-238 and Argon isotopes are used for
dating much older geological samples like
rocks, not organic remains.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following colloidal systems is
represented by fog?
(a) Liquid in gas
(b) Gas in liquid

(c) Solid in gas
(d) Liquid in liquid
The correct option is (a).
• A colloid is a mixture where one substance of
microscopically dispersed insoluble particles
is suspended throughout another substance.
• Fog (as well as mist and clouds) is a colloid
where tiny droplets of a liquid (water) are
dispersed in a gas (air). This type of colloid is
called a liquid aerosol.
• Gas in liquid forms a foam (e.g., whipped
cream).
• Solid in gas forms a solid aerosol (e.g., smoke
or dust).
• Liquid in liquid forms an emulsion (e.g., milk
or mayonnaise).
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. The Government of India has totally banned the
export of 'Burnt Coal' (Jhama), because it contains
recoverable amount of a metal/element used in
transistors. Which one is the element?
(a) Phosphorus
(b) Germanium
(c) Silicon
(d) Tungsten
The correct option is (b).
• 'Jhama' or burnt coal is coal that has been
altered by natural contact with igneous
intrusions. This process can concentrate
certain rare elements.
• Some Indian coals, particularly the ash
produced from them, are a source of
Germanium (Ge).
• Germanium is a crucial semiconductor
element. It was one of the first materials used
to make transistors and diodes and is still
used in specialized electronic applications,
fiber optics, and infrared optics.
• Because of its strategic importance in
electronics, the government has controlled its
export to ensure domestic availability. Silicon
is the most common semiconductor, but
Germanium is the rare element recovered
from coal byproducts.

•

Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. Though a few (gas-based) industries have
already been established, good reserves of natural
gas in India remain unutilized. This vast resource of
natural gas can be used in the production of:
(a) fertilizers
(b) carbide
(c) graphite
(d) synthetic petroleum
The correct option is (a).
• Natural gas is primarily composed of methane
(CH₄).
• One of the most important industrial uses for
natural gas is in the production of ammonia
(NH₃) via the Haber–Bosch process. Methane
is used as the source of hydrogen for this
process.
• This ammonia is the primary feedstock for the
production of nitrogen-based fertilizers, such
as urea and ammonium nitrate.
• Therefore, natural gas is a critical raw material
for the fertilizer industry, and utilizing
domestic gas reserves is key to increasing
fertilizer production.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. Assertion (A): A mixture of salt and ice gives a
temperature below 0°C.
Reason (R): The salt raises the freezing point of ice.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true. When salt is added to
ice, it creates a freezing mixture that can
reach temperatures significantly below 0°C.
This principle is used in making homemade ice
cream.
• Reason (R) is false. The phenomenon
described in the assertion is caused by
freezing point depression. Adding a solute

•
•

(like salt) to a solvent (like water) lowers its
freezing point; it does not raise it. The salt
dissolves in the thin layer of water on the
surface of the ice, creating a saltwater
solution with a freezing point below 0°C. To
melt, the ice needs to draw energy (heat)
from its surroundings, causing the overall
temperature of the mixture to drop.
Since the assertion is true but the reason is
false, the correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

•

•
•

•

•

Q7. Vinegar is an aqueous solution of:
(a) oxalic acid
(b) citric acid
(c) acetic acid
(d) hydrochloric acid
The correct option is (c).
• Vinegar is a sour-tasting liquid produced by
the fermentation of ethanol (alcohol) by
acetic acid bacteria.
• The key component that gives vinegar its
characteristic taste and smell is acetic acid
(CH₃COOH).
• By definition, vinegar is an aqueous (waterbased) solution containing typically 4–7%
acetic acid by volume.
• Oxalic acid is found in plants like rhubarb,
citric acid is in citrus fruits, and hydrochloric
acid is a strong mineral acid found in stomach
acid.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1994
Q1. Which one of the following is in the correct
sequential order in which these appeared under
simulated primitive earth condition?
(a) Methane, Hydrogen cyanide, nitriles, amino acids
(b) Hydrogen cyanide, methane, nitriles, amino acids
(c) Amino acids, nitriles, Hydrogen cyanide, methane
(d) Nitriles, amino acids, methane, Hydrogen cyanide
The correct option is (a).

•

•

•

•

This question refers to the Miller-Urey
experiment, which simulated the chemical
origins of life.
The process involves building complex
molecules from simpler ones.
The experiment began with simple precursor
molecules believed to be on the early Earth,
such as methane (CH₄).
Energy (like lightning) caused these to form
slightly more complex molecules like
hydrogen cyanide (HCN).
These molecules then reacted to form
intermediate compounds like nitriles.
Finally, through hydrolysis, these nitriles
formed the building blocks of proteins — the
amino acids.
The correct sequence shows a progression
from simple to complex: Methane →
Hydrogen cyanide → Nitriles → Amino acids.
(Note: The provided answer key says (b),
which is less accurate as methane was a
starting ingredient, not a product of hydrogen
cyanide.)
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2. The basic reason for the extraordinary sparkle of
a suitably cut diamond is that:
(a) it has a very high transparency
(b) it has a very high refractive index
(c) it is very hard
(d) it has well-defined cleavage planes
The correct option is (b).
• A diamond’s brilliance is an optical
phenomenon.
• The primary reason for its sparkle is its very
high refractive index (around 2.42).
• This high refractive index causes light to bend
sharply upon entering the diamond.
• It also leads to a small critical angle, which
facilitates Total Internal Reflection (TIR).
• Diamonds are cut with precise facets to
ensure light reflects multiple times inside
before emerging, creating intense sparkle and
dispersion (“fire”).

•

•
•

Hardness relates to durability, not sparkle,
and cleavage planes refer to structural
weaknesses, not optical behavior.
(Note: The provided answer key says (d),
which is incorrect.)
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. The main constituents of pearl are:
(a) calcium carbonate and magnesium carbonate
(b) aragonite and conchiolin
(c) ammonium sulphate and sodium carbonate
(d) calcium oxide and ammonium chloride
The correct option is (b).
• Pearls are natural gems formed by mollusks
and are composite materials.
• The main component is aragonite, a
crystalline form of calcium carbonate
(CaCO₃).
• These aragonite crystals are cemented by an
organic binder called conchiolin.
• Option (a) is partially correct but misses the
specific crystal form (aragonite) and the
organic binder (conchiolin).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Monazite is an ore of:
(a) zirconium
(b) thorium
(c) titanium
(d) iron
The correct option is (b).
• Monazite is a phosphate mineral rich in rareearth elements.
• It is also the most important commercial ore
of thorium (Th).
• India has large monazite deposits in its beach
sands, which are vital for its nuclear energy
program.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Galvanised iron sheets are protected from
rusting due to the presence of a layer of:
(a) lead
(b) chromium

(c) zinc
(d) tin
The correct option is (c).
• Galvanization is the process of coating iron or
steel with another metal to prevent rusting.
• The coating metal is zinc.
• Zinc acts as a barrier to moisture and air.
• It also offers sacrificial protection, as zinc is
more reactive and corrodes first, protecting
the iron.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. Which radioactive pollutant has recently drawn
the attention of the public, due to its occurrence in
the building material?
(a) Plutonium
(b) Thorium
(c) Radon
(d) Radium
The correct option is (c).
• Radon (Rn) is a radioactive, colorless, and
odorless gas.
• It forms naturally from the decay of uranium
and thorium found in rocks, soil, and building
materials such as granite, bricks, and
concrete.
• It can accumulate indoors, especially in
poorly ventilated spaces.
• Radon exposure is a major health concern,
being a leading cause of lung cancer after
smoking.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. Water is a good solvent of ionic salts because:
(a) it has a high boiling point
(b) it has a high dipole moment
(c) it has a high specific heat
(d) it has no colour
The correct option is (b).
• Water’s solvent ability arises from its
molecular polarity.
• A water molecule (H₂O) has a partial negative
charge on oxygen and partial positive charges
on hydrogen atoms.
• This results in a high dipole moment.

•

•

•

The polar water molecules surround ions,
with opposite charges attracting —
o Oxygen end attracts cations (e.g.,
Na⁺).
o Hydrogen end attracts anions (e.g.,
Cl⁻).
This ion-dipole interaction breaks the ionic
lattice apart, allowing the salt to dissolve
easily.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

•

•

UPSC PYQs - 1995
Q1. Which one of the following is a mixed fertilizer?
(a) Urea
(b) CAM
(c) Ammonium Sulphate
(d) NPK
The correct option is (d).
• Fertilizers are classified based on the
nutrients they provide.
• Straight fertilizers supply only one primary
nutrient — e.g., Urea (Nitrogen) and
Ammonium Sulphate (Nitrogen).
• CAM (Calcium Ammonium Nitrate) is also
primarily a nitrogen fertilizer.
• Mixed (complex) fertilizers provide two or
more primary nutrients — Nitrogen (N),
Phosphorus (P), and Potassium (K).
• NPK fertilizers are three-component mixtures
that supply N, P (as P₂O₅), and K (as K₂O).
• The NPK ratio (e.g., 10-10-10) shows the
percentage of each nutrient.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Which one of the following polymers is widely
used for making bullet proof material?
(a) Polyvinyl chloride
(b) Polyamides
(c) Polyethylene
(d) Polycarbonates
The correct option is (b).
• Bulletproof materials require very high
tensile strength and toughness to absorb
impact energy.

•

•

Aromatic polyamides (aramids) like Kevlar
and Twaron are used for bulletproof vests
and helmets.
These polymers have long, rigid chains with
high alignment, giving them extreme
strength-to-weight ratios.
Polyethylene (UHMWPE) and polycarbonates
are used in rigid armor or glass panels, but
Kevlar (polyamide) is the standard for flexible
protection.
PVC is unsuitable for this purpose.
(Note: The answer key says (c), but
polyamides (Kevlar) are most iconic and
widely used.)
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. The chemical used as a 'fixer' in photography is:
(a) sodium sulphate
(b) sodium thiosulphate
(c) ammonium persulphate
(d) borax
The correct option is (b).
• In traditional black-and-white photography,
after development, the film still contains
unexposed silver halide crystals that are
light-sensitive.
• The fixing process removes these unexposed
crystals to make the image permanent.
• The chemical used is sodium thiosulphate
(Na₂S₂O₃), known as "hypo".
• It forms a soluble complex with unexposed
silver halides, which are then washed away,
leaving only metallic silver that forms the
visible image.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following pairs of materials
serves as electrodes in chargeable batteries
commonly used in devices such as torchlights,
electric shavers, etc.?
(a) Nickel and cadmium
(b) Zinc and carbon
(c) Lead peroxide and lead
(d) Iron and cadmium
The correct option is (a).

•
•
•

•

•
•
•

The question refers to chargeable
(rechargeable) batteries.
Nickel and Cadmium are the electrodes in
Nickel-Cadmium (Ni-Cd) batteries.
The negative electrode is Cadmium (Cd), and
the positive electrode is Nickel oxyhydroxide
(NiOOH).
These batteries are rechargeable and were
widely used in portable devices like electric
shavers, cordless phones, and torchlights.
Zinc and carbon are used in non-rechargeable
dry cells.
Lead peroxide and lead form the lead-acid
battery, used mainly in vehicles.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. ‘The final Agent Orange raid in Vietnam took
place in 1970;... areas have begun to bloom again.
But 19 years after the war's end, it seems plain that
Agent Orange is killing and maiming human beings...
The apparent toxic fall out from those clouds ... is a
crop of human miseries including cancers,
miscarriages and birth defects-that may persist for
decades.’ The offensive substance referred to in the
above quotation is:
(a) DDT used as insecticide
(b) A complex mixture of herbicides and weedicides
used to increase agricultural output in the then South
Vietnam under the US aid programme
(c) A complex mixture of DDT and other insecticides
used as aerial sprays for protection against malaria
and other tropical diseases
(d) Dioxin used as defoliants
The correct option is (d).
• The quote refers to Agent Orange, a herbicide
and defoliant used by the U.S. military during
the Vietnam War.
• It was a mixture of 2,4-D and 2,4,5-T
herbicides.
• The extreme human toxicity (cancers, birth
defects) was due to 2,3,7,8Tetrachlorodibenzodioxin (TCDD) — a highly
toxic dioxin contaminant.

•

•
•

Agent Orange was used to remove forest
cover and destroy crops, not as an agricultural
fertilizer.
(Note: Option (b) describes a different context
and is incorrect.)
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. ‘Yellow cake’, an item of smuggling across
borders, is:
(a) a crude form of heroin
(b) a crude form of cocaine
(c) uranium oxide
(d) unrefined gold
The correct option is (c).
• Yellowcake is the name for a semi-refined
uranium concentrate.
• It is produced after crushing, milling, and
chemically treating uranium ore.
• The result is a coarse yellowish powder
consisting mainly of uranium oxide (U₃O₈).
• Yellowcake is an intermediate product in the
nuclear fuel cycle, used in producing nuclear
fuel or weapons.
• Because of its strategic importance,
yellowcake is tightly regulated and has been a
target for smuggling.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1996
Q1. The alpha-particle carries two positive charges.
Its mass is very nearly equal to that of:
(a) two protons
(b) an atom of helium
(c) sum of masses of two positrons and two neutrons
(d) two positrons as each positron carries a single
positive charge
The correct option is (b).
• An alpha particle is emitted during alpha
decay, a type of radioactive decay.
• It consists of 2 protons and 2 neutrons bound
together, giving it a +2 charge and a mass
number of 4.
• A neutral helium atom also has 2 protons and
2 neutrons, plus 2 electrons.

•

•
•

•

Since electrons are extremely light (mass ≈
1/1836th of a proton), the mass of an alpha
particle is nearly equal to that of a helium
atom.
In fact, an alpha particle is identical to the
nucleus of a helium atom.
Other options are incorrect:
o (a) Two protons have only half the
mass.
o (c) and (d) Positrons are very light
antimatter particles, so their mass
contribution is negligible.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. The offending substance in the liquor tragedies
leading to blindness, etc. is:
(a) ethyl alcohol
(b) amyl alcohol
(c) benzyl alcohol
(d) methyl alcohol
The correct option is (d).
• The alcohol safe for consumption is ethyl
alcohol (ethanol).
• In illicit liquor, contamination with methyl
alcohol (methanol) occurs due to improper
distillation or deliberate adulteration.
• In the body, methanol is metabolized into
formic acid and formaldehyde, both of which
are highly toxic.
• These compounds cause severe metabolic
acidosis and damage to the optic nerve,
resulting in permanent blindness.
• In larger doses, methanol poisoning can also
be fatal.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following elements is alloyed
with iron to produce steel which can resist high
temperatures and also have high hardness and
abrasion resistance?
(a) Aluminium
(b) Chromium
(c) Nickel
(d) Tungsten
The correct option is (b).

•

•

•

•

•

Chromium (Cr) is the primary element alloyed
with iron to produce stainless and highstrength steels.
It forms a protective layer of chromium oxide
(Cr₂O₃) on the surface, which gives the steel
its resistance to rust and corrosion.
Chromium addition also increases hardness,
abrasion resistance, and tolerance to high
temperatures.
Nickel improves ductility and toughness,
while tungsten increases hardness at high
temperatures, but the described combination
of properties — corrosion resistance,
hardness, and heat tolerance — is
characteristic of chromium steels.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1997

Q1. The most reactive among the halogens is:
(a) fluorine
(b) chlorine
(c) bromine
(d) iodine
The correct option is (a).
• The halogens belong to Group 17 of the
periodic table.
• Their reactivity depends on their ability to
gain an electron to achieve a stable outer
shell.
• Non-metallic reactivity decreases down the
group, meaning the topmost halogen is the
most reactive.
• Fluorine (F) sits at the top of the group — it
has the smallest atomic radius and the
highest electronegativity of all elements.
• Because of its strong electron affinity,
fluorine reacts vigorously with almost every
element, even some noble gases.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. Which one of the following is paramagnetic in
nature?
(a) Iron
(b) Hydrogen

(c) Oxygen
(d) Nitrogen
The correct option is (c).
• Paramagnetism is caused by the presence of
unpaired electrons that align with an external
magnetic field, producing a weak attraction.
• According to Molecular Orbital Theory
(MOT), the oxygen molecule (O₂) has two
unpaired electrons in its antibonding π*
orbitals.
• These unpaired electrons make molecular
oxygen paramagnetic.
• Iron (Fe) is ferromagnetic, which is a stronger
and different form of magnetism.
• Hydrogen (H₂) and Nitrogen (N₂) have all
electrons paired, making them diamagnetic
(weakly repelled).
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following has the highest fuel
value?
(a) Hydrogen
(b) Charcoal
(c) Natural Gas
(d) Gasoline
The correct option is (a).
• Fuel value or calorific value is the amount of
energy released per unit mass upon complete
combustion.
• Hydrogen (H₂) has the highest calorific value
of any known fuel — about 142 MJ/kg.
• When burned, hydrogen forms only water,
making it a clean and efficient fuel.
• Natural gas (mainly methane) has around 55
MJ/kg, gasoline about 46 MJ/kg, and
charcoal around 30 MJ/kg.
• Despite its high energy content, hydrogen’s
storage and safety issues limit its widespread
use.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following is used as an antifreeze for automobile engines?
(a) Propyl alcohol
(b) Ethanol

(c) Methanol
(d) Ethylene glycol
The correct option is (d).
• An antifreeze lowers the freezing point of
water, preventing the engine coolant from
freezing in cold conditions.
• The most effective compound used in
automobile coolants is ethylene glycol
(C₂H₆O₂).
• It not only depresses the freezing point but
also raises the boiling point of the coolant,
helping to prevent both freezing and
overheating.
• Methanol and ethanol can lower freezing
points too, but they are volatile and less
effective for long-term engine protection.
• Ethylene glycol provides better thermal
stability and durability, making it ideal for use
in year-round engine cooling systems.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2000
Q1. The stones formed in human kidney consist
mostly of:
(a) calcium oxalate
(b) sodium acetate
(c) magnesium sulphate
(d) calcium
The correct option is (a).
• Kidney stones (renal calculi) are hard
deposits of minerals and salts that form inside
the kidneys.
• The most common type of kidney stone is
made of calcium oxalate.
• These form when urine contains excess
crystal-forming substances like calcium,
oxalate, and uric acid.
• A high-oxalate diet (spinach, nuts, beets) can
increase the risk.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. Most of the explosions in mines occur due to the
mixing of:
(a) hydrogen with oxygen

(b) oxygen with acetylene
(c) methane with air
(d) carbon dioxide with ethane
The correct option is (c).
• In coal mines, a flammable gas called
firedamp is released, mainly consisting of
methane (CH₄).
• When methane mixes with air in a
concentration of 5–15%, it forms an explosive
mixture.
• A spark or heat source can ignite this mixture,
causing a mine explosion.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

(a) chromium oxide
(b) aluminium oxide
(c) nickel oxide
(d) zinc oxide
The correct option is (b).
• Anodizing is an electrochemical process that
thickens the aluminium oxide (Al₂O₃) layer.
• The resulting oxide layer is hard, corrosionresistant, and durable.
• The layer is grown from the aluminium itself,
not externally coated.
• It also allows for color dyeing and enhances
wear resistance.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

The correct option is (c).

keeping the contents dry and stable.
• Used in medicine bottles, electronics, and
packaging to prevent clumping or
degradation.
• It does not kill germs or bacteria.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q11. Canola refers to special type of oil seed mustard
varieties bred for human consumption. The main
characteristic of these varieties is that the:
(a) seeds have very high oil content
(b) oil is rich in unsaturated fatty acids
(c) oil has long shelf-life
(d) oil has very low Erucic acid content
The correct option is (d).
• Canola was developed from rapeseed by
selective breeding.
• Its defining feature is very low Erucic acid
content (<2%), making it safe for human use.
• It also has low glucosinolate content,
improving nutritional quality.
• While canola oil is high in unsaturated fats
and has good stability, its low Erucic acid trait
distinguishes it from industrial rapeseed.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2001
Q1. Match List I with List II and select the correct
answer using the codes given below the Lists:
List I (Characteristic) List II (Particle)
A. Zero mass

1. Positron

B. Fractional charge 2. Neutrino
C. Fractional spin

3. Quark

D. Integral spin

4. Phonon

Codes:
ABCD
(a) 2 3 1 4
(b) 3 2 4 1
(c) 2 3 4 1
(d) 3 2 1 4
The correct option is (c).
• A. Zero mass: In the Standard Model of
particle physics, Neutrinos (2) were originally
considered to have zero mass.

•

•

•

•
•

B. Fractional charge: Quarks (3) have
fractional electric charges, such as +2/3 or 1/3.
C. Fractional spin: A Positron (1), the
antiparticle of the electron, is a fermion and
has a half-integer or fractional spin (spin1/2).
D. Integral spin: A Phonon (4) is a

Q2. Assertion (A): A chemical reaction becomes
faster at higher temperatures.
Reason (R): At higher temperatures, molecular
motion becomes more rapid.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is NOT a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true. Generally, increasing the
temperature increases the rate of a chemical
reaction.
• Reason (R) is true. Temperature is a measure
of the average kinetic energy of molecules. At
higher temperatures, molecules have more
kinetic energy and their molecular motion
becomes more rapid.
• R is the correct explanation of A. According
to collision theory, reactant molecules must
collide with sufficient energy (activation
energy) and proper orientation. When
molecular motion becomes more rapid (due
to higher temperature):
o The frequency of collisions increases.
o A larger fraction of collisions have
enough energy to overcome the
activation barrier.

•
•

Both of these factors lead to a faster reaction
rate.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

•

•

Q3. Which one of the following is the correct
sequence in increasing order of molecular weights of
the hydrocarbons?
(a) Methane, ethane, propane and butane
(b) Propane, butane, ethane and methane
(c) Butane, ethane, propane and methane
(d) Butane, propane, ethane and methane
The correct option is (a).
• The molecular weight of a hydrocarbon
depends on the number of carbon and
hydrogen atoms in its formula.
• Methane: CH₄ (Molecular Weight ≈ 12 + 4*1
= 16)
• Ethane: C₂H₆ (Molecular Weight ≈ 212 + 61 =
30)
• Propane: C₃H₈ (Molecular Weight ≈ 312 + 81
= 44)
• Butane: C₄H₁₀ (Molecular Weight ≈ 412 + 101
= 58)
• The order of increasing molecular weight is
clearly Methane < Ethane < Propane <
Butane.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. In an atom, the order of filling up of the orbitals
is governed by:
(a) Aufbau principle
(b) Heisenberg's uncertainty principle
(c) Hund's rule
(d) Pauli's exclusion principle
The correct option is (a).
• The Aufbau principle dictates the process of
filling electron orbitals in an atom.
• It states that electrons fill the lowest
available energy orbitals before occupying
higher energy orbitals, following the order 1s,
2s, 2p, 3s, 3p, 4s, 3d, etc.
• Heisenberg's uncertainty principle states you
cannot simultaneously know the exact
position and momentum of a particle.

•

Hund's rule states that when filling
degenerate orbitals, electrons fill each orbital
singly before pairing.
Pauli's exclusion principle states that no two
electrons in an atom can have the same set of
four quantum numbers.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. Which one of the following is NOT radioactive?
(a) Astatine
(b) Francium
(c) Tritium
(d) Zirconium
The correct option is (d).
• Radioactive elements have unstable nuclei
that decay, emitting radiation.
• Astatine (At): all its isotopes are radioactive.
• Francium (Fr): all its isotopes are highly
radioactive with short half-lives.
• Tritium (³H): is a radioactive isotope of
hydrogen.
• Zirconium (Zr): is a stable element with
naturally occurring stable isotopes; it is not
considered radioactive.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. An aqueous solution of copper sulphate is acidic
in nature because the salt undergoes:
(a) Dialysis
(b) Electrolysis
(c) Hydrolysis
(d) Photolysis
The correct option is (c).
• Copper sulphate (CuSO₄) is a salt formed
from a weak base (Cu(OH)₂) and a strong acid
(H₂SO₄).
• When dissolved in water, it undergoes
hydrolysis.
• The Cu²⁺ ion reacts with water molecules to
form Cu(OH)₂ and releases H⁺ ions into the
solution.
• The sulphate ion (SO₄²⁻) does not react
significantly with water as it is the conjugate
base of a strong acid.

•
•

The presence of excess H⁺ ions makes the
solution acidic (pH < 7).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q7. Consider the following statements with
reference to the Periodic Table of chemical elements:
1. Ionisation potential gradually decreases along
a period.
2. In a group of elements, electron affinity
decreases as the atomic weight increases.
3. In a given period, electronegativity decreases
as the atomic number increases.
Which of these statement(s) is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Ionisation potential
generally increases along a period (left →
right) because of increasing nuclear charge
and decreasing atomic radius.
• Statement 2 is correct. Electron affinity
generally decreases down a group as atomic
size and shielding increase, making addition
of an electron less favorable.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. Electronegativity
typically increases across a period (left →
right) due to greater nuclear attraction for
bonding electrons.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2002
Q1. Which one of the following statements is
correct?
(a) The boiling point of an aqueous solution is higher
than that of pure water.
(b) Addition of solutes to a solution causes an increase
in its water potential.
(c) The vapour pressure of the water in a solution is
lower than that of pure water.
(d) When a solution is separated from water by a
semipermeable membrane, water movement can be
prevented by applying pressure to the solution.

The correct option is (d).
• This question tests the understanding of
colligative properties and osmosis.
• (a) is conditionally correct, but not universally.
Adding a non-volatile solute to water raises
the boiling point (boiling point elevation).
However, if a volatile solute with a lower
boiling point than water (like ethanol) is
added, the boiling point of the solution could
be lower.
• (b) is incorrect. Adding solutes to pure water
makes the water potential more negative,
meaning it decreases the water potential, it
does not increase it.
• (c) is conditionally correct, but not universally.
Adding a non-volatile solute lowers the
vapour pressure (Raoult's Law). However, if a
volatile solute is added, the total vapour
pressure of the solution could be higher than
that of pure water.
• (d) is correct. This statement accurately
describes the principle of osmosis and
osmotic pressure. When a solution is
separated from pure water by a
semipermeable membrane, water naturally
moves from the pure water side (high water
potential) to the solution side (low water
potential). To stop this net movement of
water, a specific amount of pressure must be
applied to the solution — this required
pressure is defined as the osmotic pressure of
the solution.
• Given that (d) is a fundamental and
universally correct definition related to
solutions, while (a) and (c) are conditional, (d)
is the best and most accurate answer.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Match List I (Oxidation number) with List II (The
element) and select the correct answer using the
codes given below this lists:
List I (Oxidation number)
A. 2
B. 3

C. 4
D. 6
List II (The element)
1. Oxidation number of Mn in MnO₂
2. Oxidation number of S in H₂S₂O₇
3. Oxidation number of Ca in CaO
4. Oxidation number of Al in NaAlH₄
Codes:
ABCD
(a) 3 4 1 2
(b) 4 3 1 2
(c) 3 4 2 1
(d) 4 3 2 1
The correct option is (a).
• We need to calculate the oxidation number
(O.N.) for the specified element in each
compound.
• For C. 3 (O.N. of Ca in CaO): Oxygen usually
has an O.N. of -2. For the compound to be
neutral, Ca + (-2) = 0. So, the O.N. of Ca is +2.
This means A matches with 3.
• For D. 4 (O.N. of Al in NaAlH₄): Sodium (Na) is
+1. Hydrogen in metal hydrides has an O.N. of
-1. So, (+1) + Al + 4(−1) = 0* → Al − 3 = 0 →
O.N. of Al is +3. This means B matches with 4.
• For A. 1 (O.N. of Mn in MnO₂): Oxygen is -2.
So, Mn + 2(−2) = 0* → Mn − 4 = 0 → O.N. of
Mn is +4. This means C matches with 1.
• For B. 2 (O.N. of S in H₂S₂O₇): H is +1, O is -2.
So, 2(+1) + 2S + 7(−2) = 0** → 2 + 2S − 14 = 0
→ 2S − 12 = 0 → 2S = 12 → O.N. of S is +6.
This means D matches with 2.
• The correct matching is A–3, B–4, C–1, D–2.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Assertion (A): Synthetic detergents can lather
well in hard water.
Reason (R): Synthetic detergents form soluble
calcium and magnesium salts with hard water.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true

The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true. Unlike traditional soaps,
synthetic detergents are effective cleaning
agents even in hard water and produce a
good lather.
• Reason (R) is true. Hard water contains
dissolved calcium (Ca²⁺) and magnesium
(Mg²⁺) ions. Traditional soaps react with these
ions to form an insoluble precipitate (scum),
which prevents lathering. Synthetic
detergents (like alkylbenzene sulfonates) are
designed so that their calcium and
magnesium salts are soluble in water.
• R is the correct explanation of A. The very
reason detergents work well in hard water is
that they do not form an insoluble scum. By
forming soluble salts with the calcium and
magnesium ions, the detergent molecules
remain dissolved and active, allowing them
to lather and perform their cleaning function
effectively.
• Since both statements are true and the
reason correctly explains the assertion, the
answer is (a).
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2003
Q1. Which one of the following statements is
correct?
(a) Liquid Sodium is employed as a coolant in nuclear
reactors.
(b) Calcium carbonate is an ingredient of tooth paste.
(c) Bordeaux mixture consists of Sodium sulphate and
lime.
(d) Zinc amalgams are used as a dental filling.
The correct option is (a).
• Let's analyze each statement for correctness.
• (a) is correct. Liquid sodium is an excellent
heat transfer agent and is used as a coolant in
certain types of nuclear reactors, specifically
fast breeder reactors. Its high thermal
conductivity and boiling point make it very
effective at removing heat from the reactor
core.

•

•

•

•

•

(b) is correct. Calcium carbonate is commonly
used as a mild abrasive in toothpaste to help
remove plaque and surface stains from teeth.
(c) is incorrect. Bordeaux mixture is a
fungicide made from copper sulphate and
lime (calcium hydroxide), not sodium
sulphate.
(d) is incorrect. Dental amalgams are alloys of
mercury with other metals, most commonly
silver, tin, and copper. While zinc has been
used in some formulations, silver amalgam is
the standard term, not zinc amalgam.
Conclusion: Both (a) and (b) are factually
correct statements. However, in the context
of common-knowledge questions, both are
valid. The provided answer key selects (b).
Liquid sodium as a coolant is a more
specialized fact but equally true. Given the
potential for multiple correct answers, a
judgment call is needed; (a) describes a more
critical and unique industrial application.
Hence, option (a) is a correct statement, as is
(b). This is a potentially flawed question with
multiple correct answers.

Q2. Which one of the following is produced during
the formation of photochemical smog?
(a) Hydrocarbons
(b) Nitrogen Oxides
(c) Ozone
(d) Methane
The correct option is (c).
• Photochemical smog is a type of air pollution
that is formed when primary pollutants react
in the presence of sunlight.
• The primary pollutants are Nitrogen Oxides
(NOx) and Volatile Organic Compounds
(VOCs, including hydrocarbons), which are
emitted from vehicle exhaust and industrial
processes.
• When these primary pollutants are exposed
to strong sunlight, they undergo a series of
complex chemical reactions.
• A key result of these reactions is the
formation of secondary pollutants, the most

•

significant of which is ground-level Ozone
(O₃). Ozone is the main component of
photochemical smog and is responsible for
many of its harmful health effects.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q3. With reference to ionic compounds, consider the
following statements:
1. Ionic compounds are insoluble in alcohol.
2. Ionic compounds in the solid state are good
conductors of electricity.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) Only 1
(b) Only 2
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. Ionic compounds (like
salt) are polar and dissolve well in polar
solvents like water. Alcohol (ethanol) is much
less polar than water. Following the principle
of "like dissolves like," ionic compounds are
generally insoluble or have very low solubility
in alcohol.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. For a substance to
conduct electricity, it must have mobile
charged particles. In the solid state, the ions
in an ionic compound are locked in a fixed
crystal lattice and are not free to move.
Therefore, ionic compounds in the solid state
are poor conductors of electricity
(insulators). They only conduct electricity
when molten or dissolved in water, as their
ions are then free to move.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following statements is NOT
correct?
(a) The presence of NaCl increases the rate of setting
of Plaster of Paris.
(b) Gypsum is added to the cement to slow down its
rate of setting.
(c) All alkaline earth metals form hydrated salts.
(d) Barium and Strontium are found free in nature.
The correct option is (d).

•
•

•

•

•

•

This question asks to identify the incorrect
statement.
(a) is correct. Sodium chloride (NaCl) acts as
an accelerator for the setting of Plaster of
Paris (calcium sulfate hemihydrate), making
it harden faster.
(b) is correct. Gypsum is added to cement
clinker to act as a retarder, slowing down the
initial "flash set" and allowing time for the
concrete to be worked.
(c) is correct. Alkaline earth metals (Group 2)
readily form hydrated salts (e.g.,
MgSO₄·7H₂O, CaCl₂·6H₂O) because their ions
have a high charge density, which strongly
attracts water molecules.
(d) is NOT correct. Barium (Ba) and Strontium
(Sr) are highly reactive alkaline earth metals.
Due to their high reactivity, they readily form
compounds with other elements and are
never found free (as pure elements) in
nature. They are always found as minerals like
barite (BaSO₄) and celestite (SrSO₄).
Hence, option (d) is the incorrect statement.

Q5. Consider the following statements:
The purpose of adding sodium sulphate and sodium
silicate to the detergent in a washing powder is:
1. to keep the washing powder dry.
2. to maintain the alkalinity of the powder.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) Only 1
(b) Only 2
(c) both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Detergent powders are complex mixtures
with several ingredients serving different
functions.
• Sodium sulphate (Na₂SO₄) is primarily used as
a "filler" or "bulking agent." Its main purpose
is to prevent the powder from caking and
clumping, ensuring it remains a free-flowing,
dry powder that is easy to measure and
dissolves well. So, statement 1 is correct.

•

•

•

Sodium silicate (Na₂SiO₃) serves multiple
purposes. It is a "builder" that helps soften
hard water. It also hydrolyzes in water to
create an alkaline solution. This alkalinity is
crucial for effective cleaning, as it helps to
break down fats and oils. So, statement 2 is
correct.
Since both statements describe valid
functions of these additives, the correct
answer is (c). (Note: The provided answer key
says (b), which is only partially correct as it
ignores the primary role of sodium sulphate
as a flow agent).
Hence, option (c) is the most complete
answer.

Q6. Assertion (A): In the periodic table of chemical
elements, electron affinity is always found to
increase from top to bottom in a group.
Reason (R): In a group, the atomic radii generally
increase from top to bottom.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is false. Electron affinity is the
energy released when an electron is added to
a neutral atom. As you go down a group, the
atomic radius increases, and the added
electron is further from the nucleus and more
shielded. This reduces the attraction, so
electron affinity generally decreases from top
to bottom in a group (with some exceptions).
The statement says it increases, which is
incorrect.
• Reason (R) is true. As you move from top to
bottom in a group, new electron shells are
added, causing the atomic radii to generally
increase.
• Since the assertion is false but the reason is a
true statement, the correct option is (d).
(Note: The provided answer key says (a),

•

which is completely incorrect based on
established chemical principles).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q7. Assertion (A): Coal-based thermal power stations
contribute to acid-rain.
Reason (R): Oxides of carbon are emitted when coal
burns.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (b).
• Assertion (A) is true. Coal often contains
sulfur and its combustion at high
temperatures also fixes atmospheric nitrogen.
This releases sulfur dioxide (SO₂) and
nitrogen oxides (NOx) into the atmosphere.
These gases react with water vapor to form
sulfuric acid and nitric acid, which fall as acid
rain. Thus, coal power stations are a major
contributor.
• Reason (R) is true. The primary combustion
reaction of coal (which is mostly carbon)
produces oxides of carbon (CO₂ and CO).
• R is NOT the correct explanation of A. While
it is true that burning coal produces carbon
oxides, these are not the primary cause of
acid rain. Acid rain is caused by sulfur oxides
and nitrogen oxides. The reason statement,
while true on its own, does not explain why
the assertion is true.
• Since both statements are individually true
but the reason does not correctly explain the
assertion, the answer is (b).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q8. Assertion (A): Unsaturated fats are more reactive
compared with the saturated fats.
Reason (R): Unsaturated fats have only single bonds
in their structure.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A

(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true. Unsaturated fats are
chemically more reactive than saturated fats.
They are more susceptible to oxidation
(which leads to rancidity) and can undergo
addition reactions.
• Reason (R) is false. The reason for their higher
reactivity is the presence of one or more
carbon–carbon double or triple bonds in their
fatty acid chains. Saturated fats have only
single bonds. The statement incorrectly
describes the structure of unsaturated fats.
The presence of the electron-rich
double/triple bonds is what makes them
reactive sites.
• Since the assertion is true but the reason is
false, the correct option is (c).
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9. Regarding the atom of a chemical element, the
magnetic quantum number refers to:
(a) orientation
(b) shape
(c) size
(d) spin
The correct option is (a).
• In quantum mechanics, four quantum
numbers are used to describe the state of an
electron in an atom.
• The Principal Quantum Number (n) refers to
the energy level or size of the orbital.
• The Azimuthal or Angular Momentum
Quantum Number (l) refers to the shape of
the orbital (s, p, d, f).
• The Magnetic Quantum Number (mₗ)
describes the orientation of the orbital in
space relative to a magnetic field. For
example, a p-orbital (l = 1) has three possible
orientations (pₓ, pᵧ, p_z) corresponding to mₗ
= −1, 0, +1.

•

•

The Spin Quantum Number (mₛ) refers to the
intrinsic angular momentum or spin of the
electron (+1/2 or −1/2).
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2004

Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. Baking soda is used in fire extinguishers.
2. Quicklime is used in the manufacture of glass.
3. Gypsum is used in the manufacture of Plaster of
Paris.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is correct. Baking soda (sodium
bicarbonate) is the active component in some
types of dry chemical fire extinguishers (e.g.,
for Class B and C fires).
• When heated, it decomposes and releases
carbon dioxide gas, which smothers the fire
by displacing oxygen.
• Statement 2 is correct. Quicklime (calcium
oxide, CaO) is a fundamental ingredient in the
most common type of glass, soda-lime glass.
• It acts as a stabilizer, making the glass
insoluble in water and increasing its
durability.
• Statement 3 is correct. Plaster of Paris is
produced by heating gypsum (calcium sulfate
dihydrate, CaSO₄·2H₂O) to about 150°C.
• This process drives off some of the water
molecules to form calcium sulfate
hemihydrate (CaSO₄·½H₂O), which is Plaster
of Paris.
• Since all three statements are correct, the
answer is (d).
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Salts of which of the following elements provide
colours to fireworks?
(a) Zinc and sulphur

(b) Potassium and mercury
(c) Strontium and barium
(d) Chromium and nickel
The correct option is (c).
• The vibrant colors in fireworks are created by
adding specific metallic salts that emit light of
a characteristic color when heated.
• This is known as pyrotechnic colorimetry.
• Strontium salts are used to produce a brilliant
red color.
• Barium salts are used to produce a bright
green color.
• Other common colorants include sodium for
yellow/orange, copper for blue, and calcium
for orange.
• The elements in the other options are not
used as primary colorants. Zinc and sulphur
are used as fuels or smoke agents, and
mercury compounds are toxic and not used.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Match List-I (Fuel Gases) with List-II (Major
Constituents) and select the correct answer using the
codes given below the Lists:
List-I (Fuel Gases)
A. CNG
B. Coal gas
C. LPG
D. Water gas
List-II (Major Constituents)
1. Carbon monoxide, Hydrogen
2. Butane, Propane
3. Methane, Ethane
4. Hydrogen, Methane, Carbon monoxide
Codes:
ABCD
(a) 2 1 3 4
(b) 3 4 2 1
(c) 2 4 3 1
(d) 3 1 2 4
The correct option is (b).
• A. CNG (Compressed Natural Gas): Is
primarily composed of natural gas, which is
mostly methane, with some ethane. So, A
matches with 3.

•

•

•

•
•

B. Coal gas: Is produced by the destructive

chiefly hydrogen, methane, and carbon
monoxide. So, B matches with 4.
