Q4. Standard 18-carat gold sold in the market
contains:
(a) 82 parts gold and 18 parts other metals
(b) 18 parts gold and 82 parts other metals
(c) 18 parts gold and 6 parts other metals
(d) 9 parts gold and 15 parts other metals
The correct option is (c).
• The purity of gold is measured in carats (k),
which is a system based on 24 parts.
• Pure gold is 24-carat (24k).
• 18-carat gold means that out of a total of 24
parts, 18 parts are pure gold and the
remaining 6 parts (24 - 18 = 6) are other
metals (like copper, silver, or zinc) added to
form an alloy.
• This corresponds to 75% pure gold (18/24 =
0.75) and 25% other metals.
• Therefore, the correct description is 18 parts
gold and 6 parts other metals.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2005
Q1. Consider the following statements:
1. Anhydrous sodium carbonate is commonly known
as baking soda.
2. Baking soda is used in fire extinguishers.
3. Bleaching powder is manufactured in the
Hasenclever plant.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 3 only
(d) 1 and 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Anhydrous sodium
carbonate (Na₂CO₃) is commonly known as
soda ash or washing soda. Baking soda is the
common name for sodium bicarbonate
(NaHCO₃).
• Statement 2 is correct. Baking soda (sodium
bicarbonate) is used in certain types of dry
chemical fire extinguishers. When heated, it
decomposes to release carbon dioxide (CO₂)
gas, which smothers the fire by displacing
oxygen.
• Statement 3 is correct. The Hasenclever plant
is an industrial apparatus designed for the
large-scale manufacture of bleaching powder
(calcium oxychloride, CaOCl₂) by reacting
chlorine gas with slaked lime.
• Since statements 2 and 3 are correct, the
answer is (b).
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. Assertion (A): The main constituent of Liquified
Petroleum Gas is methane.
Reason (R): Methane can be used directly for burning
in homes and factories where it can be supplied
through pipelines.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is false. The main constituents
of Liquefied Petroleum Gas (LPG) are
propane and butane, not methane.
• Methane is the main constituent of Natural
Gas, which is supplied via pipelines and also
compressed as CNG, not LPG, which is stored
in cylinders.

•

•
•

Reason (R) is true. Methane (as natural gas)
is used directly for burning in homes and
factories, as it is an efficient and cleanburning fuel supplied through pipelines.
Since the assertion is false but the reason is
true, the correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q3. Which one of the following is the correct
sequence of the given substances in the decreasing
order of their densities?
(a) Steel > Mercury > Gold
(b) Gold > Mercury > Steel
(c) Steel > Gold > Mercury
(d) Gold > Steel > Mercury
The correct option is (b).
• We need to arrange the substances from
most dense to least dense.
• Gold (Au): Has a very high density of
approximately 19.3 g/cm³.
• Mercury (Hg): A dense liquid metal with a
density of approximately 13.6 g/cm³.
• Steel: An iron alloy with an average density of
about 7.85 g/cm³.
• Therefore, the correct decreasing order of
density is Gold > Mercury > Steel.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2006
Q1. Consider the following chemicals: 1. Benzene 2.
Carbon tetrachloride 3. Sodium Carbonate 4.
Trichloroethylene
Which of the above is/are used as dry cleaning
chemicals?
(a) 1 Only
(b) 2 Only
(c) 1, 2 and 4 Only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
The correct option is (c).
• Dry cleaning is a cleaning process that uses a
chemical solvent instead of water. The
solvents are chosen for their ability to dissolve
oils and grease.

•

1. Benzene: A powerful organic solvent, it was
used as a dry cleaning agent in the past. Its
use has been discontinued due to its high
flammability and carcinogenic nature.
• 2. Carbon tetrachloride (CCl₄): This is another
effective non-polar solvent that was widely
used for dry cleaning. It was phased out
because of its high toxicity and its role in
ozone layer depletion.
• 3. Sodium Carbonate (Na₂CO₃): Known as
washing soda, this is a water-soluble salt used
as a builder and water softener in traditional
laundry detergents (i.e., with water). It is not
a dry cleaning solvent.
• 4. Trichloroethylene (TCE): This is a strong
chlorinated solvent that has been used in dry
cleaning, especially for industrial degreasing.
Its use has also been reduced due to health
concerns.
• Since Benzene, Carbon tetrachloride, and
Trichloroethylene have all been used as dry
cleaning solvents at various times, this option
is correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. What are the materials generally employed as
solder in soldering operations?
(a) Iron and tin
(b) Lead and tin
(c) Aluminum and lead
(d) Aluminum and iron
The correct option is (b).
• Solder is a metal alloy with a low melting
point used to join two or more metal pieces
together without melting the pieces
themselves.
• The most traditional and widely used type of
solder for electronics and plumbing for many
decades has been an alloy of lead and tin.
• The ratio of tin to lead can be varied to create
solders with different melting points and
mechanical properties. A common
formulation was 60% tin and 40% lead (60/40
solder).

•

While lead-free solders (often containing tin,
copper, and silver) are now mandated for
many applications due to lead's toxicity, the
classic and general answer for materials used
as solder is lead and tin.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2007
Q1. Which one among the following is called
philosopher's wool?
(a) Zinc bromide
(b) Zinc nitrate
(c) Zinc oxide
(d) Zinc chloride
The correct option is (c).
• Philosopher's wool is an old alchemical name
for zinc oxide (ZnO).
• The name originated from the process by
which it was made — alchemists (then called
philosophers) burned zinc metal in air.
• The combustion process produces a fine,
white, fluffy powder of zinc oxide that
collects in airy clumps, resembling tufts of
wool.
• Hence, the substance was called
"philosopher's wool."
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. Which one of the following does not contain
silver?
(a) Horn Silver
(b) German silver
(c) Ruby silver
(d) Lunar caustic
The correct option is (b).
• German silver, also known as nickel silver, is a
copper alloy containing nickel and zinc.
• Despite its name and silvery appearance, it
contains no elemental silver.
• Horn Silver is an old name for cerargyrite, a
natural form of silver chloride (AgCl).
• Ruby Silver refers to minerals like proustite,
which contain silver, arsenic, and sulfur.

•

•
•

Lunar Caustic is silver nitrate (AgNO₃), used
historically for its caustic and antiseptic
properties.
The term "lunar" relates to silver’s alchemical
association with the moon (luna).
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. Which one of the following types of glass can cut
off ultraviolet rays?
(a) Soda glass
(b) Pyrex glass
(c) Jena glass
(d) Crookes glass
The correct option is (d).
• Crookes glass is a special glass formulated by
Sir William Crookes, containing cerium oxide.
• The cerium oxide component allows it to
absorb harmful ultraviolet (UV) radiation
while remaining transparent to visible light.
• Due to this property, Crookes glass is used for
lenses in sunglasses and protective eyewear.
• Other types of glass—Soda glass, Pyrex
(borosilicate) glass, and Jena glass—are made
for different purposes such as chemical
resistance or thermal stability, not UV
protection.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following non-metals is not a
poor conductor of electricity?
(a) Sulphur
(b) Selenium
(c) Bromine
(d) Phosphorus
The correct option is (b).
• Most non-metals are poor conductors of
electricity.
• Sulphur, Bromine, and Phosphorus are
insulators.
• Selenium (Se), however, is a semiconductor—
its electrical conductivity increases with light
exposure, a property known as
photoconductivity.
• This makes selenium not a poor conductor in
comparison with other non-metals.

•
•

It is used in photocopiers, light meters, and
solar cells due to this property.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. Which one of the following is another name for
RDX?
(a) Cyanohydrin
(b) Selenium
(c) Cyclohexane
(d) Cyclonite
The correct option is (d).
• RDX (Research Department Explosive) is a
powerful military explosive, chemically
known as Cyclotrimethylenetrinitramine.
• It is also known by alternative names such as
Cyclonite, Hexogen, and T4 (in different
contexts).
• Cyclonite is the most commonly used
synonym.
• Cyanohydrin and Cyclohexane are organic
compounds, not explosives, and Selenium is a
chemical element.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2008
Q1. Which one of the following pairs of metals
constitutes the lightest metal and the heaviest
metal, respectively?
(a) Lithium and mercury
(b) Lithium and osmium
(c) Aluminium and osmimum
(d) Aluminium and mercury
The correct option is (b).
• The "lightest" and "heaviest" metals refer to
their density (mass per unit volume).
• Lightest Metal: Lithium (Li) is an alkali metal
and is the least dense of all metals, and
indeed the least dense of all solid elements
under standard conditions.
• Its density is only 0.534 g/cm³, which is so low
that it can float on water (and oil).
• Heaviest Metal: Osmium (Os) is a platinumgroup metal and is the densest naturally
occurring element.

•
•

•

Its density is extremely high at approximately
22.59 g/cm³. Iridium is a very close second.
Therefore, the correct pair representing the
lightest and heaviest metals is Lithium and
Osmium.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. A mixture of which one of the following pairs of
gases is the cause of occurrence of most of the
explosions in mines?
(a) Hydrogen and oxygen
(b) Oxygen and acetylene
(c) Methane and air
(d) Carbon dioxide and methane
The correct option is (c).
• Underground coal seams naturally release a
flammable gas known as "firedamp."
• The primary component of firedamp is
methane (CH₄).
• When this methane mixes with the air in the
mine tunnels within a specific concentration
range (the explosive or flammable range,
typically 5–15%), it forms a highly explosive
mixture.
• If this mixture is ignited by a spark from
machinery, static electricity, or an open flame,
it can cause a catastrophic explosion.
• This is a well-known and major hazard in coal
mining.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following is also called Stranger
Gas?
(a) Argon
(b) Neon
(c) Xenon
(d) Nitrous oxide
The correct option is (c).
• The noble gas Xenon (Xe) gets its name from
the Greek word xenos, which means
"stranger," "foreign," or "guest."
• It was discovered in 1898 by William Ramsay
and Morris Travers, who found it as a trace
component left over after evaporating most
of the components of liquid air.

•
•
•

Because it was an unknown and rare residual
gas, they named it "the stranger gas."
Argon means "lazy" or "inactive," and Neon
means "new."
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. What are Rubies and Sapphires chemically
known as?
(a) Silicon dioxide
(b) Aluminum oxide
(c) Lead tetraoxide
(d) Boron nitride
The correct option is (b).
• Both rubies and sapphires are gemstone
varieties of the same mineral, corundum.
• The chemical formula for corundum is
Aluminum oxide (Al₂O₃).
• The different colors are caused by trace
amounts of other elements acting as
impurities within the aluminum oxide crystal
lattice.
• A ruby is red corundum, with the color caused
by the presence of chromium.
• A sapphire refers to any other color of gemquality corundum, most famously blue
(caused by iron and titanium), but also
yellow, pink, green, etc.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. What is Bisphenol A (BPA)?
(a) A Medical test for detecting cancer
(b) A test for testing the use of drugs to improve
performance by athletes
(c) A chemical used for the development of foodpackaging materials
(d) A special type of alloy steel
The correct option is (c).
• Bisphenol A (BPA) is an industrial organic
chemical that has been widely used for
several decades.
• Its primary application is as a monomer in the
production of polycarbonate plastics and
epoxy resins.
• These materials are commonly used to make a
wide variety of consumer goods, including

•

•

food-packaging materials such as reusable
water bottles, baby bottles, and as a
protective lining on the inside of metal food
and beverage cans.
Its use has become controversial due to
concerns about its potential health effects as
an endocrine disruptor.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2009

Q1. Which one of the following is used as an
explosive?
(a) Phosphorus trichloride
(b) Mercuric oxide
(c) Graphite
(d) Nitroglycerine
The correct option is (d).
• Nitroglycerine is a heavy, colorless, oily, and
highly sensitive liquid explosive.
• It is an ester of glycerol and nitric acid.
• Upon detonation, it decomposes rapidly,
releasing a large volume of gas and heat,
which produces a powerful shockwave.
• Due to its instability, pure nitroglycerine is
rarely used directly. Instead, it is the primary
active ingredient in dynamite, where it is
absorbed into kieselguhr (a stabilizing
medium) to make it safer to handle.
• It is also a major component of smokeless
gunpowders.
• The other options are not explosives:
o Phosphorus trichloride is a chemical
reagent.
o Mercuric oxide is a chemical
compound.
o Graphite is a form of carbon.
• Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. In a dry cell (battery), which of the following are
used as electrolytes?
(a) Ammonium chloride and Zinc chloride
(b) Sodium chloride and Calcium chloride
(c) Magnesium chloride and Zinc chloride
(d) Ammonium chloride and Calcium chloride

The correct option is (a).
• The dry cell battery (Leclanché cell) is a
common, non-rechargeable type of battery.
• The negative electrode is the zinc casing,
while the positive electrode is a carbon
(graphite) rod at the center.
• The electrolyte is a moist paste, not a free
liquid, that allows ion conduction.
• This electrolyte paste consists of Ammonium
chloride (NH₄Cl) and Zinc chloride (ZnCl₂).
• It also contains manganese dioxide (MnO₂) as
a depolarizer and carbon powder to improve
conductivity.
• Ammonium chloride acts as the primary
electrolyte, facilitating the flow of ions during
the electrochemical reaction.
• Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2010
Q1. Chlorination is a process used for water
purification. The disinfecting action of chlorine is
mainly due to:
(a) The formation of hydrochloric acid when chlorine
is added to water
(b) The formation of hypochlorous acid when chlorine
is added to water
(c) The formation of nascent oxygen when chlorine is
added to water
(d) The formation of hydrogen when chlorine is added
to water
The correct option is (b).
• When chlorine (Cl₂) is added to water (H₂O),
it reacts to form two acids: hydrochloric acid
(HCl) and hypochlorous acid (HOCl).
• The reaction is: Cl₂ + H₂O ⇌ HOCl + HCl.
• It is the hypochlorous acid (HOCl) that acts as
the primary disinfecting agent.
• HOCl is a powerful but unstable oxidizing
agent that kills microorganisms by
penetrating their cell walls and disrupting
essential enzymes and cellular structures.
• While HOCl can decompose to release
nascent oxygen (O), the main disinfectant
molecule is the hypochlorous acid itself.

•
•

The formation of HCl is a byproduct and does
not contribute to disinfection.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. Consider the following:
1. Oxides of Hydrogen
2. Oxides of Nitrogen
3. Oxides of Sulphur
Which of the above causes/cause acid rain?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Acid rain is caused when pollutants in the
atmosphere react with water, oxygen, and
other chemicals, forming acidic compounds.
• Oxides of Sulphur (SOx) — mainly sulfur
dioxide (SO₂) — are emitted from burning
fossil fuels like coal.
o In the atmosphere, SO₂ reacts with
water to form sulfuric acid (H₂SO₄), a
major component of acid rain.
• Oxides of Nitrogen (NOx) — such as nitrogen
dioxide (NO₂) — are emitted from vehicles
and industrial combustion.
o They react with water to form nitric
acid (HNO₃), another contributor to
acid rain.
• Oxides of Hydrogen (H₂O) are not acidic and
act only as a medium for these reactions, not
as a cause.
• Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Excessive release of the pollutant carbon
monoxide (CO) into the air may produce a condition
in which oxygen supply to the human body
decreases. What causes this condition?
(a) When inhaled into the human body, CO is
converted into CO₂
(b) The inhaled CO has a much higher affinity for
haemoglobin as compared to oxygen
(c) The inhaled CO destroys the chemical structure of
haemoglobin

(d) The inhaled CO adversely affects the respiratory
centre in the brain
The correct option is (b).
• Oxygen (O₂) is transported from the lungs to
tissues by binding with haemoglobin (Hb) in
red blood cells.
• When carbon monoxide (CO) is inhaled, it
enters the bloodstream and also binds to
haemoglobin.
• CO has 200–250 times greater affinity for
haemoglobin than oxygen.
• This forms a stable compound called
carboxyhaemoglobin (HbCO).
• Once CO binds, it blocks oxygen from binding
and being transported, drastically reducing
oxygen delivery to tissues.
• This leads to hypoxia (oxygen deficiency) in
vital organs, which can be fatal at high
exposures.
• Hence, option (b) is correct.

o

•

•

UPSC PYQs - 2011
Q1. A company marketing food products advertises
that its items do not contain trans-fats. What does
this campaign signify to the customers?
1. The food products are not made out of
hydrogenated oils.
2. The food products are not made out of animal
fats/oils.
3. The oils used are not likely to damage the
cardiovascular health of the consumers.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1:
o Artificial trans-fats are created
through an industrial process called
hydrogenation, where hydrogen is
added to liquid vegetable oils to
make them semi-solid (as in
margarine or vanaspati ghee).

•

A label claiming “no trans-fats”
means the product does not use
partially hydrogenated oils.
o Hence, statement 1 is correct.
Statement 2:
o Some animal fats (from cows, sheep,
etc.) naturally contain small amounts
of trans-fats, but these are not the
main concern in public health
campaigns.
o The “no trans-fats” label refers
specifically to the absence of
industrial/artificial trans-fats, not
necessarily the absence of all animal
fats.
o Hence, statement 2 is incorrect.
Statement 3:
o Artificial trans-fats are strongly linked
to poor cardiovascular health — they
increase LDL (“bad” cholesterol) and
lower HDL (“good” cholesterol).
o Therefore, foods without trans-fats
are less likely to harm heart health.
o Hence, statement 3 is correct.
Since statements 1 and 3 are correct,
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2012

Q1. The increasing amount of carbon dioxide in the
air is slowly raising the temperature of the
atmosphere, because it absorbs:
(a) the water vapour of the air and retains its heat
(b) the UV part of the solar radiation
(c) all the solar radiations
(d) the infrared part of the solar radiation
The correct option is (d).
• The question explains the mechanism of the
greenhouse effect, which is the main cause of
global warming.
• Solar energy reaches the Earth mainly as
short-wavelength radiation — visible light
and ultraviolet (UV) rays.

•

•

•

•

•

•

•
•

The atmosphere is transparent to most of this
incoming radiation, allowing it to reach and
warm the Earth's surface.
The warmed Earth then emits energy back as
long-wavelength infrared (IR) radiation
(heat).
Greenhouse gases like carbon dioxide (CO₂),
methane (CH₄), and water vapour (H₂O)
absorb this outgoing infrared radiation due to
their molecular structure.
After absorbing the IR radiation, these gases
re-radiate the energy in all directions,
including back toward the Earth’s surface,
trapping heat and warming the lower
atmosphere.
CO₂ does not absorb UV radiation — that
function is primarily performed by the ozone
layer.
It also does not absorb all solar radiation, as
most visible light passes through the
atmosphere freely.
The infrared radiation is specifically what CO₂
absorbs, causing atmospheric heating.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2014

Q1.Which of the following is/are examples of
chemical change?
1. Crystallization of sodium chloride
2. Melting of ice
3. Souring of milk
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) None
Correct Option: (b)
• Crystallization of sodium chloride:
o Only a physical change — the salt
changes from a solution to solid
crystals.
o No new substance is formed;
chemical composition remains same.
• Melting of ice:

o

Also a physical change — water
changes from solid to liquid state.
o Reversible process, no new substance
formed.
• Souring of milk:
o A chemical change, as milk gets
converted into lactic acid due to
bacterial action.
o New substances with different taste,
smell, and properties are formed.
o The process is irreversible.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
